# Ducky 2.0 Specification

Status: **authoritative** (supersedes the three proposals under `_design/proposals/`).
Base: proposal *correctness-first*, with ideas grafted from *minimal-core* and *dx-first* as the judges recommended. Every decision that departs from the matrix, or that settles a matrix conflict, has an ADR under `adr/`.
Inputs: `_analysis/matrix.json` (71 features, 18 conflicts, 25 breaking changes, 20 open questions, quality baseline), `_analysis/repos.json`, the Ducky 1.x, Mutty and sibling sources, and Fallout 10.4.0 (`../../public/Fallout`).

Normative language: **MUST**, **MUST NOT**, **SHOULD** and **MAY** follow RFC 2119. Every behavioural rule points to an invariant (`INV-nn`, §4). Every invariant is enforced in exactly one place and has named tests and a gate that runs them. If something in 2.0 can't state an invariant, it doesn't ship.

## 1. Goals and non-goals

### 1.1 Goals

1. One small, strict Redux store for .NET and Blazor. Typed slices with pure `On<TAction>` reducers that match on the exact runtime type. Single-drainer dispatch. Immutable snapshots that are read without a lock.
2. Every concurrency and lifecycle claim is an invariant with a named test that runs against the real store (§4, §17).
3. The same load-effect rule works in every Blazor render mode: WASM, Interactive Server, Interactive Auto, and static SSR with prerender (§11.3).
4. AOT and trimming safety is enforced by CI: `IsAotCompatible`, zero IL warnings, a NativeAOT smoke test and a trimmed AOT-WASM smoke test (§10).
5. The promoted scope ships in 2.0: the Zustand-style `SliceStore` (API-01), `Ducky.Reactive` on R3 (EFF-04), `Ducky.Draft` with an Immer-style `Produce` that absorbs Mutty (IMM-02), and a server-side `IDistributedCache` persistence provider (PER-04 subset).
6. Loud, precise failures. Configuration errors are coded, reported together, and carry the exact fix (§8.3). Misuse is caught by analyzers wherever possible (§16).
7. Maximal quality bar: 100% line and branch coverage of all library C#, Stryker mutation score ≥ 90% target with the build breaking below 85%, property tests, named concurrency tests, Playwright for the JS, the AOT gate, PublicAPI tracking, package validation, required CI, warnings as errors (§17).

### 1.2 Non-goals for 2.0 (matrix decision `later` or `reject`, kept that way)

- **Later**: EFF-03 stream effects, EFF-06 reducer-returned commands, EFF-07 state-driven subscriptions, EFF-08 optimistic updates, EFF-09 undo/redo, CMP-03 component-scoped stores, CMP-06 SelectKey, GEN-03 generated reducers (benchmark first), GEN-07 auto-aggregated registration, SSR-04 rich hydration metadata, DT-03 in-app DevTools panel, DX-02 `dotnet new` template, the IndexedDB provider, DUCKY007, and analyzer code fixes (deferred to 2.1, ADR-0038).
- **Rejected**: MW-03 order validator, EFF-05 reactive grab-bag, EFF-10 resilience/debounce middleware, CMP-02 generated component bases, CMP-04 render guard and error boundary, PER-05 per-save lifecycle actions, SSR-05 cross-circuit hubs, DT-05 console logger, GEN-04 speculative generators, REJ-01/02, the MemoryPack serializer, net8.0 targets, a 1.x persisted-data reader, onion middleware, `IAction` markers, and the 1.x `Ducky.Blazor.Router` feature (`RouterStoreState`, `RouterReducers`, `Router*Action`): routing stays out of the store, and the matrix never carried it (§22.1).

## 2. Owner decisions (binding) and departures from the matrix

### 2.1 Binding owner decisions

| Decision | Where applied |
|---|---|
| Package IDs `Ducky` / `Ducky.Blazor` 2.0.0; `net10.0` only | §3, ADR-0003 |
| `Ducky.Generator` deprecated; generator and analyzers ship in `Ducky` under `analyzers/dotnet/cs` | §3, §16, ADR-0002, ADR-0045 |
| Build and release with Fallout (NUKE fork, `Fallout.*` 10.4.0) | §19–21, ADR-0035 |
| In scope: SliceStore (API-01), Ducky.Reactive on R3 (EFF-04), Ducky.Draft absorbing Mutty (IMM-02), `IDistributedCache` provider (PER-04 subset) | §12–14, §11.6 |
| Maximal quality bar | §17, ADR-0037 |
| Clean rewrite on a new branch of phmatray/Ducky; concepts ported selectively | §18, ADR-0001 |
| `IAction` markers removed | ADR-0021 |
| 1.x persisted data discarded (no migration reader) | §11.5, ADR-0033 |
| R3 for Rx | §14, ADR-0006 |

### 2.2 Departures from the matrix sketches

| # | Matrix says | 2.0 does | Why | ADR |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Key = namespace + type name, kebab | Key = type name only (nested: `Outer-Inner`), kebab, with one `Slice`/`Store`/`Reducer(s)` suffix removed. Duplicates are a configuration error | Storage keys survive namespace refactors. DevTools labels stay readable | 0031 |
| D2 | Previous state kept on reducer throw | Atomic: if any slice reducer throws, no slice commits for that action | A partly applied action is torn state (INV-08) | 0023 |
| D3 | `DispatchAsync` returns `Task` | `Task<DispatchResult>`. It never faults or cancels and always completes | Every outcome is observable and testable (INV-10) | 0024 |
| D4 | `ActionContext.Abort()` + `Metadata` | Removed. `MayDispatch` is the only veto. `ActionContext` is immutable | One veto path, one test matrix | 0004 |
| D5 | Singleton in the browser, Scoped on the server | Kept (the matrix is right; correctness-first's "Scoped everywhere" is rejected). A test asserts one store per browser app | Avoids a split store between the root provider and the renderer scope in WASM (INV-22) | 0025 |
| D6 | Hydration gate = built-in middleware | The store's own init buffer is the gate. It lives in the dispatcher core, is bounded, and has an overall init timeout | One buffer, one FIFO rule, one load-effect rule in every render mode (INV-13) | 0015 |
| D7 | Seed persisted in `OnPersisting` | The seed is written only from a quiescent store (`WhenIdleAsync` with a timeout; long-running effects excluded) | A seed taken mid-load would be marked `WasRestored` and stay stuck (INV-15) | 0027 |
| D8 | Switch cancels the previous run | A superseded or disposed run also can't dispatch through `EffectContext` | A run that ignores its token can't publish stale results (INV-11) | 0024 |
| D9 | Change check after each reduce | The change check runs on the component's renderer, through one coalesced `InvokeAsync` per component | Reading `[Parameter]` fields on a thread-pool drainer races the renderer (INV-19) | 0028 |
| D10 | CircuitHandler + first render | No `CircuitHandler`. Interactivity comes from `ComponentBase.RendererInfo.IsInteractive` or `OperatingSystem.IsBrowser()` | `CircuitHandler` is server-only and can't be referenced from a WASM-compatible RCL | 0029 |
| D11 | Values cross interop as strings | JS→.NET payloads larger than `InlinePayloadBytes` (16 KiB) go through `IJSStreamReference` | Blazor Server's 32 KB `MaximumReceiveMessageSize` would otherwise close the circuit | 0030 |
| D12 | Causal depth flows through effects | Depth measures **synchronous** causality. A dispatch after a real asynchronous yield starts a new chain at depth 0 and keeps the correlation id | Legitimate polling effects must outlive 64 ticks. Synchronous loops are still stopped (INV-06) | 0026 |
| D13 | FsCheck | CsCheck (`SampleParallel` linearizability against a sequential model) | Concurrency correctness is checked, not just sampled | 0034 |
| D14 | 100% coverage | Plus an exclusion ban: no `[ExcludeFromCodeCoverage]` in `src/`, no reasonless `Stryker disable`, no unjustified `#pragma warning disable`, and no coverage or Stryker configuration that narrows what is measured or mutated (§17.1) | Otherwise the bar can be gamed | 0037 |
| D15 | `IPersistenceProvider` public | Internal. Storage is chosen with the `PersistStorage { Local, Session, Server }` enum | Smaller surface. It becomes public when IndexedDB lands (2.1) | 0016 |
| D16 | `HydrationStatus` via a service | A library slice, `PersistenceState`, changed only by the drainer and selectable like any state | Single writer. Loading UIs use the normal `Select` | 0015 |
| D17 | `SyncAcrossTabs` on any storage | `Local` only. Anything else is a configuration error | sessionStorage fires no cross-tab event. Server-cache cross-tab is not designed | 0046 |
| D18 | Missing/malformed envelope falls back | An envelope with a *newer* version (rollback) is also treated as not found (Warning), on hydration and on cross-tab receipt | The code can't migrate forward from the future | 0047 |
| D19 | Built-in fixed stages | Built-ins are ordinary internal `Middleware` subclasses registered through public API. No `StoreExtension`, and no `InternalsVisibleTo` between shipped packages | The core is covered and kept honest through its public API | 0032 |
| D20 | DevTools auto-enables through the host environment | Explicit `UseDevTools()`, guarded by the app's own `IsDevelopment()` | No host-environment dependency in a WASM-safe RCL | 0043 |
| D21 | `DuckySelectorComponent`, `ISelection`, `SelectResult` | One `Selection<T>` type everywhere (components, `<DuckySelect>`, `IStore.Select`) | One concept, one type | 0040 |
| D22 | 1.x types simply removed | Error-level `[Obsolete]` tombstones for the 1.x types user code derives from | One precise error per usage instead of a CS0246 cascade | 0038 |

## 3. Packages and dependencies

All packages: version 2.0.0, Apache-2.0 (ADR-0042), SourceLink, deterministic, symbols (`.snupkg`) for the five library packages (the assembly-less tombstone has none), strong-named with one checked-in key (used by the `@ducky/` key reservation, §6.1). The runtime libraries (Ducky, Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive, Ducky.Draft, Ducky.Testing) target `net10.0` only and set `IsAotCompatible=true` unconditionally in each csproj (never behind a condition in `Directory.Build.props`). The generator projects (netstandard2.0, where the SDK rejects `IsAotCompatible` with NETSDK1210) and the tombstone package project are excluded. The `Compile` target fails if a packable net10.0 project under `src/` does not set the property.

| Package | nupkg contents | Runtime dependencies | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ducky** | `lib/net10.0/Ducky.dll`; `analyzers/dotnet/cs/Ducky.Generators.dll` (generators and analyzers, netstandard2.0); `buildTransitive/Ducky.targets` (DUCKY900, DUCKY902) | `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions` 10.0.x, `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Abstractions` 10.0.x | STJ, `TimeProvider`, `ActivitySource`, `Meter`, `System.Threading.Lock`, frozen and immutable collections are all in-box |
| **Ducky.Blazor** | `lib/net10.0/Ducky.Blazor.dll`; `staticwebassets/ducky.js`; the Razor SDK's generated `build/Ducky.Blazor.props` and `buildTransitive/Ducky.Blazor.props` (static web assets manifest import) | Ducky, `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.Web` 10.0.x, `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.Authorization` 10.0.x, `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Abstractions` 10.0.x | `Sdk="Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Razor"` with `<SupportedPlatform Include="browser" />`: only the Razor SDK turns `wwwroot/ducky.js` into a static web asset that consuming apps serve at `_content/Ducky.Blazor/ducky.js` (a plain-SDK pack compiles but 404s at run time; PackageSmoke step 5 checks the published output, §17.9). WASM-safe: no `Microsoft.AspNetCore.App` framework reference, no `CircuitHandler`, no host-environment dependency. No `.razor` files: components are C# with hand-written `BuildRenderTree` |
| **Ducky.Reactive** | `lib/net10.0/Ducky.Reactive.dll` | Ducky, `R3` (1.3.x, Renovate major ignore). The exact pin lives only in `Directory.Packages.props` (a plain `Version="1.3.x"`, never a bracketed range) and the lock file; the packed nuspec dependency is the minimum `R3 >= 1.3.x`, so a consumer on a newer R3 gets no NU1107/NU1608 (PackageSmoke step 2) | Built only on Ducky's public API |
| **Ducky.Draft** | `lib/net10.0/Ducky.Draft.dll`; `analyzers/dotnet/cs/Ducky.Draft.Generators.dll`; `buildTransitive/Ducky.Draft.targets` (DUCKY901) | none | Does **not** depend on Ducky. It is Mutty's successor for non-Ducky users too |
| **Ducky.Testing** | `lib/net10.0/Ducky.Testing.dll` | Ducky, `Microsoft.Extensions.TimeProvider.Testing`, `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection` | No test-framework dependency. Assertions throw `DuckyAssertionException` |
| ~~Ducky.Generator~~ | final **2.0.0 tombstone package**: no assemblies, only `buildTransitive/Ducky.Generator.targets`, which raises DUCKY900 with the migration link | none | All versions deprecated on nuget.org, alternate package `Ducky` (ADR-0045). `src/Ducky.Generator.Tombstone` is a `Microsoft.Build.NoTargets` project (no assembly, so it is on no coverage or AOT list) with a fixed `Version=2.0.0`, `MinVerSkip=true`, `EnablePackageValidation=false`, `IncludeSymbols=false` (no `.snupkg`, no NU5017), `SuppressDependenciesWhenPacking=true` (no empty `net10.0` dependency group, so no NU5128 under warnings as errors) and `PackageOutputPath=$(RepoRoot)artifacts/tombstone/` (anchored at the repository root: `RepoRoot` is `$(MSBuildThisFileDirectory)` of the root `Directory.Build.props`, §18; a relative path would resolve against the project directory and land in `src/Ducky.Generator.Tombstone/artifacts/`). `Pack` packs it on every run, into that directory only, for PackageSmoke's local feed; `Publish` pushes it **once**, only when the tag is exactly `v2.0.0`, never for a prerelease or patch tag (§19, §21) |
| ~~Mutty~~ | not republished | – | All versions deprecated, alternate package `Ducky.Draft`. The deprecation message and `docs/migration/mutty.md` say that Ducky.Draft needs net10.0 and that the last Mutty release keeps working, unmaintained, on older TFMs. Repository archived after one Ducky.Draft minor |

Rules:
- **R-PKG-1** No package may bring `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.*`, `xunit*`, `bunit`, `Microsoft.Reactive.Testing`, `System.Reactive`, `FakeItEasy` or a `Microsoft.AspNetCore.App` framework reference into a consumer's restore graph. `PackageSmoke` enforces this (§17.9).
- **R-PKG-2** No `InternalsVisibleTo` from a shipped assembly to another shipped assembly. `InternalsVisibleTo` to the package's own test projects is allowed (Ducky.Blazor has two: `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` and `Ducky.Blazor.Generated.Tests`, §18), and so is `InternalsVisibleTo` to `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`, the one shared real-interleaving project (it holds the Ducky.Blazor `SubscriptionCore` race test, §17.3).
- **R-PKG-3** Generator assemblies target netstandard2.0 and compile against the `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` version shipped with SDK 10.0.100 (expected 5.0.0; spike S-1 confirms). It is pinned in `Directory.Packages.props` with a Renovate ignore rule, and packed with `IncludeBuildOutput=false` and `PrivateAssets=all`. Analyzers and generators only; no `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.Workspaces` reference (RS1038).
- **R-PKG-4** No Fluxor-derived or EF Core-derived code is carried over. The generators use a new ~40-line `CodeWriter`. Mutty's own code (same owner, Apache-2.0) keeps its NOTICE attribution.
- **R-PKG-5** Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive and Ducky.Testing reference the Ducky project with `PrivateAssets="none"`. Otherwise `dotnet pack` writes their Ducky dependency as `exclude="Build,Analyzers"`, and an app that installs only Ducky.Blazor (the normal install) would get neither `Ducky.Generators.dll` nor `buildTransitive/Ducky.targets`. `PackageSmoke` asserts that no nuspec dependency on Ducky carries an `exclude` (§17.9). Generator assets never flow through a `ProjectReference` (the default `PrivateAssets` of a `PackageReference` includes `analyzers;build`), so every project that declares actions, slices or `[Draftable]` records needs its own direct `Ducky` (or `Ducky.Draft`) `PackageReference`. The getting-started guide and `docs/diagnostics/DUCKY016.md` say so. Inside this repository, consumers that reference `src/` projects load the generators and `buildTransitive/Ducky.targets` through the `DuckyUseGenerators`/`DuckyUseDraftGenerator` switches of `Directory.Build.targets` (§18); PackageSmoke stays the check of the packaged path.
- **R-PKG-6** `Directory.Build.props` sets `IsPackable=false`; only the six shipping projects (Ducky, Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive, Ducky.Draft, Ducky.Testing, Ducky.Generator.Tombstone) set `IsPackable=true`, so samples, benchmarks and test projects can never be pushed under the `Ducky.*` IDs. `Pack` empties both directories first, then fails unless `artifacts/packages` holds exactly the five library IDs (plus their five `.snupkg`) at `$(MinVerVersion)` and `artifacts/tombstone` holds exactly `Ducky.Generator.2.0.0.nupkg` (§19).

`buildTransitive/Ducky.targets` (the whole file):

```xml
<Project>
  <ItemGroup>                                                     <!-- read by DUCKY004 -->
    <CompilerVisibleProperty Include="UsingMicrosoftNETSdkWeb" />
    <CompilerVisibleProperty Include="UsingMicrosoftNETSdkBlazorWebAssembly" />
  </ItemGroup>
  <Target Name="_DuckyRejectLegacyGenerator" BeforeTargets="CoreCompile"
          Condition="'@(PackageReference->WithMetadataValue('Identity','Ducky.Generator'))' != ''">
    <Error Code="DUCKY900"
           Text="Ducky.Generator is deprecated: the generator ships inside Ducky 2.x. Remove the Ducky.Generator PackageReference. https://github.com/phmatray/Ducky/blob/main/docs/diagnostics/DUCKY900.md" />
  </Target>
  <!-- Ducky.Blazor 1.x declares Ducky >= 1.x, so Ducky 2 satisfies it and it fails at run time (TypeLoadException).
       1.x shipped only Ducky, Ducky.Blazor and Ducky.Generator: there is no 1.x Ducky.Reactive or Ducky.Testing to reject -->
  <Target Name="_DuckyRejectSkewedCompanions" AfterTargets="ResolvePackageAssets">
    <ItemGroup>
      <_DuckySkewed Include="@(RuntimeCopyLocalItems);@(ResolvedCompileFileDefinitions)"
          Condition="'%(NuGetPackageId)' == 'Ducky.Blazor'
                     and $([System.Version]::Parse($([System.String]::Copy('%(NuGetPackageVersion)').Split('-')[0])).Major) &lt; 2" />
    </ItemGroup>
    <Error Code="DUCKY902" Condition="'@(_DuckySkewed)' != ''"
           Text="@(_DuckySkewed->'%(NuGetPackageId) %(NuGetPackageVersion)'->Distinct()) is a 1.x package and can't run on Ducky 2.x. Update it to 2.x. https://github.com/phmatray/Ducky/blob/main/docs/diagnostics/DUCKY902.md" />
  </Target>
</Project>
```

PackageSmoke is the arbiter of the item and metadata names (a consumer with a committed fake `Ducky.Blazor.1.0.292.nupkg`, §17.9).

`Ducky.Draft.targets` raises **DUCKY901** (warning) the same way when `Mutty` is referenced.

## 4. Invariants catalogue

Each invariant is enforced in one place (column 3) and verified by the named tests (column 4). The gate that runs each test project is listed in §17.1. Test names are normative: renaming one needs a spec change. `SpecTraceGate` (§19) reads the machine-readable manifest `docs/spec/tests.yaml` (name → project(s) → invariant) and fails the build when a manifest test is missing from any of its projects, when a manifest name does not appear in SPEC.md, or when a name in the "Named tests" column below is missing from the manifest; manifest names mapped to `Ducky.AotSmoke` are checked against the binary's output by the `AotSmoke` target instead (§19). The manifest is staged: each entry carries the §24 step that implements it, and existence is checked only for entries at or below the manifest's `activeStage`, while the name checks always run (§19, §24). Each invariant also carries its own stage in the manifest (`invariants: { INV-01: 2, …, INV-32: 8 }`, the §24 step that introduces it): for every invariant whose stage is at or below `activeStage`, except INV-24 (whose gate is the `aot` job itself), at least one active manifest entry mapped to it is in a covered project (§17.1); `--strict` checks all 32. Every backticked token in that column is a test name (project and target names are written as plain text).

| ID | Invariant | Enforced in | Named tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| **INV-01** | Reducers never run concurrently | `Dispatcher` single drainer | `Reducers_NeverRunConcurrently`, `Linearizability_DispatchVsModel`, `Reducers_DispatchFromReducer_QueuedNotNested` |
| **INV-02** | Each accepted action is processed exactly once. A drop is explicit, typed (`DispatchResult.Dropped`), logged and counted (`ducky.dispatch.dropped`, tagged `ducky.drop.reason` = `depth` for the step-1 drop, `run` for a superseded Switch run, whether the drop happens at call time in `EffectContext`/`SliceStore` or at step 3; a dispatch after disposal began completes `Disposed`, never `Dropped`, and is not counted; `DispatchAsync_EveryPath_ReturnsExpectedResult` asserts the counter on every Dropped path and its absence on the after-disposal paths) | `Dispatcher` | `Dispatch_FromNThreads_AppliesEveryActionExactlyOnce`, `Dispatch_ReentrantFromSubscriberAndEffectPrefix_EachProcessedOnce` |
| **INV-03** | No stranded action: when `Dispatch` returns, the action is in a queue that some drainer is guaranteed to drain. No exception ever escapes `Process`, so the drain loop always reaches its release. No Ducky log call can throw (every package logs through its `SafeLogger`, §6.3), and no `ActivityListener` or `MeterListener` callback can skip a step (every telemetry call goes through `SafeTelemetry`, §6.3). The catch-all completes the action before it logs, so even a fatal rethrow from its log call can't leave the drain held | empty check and drain release in one critical section; catch-all around `Process`; `SafeLogger` and `SafeTelemetry` (§6.3) | `Dispatch_AfterDrainRelease_NoStrandedAction`, `Drain_ProcessThrowsUnexpectedly_NextActionStillDrained`, `Drain_ProcessThrowsAndLogRethrowsFatal_ActionCompletedAndNextDrained`, `SafeLogger_ProviderThrows_NeverEscapes`, `Telemetry_ListenerThrows_LaterStepsStillRun` |
| **INV-04** | FIFO within the main queue and within the init buffer, and per-producer order, including across DevTools time travel. Before `Ready`, the main queue holds only system/restore actions; at `Ready` the store appends, atomically, `StoreInitialized` and then the buffered user actions in their original order, so the processing order is: everything queued before `Ready`, then `StoreInitialized`, then the buffer, then later actions. Across origins before `Ready` the processing order is therefore not the enqueue order, and `ActionContext.Id` (enqueue order) is not monotonic in processing order across the gate (§5.6) | `Dispatcher` (queue + init buffer) | `Dispatch_PreservesPerProducerOrder`, `InitBuffer_PreservesUserActionOrderAcrossGate` (property), `InitBuffer_ActionsAfterReady_FollowBufferedActions` |
| **INV-05** | Deadlock freedom: `Dispatch`, `DispatchAsync`, `Restore`, `Select`, `State` and `InitializeAsync` never block on another thread. One carve-out: concurrent first users may wait while effects and middleware are materialized (§6.6); their constructors must not block. An init-buffer overflow abort never runs on the caller: it is queued to the thread pool through the store's `QueueWorkItem` seam (§6.7), so no caller waits for the persistence `_issue` lock. No user code and no init start runs while `_gate` is held | `Dispatcher` | `Dispatch_OwnerBlocksOnCrossThreadDispatch_DoesNotDeadlock` (fails on 1.x), `InitStart_NeverRunsUnderGate`, `Subscriber_ReadsStateAndDispatchesDuringNotify_NoDeadlock`, `Materialization_ConcurrentFirstUse_SecondCallerWaitsThenSucceeds`, `Init_OverflowAbort_NeverRunsOnCaller` |
| **INV-06** | Causal depth measures synchronous causality, for every origin (`Local`, `Effect`, `System` through `DispatchSystem`, and `Restore`). The only exception is the core's own failure actions, which are enqueued at depth 0. A dispatch made while its parent is being processed on the same flow gets depth + 1. A dispatch after a real asynchronous yield, or while the store is idle, starts a new chain at depth 0 and keeps the correlation id. Unrelated producers start at 0, and so does code running on another store's flow: the causal and effect-run scopes are per-store instance fields, never shared (§6.5). Depth above `MaxDispatchDepth` (64) is dropped, and the `ReducerFailed(DispatchLoopException)` is enqueued at depth 0 so it is itself reduced | `Dispatcher` (per-store `_causal`, §6.5) | `SelfDispatchLoop_IsStoppedByDepthGuard`, `EffectPingPong_SynchronousPrefix_IsStoppedByDepthGuard`, `PollingEffect_RunsBeyondMaxDepthTicks`, `AsyncDispatchWhileStoreIdle_StartsNewChainAtDepthZero`, `DispatchSystemLoopFromAfterReduce_IsStoppedByDepthGuard`, `LoopGuardFailure_IsReducedNotDropped`, `TwoStores_EffectRunOfA_DoesNotScopeDispatchSetOrWhenIdleOfB` |
| **INV-07** | Snapshot versions strictly increase. A snapshot is never torn (all slices come from one commit). After `DispatchAsync` completes with `Reduced`, `State.Version` ≥ that action's commit version | single writer + `Volatile.Write` | `Snapshot_VersionStrictlyMonotonic`, `Snapshot_NeverTorn`, `Snapshot_AfterConcurrentReduces_IsNeverStale`, `Snapshot_DispatchAsyncReduced_StateVersionAtLeastCommitVersion` |
| **INV-08** | An action commits to all slices or to none. A veto or a throwing `MayDispatch` ends processing; an action that fails in `BeforeReduce` or a reducer commits nothing and notifies no subscriber, but still runs `AfterReduce` (with no changed key) and starts its effects (§6.4, "What runs after a veto or a failure") | `Dispatcher.Reduce` | `ReducerThrow_InOneSlice_CommitsNoSliceForThatAction`, `BeforeReduceThrows_NoCommitNoNotify_EffectsStart` |
| **INV-09** | Subscribers are never invoked under a lock. A throwing subscriber (its selector, comparer or `onChange`) is isolated, logged, and stays subscribed with its last value unchanged; the other subscribers are still notified and the action's effects still start. Subscribing or unsubscribing during notification is safe. A subscription disposed before the drainer reaches it is not invoked (a callback already running may finish after `Dispose` returns; `Dispose` never waits for it). A subscriber may read state and dispatch | `SubscriberList` (immutable array swap) + the subscription's volatile `_disposed` flag + per-step isolation in `Process` (§6.4) | `Subscriber_UnsubscribesItselfDuringNotify`, `Subscriber_Throws_OthersStillNotified_StaysSubscribed`, `Subscriber_SelectorThrows_OthersNotifiedAndEffectsStillStart`, `Subscriber_DisposedBeforeNotify_NotInvoked` |
| **INV-10** | Every `DispatchAsync` task completes exactly once with the correct `DispatchResult`. It never faults or cancels, and its continuations never run inline on the drainer. The never-fault set is exactly: `IDispatcher.DispatchAsync`, `EffectContext.DispatchAsync`, `SliceStore.SetAsync`, `DuckyComponent.DispatchAsync`, `IStore.DisposeAsync`, and `InitializeAsync`/`WhenIdleAsync` with a non-cancelled token: their tasks never fault or cancel, and their only exceptions are the programmer errors of §7.4, thrown synchronously before a task exists (among them `WhenIdleAsync` called from a non-`LongRunning` effect run, which would otherwise wait for itself forever). | **INV-14** | Hydration. Every attempt carries its scope epoch. Exactly one terminal action (`HydrationCompleted` or `HydrationFailed`, carrying that epoch) per attempt that is still current; an attempt superseded by a scope switch issues nothing, and the superseding attempt owns the terminal. Every attempt ends, including a switch to a null scope. A switch re-reads its scoped keys and any unscoped key the superseded attempt did not restore, so a switch during the initial attempt never leaves unscoped keys unhydrated; a null scope drops only the scoped keys, so the carried unscoped keys are still read. The `HydrationTimeout` armed in the synchronous prefix is an init-phase deadline that acts on whichever attempt is current when it fires, so the initial terminal (whichever attempt issues it) still precedes `StoreInitialized` within one `HydrationTimeout`. The init phase ends under `_issue` (the `_initPhase` flag, §11.5): when the attempt current during init issues its terminal, or when the init-token callback runs; from then on the init-phase deadline and the init-token callback are no-ops, and a switch arms its own per-attempt timer iff `_initPhase` is clear, decided under `_issue`, so every switch attempt has exactly one live deadline. An attempt's result restore and its terminal are enqueued atomically with respect to init abort and scope switches (§11.5), so the initial attempt's terminal always precedes `StoreInitialized`. `PersistenceState.Status` starts `Hydrated` (`PersistenceSlice.Initial`); with readable slices it goes `Hydrating→(Hydrated \| Failed)`, and in a NonInteractive store with no readable slice it goes to `NotStarted`; it changes only on the drainer. Slices inert in this host (Server storage in the browser) count as not persisted, so a browser store whose persisted slices are all Server storage stays `Hydrated`. A late read result is discarded. One slice that fails to deserialize keeps its current state and the others still restore. With nothing persisted, Status starts `Hydrated` and nothing is gated beyond init. | **INV-15** | Prerender. The seed persisting callback is registered with an explicit render mode (`InteractiveAuto`), so it works in single-mode, mixed-mode and Auto apps and on circuit pause. The seed is written only from a quiescent store and only for slices that keep its estimated wire size within `PrerenderSeedMaxWireBytes`. Quiescence excludes `LongRunning` effects and all Ducky.Reactive work, so a `Prerender<T>` slice must be loaded by an `Effect<T>`. On timeout nothing is written and the timeout is logged once per store. `WasRestored(k)` is true iff slice k was restored with `Origin.Hydration` in this store (a cross-tab value received for a key whose hydration attempt has no terminal yet is restored with that origin, §11.7, so a key filled from storage never loads twice). A seed is serialized from one snapshot taken after the idle wait, and everything it carries (values, left-out keys, scope hash, dirty keys, per-key `PersistOptions.Version` map) derives from that snapshot; a persisted key whose recorded version differs from the current one is never applied from a seed, so a seed that outlives a deploy can't bypass the migrations (§11.4). The seed is applied before the first interactive render, whoever touched the store first (a `State` read included); in the browser the wait for it is short and bounded, and never stalls an app without Ducky components. Storage wins over a prerender seed, and a pause seed wins over browser storage only for the browser-storage keys that were dirty when the pause was taken (their value differed from the last successful write, read or receipt, recorded in the envelope's `dirty` list); a clean carried key follows prerender precedence, so another tab's newer write is never rolled back (Server storage wins over any seed); a pause seed's scoped keys are applied only under the scope they were captured in, and a pause never captures a key whose hydration attempt is still running (ADR-0011); persistence never issues its restore before the seed has settled. A restored slice's load effect runs at most once across prerender→interactive; a state the app marks as not seedable (an error or incomplete load, through the `Prerender<TSlice, TState>(include)` predicate) is left out of the seed, so the interactive side loads it again. | **INV-16** | Persistence writes, per store. No write is triggered by a `Hydration`, `CrossTab` or `DevTools` origin. Writes, removes and scope-switch reads per key never overlap: a clear and a switch attempt's read each run as a command in the key's writer, so neither an in-flight write nor a pending debounced change can bring a cleared key back, and a switch read of a key always sees the old scope's final write to that key. A write serializes the committed state current at write time, so the last write equals the final state; dedupe compares the `s` payload with one per-key `LastKnownPayload`, which always holds a **local** serialization. The skip and its recheck use one predicate, `ShouldSkip(snap, key)` (a hydration in flight for that key, or a scoped key whose epoch scope is still unknown, §11.5): a skipped write keeps its key dirty and runs after the terminal, even when the skip races the terminal (add-then-recheck), and a key deferred for an unknown scope is signalled when that scope is recorded, so it is neither stranded nor spun on. At a scope switch, the last state of the old scope (the snapshot the reset was reduced against) is written under the old scope's key, and a failed write of that snapshot is retried with that snapshot under that key, never with `Store.State`. A write that fails (a disconnected circuit, an interop timeout, or any storage-provider error such as a Redis timeout) is retried until it succeeds or the store is disposed, and a failed `ducky.js` import is never cached: a change is lost only if the store is disposed before a successful write (the dispose flush cancels any pending retry backoff and makes one immediate attempt per dirty key, and a switch read, a removal or a final old-scope snapshot ends a pending backoff too (one immediate attempt, then the backoff resumes if it fails), and every key still dirty when the flush ends is counted `ducky.persistence.lost`, whatever the storage), or if the WASM page unloads before the next scheduler turn. A cross-tab resync never resets a key whose baseline is absent, so it can't discard a local change still waiting for its first write. The base is never mutated. An assignment is never lost: assigning null, replacing a collection or replacing a nested draft is reflected by `Build`; a recipe that discards the new instance returned by a plain get/set member's method (`d.Pending.Enqueue(job)` on an `ImmutableQueue`, `d.Todos.Upsert(t)` on an `EntityState`) gets DUCKY109 at compile time instead of a silent no-op. A revoked draft throws, including nested and collection element drafts whose record lives in another assembly; an element draft flushed by a structural list operation and a child draft replaced by an assignment are revoked too, so a write through a stale draft throws instead of being lost. `Produce` equals the same edits written with `with`, compared structurally (§17.7) | Draft runtime + generated code | `Produce_NoOpRecipe_ReturnsSameReference`, `Produce_EqualsManualWithExpressions`, `Produce_UntouchedBranches_ReferenceEqual`, `Produce_BaseDeepEqualBeforeAndAfter`, `Produce_RevokedDraftAccess_Throws`, `Produce_AssignEqualScalar_IsNoOp` (all CsCheck), `Produce_NoOp_ImmutableArrayMember_ReturnsSameReference`, `Produce_DefaultImmutableArrayMember_Works`, `Produce_NullableMemberNullOriginal_GetReturnsNull`, `Produce_ElementDraftAfterStructuralOp_Throws`, `Produce_ReplacedChildDraft_Throws`, `Produce_AssignRecordToDraftableMember_Builds`, `Produce_RevertedScalar_ReturnsSameReference` |
| **INV-27** | Reactive. Disposed with the store. A failed pipeline resubscribes with backoff. Every reactive failure is published as a `System` failure action (never vetoed, never buffered, never "unhandled"). Synchronous loops are depth-bounded. Values have a single writer (the drainer); subscription changes, which happen on timer, pool and disposing threads, are synchronized (`SynchronizedReactiveProperty`, R3's locked `Subject` subscription list). The host never blocks `WhenIdleAsync`: reactive work is excluded from quiescence, like `LongRunning` effects. A pending resubscribe never fires after disposal. The `state` observable's first value is the current snapshot, never null | `ReactiveHost` + `ReactiveMiddleware` | `Reactive_DisposedWithStore_NoEmissionAfterDispose`, `Reactive_FailureCompletion_ResubscribesWithBackoff`, `Reactive_OnErrorResume_ContinuesStream`, `Reactive_Failure_IsSystemFailureAction_StrictModeClean`, `Reactive_SyncSelfLoop_StoppedByDepthGuard`, `Reactive_DoesNotBlockWhenIdle`, `Reactive_ResubscribePendingAtDispose_NeverResubscribes`, `Reactive_StateObservable_FirstValueIsCurrentSnapshot`, `Reactive_ResubscribeConcurrentWithDrain_FirstValueCurrent_NoLostSubscriber`, `Reactive_ResubscribeConcurrentWithDrain_FirstValueCurrent_NoLostSubscriber_Deterministic` |
| **INV-28** | DevTools. A jump restores the exact typed snapshot (reference-equal slices), even when it races a send on another thread: the history is one immutable reference that only the drainer replaces. User actions are vetoed during time travel (a volatile flag). Sanitizers apply to every payload, including `@@INIT` | `DevToolsMiddleware` | `DevTools_JumpToRecordState_RestoresReferenceEqualSlices`, `DevTools_DuringTimeTravel_UserActionVetoed`, `DevTools_SanitizerAppliedToInitAndEverySend`, `DevTools_OptionsReachExtension`, `DevTools_JumpConcurrentWithSend_RestoresMatchingEntry`, `DevTools_JumpConcurrentWithSend_RestoresMatchingEntry_Deterministic` |
| **INV-29** | Dispose. Idempotent, re-entrant calls included: every caller gets the one disposal task, published before any step runs. Disposing a store that was never materialized constructs nothing, and no entry point materializes after disposal has begun (each first checks `_state`); a materialization in flight is awaited (bounded) and everything it built is disposed, and a factory that throws disposes the instances it already built. Always awaits, asynchronously and with a bound, the in-flight drain's exit, including when called from the drainer itself. Every queued and buffered pending action completes with `Disposed`. Persistence flushes and middleware disposal run only after the drain has exited, after any in-flight materialization has ended, and each middleware's only after its own started init has ended (bounded); effect, reactive-effect and scope disposal additionally wait for the effect runs (bounded). This holds on every path (timeout, sync `Dispose()`, re-entrant call, a dispose racing `Start`'s synchronous prefix): nothing is run inline while a drain may be in flight, and a hung effect run delays only effect and scope disposal, never the flush. Each middleware waits only for its own init task, and each middleware's `DisposeAsync` is bounded, so a hung init or hung `DisposeAsync` of one middleware delays only that middleware; `DisposeAsync` completes once every phase has finished or reached its bound, and when the drain wait itself expires it completes at that bound while the later phases still run, chained on the drain's exit (§6.11). An effect run that calls `DisposeAsync` (first or later call) is never waited for by that disposal. Idle waiters complete at step 1, so a wait registered before disposal can't hold disposal. Cancellation callbacks registered on effect and init tokens (Ducky.Reactive's subscription disposal included) are the exception: they run at cancellation (step 2) and may overlap an in-flight drain. A throwing disposal or callback is logged and never stops the rest or faults `DisposeAsync`. A retired init never aborts after disposal. On the server, the store is disposed before the scoped dependencies its effects and middleware resolved | `Store.DisposeAsync` | `Dispose_DuringConcurrentDispatch_NoExceptionNoLeak_AllTasksComplete`, `Dispose_WaitsForInFlightDrainBeforeMiddlewareDispose`, `Dispose_FromDrainer_DoesNotDeadlock`, `Dispose_FromReducer_MiddlewareDisposedAfterDrainExit`, `SyncDispose_DuringDrain_NoAfterReduceOnDisposedMiddleware`, `Dispose_DrainTimeout_MiddlewareDisposedAfterDrainExit`, `Dispose_FromRacingEffectContinuation_WaitsForDrain`, `Dispose_Twice_IsNoOp`, `Dispose_MiddlewareDisposeThrows_OthersStillDisposed`, `Dispose_ThrowingLifetimeCallback_CompletesAndDisposesMiddleware`, `Dispose_DuringInit_TimerNeverFires_NoAbortLog`, `Dispose_MiddlewareInitInFlight_DisposeAsyncAfterInitTaskEnds`, `Dispose_AwaitsMergeAndLongRunningRuns`, `ServerScopeDispose_EffectScopedDependencyDisposedAfterStoreDispose`, `Dispose_HungEffectRun_PersistenceStillFlushed`, `Dispose_DuringStartSyncPrefix_AwaitsStartedInitsAndStartsNoMore`, `Dispose_InitAwaitingWhenIdle_MiddlewareStillDisposed`, `Lifecycle_ThrowingLoggerProvider_StoreReadyAndDisposeComplete`, `Dispose_NeverMaterialized_ConstructsNothing`, `Dispatch_AfterDisposeOnUnmaterializedStore_IgnoredNotThrown`, `Dispose_ConcurrentWithFirstUse_EveryConstructedMiddlewareDisposed`, `Dispose_ConcurrentWithFirstUse_EveryConstructedMiddlewareDisposed_Deterministic`, `Dispose_HungMiddlewareInit_PersistenceStillFlushed`, `Dispose_HungMiddlewareDispose_OthersDisposedAndDisposeAsyncCompletes`, `Dispose_ReentrantFromLifetimeCallback_RunsOnce`, `Dispose_SecondCallFromEffectRun_DoesNotWaitForItself`, `Dispose_DrainOverrunsTimeout_ServerScope_DisposeAsyncCompletesAtBound_LateFlushCountsLost` |
| **INV-30** | SliceStore. A mutator runs at reduce time, on the drainer, against the latest state. Concurrent `Set` calls all apply. The injected facade is the store-owned slice instance. A `Set` is scoped to an effect run only when called from that run's own flow, never from code the drainer runs for other actions, and never from a run of another store (`TwoStores_EffectRunOfA_DoesNotScopeDispatchSetOrWhenIdleOfB`, mapped to INV-06) | `SliceStore<TState>` | `SliceStore_ConcurrentSetFromNThreads_AppliesAll`, `SliceStore_InjectedInstance_IsStoreOwnedSlice`, `SliceStore_SameReference_NoCommit`, `SliceStore_SetFromSubscriberDuringDrainOwnedByEffectContinuation_IsLocal` |
| **INV-31** | Configuration. Every configuration problem that can be found without running user constructors (self-contained, DI-dependent including an unresolvable effect, middleware or reactive-effect constructor parameter (reactive effects reach the check through `DuckyBuilder.RequireResolvableConstructor`, §5.1), from `AddValidation` rules, or from a throwing slice constructor) is reported together in one `DuckyConfigurationException` at first store resolution. `AddDucky` itself throws only DUCKY300. A constructor that throws when effects and middleware are materialized (an `EffectGroup` with a duplicate `On<T>`, or any user code) surfaces as a `DuckyConfigurationException` (DUCKY353) from the first store use, and every later call rethrows that same instance (except after disposal began, when each entry point takes its after-disposal path and `DisposeAsync` never rethrows); the store-owned instances built before the throw are disposed in reverse order first. Each entry has a code and follows the message template (what, types, fix, link) | `DuckyBuilder.Validate` + materialization (§6.6) | `Build_WithFiveMisconfigurations_ReportsAllFive`, `Build_SelfContainedAndDiDependentErrors_ReportedTogether`, `Build_EffectCtorDependencyMissing_ReportedWithOtherErrors`, `Build_EffectCtorKeyedDependency_NotReported`, `Build_EffectCtorKeyedDependencyMissingKey_Reported`, `Materialization_CtorThrows_EveryCallRethrowsSameConfigurationException`, `Materialization_CtorThrows_EarlierInstancesDisposed`, `Build_ReactiveEffectCtorDependencyMissing_ReportedWithOtherErrors` (in Ducky.Reactive.Tests), `EveryErrorCode_FollowsMessageTemplate` (in Ducky.Tests for the Ducky codes and in Ducky.Blazor.Tests for DUCKY310–317 and DUCKY352) |
| **INV-32** | `EntityState` operations agree with a model (`Dictionary` + insertion-ordered list). An operation that changes nothing returns the same instance | `EntityState<TKey,TEntity>` | `EntityState_ModelBased` (property), `EntityState_NoOp_ReturnsSameInstance` |


---

## 5. Ducky core public API

Namespace `Ducky` unless stated otherwise. Nullable is enabled. `PublicAPI.Shipped.txt` contains exactly this listing, plus the tombstones of §22.1. Null checks and XML docs are omitted here, but XML docs are required on every public member (CS1591 is an error). `[DAM]` means `[DynamicallyAccessedMembers(DynamicallyAccessedMemberTypes.PublicConstructors)]`.

### 5.1 Registration

```csharp
public static class DuckyServiceCollectionExtensions
{
    /// Call once per IServiceCollection. A second call throws DuckyConfigurationException (DUCKY300).
    /// Every other rule (self-contained, DI-dependent, AddValidation) is checked together at first store resolution (INV-31).
    public static IServiceCollection AddDucky(this IServiceCollection services, Action<DuckyBuilder> configure);
}

public sealed class DuckyBuilder
{
    public IServiceCollection Services { get; }
    public ServiceLifetime Lifetime { get; set; }            // default (unset): IsBrowser ? Singleton : Scoped, resolved when AddDucky
                                                             // registers the store, after configure ran (see below). Transient => DUCKY301
    public int MaxDispatchDepth { get; set; } = 64;
    public bool ThrowOnUnhandledAction { get; set; }         // false; TestStore sets true before running configure
    public int InitBufferCapacity { get; set; } = 1024;      // soft bound; overflow aborts init, drops nothing (INV-13)
    public TimeSpan InitTimeout { get; set; } = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10);
    public TimeSpan DisposeTimeout { get; set; } = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(2);

    public DuckyBuilder UseJson(IJsonTypeInfoResolver resolver);                        // last call wins (UseJson(options) included); a
                                                                                        // JsonSerializerContext brings its own options (§10)
    public DuckyBuilder UseJson(JsonSerializerOptions options);                        // stores the reference only; a null TypeInfoResolver is DUCKY306 at
                                                                                        // first resolution; the store freezes a copy, never the caller's instance
    public DuckyBuilder AddSlice<TSlice>() where TSlice : Slice, new();                 // idempotent; also registers TSlice for DI injection
    public DuckyBuilder AddEffect<[DAM] TEffect>() where TEffect : Effect;              // idempotent; store-created, store-owned; a no-op when an instance of
                                                                                        // TEffect was (or later is) registered: one runner per effect type
    public DuckyBuilder AddEffect<TEffect>(TEffect instance) where TEffect : Effect;    // uses this instance and replaces a type registration of the same
                                                                                        // TEffect, whatever the call order; the store never disposes it (EffectTest)
    public DuckyBuilder Use<[DAM] TMiddleware>() where TMiddleware : Middleware;        // idempotent; first registration fixes the position

    // For extension packages (Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive) and advanced users:
    public DuckyBuilder RequireJsonTypeInfo(Type type, string requiredBy);             // checked with the other rules
    public DuckyBuilder RequireResolvableConstructor([DAM] Type type, string requiredBy); // queues type for the DUCKY309 constructor check at first
                                                                                        // resolution (a type the core doesn't construct itself, such
                                                                                        // as a reactive effect, §14); requiredBy is named in the error
    public DuckyBuilder AddValidation(Func<IServiceProvider, IEnumerable<DuckyError>> rule); // DI-dependent rule, run with the core rules at first resolution
}
```

- `TimeProvider` comes from DI (`TryAddSingleton(TimeProvider.System)`). Tests register a `FakeTimeProvider` before `AddDucky`.
- `OperatingSystem.IsBrowser()` is read once per package, branch-free, into an internal instance value: `internal bool IsBrowser { get; set; } = OperatingSystem.IsBrowser();` on `DuckyBuilder` (Ducky) and on `BlazorOptions` (Ducky.Blazor). It is settable (not `init`) because tests only reach both objects inside the `configure` lambda, where assigning an `init` property is CS8852. Every browser-dependent branch (the `Lifetime` default, the `InteractivityGate`, readable slices, the auth subscription path, Server-storage inertness, the DUCKY314/315 skip) reads that value when it is used, never at construction: `Lifetime` is backed by an internal `ServiceLifetime? _lifetime`, and `AddDucky` resolves `_lifetime ?? (IsBrowser ? Singleton : Scoped)` after `configure` returns (`Lifetime_Default_FollowsIsBrowserSetInConfigure`). Each package's own test project sets `IsBrowser` in `configure` through its allowed `InternalsVisibleTo`, so both sides of every branch run under `dotnet test` (100% branch coverage, §17.1). A banned-API rule (RS0030) allows `OperatingSystem.IsBrowser` only in those two initializers, each written as `#pragma warning disable RS0030 // justification: the single IsBrowser read of this package (§5.1)`.
- `AddSlice` constructs its throwaway instance inside a try/catch: a slice constructor that throws (duplicate `On<T>`, non-concrete `T`) is recorded as DUCKY308/DUCKY307 and reported with the other errors (INV-31).
- **Constructor dependencies are checked at first resolution** (DUCKY309), with the other rules. For each registered effect and middleware, and each type queued with `RequireResolvableConstructor` (Ducky.Reactive's `AddReactiveEffect<T>` queues `typeof(T)`, because it registers `T` neither in DI nor with the core, §14), the check takes the constructor `ActivatorUtilities` would pick (the one marked `[ActivatorUtilitiesConstructor]`, else the public constructors; the registration stores a `[DAM]`-annotated `Type`, so this is trim-safe) and passes if, for at least one candidate, every parameter without a default value is resolvable according to `IServiceProviderIsService`. A parameter marked `[FromKeyedServices(key)]` is checked with `IServiceProviderIsKeyedService.IsKeyedService(type, key)` instead (`IsService` returns false for a service registered only under a key, such as the .NET 9+ keyed `HttpClient`, which `ActivatorUtilities` would build without problem: `Build_EffectCtorKeyedDependency_NotReported`, and `Build_EffectCtorKeyedDependencyMissingKey_Reported` for an unregistered key); a parameter marked `[ServiceKey]` is unresolvable, because store-created effects and middleware are never keyed. `IStore`, `IDispatcher`, slice and `SliceStore` types count as resolvable. If the container offers no `IServiceProviderIsService` (or, for a keyed parameter, no `IServiceProviderIsKeyedService`), the check is skipped with a Debug log. So the most common mistake, an effect that injects an unregistered service, is reported in the aggregated exception (`Build_EffectCtorDependencyMissing_ReportedWithOtherErrors`).
- **Constructors that throw** are only found by running them, which happens at materialization (§6.6, first store use), never at first resolution: an `EffectGroup` constructor's duplicate or non-concrete `On<T>` (DUCKY014/DUCKY005 catch both at compile time), or any other user exception. It surfaces as a `DuckyConfigurationException` with code DUCKY353 (wrapping the original, and naming DUCKY308/DUCKY307 when it is one of those) from that first use, and every later call rethrows the same cached instance (`Materialization_CtorThrows_EveryCallRethrowsSameConfigurationException`); the instances built before the throw are disposed first (§6.6, `Materialization_CtorThrows_EarlierInstancesDisposed`). Ducky never constructs throwaway effects: their constructors take user services and may have side effects.
- "Registration takes the slice type, reads take the state type": `AddSlice<CartStore>()`, `@inject CartStore`, `State.Get<CartState>()`, `WasRestored<CartState>()`.
- There is no `AddSliceStore`. `AddSlice<T>` registers every slice as an injectable service resolved to the store-owned instance (INV-30).
- **One registration per effect type.** `AddEffect(instance)` wins over `AddEffect<TEffect>()` for the same `TEffect` whatever the call order, and the replaced type registration is excluded from DUCKY309, so a test that passes an instance and also calls `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}()` (which emits `AddEffect<TEffect>()`) gets one runner whose handler runs once per action (`AddEffect_InstanceAndType_SameEffect_OneRunner`, `EffectTest_ConfigureAlsoRegistersEffectType_HandlerRunsOnce`).

### 5.2 Store and dispatch

```csharp
public interface IDispatcher
{
    /// Enqueues and returns. Never throws for reducer, middleware or effect failures at run time. Throws only
    /// ArgumentNullException, or the store's cached DuckyConfigurationException (DUCKY353) when its effects or
    /// middleware could not be constructed (§6.6; never once disposal has begun). The action is reduced before this returns only if the caller
    /// became the drainer. Use DispatchAsync for read-your-writes.
    void Dispatch(object action);

    /// Completes (never inline on the drainer) once this action is reduced, vetoed, dropped or the store is
    /// disposed. Completes before the action's effects finish. Never faults, never cancels. Throws synchronously,
    /// before returning a task, only in the two cases Dispatch throws.
    Task<DispatchResult> DispatchAsync(object action);
}

public enum DispatchResult { Reduced, Vetoed, Dropped, Failed, Disposed }

public interface IStore : IDispatcher, IDisposable, IAsyncDisposable
{
    StateSnapshot State { get; }                                           // lock-free Volatile.Read; starts init (idempotent: one volatile read
                                                                           // once started, never under _gate), so a first read applies the seed (INV-13)
    StateSnapshot InitialState { get; }                                    // every slice's Initial, built once at store creation (Version 0)
    IReadOnlyList<Slice> Slices { get; }                                   // the store-owned slice instances in registration order (public Key and
                                                                           // declared StateType): registry data, so reading it starts no init,
                                                                           // materializes nothing and works after disposal
    DuckyJson Json { get; }                                                // the store's UseJson resolver (§10)
    Selection<T> Select<T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T> selector,
                           Action<T>? onChange = null,                     // invoked on the drainer, only when the projected value changed
                           IEqualityComparer<T>? comparer = null);
    void Restore(IReadOnlyDictionary<string, object> values, Origin origin); // value = TState instance or JsonElement (cloned synchronously
                                                                           // before enqueue, so the caller may dispose its JsonDocument);
                                                                           // origin ∈ {Hydration, CrossTab, DevTools}
    Task InitializeAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);   // starts init; returns sharedInitTask.WaitAsync(token): the token cancels only
                                                                           // this caller's wait, never init. The shared task completes once
                                                                           // StoreInitialized has been processed, or at disposal; never faults
    Task WhenIdleAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);     // starts init; Ready ∧ queue empty ∧ not draining ∧ no running non-long-running
                                                                           // effect. Completes Canceled only if *the caller's* token is cancelled.
                                                                           // Called from a non-LongRunning effect run of this store (which counts itself as running):
                                                                           // InvalidOperationException, synchronously (§7.4)
}

public static class StoreExtensions
{
    public static Selection<T> Select<TState, T>(this IStore store, Func<TState, T> selector,
        Action<T>? onChange = null, IEqualityComparer<T>? comparer = null) where TState : class; // fast path: skips the projector when the slot reference is unchanged
}

public sealed class StateSnapshot
{
    public long Version { get; }
    public IReadOnlyList<string> Keys { get; }
    public TState Get<TState>() where TState : class;                      // DuckyConfigurationException DUCKY350 if no slice has TState (message names the likely AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm})
    public bool TryGet<TState>([NotNullWhen(true)] out TState? state) where TState : class;
    public object Get(string key);                                         // KeyNotFoundException
    public bool WasRestored<TState>() where TState : class;                // restored with Origin.Hydration in this store
    public bool WasRestored(string key);
}

public sealed class Selection<T> : IDisposable
{
    /// For extension packages (Ducky.Blazor's SubscriptionCore): Value calls `read`, then `onRead(value)`; Dispose calls `onDispose` once.
    public static Selection<T> Create(Func<T> read, Action<T>? onRead = null, Action? onDispose = null);
    public T Value { get; }                                  // evaluated on read against the current snapshot (memoized by input references)
    public static implicit operator T(Selection<T> selection);
    public override string? ToString();                      // Value?.ToString(), so `@count` renders in Razor
    public void Dispose();                                   // idempotent; for IStore.Select, the drainer never invokes the selector,
                                                             // comparer or onChange once Dispose has returned (a call already running
                                                             // may finish; Dispose never waits for it, §6.8)
}

public enum Origin { Local, Effect, Hydration, CrossTab, DevTools, System }

/// One per store: the options built by UseJson. AOT-safe (JsonTypeInfo only). Never throws for missing type info or for any
/// non-fatal exception raised while (de)serializing, including exceptions thrown by user constructors and init accessors:
/// it returns false/null and logs (§10). Public so Ducky.Blazor can use it without IVT.
public sealed class DuckyJson
{
    public JsonSerializerOptions Options { get; }                          // read-only, built by UseJson
    public bool TryGetTypeInfo(Type type, [NotNullWhen(true)] out JsonTypeInfo? typeInfo);
    public bool TrySerialize(object value, Type declaredType, [NotNullWhen(true)] out string? json);
    public JsonNode? ToNode(object value, Type declaredType);
    public bool TryDeserialize(JsonElement json, Type type, out object? value, string? key = null); // key: slice or storage key named in the Warning
}
```

`Origin` semantics:

| Origin | Produced by | Init buffer | `MayDispatch` veto (user middleware, DevTools time travel) | Unhandled check | Triggers a write |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Local` | `IStore.Dispatch`/`DispatchAsync`, generated helpers on `IStore`, `SliceStore.Set` outside an effect run | buffered before `Ready` | consulted | yes | yes |
| `Effect` | `EffectContext.Dispatch`, Reactive emissions, `SliceStore.Set` inside an effect run (scoped to that run, §12) | buffered before `Ready` | consulted | yes | yes |
| `Hydration` | `Restore(…, Hydration)`: storage, prerender seed, server cache, scope switch | bypasses | **never** | no | **no** |
| `CrossTab` | `Restore(…, CrossTab)` | bypasses | **never** | no | **no** |
| `DevTools` | `Restore(…, DevTools)` | bypasses | **never** | no | **no** |
| `System` | the core (`StoreInitialized`, failure actions), `Middleware.DispatchSystem` | bypasses | **never** | no | yes |

`Restore` with `Local`, `Effect` or `System` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (programmer error). Every origin is subject to the causal depth rule (§6.5). Because library traffic is never vetoed, a broad user veto ("veto everything while logged out") can't block `StoreInitialized`, a hydration terminal or a restore (`Veto_NeverAppliesToSystemOrRestoreOrigins`).

### 5.3 Slices

```csharp
public abstract class Slice
{
    private protected Slice();                               // only Slice<TState> derives
    public virtual string Key { get; }                       // default: SliceKey.FromType(GetType()) (§6.1), cached
    public abstract Type StateType { get; }
}

public abstract class Slice<TState> : Slice where TState : class
{
    protected Slice();
    public sealed override Type StateType { get; }           // typeof(TState): declared, never the runtime type
    protected abstract TState Initial { get; }               // read once when the store is built, cached
    protected void On<TAction>(Func<TState, TAction, TState> reducer) where TAction : notnull;
    protected void On<TAction>(Func<TState, TState> reducer) where TAction : notnull;
}
```

`TState : class` is required: change detection is `ReferenceEquals` (ADR-0007), which is meaningless on boxed value types.

### 5.4 SliceStore facade (API-01, see §12)

```csharp
public abstract class SliceStore<TState> : Slice<TState> where TState : class
{
    protected SliceStore();                                  // registers the internal SetState<TState> reducer
    public TState State { get; }                             // point-in-time read of the committed value (reads IStore.State, so it starts
                                                             // init); DuckyConfigurationException DUCKY351 before the store attached it
    protected void Set(Func<TState, TState> mutator, [CallerMemberName] string name = "");
    protected Task<DispatchResult> SetAsync(Func<TState, TState> mutator, [CallerMemberName] string name = ""); // DUCKY351 thrown
                                                             // synchronously before attach, like State
}
```

### 5.5 Effects

```csharp
public abstract class Effect
{
    private protected Effect();
    public virtual bool LongRunning => false;                // excluded from WhenIdleAsync (pollers, listeners)
}

public abstract class Effect<TAction> : Effect where TAction : notnull
{
    protected Effect();
    public virtual Concurrency Policy => Concurrency.Merge;
    protected virtual object? ConcurrencyKey(TAction action) => null;   // null = one global slot; keys need value equality (DUCKY013)
    public abstract Task Handle(TAction action, EffectContext context, CancellationToken cancellationToken);
}

public abstract class EffectGroup : Effect                  // several action types in one DI class (the unreleased dev branch's AsyncEffectGroup)
{
    protected EffectGroup();
    protected void On<TAction>(Func<TAction, EffectContext, CancellationToken, Task> handler,
                               Concurrency policy = Concurrency.Merge,
                               Func<TAction, object?>? key = null) where TAction : notnull;   // constructor only; duplicate => throws
}

public enum Concurrency { Merge, Switch, Exhaust, Queue }

public sealed class EffectContext : IDispatcher              // generated dispatch helpers work on ctx
{
    public StateSnapshot State { get; }                      // live: each read returns the latest snapshot
    public ActionContext Trigger { get; }
    public TimeProvider Time { get; }
    public void Dispatch(object action);                     // Origin.Effect; ignored with Debug log once the store's disposal began;
                                                             // on a live store, dropped (Debug log, counted) if this run is superseded
    public Task<DispatchResult> DispatchAsync(object action);// Disposed, or Dropped, in the same cases (§6.6)
}

public static class EffectContextExtensions                  // EFF-02
{
    /// Dispatches `started`, awaits `work`, then dispatches `succeeded(result)`. Any exception other than an
    /// OperationCanceledException for *this run's* token dispatches `failed(ex)` (an HttpClient timeout is a failure).
    /// Cancellation of this run's token propagates as cancellation (no action).
    public static Task Run<TResult>(this EffectContext context, object started,
        Func<CancellationToken, Task<TResult>> work, Func<TResult, object> succeeded,
        Func<Exception, object> failed, CancellationToken cancellationToken);
}
```

### 5.6 Middleware

```csharp
public abstract class Middleware : IAsyncDisposable
{
    protected Middleware();
    protected IStore Store { get; }                          // attached by the store before InitializeAsync; InvalidOperationException before
    protected TimeSpan DisposeTimeout { get; }               // DuckyBuilder.DisposeTimeout, attached with Store: how long the store waits for
                                                             // this middleware's DisposeAsync (§6.11 5a); bound any flush by it
    protected void DispatchSystem(object action, bool isFailure = false); // Origin.System: bypasses the init buffer and every veto.
                                                             // isFailure marks a failure action (INV-12: failures under it are logged only)
    public virtual ValueTask InitializeAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken) => default; // token cancelled on init timeout/abort or dispose.
                                                             // Use DispatchSystem here: awaiting DispatchAsync of a user action self-deadlocks
                                                             // until InitTimeout, because user actions are buffered until Ready
    public virtual bool MayDispatch(ActionContext context) => true;    // consulted only for Local and Effect origins; false => Vetoed
    public virtual void BeforeReduce(ActionContext context) { }
    public virtual void AfterReduce(ActionContext context) { }
    public virtual ValueTask DisposeAsync() => default;       // called after this middleware's own init task ended (bounded) and awaited
                                                             // with WaitAsync(DisposeTimeout): past that, the store logs Warning 1016
                                                             // and moves on to the next middleware (§6.11)
}

public sealed class ActionContext                            // immutable
{
    public object Action { get; }
    public string ActionType { get; }                        // DT-02 readable name
    public Origin Origin { get; }
    public int Depth { get; }
    public long Id { get; }                                  // per-store, strictly increasing in enqueue-linearization order (assigned under _gate);
                                                             // not in processing order across the init gate (INV-04): anything that
                                                             // needs processing order uses its own counter (as DevTools does, §11.8)
    public long CorrelationId { get; }                       // Id of the root action of the causal chain
    public StateSnapshot PreviousState { get; }
    public StateSnapshot State { get; }                      // == PreviousState before the reduce
    public IReadOnlyList<string> ChangedKeys { get; }        // empty before the reduce
}
```

**Hooks versus init.** The store may call a middleware's `BeforeReduce`/`AfterReduce` before its `InitializeAsync` has started (a restore or `DispatchSystem` reduced inside an earlier middleware's synchronous init prefix) and concurrently with the asynchronous part of its `InitializeAsync` (which continues on whatever thread its awaits resume on, and may outlive `Ready` after an init abort). State shared between the hooks and `InitializeAsync` must be thread-safe, and code that runs after the init token was cancelled must not restore or dispatch stale data (the built-in persistence guards this with its attempt claim, §11.5). Documented on `Middleware`.

The store creates, owns and disposes middleware, effects and reactive effects (single ownership; DI never disposes them). The one exception is an instance passed to `AddEffect(instance)`, which the store uses but never disposes. There is no `GetService`-first resolution: registering an effect type in DI changes nothing (`Effect_RegisteredInDi_StillStoreOwnedPerStore`).

### 5.7 Library actions and exceptions

```csharp
public sealed record StoreInitialized;
public sealed record ReducerFailed(string ActionType, string? SliceKey, Exception Exception);
public sealed record EffectFailed(string EffectType, string ActionType, Exception Exception);
public sealed class DispatchLoopException : Exception { public string ActionType { get; } public int Depth { get; } }
public sealed class UnhandledActionException : Exception { public string ActionType { get; } }

public sealed class DuckyConfigurationException : InvalidOperationException
{
    public IReadOnlyList<DuckyError> Errors { get; }         // every problem found, not just the first
}
public sealed record DuckyError(string Code, string Message, string Fix, string HelpLink);
```

`HydrateSlices` (the batched restore action) and `SetState<TState>` are **internal**. `HydrateSlices`' restore origins keep it out of the veto and the unhandled check (§6.4 steps 4 and 12), so `TestStore.Seed` and restores are clean in strict mode. `SetState<TState>` is a `Local`/`Effect` action like any user action: it is offered to `MayDispatch` and passes the unhandled check only because its SliceStore always handles it. Middleware recognises `HydrateSlices` by its restore `Origin` and the `@ducky/restore` ActionType, and `SetState` only by its `{sliceKey}/{name}` ActionType, which can coincide with a user `[ActionType("cart/Add")]` (documented on `ActionContext.ActionType`).

### 5.8 Attributes (runtime assembly; read by the generator, `[ActionType]` also at run time)

```csharp
[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class | AttributeTargets.Struct, Inherited = false)]
public sealed class DuckyActionAttribute : Attribute { public string? MethodName { get; init; } }

[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class | AttributeTargets.Struct, Inherited = false)]
public sealed class ActionTypeAttribute(string name) : Attribute { public string Name { get; } = name; }

[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class, Inherited = false)]
public sealed class PersistAttribute : Attribute
{
    public int Version { get; init; } = 1;
    public PersistStorage Storage { get; init; } = PersistStorage.Local;
    public bool SyncAcrossTabs { get; init; }
}

[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class, Inherited = false)]
public sealed class PrerenderAttribute : Attribute;

public enum PersistStorage { Local, Session, Server }        // consumed by Ducky.Blazor; lives here so [Persist] can use it
```

The runtime never reflects over `[DuckyAction]`, `[Persist]` or `[Prerender]`: GEN-02 turns `[Persist]` and `[Prerender]` into builder calls (ADR-0012). `[ActionType]` is the one attribute read at run time, once per action type per store (§9), with `type.GetCustomAttribute<ActionTypeAttribute>(inherit: false)`: it must work for actions from assemblies that never ran the generator, and it is trim-safe (no IL2xxx; attributes on kept types survive trimming). The AOT smoke asserts it after ILC (`ActionType_AttributeName_SurvivesAot`, §10).

### 5.9 Selectors (SEL-01)

```csharp
public static class Selector
{
    public static SelectorDef<T> Define<T1, T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T1> in1, Func<T1, T> projector);
    public static SelectorDef<T> Define<T1, T2, T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T1> in1, Func<StateSnapshot, T2> in2, Func<T1, T2, T> projector);
    public static SelectorDef<T> Define<T1, T2, T3, T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T1> in1, Func<StateSnapshot, T2> in2, Func<StateSnapshot, T3> in3, Func<T1, T2, T3, T> projector);
    public static SelectorDef<T> Define<T1, T2, T3, T4, T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T1> in1, Func<StateSnapshot, T2> in2, Func<StateSnapshot, T3> in3, Func<StateSnapshot, T4> in4, Func<T1, T2, T3, T4, T> projector);
    public static SelectorDef<TArg, T> Define<T1, TArg, T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T1> in1, Func<T1, TArg, T> projector);
    public static SelectorDef<TArg, T> Define<T1, T2, TArg, T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T1> in1, Func<StateSnapshot, T2> in2, Func<T1, T2, TArg, T> projector);
    public static SelectorDef<TArg, T> Define<T1, T2, T3, TArg, T>(/* 3 inputs */);
    public static SelectorDef<TArg, T> Define<T1, T2, T3, T4, TArg, T>(/* 4 inputs */);
}

public sealed class SelectorDef<T>                            // immutable, holds no cache: safe as a static field
{
    public T Evaluate(StateSnapshot state);                   // uncached
    public Func<StateSnapshot, T> Create();                   // new single-entry cache per call
}

public sealed class SelectorDef<TArg, T>
{
    public T Evaluate(StateSnapshot state, TArg arg);
    public Func<StateSnapshot, T> Create(Func<TArg> argument, IEqualityComparer<TArg>? argComparer = null);
}
```

Usage: `done = Select(DoneCount.Create());`, `todo = Select(ById.Create(() => Id));`. One `Select` signature is used throughout the library.

### 5.10 Entities (IMM-01)

```csharp
public interface IEntity<out TKey> where TKey : notnull { TKey Id { get; } }
public enum MergeStrategy { FailIfDuplicate, PreferExisting, PreferIncoming }

public sealed class EntityState<TKey, TEntity> where TKey : notnull where TEntity : class, IEntity<TKey>
{
    public static EntityState<TKey, TEntity> Empty { get; }
    [JsonConstructor] public EntityState(ImmutableList<TEntity> items);        // throws ArgumentException on duplicate ids
    public ImmutableList<TEntity> Items { get; }                              // insertion order; the only serialized member
    [JsonIgnore] public int Count { get; }
    public TEntity? Find(TKey id);
    public bool Contains(TKey id);
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> SetAll(IEnumerable<TEntity> entities);
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> Add(params ReadOnlySpan<TEntity> entities);        // ArgumentException on existing id
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> Upsert(params ReadOnlySpan<TEntity> entities);     // `this` if every entity is ReferenceEquals to the stored one
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> Update(TKey id, Func<TEntity, TEntity> update);    // `this` if missing or unchanged
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> Map(Func<TEntity, TEntity> map);                   // `this` if no entity changed
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> Remove(params ReadOnlySpan<TKey> ids);             // `this` if nothing removed
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> RemoveWhere(Func<TEntity, bool> predicate);
    public EntityState<TKey, TEntity> Merge(IEnumerable<TEntity> entities, MergeStrategy strategy);
}
```

The id index is a lazily built `FrozenDictionary<TKey,int>` cached per instance (never recomputed per access). This fixes the 1.x `AllIds` recomputation and keeps insertion order (INV-32).

---

## 6. Core behaviour

### 6.1 Slice keys and registry

- `SliceKey.FromType(Type t)`:
  1. Take `t.Name`. For a nested type, prefix each containing type's name joined with `-`.
  2. Strip one trailing `Slice`, `Store`, `Reducers` or `Reducer`.
  3. Kebab-case with acronym handling.

  Examples: `TodoSlice` → `todo`, `ShoppingCartReducers` → `shopping-cart`, `UIStateSlice` → `ui-state`, `CartStore` → `cart`, `Features.TodosSlice` (nested) → `features-todos`. A generic slice class must override `Key` (DUCKY302). Table test: `SliceKey_FromType_Table`.
- An overridden `Key` must match `^(@ducky/)?[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$` (DUCKY303). The `@ducky/` prefix is allowed only for a slice whose assembly carries the same public key token as `Ducky.dll` (the shipped packages share one strong-name key, §3), so library slices such as `@ducky/persistence` pass and user assemblies can't claim the prefix by accident (the reservation is anti-accident only: the key is checked in, §18).
- `AddSlice<TSlice>()` creates one throwaway `new TSlice()` at registration to read `Key` and `StateType`. Slices are pure and cheap. Duplicate keys (DUCKY304) and duplicate state types (DUCKY305) are configuration errors.
- `On<T>` writes into a private `Dictionary<Type, Func<TState, object, TState>>`. It throws `InvalidOperationException` on a duplicate `T` (DUCKY308 at runtime, DUCKY014 at compile time), when called after the store froze the slice, or when `T` is abstract, an interface or `object` (DUCKY307 at runtime, DUCKY005 at compile time). `AddSlice` records these as configuration errors (§5.1). At store build, the table becomes a `FrozenDictionary` keyed by `action.GetType()`: exact match only (ADR-0008).
- Internal members of `Slice`: `object InitialState` (published to other packages through `IStore.InitialState`), `bool CanHandle(Type)` (excludes the internal restore handler), `bool TryReduce(object state, object action, out object next)`, and `bool TryRestore(object value, DuckyJson json, out object state)`. `TryRestore` deserializes with the `JsonTypeInfo` of the declared `StateType`.

### 6.2 Snapshot

`StateSnapshot` holds a reference to a frozen `Registry`, an `object[] _states` (index = slice ordinal fixed at build), a `ulong[] _restored` bitset, and `long Version`. The `Registry` holds `string[] Keys`, `FrozenDictionary<Type,int>` by state type, `FrozenDictionary<string,int>` by key, and the slices. A commit that changes k slices clones `_states` (O(slice count)), writes the k slots, increments `Version` and publishes with `Volatile.Write`. A commit that changes nothing publishes nothing. `_restored` is copied only by a Hydration-origin restore. Reads cost one volatile read and one dictionary lookup, with no lock.

### 6.3 Dispatcher

```csharp
internal sealed class Dispatcher
{
    private readonly Lock _gate = new();
    private readonly Queue<Pending> _queue = new();       // main queue, plain FIFO
    private readonly Queue<Pending> _initBuffer = new();  // user actions (Local/Effect) before Ready
    private StoreState _state;                            // Created → Initializing → Ready → Disposed
    private bool _draining;
    private long _lastId;                                 // Ids are assigned under _gate: enqueue-linearization order (INV-04); the first Id is 1
    private int _runningEffects;                          // non-long-running runs; mutated under _gate
    private TaskCompletionSource? _drainExited;           // RunContinuationsAsynchronously; created when a drain starts, set when it releases
    private long _processingSeq;                          // Id of the action being processed; 0 between actions and while idle (§6.5)
    private readonly AsyncLocal<Cause?> _causal = new();  // the causal scope (§6.5): per store, never static
    private readonly AsyncLocal<EffectRunToken?> _effectRun = new(); // the effect-run scope (§6.6): per store, never static

    public void Enqueue(Pending p)                        // p.Depth/p.CorrelationId/p.InFailure were computed from this store's _causal (§6.5)
    {
        bool startInit = false, abort = false, drain = false, disposed = false;
        lock (_gate)
        {
            if (_state == StoreState.Disposed) disposed = true;
            else
            {
                p.Id = ++_lastId;
                if (p.CorrelationId == 0) p.CorrelationId = p.Id;       // a new chain
                if (_state != StoreState.Ready && p.Origin is Origin.Local or Origin.Effect)
                {
                    _initBuffer.Enqueue(p);                             // never dropped (INV-13)
                    if (_state == StoreState.Created) { _state = StoreState.Initializing; startInit = true; }
                    abort = _initBuffer.Count > _capacity;              // soft bound
                }
                else
                {
                    _queue.Enqueue(p);
                    if (!_draining) { _draining = true; _drainExited = new(RunContinuationsAsynchronously); drain = true; }
                }
            }
        }
```
