```csharp
        // Everything below runs outside the lock (INV-05): logging providers and completions are third-party code.
        if (disposed) { p.Complete(DispatchResult.Disposed); Log.DispatchAfterDispose(p.Type); return; }
        if (startInit) _initializer.Start();
        if (abort) _initializer.RequestOverflowAbort();                  // queues Abort(BufferOverflow) through QueueWorkItem, never runs it here;
                                                                        // a no-op until Start has started every middleware (§6.7)
        if (drain) Drain();                                             // on the caller's context: WASM and the Blazor sync context need no hop
    }

    private void Drain()
    {
        while (true)
        {
            Pending p;
            TaskCompletionSource? exited = null;
            IdleWaiters? idle = null;
            lock (_gate)
            {
                if (_state == StoreState.Disposed || !_queue.TryDequeue(out p))
                {
                    _draining = false;                                  // same critical section as the empty check (INV-03)
                    exited = _drainExited;
                    idle = TakeIdleWaitersIfIdleLocked();               // detaches the waiters; completes nothing
                }
            }
            if (exited is not null) { exited.TrySetResult(); idle?.CompleteAll(); return; }   // outside the lock
            try { Process(p); }                                         // no lock held (INV-05, INV-09)
            catch (Exception ex)                                        // INV-03
            {
                p.Complete(DispatchResult.Failed);                      // first: the action is released before anything else can throw
                try { Log.ProcessEscaped(ex, p.Type); } catch { }       // even SafeLogger's OutOfMemoryException rethrow can't hold the drain
            }
        }
    }

    // Called once by the initializer when init completes, times out or is aborted.
    internal void MarkReady()
    {
        bool drain = false;
        lock (_gate)
        {
            if (_state is StoreState.Ready or StoreState.Disposed) return;   // exactly once (INV-13)
            _state = StoreState.Ready;
            var init = Pending.System(new StoreInitialized(), completion: _sharedInit);   // the shared init TCS (§6.7): it completes when
            init.Id = init.CorrelationId = ++_lastId;                                     // StoreInitialized is processed, never here
            _queue.Enqueue(init);                                            // after any system/restore action already queued
            while (_initBuffer.TryDequeue(out var b)) _queue.Enqueue(b);      // original order (INV-04); Ids were assigned at enqueue
            if (!_draining) { _draining = true; _drainExited = new(RunContinuationsAsynchronously); drain = true; }
        }
        if (drain) Drain();
    }
}
```

- `Pending` is a sealed class: `(object Action, Origin Origin, int Depth, long CorrelationId, bool InFailure, bool IsFailure, TaskCompletionSource<DispatchResult>? Tcs, EffectRunToken? Run, ActivityContext ParentActivity)` plus `long Id`, assigned under `_gate` in `Enqueue` (and in `MarkReady` for `StoreInitialized`). `Id` is the per-store monotonic `ActionContext.Id` and the sequence number used by the causal scope (`_causal`, §6.5). `ParentActivity` is `Activity.Current?.Context` captured on the producer's flow when the `Pending` is created, before `Enqueue` (§6.4 step 2, §9). Only `DispatchAsync` creates a TCS, always with `RunContinuationsAsynchronously` (INV-10); the one exception is `StoreInitialized`, whose `Pending` carries the store's shared init TCS (also `RunContinuationsAsynchronously`), so the shared init task completes exactly when `StoreInitialized` completes, whatever the outcome (step 8, or `Failed` when a reducer throws on it), or `Disposed` if the store is disposed first (§6.7).
- **`Process` never escapes (INV-03), and one throw never skips a later step.** Every step of `Process` isolates the user code it calls, per call, not just `Process` as a whole: a committed action that already completed `Reduced` must still reach every subscriber, start every effect and run the unhandled check, and an action that failed in `BeforeReduce` or a reducer still reaches `AfterReduce` and starts its effects (§6.4, "What runs after a veto or a failure"). Step 10 runs each subscription's selector, comparer and `onChange` together inside that subscription's try/catch (a throw is logged, `last` stays unchanged and the subscription stays, `Subscriber_SelectorThrows_OthersNotifiedAndEffectsStillStart`); step 11 computes each effect's `ConcurrencyKey` or `EffectGroup` key function inside that effect's try/catch and routes a throw to `FailureRouter` as `EffectFailed` for that effect only, while the other effects still start (`Effect_ConcurrencyKeyThrows_EffectFailedOtherEffectsStart`); steps 9, 10, 11 and 12 are each wrapped separately, and neither a log call nor a telemetry call can throw (the `SafeLogger` and `SafeTelemetry` rules below), so a throwing logging provider, `ActivityListener` or `MeterListener` can't skip a step. The catch-all in `Drain` stays as the last line of defence: it completes `p` with `Failed` if it is still pending (`Complete` is a `TrySetResult`), then logs Error (EventId 1012, "unexpected exception while processing {Type}") inside its own `try { } catch { }`, and continues the loop, so `_draining` is always released and `_drainExited` always completes, even if the log call rethrows a fatal exception. `Process`'s own `finally` has already restored `_causal`, `_effectRun`, `_processingSeq` and `Activity.Current`. Because every step is isolated, the catch-all is reachable only through an internal IVT seam on the store, `internal Action<object>? BeforeProcessHook` (null in production, invoked with the action at the top of `Process`, like `AfterSubscribeHook`, §17.1): `Drain_ProcessThrowsUnexpectedly_NextActionStillDrained` throws from that hook and asserts that the action completes `Failed` and the next action is drained, and `Drain_ProcessThrowsAndLogRethrowsFatal_ActionCompletedAndNextDrained` adds a logging provider whose EventId 1012 log rethrows as `OutOfMemoryException`, pinning the order (complete first) and the inner `try/catch`.
- **No telemetry call can throw (`SafeTelemetry`).** `ActivitySource.StartActivity`, `Activity.Stop` and every `Meter` instrument call (`ducky.dispatch.dropped` in steps 1 and 3, `ducky.effect.dropped` in step 11, `ducky.dispatch.duration`, `ducky.effect.failures`, and Ducky.Blazor's `ducky.persistence.*`) run `ActivityListener`/`MeterListener` callbacks synchronously, and those are user code (an exporter, a test `MetricCollector`). Each package that records telemetry (Ducky, Ducky.Blazor) calls it only through its own small internal `SafeTelemetry` (`StartActivity`, `Stop`, `Add`, `Record`), each wrapped in a `try/catch` that logs Warning (EventId 1017 in Ducky, 2024 in Ducky.Blazor) through `SafeLogger`. So a throwing listener on a depth-dropped action still lets step 1 enqueue its `ReducerFailed(DispatchLoopException)` (INV-06), and a throwing Exhaust drop counter still lets the action's other effects start (`Telemetry_ListenerThrows_LaterStepsStillRun`, in `Ducky.Tests` with a throwing `ActivityListener.ActivityStarted` and a throwing `MeterListener` callback on a depth drop and an Exhaust drop, and in `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` for its persistence counters).
- **No `Cancel()` of a user-registrable token escapes.** Every cancellation of a CTS that user code can register on is either `CancelAsync()`, which keeps callbacks off the calling thread (the Switch supersession on the drainer, §6.6), or `Cancel()` wrapped in `try/catch (AggregateException)` that logs Error (EventId 1013) (`_initCts` in `Abort`, whose callbacks must run synchronously, §6.7, and the store-lifetime CTS in `DisposeAsync`, §6.11). `Cancel()` runs every callback even when one throws, so the persistence callback still runs.
- **No completion under `_gate`.** Every TCS the dispatcher completes (dispatch results, `_drainExited`, idle waiters, the init task) is created with `RunContinuationsAsynchronously` and completed after the lock is released. Log calls are also made after the release (INV-05).
- **No log call can throw (`SafeLogger`).** A logging provider is third-party code, and `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Logger` rethrows provider failures as an `AggregateException`. Rather than guarding each call site, each package (Ducky, Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive) wraps every `ILogger` it logs through, once, where it obtains it (the store at construction; each Ducky.Blazor and Ducky.Reactive middleware, effect and component in its constructor), in its own internal `SafeLogger : ILogger` whose `Log`, `IsEnabled` and `BeginScope` catch every non-fatal exception (anything except `OutOfMemoryException`, as in §10; `BeginScope` then returns a no-op scope). Every `[LoggerMessage]` method takes that wrapper, so no Ducky log call anywhere (the lifecycle paths outside the drain included: `Abort` before `MarkReady`, the EventId 1013 log inside the `Cancel()` guard, `Start` step 4's faulted-init log, dispose's EventId 1015 logs, `Enqueue`'s `DispatchAfterDispose`, and Ducky.Blazor's timeout callbacks and auth handler) can skip the step that follows it, terminate the process from a timer or thread-pool callback, fault `DisposeAsync` or throw out of `Dispatch`. Each package's copy is covered in its own test project (`SafeLogger_ProviderThrows_NeverEscapes` in `Ducky.Tests`, `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` and `Ducky.Reactive.Tests`); `Lifecycle_ThrowingLoggerProvider_StoreReadyAndDisposeComplete` (§17.5) runs an overflow abort, a timer abort, a faulted middleware init, a throwing middleware `DisposeAsync` (1015) and a `Dispatch` after dispose with a throwing provider, and asserts that the store reaches `Ready`, `DisposeAsync` completes without faulting and `Dispatch` does not throw.
- The main queue holds only `System`, `Hydration`, `CrossTab` and `DevTools` actions until `Ready`. `MarkReady` appends `StoreInitialized` and then the buffer under one lock. This makes the terminal hydration action (queued earlier) precede `StoreInitialized`, which precedes the replayed user actions, which precede anything dispatched after `Ready` (INV-04). No `PushFront` is needed. A buffered action keeps the Id it got at enqueue, so Ids follow enqueue-linearization order even across the gate; they are therefore not monotonic in processing order across it (`StoreInitialized` gets the largest Id at `MarkReady` and is processed before lower-Id buffered actions, INV-04).

### 6.4 `Process(p)`: the pipeline, in fixed order

1. **Depth check.** If `p.Depth > MaxDispatchDepth`: complete `Dropped` and count `ducky.dispatch.dropped` (`ducky.drop.reason = depth`). Then, through `FailureRouter`, enqueue `ReducerFailed(type, null, new DispatchLoopException(type, depth))` as a core failure action at depth 0, or only log it when `p.InFailure` (the chain descends synchronously from a failure action, INV-12). This step runs before step 2 installs `p`'s scope, so `_causal.Value` is still the drainer's ambient value (null, or a stale scope carried by whatever flow became the drainer): the router takes `CorrelationId = p.CorrelationId` and `InFailure = p.InFailure` from `p` explicitly, never from `_causal` (`LoopGuardFailure_IsReducedNotDropped` asserts the correlation id). The same check applies to every origin (INV-06).
2. **Scope.** `Volatile.Write(ref _processingSeq, p.Id)`, `_causal.Value = (p.Id, p.Depth, p.CorrelationId, InFailure: p.InFailure || p.IsFailure)` and `_effectRun.Value = null` (§6.5, §6.6), both on this store's own instance fields. All three are restored in `finally`: the two scopes to their previous values and `_processingSeq` to 0, so "currently processing" is false between actions and while idle (§6.5). Clearing `_effectRun` matters when an effect continuation became the drainer: it drains other producers' actions inline, and a `SliceStore.Set`, `SetAsync` or `DisposeAsync` called by a subscriber, `onChange`, middleware hook or synchronous reactive emission during that drain must not be tagged as that run's (`SliceStore_SetFromSubscriberDuringDrainOwnedByEffectContinuation_IsLocal`). Only `RunAsync` sets `_effectRun`, for its own run, so an effect's synchronous prefix keeps its scope. Save `Activity.Current`, then, if a listener exists, start the `ducky.dispatch` Activity with `p.ParentActivity` as its explicit parent (the producer's span, captured when the `Pending` was created, §6.3), never the drainer's ambient activity: with one inline drainer, actions enqueued by other threads would otherwise get the drainer's span, or the previous action's, as parent (`Tracing_ActionFromOtherThread_ParentIsProducerActivity`). The same `finally` stops it and restores the saved `Activity.Current` (an `AsyncLocal`; `Stop` alone would set it to the new activity's parent, which is not the drainer's), so no activity leaks into the caller's flow after `Dispatch` returns, the bug class fixed for the causal scope (`Tracing_ActivityCurrentRestoredAfterDispatch`). Both calls go through `SafeTelemetry` (§6.3).
3. **Run check.** If `p.Run` is set and its token is cancelled: when the store's disposal has begun (one volatile read of `_disposal`, §6.11; a dispose that ran its steps 1–2 after `p` was dequeued, which `BeforeProcessHook` reaches deterministically), complete `Disposed`, with no drop count, and return; otherwise the run was superseded (Switch, D8): complete `Dropped`, count `ducky.dispatch.dropped` (`ducky.drop.reason = run`), log Debug, return. This happens at process time, so FIFO guarantees that a stale Switch result queued before the supersession is still dropped.
4. **Veto.** Only for `Local` and `Effect` origins: call `MayDispatch` on each middleware in registration order. The first `false` completes `Vetoed` (Debug log). A throw becomes a failure (below) and the action completes `Failed`. Library origins (`System`, `Hydration`, `CrossTab`, `DevTools`) are never vetoed.
5. **BeforeReduce** on each middleware. A throw becomes a failure and `Failed`.
6. **Reduce.** An internal `HydrateSlices` goes to `TryRestore` on each named slice. A slice whose value fails to deserialize (`DuckyJson.TryDeserialize` returns false, §10) keeps its current state and is logged at Warning with its key and type; the other slices of the batch still restore, and a deserialization failure is never a reducer failure (`Hydration_OneSliceCtorThrows_OtherSlicesStillRestored`). Any other action goes, for each slice with `CanHandle(type)`, into a scratch array. If any reducer throws, the scratch array is discarded (INV-08) and the action completes `Failed`.
   - **Failures** (steps 4–6) go to `FailureRouter`: it enqueues one `ReducerFailed(type, sliceKey, ex)` as a core failure action at depth 0, unless the current scope has `InFailure` set (the action is a failure action or descends synchronously from one), in which case it only logs (INV-12).
   - **What runs after a veto or a failure (normative, INV-08).** A veto (step 4) completes `Vetoed` and ends `Process`; so does a throwing `MayDispatch`, which completes `Failed` (the action was never admitted). A `BeforeReduce` or reducer failure (steps 5–6) commits nothing, completes `Failed` in place of step 8, and skips steps 7, 10 and 12, but still runs step 9, with `ActionContext.State` equal to `PreviousState` and `ChangedKeys` empty, and step 11: effects react to the action, not to the commit, so one throwing reducer or hook can't disable the `StoreInitialized` effects (every load effect, and `ReactiveHost`, which subscribes every reactive pipeline, §14) for the store's lifetime (`ReducerThrowsOnStoreInitialized_LoadEffectAndReactiveHostStillStart`, `BeforeReduceThrows_NoCommitNoNotify_EffectsStart`). So the `AfterReduce` observers see a failed action too: DevTools sends it, `TestStore.Processed` records it, and persistence writes nothing for it (no key changed).
7. **Commit.** If any slot changed (`!ReferenceEquals`): new snapshot, `Version + 1`, `Volatile.Write` (INV-07).
8. **Complete** `p.Tcs` with `Reduced` (asynchronously).
9. **AfterReduce** on each middleware (persistence, DevTools, reactive bridge and user middleware, in registration order). A throw is logged and the other middleware still run.
10. **Notify** subscribers, only if the snapshot changed. Iterate the `ImmutableArray` captured at this point; each subscription whose volatile `_disposed` flag is still clear right before its selector runs has its selector, comparer and `onChange` run together in its own try/catch (INV-09, §6.8).
11. **Effects.** If the store-lifetime token is cancelled (disposal has begun, possibly re-entrantly from this action's reducer, subscriber or `AfterReduce`, or concurrently), start none, with a Debug log (`Dispose_FromAfterReduce_NoEffectStartedForThatAction`); this is checked once, before the first run. Otherwise start each effect registered for the exact type, inline up to the handler's first `await` (§6.6); a throwing key function fails only its own effect (above).
12. **Unhandled check** (CORE-08), only for `Local` and `Effect` origins. If no slice can handle the type and no effect matched: log at Debug (guarded by `IsEnabled`) "Action {Type} matched no reducer or async effect (reactive effects and middleware not inspected)". With `ThrowOnUnhandledAction`, route `UnhandledActionException` to `FailureRouter` instead. Library traffic (`HydrateSlices`, `StoreInitialized`, restores) is never checked, so `TestStore.Seed` and persistence work in strict mode (`TestStore_Seed_NoFailure`).

The drainer never awaits. Effects return to the drainer at their first real `await`, so the Blazor sync context is never blocked by effect I/O.

### 6.5 Causal depth (INV-06, ADR-0026)

- The causal scope is the dispatcher's instance field `private readonly AsyncLocal<Cause?> _causal = new()`, holding `(long Seq, int Depth, long CorrelationId, bool InFailure)`, set in step 2 and restored in `finally`, together with the reset of `_processingSeq` to 0. It is never left set in the caller's context: this is what dx-first got wrong (step 10, no restore). Its sibling, the effect-run scope, is the instance field `private readonly AsyncLocal<EffectRunToken?> _effectRun = new()` (§6.6).
- **A scope never crosses stores.** Both fields are per store, never static, and every reader (`Enqueue`, `EffectContext`, `SliceStore.Set`/`SetAsync`, `DisposeAsync`, `WhenIdleAsync`) reads the fields of the store it belongs to. Code that runs on store A's flow (A's effect run, A's drain) and calls into store B (the circuit-accessor pattern of §23: a singleton broadcaster, called from circuit A's effect, dispatching into circuit B's store) is, for B, an unrelated producer: B sees `Origin.Local` at depth 0 on a new chain, never A's run token (so superseding A's Switch run or disposing A never drops B's action), never A's `CountedForIdle` (so `B.WhenIdleAsync()` doesn't throw), never A's `Seq` (which B would compare with its own `_processingSeq`), and `B.DisposeAsync()` never completes A's run's `DisposeCalled` (`TwoStores_EffectRunOfA_DoesNotScopeDispatchSetOrWhenIdleOfB`). An `AsyncLocal` shared by every store would do all four; this is also why neither field is on the `NoStaticMutableFields` allow-list (§17.5), which stays exact.
- On `Enqueue` of **any** action (every origin: `Local`, `Effect`, `System` through `DispatchSystem`, and `Restore`), with `c = _causal.Value`:
  - a **core failure action** (`ReducerFailed`/`EffectFailed` from steps 1 and 6 and from effect observation) → depth 0, `IsFailure`, and the correlation id of the failed action: `c?.CorrelationId` from steps 4–6 and effect observation, where the scope is the failed action's own, but `p.CorrelationId` passed explicitly from step 1, which runs before the scope is installed (§6.4). This is the only exception to the rule below;
  - `c == null` → depth 0, a new chain (`CorrelationId` = the action's own `Id`, set under `_gate`), `InFailure` false;
  - `c.Seq == Volatile.Read(ref _processingSeq)` → **synchronous child**: depth `c.Depth + 1`, correlation `c.CorrelationId`, `InFailure = c.InFailure`. This covers dispatches and restores from reducers (forbidden by analyzers, but handled), middleware (`DispatchSystem` in `AfterReduce` included), subscribers (a `Restore` on every change included), and effect code that has not yet yielded (including `await` on an already completed task);
  - otherwise → **asynchronous continuation**: the parent has finished processing, so the effect has really yielded. Depth 0, correlation `c.CorrelationId` (the trace survives, the depth budget restarts), `InFailure` false.
- `_processingSeq` is 0 whenever no action is being processed, and Ids start at 1. A continuation that runs after its parent finished can therefore never match, including one driven inline on the test thread by `FakeTimeProvider.Advance` while the store is idle (`AsyncDispatchWhileStoreIdle_StartsNewChainAtDepthZero`).
- A continuation that races its own parent on another thread (parent still inside AfterReduce/notify) may be counted as synchronous. That is conservative: the chain stays bounded. Nothing else relies on this comparison; dispose does not (§6.11).
- `DispatchSystem(action, isFailure: true)` follows the normal depth rule and additionally marks the action as a failure action, so failures raised while handling it are logged only (INV-12).
- A consequence: a polling effect (`Tick → await Time.Delay → Dispatch(Tick)`) runs indefinitely (`PollingEffect_RunsBeyondMaxDepthTicks`, 200 ticks with `FakeTimeProvider`), while a synchronous ping-pong between two effects stops at 64 (`EffectPingPong_SynchronousPrefix_IsStoppedByDepthGuard`) and so does a `DispatchSystem` loop from `AfterReduce` (`DispatchSystemLoopFromAfterReduce_IsStoppedByDepthGuard`). Loops closed through a real asynchronous yield are recurrence, not dispatch loops. Use `Exhaust`/`Switch` and cancellation to control them (documented).

### 6.6 Effect runner (EFF-01, INV-11)

- **Materialization.** Effects and middleware are instantiated on the store's first use (the first `Enqueue`, `Restore`, `Select`, `State` read, `InitializeAsync` or `WhenIdleAsync`), after the `IStore` DI factory has returned, never inside it. Each is created with `ActivatorUtilities.CreateInstance<T>(storeServices)` (§6.10), or is the instance passed to `AddEffect(instance)`. The effect index (action type → runners) is built at the same time, once (`Lazy<T>`, `ExecutionAndPublication`; user constructors never run under `_gate`). Concurrent first users wait for that one materialization (the INV-05 carve-out; constructors must not block, and a constructor that hands work needing the store to another thread and waits for it deadlocks, which is exactly the blocking the rule forbids; `Materialization_ConcurrentFirstUse_SecondCallerWaitsThenSucceeds` in §17.3 races two first callers and asserts both complete with one materialization). If a constructor throws, the `Lazy` caches a `DuckyConfigurationException` (DUCKY353, §5.1) and every call that needs materialization, while the store is not disposed, rethrows that same instance, from `Dispatch`/`DispatchAsync`/`Restore`/`Select`/`State`/`InitializeAsync`/`WhenIdleAsync` synchronously (§7). Unresolvable constructor parameters never get this far: DUCKY309 reports them at first resolution. An effect or middleware constructor may inject a slice or a `SliceStore` (`Effect_InjectsSliceStore_Resolves`). A constructor that *uses* the store re-enters materialization and throws `InvalidOperationException`; DUCKY008 warns on injecting `IStore`/`IDispatcher` into effect and middleware constructors.
- **Materialization and disposal (INV-29).** Every materializing entry point (`Enqueue`'s callers `Dispatch`/`DispatchAsync`, `Restore`, `Select`, `State`, `InitializeAsync`, `WhenIdleAsync`) first calls `BeginMaterialize()`: under `_gate`, if `_state` is `Disposed` it returns false and the caller takes its after-disposal path (§6.11) without constructing anything; otherwise it sets `_materializationStarted` (and creates `_materialized`, a `RunContinuationsAsynchronously` TCS, the first time) and returns true, and the caller runs the `Lazy` outside the lock. Once materialization has completed, the fast path is one volatile read, and each entry point's own after-disposal check applies (`Enqueue`'s under `_gate`, §6.3). `DisposeAsync` step 1 sets `Disposed` in the same critical section, so materialization is either never started, and then never starts (5a and 5b dispose nothing; a server `DuckyComponent` that injected `IStore` but never selected is disposed without running a single user constructor or resolving from the disposing scope: `Dispose_NeverMaterialized_ConstructsNothing`, `Dispatch_AfterDisposeOnUnmaterializedStore_IgnoredNotThrown`), or in flight or done, and then dispose awaits `_materialized.Task` within `DisposeTimeout` (chained on it past the bound, like every dispose wait) before 5a and 5b dispose what it created (`Dispose_ConcurrentWithFirstUse_EveryConstructedMiddlewareDisposed` in §17.3, and its `…_Deterministic` twin in `Ducky.Tests`, whose middleware constructor calls `DisposeAsync` through a test-held reference and receives an incomplete task). The factory completes `_materialized` in its `finally`; it checks `_state` before resolving `StoreDisposeHook` and skips it once disposal has begun (the hook exists only to start a disposal that has already started). A factory that throws disposes the store-owned instances it already built (constructed by `ActivatorUtilities`, so DI doesn't track them; an `AddEffect(instance)` instance is never disposed), in reverse construction order, each in its own try/catch (Error, EventId 1015), and only then caches DUCKY353 (`Materialization_CtorThrows_EarlierInstancesDisposed`). If the throw happens after disposal began (a constructor resolving from a scope being disposed), the caller takes its after-disposal path instead of rethrowing, and `DisposeAsync` never rethrows DUCKY353.
- Each registration has one `EffectRunner` holding `ConcurrentDictionary<object, Slot>`. The key is `ConcurrencyKey(a) ?? NullKey` (a private sentinel), computed in step 11 inside that effect's own try/catch: a throwing key function (or its `Equals`/`GetHashCode` during install) becomes `EffectFailed` for that effect only, through `FailureRouter`, and the action's other effects still start (§6.3).
- **Every start installs a new immutable `Slot` instance. A completing run calls `TryRemove(KeyValuePair.Create(key, mySlot))`, which succeeds only while `mySlot` is still the current slot for that key.** This rule fixes the Queue flaw common to all three proposals.
- **Install (Switch, Queue).** On the drainer: build a side-effect-free `Slot` first (Switch: a new `Cts` linked to the store lifetime; Queue: a new `TaskCompletionSource Done` with `RunContinuationsAsynchronously`), then `AddOrUpdate(k, _ => { prev = null; return slot; }, (_, old) => { prev = old; return slot; })`. The factories only return the pre-built slot and assign the local `prev`; `AddOrUpdate` may call them several times, and the last call before it succeeds is the one whose result was installed, so `prev` is exactly the slot that was replaced (or null). There is no retry loop and no dead `TryAdd` branch in Ducky's code. Only after the install does the drainer cancel `prev.Cts` (Switch) or start the run chained on `prev?.Done` (Queue). The test `Queue_InstallRacesCompletionRemove_HandlerRunsOncePerAction` (CsCheck `SampleParallel`) races installs against completing runs and asserts one handler call per action.
- **Switch CTS ownership.** Whoever takes a slot out of the dictionary owns its `Cts`: the run, if its own `TryRemove` succeeds, disposes it in `finally`; otherwise the drainer that replaced it cancels it with `CancelAsync()` and disposes it in a continuation of that task, which also logs a callback fault at Warning (EventId 1014). So a `Cts` is never cancelled after it was disposed (no `ObjectDisposedException` path exists), user cancellation callbacks never run on the drainer, and a throwing callback can't reach `Process` (`Switch_CancelCallbackThrows_DrainerSurvives`).

| Policy | Slot contents | On a new action for key k |
|---|---|---|
| Merge | – (no slot; counted only) | start with the store-lifetime token itself (no per-run CTS, so nothing to dispose and no registration left on the store token) |
| Switch | `Cts` (linked to the store lifetime) | install, then (if `prev` exists) `prev.Cts.CancelAsync()` with the ownership rule above, then start. Each run compare-and-removes its own slot in `finally` and disposes its `Cts` only when that removal succeeded |
| Exhaust | `Running` marker | `TryAdd(k, new Slot())` fails → drop (Debug log, `ducky.effect.dropped`), else start with the store-lifetime token itself, like Merge (the marker needs no CTS) |
| Queue | `Done` TCS | install, then start a run that awaits `prev.Done.Task.WaitAsync(runToken)` when `prev` exists (the `Done` task itself never faults). If the run token is cancelled while it waits (store disposal), the handler is **never** invoked; the resulting `OperationCanceledException` is our cancellation, not a failure. In `finally`, whether the handler ran or not, the run compare-and-removes its own slot, then completes its own `Done`, so a successor never waits on a disposed store's handler. Only the run whose slot is still current removes it, so run 3 always chains after run 2 (`Queue_DisposeFromRunWithQueuedSuccessor_CompletesWithoutTimeout`, `Queue_AfterDispose_HandlerNeverInvoked`) |

- **Run registry.** Every started run, whatever its policy (Merge, Switch, Exhaust, Queue, `LongRunning` included), is registered in the store's `ConcurrentDictionary<long, (Task Run, EffectRunToken Token)> _runs`: in step 11 the drainer takes a run id, starts `RunAsync`, and, if the returned task is not already complete, adds the entry and attaches `ContinueWith(_ => _runs.TryRemove(id, out _), ExecuteSynchronously)` (a task that completes between the check and the add is removed by that continuation at once). `_runningEffects` stays the idle counter; `_runs` is what dispose awaits (§6.11 step 4), so Merge and `LongRunning` runs are awaited too (`Dispose_AwaitsMergeAndLongRunningRuns`).
- `EffectRunToken` is a **new object per run**: it carries the run id (the `_runs` key) and the run's `CancellationToken` (the store-lifetime token for Merge and Exhaust, the slot's linked token for Switch, the Queue run's token). Several runs may share one `CancellationToken`, so identity is always the `EffectRunToken` reference or its run id, never `CancellationToken` equality. It also carries a `DisposeCalled` TCS (`RunContinuationsAsynchronously`), completed by every `DisposeAsync` call made from that run (§6.11), and a `CountedForIdle` flag, true for a non-`LongRunning` run until its idle decrement runs (below), which the same store's `WhenIdleAsync` reads through `_effectRun` (§7.4). `EffectContext.Dispatch`/`DispatchAsync` first checks the store's disposal state (one volatile read of `_disposal`, which `DisposeAsync` publishes before its step 1, §6.11): once disposal has begun, the call completes `Disposed` (`Dispatch`: ignored with a Debug log), whatever the run's token, and nothing is counted, so shutdown traffic of Merge, Exhaust and Queue runs (whose token is the store-lifetime token or linked to it) never shows up as dropped runs. Only on a live store does it check its run token, where a cancelled token can only mean Switch supersession, and it stores the token in `Pending.Run`, so the dispatcher checks it again at process time (step 3); a call-time drop (here and in `SliceStore.Set`/`SetAsync`, §12, which apply the same precedence) is logged at Debug and counted like step 3 (`ducky.dispatch.dropped`, `ducky.drop.reason = run`, INV-02). While a run executes, its store's `_effectRun` (§6.5) holds that `EffectRunToken` object (set only by `RunAsync`, cleared by `Process` for every action, §6.4 step 2), which lets that store's `DisposeAsync` recognise the calling run by reference (§6.11) and lets its `SliceStore.Set`/`SetAsync` scope themselves to the run (§12); another store never sees it (§6.5).
- Observation: every run is an `async Task RunAsync()` with try/catch/finally:
  - `_runningEffects` (non-long-running only) is incremented under `_gate` when `RunAsync` is created in step 11, before it awaits any Queue tail, and decremented under `_gate` in `finally`. **Order inside the `finally` (normative):** the slot compare-and-remove, then the Queue `Done` completion, then the Switch `Cts` disposal (when this run owns it), and only then, as the last actions of `RunAsync`, the `_runningEffects` decrement (which also clears the token's `CountedForIdle`) and the idle-waiter take. An idle waiter's continuation, which runs asynchronously on the thread pool, therefore never observes the run's slot still present, whichever thread the run completed on. When the decrement reaches 0 the run takes the idle waiters if the store is idle (`TakeIdleWaitersIfIdleLocked`) and completes them after releasing the lock. An effect-caused action is enqueued before the run ends, so idle can't fire while it is pending (`WhenIdle_NeverCompletesWhileEffectCausedActionPending`), and idle can't fire between two Queue runs.
  - An `OperationCanceledException` is cancellation iff `ex.CancellationToken == runToken || runToken.IsCancellationRequested`. Anything else (including an `HttpClient` timeout) goes to `FailureRouter`, which enqueues `EffectFailed` as a core failure action at depth 0, unless the run's trigger was a failure action (its scope had `InFailure` set): then the fault is logged only (INV-12, `EffectOnEffectFailed_ThrowsSync_LogsOnly_NoLoop`, `EffectOnEffectFailed_ThrowsAfterAwait_LogsOnly`).
  - A synchronous throw before the first `await` is caught the same way.
  - The observer never uses the cancellable token (the 1.x lost-fault bug; `Effect_FaultDuringDispose_StillObserved`).
- `EffectRunner.SlotCount` is an ordinary `internal` property in every configuration, exposed to the package's own test assembly through `InternalsVisibleTo` (allowed by R-PKG-2). The rule the effect tests assert: no non-`LongRunning` slot remains once `WhenIdleAsync` has completed (`WhenIdleAsync` ignores `LongRunning` runs, so a `LongRunning` Switch, Exhaust or Queue effect may still hold its slot then), and every slot dictionary is empty once `_runs` is empty. `Queue_ThreeRuns_NeverOverlap_SlotEmptyAtIdle` creates its last run's gating TCS with `RunContinuationsAsynchronously`, so that run's `finally` executes on a thread-pool thread while the test awaits `WhenIdleAsync`, and asserts `SlotCount == 0` when the wait completes.

### 6.7 Initialization lifecycle (SSR-02, INV-13, ADR-0015)

- Init starts at the first of: `InitializeAsync`, `WhenIdleAsync`, `Dispatch`/`DispatchAsync` of a user action, `Select`, a read of `IStore.State` (and so of `SliceStore.State`). The trigger never starts init under `_gate`: `Enqueue` flags it, then calls `_initializer.Start()` after releasing the lock. The other triggers call `Start()` directly; its fast path, once started, is one volatile read.
- `InitCoordinator` is an `Interlocked` state machine `NotStarted → Starting → Running → Completed`. `_initCts` (linked to the store lifetime) is created with the coordinator, so it exists before any transition.
  - `Start()`: CAS `NotStarted → Starting`, else return (idempotent).
    1. **Start** every middleware's `InitializeAsync(_initCts.Token)` in registration order, without awaiting between them. Each runs synchronously up to its first `await`. This guarantees that the synchronous part of every middleware (for example the prerender seed take in §11.4, and every registration on the init token) runs before `Start()` returns, whatever the registration order. Middleware that registers on the init token MUST do so in this synchronous part. Normatively, each call is `if (state != Starting) break; try { t = mw.InitializeAsync(tok).AsTask(); } catch (Exception ex) { t = Task.FromException(ex); } _initTasks.Add(t);`: a middleware that throws synchronously (any non-async override, for example one calling `Store.Restore(values, Origin.System)`, which throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`) behaves exactly like one whose task faults, the next middleware still starts, and nothing escapes `Start()` into `Dispatch`, `State`, `Select` or `InitializeAsync` (`Init_MiddlewareInitializeThrowsSynchronously_StoreStillReady`). The state check stops the loop once `Retire()` has run (a dispose on another thread, or re-entrantly from a synchronous prefix), so no further `InitializeAsync` starts after disposal began.
    2. Whatever happens next, publish `InitTasks`, one entry per middleware by registration index (its init task, or none when it was never started), with `Task.WhenAll` of the started ones, and then complete `_prefixDone` (a TCS with `RunContinuationsAsynchronously`), so dispose always sees every init that really started (§6.11 step 4). Then CAS `Starting → Running`; if it fails, `Retire()` won: return without arming anything. Otherwise arm the `InitTimeout` timer on `TimeProvider`. The timer is armed only after every synchronous part returned, so no cancellation of `_initCts` can race a registration made there (a synchronous part must not block; `InitTimeout` bounds the asynchronous part).
    3. If the init buffer is over `InitBufferCapacity` (read under `_gate`), call `RequestOverflowAbort()`: an overflow that happened while `Starting` or before `Start` was a no-op and is handled here, from `Running`.
    4. Await `InitTasks`, observing each task individually: a middleware whose task faults (synchronously or asynchronously) is logged at Error and counted as finished.
    5. Then `Complete()`.

    When every started `InitializeAsync` returned an already-completed task, steps 4–5 run synchronously on the calling thread (no yield), so `Complete()`→`MarkReady()` drains inline when no other drain is active, and the `Dispatch` that triggered init returns after its action is reduced (`Init_AllInitsCompleteSynchronously_FirstDispatchReducedInline`). Ducky.Testing's synchronous `SliceTest.Then` relies on it (§15).
  - `Complete()`: CAS `Running → Completed`, else return. Dispose the timer, then `Dispatcher.MarkReady()` (`Init_Completed_TimerNeverAborts`).
  - `RequestOverflowAbort()` (from `Enqueue` and `Start` step 3): if the state is `Running` and a `_overflowAbortQueued` flag CASes 0→1, hand `Abort(BufferOverflow)` to the store's internal work-item seam, `internal Action<Action> QueueWorkItem { get; set; } = static a => ThreadPool.UnsafeQueueUserWorkItem(static s => ((Action)s!)(), a, preferLocal: false);` (an IVT-only instance delegate defaulting to the production call, like `AfterSubscribeHook`, §17.1). Otherwise it does nothing: from `NotStarted`/`Starting` without setting the flag, so `Start` step 3 re-checks (the buffer bound is soft, and the thread that flagged `startInit` is guaranteed to call `Start()`, so every middleware's `InitializeAsync` always runs before `Ready`: seed, hydration, cross-tab watch and DevTools are never skipped; `Init_OverflowBetweenFlagAndStart_MiddlewareInitStillRuns` races it for real, and `Init_OverflowDuringSyncPrefix_AbortsFromRunning`, a middleware whose synchronous part dispatches `InitBufferCapacity + 1` user actions, covers the deferred path deterministically); from `Completed`, one volatile read; and when the flag is already 1 (an abort is queued but has not run yet), the failed flag CAS. Abort cancels `_initCts` synchronously, and the persistence callback then waits for `_issue`, which another thread may hold while it drains inline under a hot producer; running it on the thread pool keeps that wait off the caller (a `State` read or `Select` on the Blazor renderer thread included), so INV-05 holds without a second carve-out. Ordering is unchanged: the callback still takes `_issue` and queues the terminal before `MarkReady`, and nothing is dropped. `Ducky.Tests` replaces `QueueWorkItem` with a capturing queue and runs the captured abort at chosen points, so every branch is deterministic: `Init_OverflowAbort_NeverRunsOnCaller` (a middleware whose init-token callback waits on a TCS, as persistence waits for `_issue`, and an overflowing `Dispatch` that returns before the captured abort runs), `Init_OverflowTwiceBeforeQueuedAbortRuns_QueuedOnce` (the flag CAS fails, one work item is captured) and `Init_QueuedOverflowAbortRunsAfterComplete_NoOp` (`Abort`'s CAS fails).
  - `Abort(reason)` (the `InitTimeout` timer or a queued overflow abort) is one CAS `Running → Completed` with a single failure arm:
    - on success: dispose the timer, cancel `_initCts` **outside any lock** with `Cancel()` in a `try/catch (AggregateException)` that logs Error (EventId 1013) and continues (cancellation callbacks run synchronously and all of them run even if one throws; the persistence middleware enqueues its terminal `HydrationFailed` from there as a `System` action, atomically with any concurrent read completion (§11.5), so it is queued before `StoreInitialized`), log Error `StoreInitAborted(reason)` once, then `MarkReady()` (`Init_AbortCallbackThrows_StoreStillReady`). Every log call here goes through `SafeLogger` (§6.3), so none can skip `MarkReady`. Middleware tasks that finish later are observed and logged, never awaited;
    - on failure (`Complete()`, `Retire()` or the other abort won; deterministically, a queued overflow abort that runs after `Complete()`): return, doing nothing.
  - `Retire()` (called by `DisposeAsync`, §6.11 step 1): CAS any state to `Completed`, dispose the timer, and never call `MarkReady` (dispose completes the shared init task itself). An armed `InitTimeout` therefore never fires on a disposed store: no spurious `StoreInitAborted` Error, no `_initCts` cancellation from the timer thread (`Dispose_DuringInit_TimerNeverFires_NoAbortLog`). It returns `_prefixDone.Task` when the state it replaced was `Starting` (a `Start()` is inside step 1: it starts no further middleware, publishes `InitTasks` for those it did start, and then completes `_prefixDone`), and a completed task otherwise; a `Start()` that has not yet won `NotStarted → Starting` loses its CAS and starts nothing. Dispose awaits `_prefixDone` and then `InitTasks` within its bound (§6.11 step 4); a dispose called from a synchronous prefix only receives an incomplete task, so it can't deadlock (`Dispose_DuringStartSyncPrefix_AwaitsStartedInitsAndStartsNoMore`, made deterministic by a middleware whose synchronous prefix calls `DisposeAsync`).
- `MarkReady` runs exactly once. The shared init task is the completion of `StoreInitialized`'s `Pending` (§6.3): it completes when `StoreInitialized` has been processed, never directly from `MarkReady`, because `MarkReady` drains only when no drain is active, and with another thread draining (a cross-tab event on the renderer, a restore queued behind a pool-thread drain) persistence's restore and terminal, `StoreInitialized` and the replayed buffer may all still be queued when `MarkReady` returns. So after `await store.InitializeAsync()` a non-Blazor host reads hydrated state, and `DuckyInitializer` renders `ChildContent` after hydration and `StoreInitialized` are reduced, the read-your-writes guarantee `DispatchAsync` gives (`InitializeAsync_WithConcurrentDrainer_CompletesAfterStoreInitializedReduced`, §17.3). Disposal completes it too (a detached `StoreInitialized` completes `Disposed`; before `Ready` dispose completes the TCS itself: `InitializeAsync_DisposedBeforeReady_Completes`), and it never faults. `InitializeAsync(token)` returns `sharedInitTask.WaitAsync(token)`: a cancelled token ends only that caller's wait.
- `DuckyInitializer` (Blazor, in `OnInitializedAsync`, never as a fire-and-forget continuation, §11.1, §11.6) and non-Blazor hosts `await store.InitializeAsync()`. A host that never calls it still works, because the first dispatch, select, `State` read or idle wait starts init.
- A middleware whose `InitializeAsync` awaits `Store.DispatchAsync(userAction)` waits until `InitTimeout`, because the action is buffered until `Ready`. It must use `DispatchSystem` (documented on the member; `Init_MiddlewareAwaitsDispatchAsync_TimesOutNotHangs`). Likewise, a middleware whose `InitializeAsync` awaits `Store.WhenIdleAsync()` waits until `InitTimeout`, because idle requires `Ready` (documented next to the `DispatchAsync` note; `Init_MiddlewareAwaitsWhenIdle_TimesOutNotHangs`). An effect run can't make that mistake silently: a non-`LongRunning` run counts itself as running, so `WhenIdleAsync` (and `TestStore.Settled`) from it throws `InvalidOperationException` synchronously (§7.4).

### 6.8 Subscribers and `Select`

- `SubscriberList` is an `ImmutableArray<Subscription>` swapped with `ImmutableInterlocked.Update`. Unsubscribing is idempotent. Notification iterates the array captured before the loop, so each `Subscription` has a volatile `_disposed` flag, set by `Selection.Dispose` before it removes the subscription and checked by the drainer right before the selector runs: a subscription disposed on another thread after the capture is skipped, so a caller that disposes its subscription and then the resources its `onChange` uses gets no later callback. A callback already running may finish after `Dispose` returns; `Dispose` never waits for it (it may be called from that callback). `Subscriber_DisposedBeforeNotify_NotInvoked` is deterministic through the store's internal IVT hook `BeforeNotifyHook` (an instance `Action?`, null in production, invoked between the capture and the iteration, like `AfterSubscribeHook`), from which the test disposes the subscription.
- `IStore.Select(selector, onChange, comparer)` runs in this order:
  1. `Start()` init (which may drain inline before the subscription exists, so no `onChange` can fire before `Select` returns on this thread);
  2. add a `Subscription<T>` with `last = Unset`;
  3. read `State` **after** the add and install `last = selector(state)` with a CAS from `Unset`;
  4. return a `Selection<T>`.
  - After each changed commit (step 10), the drainer computes `next = selector(snapshot)`. If it can CAS `last` from `Unset` to `next`, it stops there without calling `onChange`. Otherwise, if `!comparer.Equals(last, next)`, it sets `last = next` and calls `onChange(next)`. A commit racing the registration is therefore never lost: either the drainer saw the subscription and installed or compared, or step 3 read the committed snapshot (`Select_ConcurrentWithCommit_NoLostOnChange`, CsCheck `SampleParallel`). With `onChange == null`, nothing is evaluated on the drainer. The selector, the comparer and `onChange` run together inside the subscription's own try/catch: a throw from any of them (`s.Get<X>().Items[0]` on an empty list) is logged, leaves `last` unchanged and keeps the subscription (INV-09).
  - Both CAS outcomes are also reached deterministically, for coverage (§17.1), through the store's internal interleaving hook `AfterSubscribeHook` (an instance `Action?`, invoked between steps 2 and 3, null in production, set only by Ducky.Tests through `InternalsVisibleTo`). The test dispatches from inside the hook on the same thread, which becomes the drainer: the drainer installs `last` from `Unset`, and the registering thread's step-3 CAS then fails (`Select_CommitBetweenAddAndRead_InstalledByDrainer`).
  - `Selection.Value` evaluates `selector(store.State)` on each read (evaluate-on-read).
- `StoreExtensions.Select<TState,T>` skips the projector when the `TState` slot is `ReferenceEquals` to the previous one. Its cache is read by `Selection.Value` on the renderer and by the drainer concurrently, so it is one immutable `Entry(slot, result)` swapped with `Volatile.Write`, exactly as in §6.9: the slot and the result always come from the same entry.
- ponytail: every live subscription with an `onChange` re-runs its selector on every changed commit, which is O(subscribers). Index subscriptions by slice key if profiling shows a need.

### 6.9 Selector memoization (INV-21)

`Create()` returns a closure over a private immutable `Entry(object?[] inputs, T result)`, swapped with `Volatile.Write`. An input matches when it is `ReferenceEquals` to the cached one, or, for value types, when `EqualityComparer<TIn>.Default.Equals` holds. The arg overload also compares `argument()` with its comparer. Two threads may compute at once, and the last write wins. Both results are valid because projectors must be pure (DUCKY001–003 apply to projector lambdas). There is no static or shared cache.

### 6.10 DI registration and store identity (INV-22, ADR-0025)

- `AddDucky` registers:
  - `IStore` with `Lifetime` through a factory `sp => DuckyStore.Create(sp, config)`;
  - `IDispatcher` forwarding to `IStore`;
  - each slice type `T` with the same lifetime as `sp => ((DuckyStore)sp.GetRequiredService<IStore>()).GetSlice<T>()`.
- **Browser (`Lifetime = Singleton`).** The store is created from the root provider, so resolving `IStore` from the root (for example a Program.cs preload through `host.Services`), from the renderer scope, or from an `IHttpClientFactory` handler scope yields the same instance. The store creates and owns one `AsyncServiceScope` (the *store scope*) and resolves effects and middleware from it. Resolving scoped dependencies (the WASM template's scoped `HttpClient`) is therefore valid even under Development scope validation. In the browser that scope lives as long as the app. Documented consequence: a scoped service injected into an effect is a different instance from the renderer scope's one. Framework services that must be the renderer's (`PersistentComponentState`, `IJSRuntime`) are singletons in WASM; spike S-7 confirms this for .NET 10, and the fallback is that `DuckyComponent` hands its scoped instances to Ducky.Blazor's `InteractivityGate`. `AuthenticationStateProvider` is scoped in common WASM setups (RemoteAuthenticationService/MSAL), so Ducky.Blazor always takes it from the renderer scope through that hand-off (§11.6). A Program.cs preload may start init before the renderer exists; `PrerenderHandoff` then waits, briefly and with a bound, for the first component registration before taking the seed (§11.4), and persistence never restores before the seed has settled, so a preload can't invert precedence.
- **Server (`Lifetime = Scoped`).** One store per DI scope (the HTTP request for prerender and static SSR, the circuit for Interactive Server). Effects and middleware resolve from that scope. Code that resolves `IStore` from another scope (an `IHttpClientFactory` `DelegatingHandler`, a hosted service, a SignalR hub) gets a different, empty store; it must reach the circuit's services through the app's circuit-accessor pattern (documented, §23; `ServerLifetime_HandlerScope_GetsDistinctStore` pins the behaviour).
- A `Singleton` lifetime on a non-browser host logs Warning (EventId 1004) at construction. DUCKY004 flags it at compile time in web projects that are not Blazor WebAssembly.
- `Lifetime = Transient` is DUCKY301.
- The library holds **no static mutable state**: no static caches, no static `Meter` (it is created through `IMeterFactory` when one is registered), no static CTS. The permitted statics are listed exactly in §17.5 (`NoStaticMutableFields`): `static readonly ActivitySource` (stateless per the OpenTelemetry guidance), source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` delegates, Ducky.Blazor's internal source-generated wire JSON context (`BlazorWireContext`: fixed wire types, no user or circuit data), sentinels and immutable library values.

### 6.11 Dispose (INV-29)

`DisposeAsync` (idempotent, re-entrant calls included). Before any step, it reads the calling run (this store's `_effectRun.Value`, §6.5, §6.6; a run of another store is never a calling run of this one) and, if there is one, completes that run's `DisposeCalled`. Then it publishes the disposal TCS (`RunContinuationsAsynchronously`, never faulted) with `Interlocked.CompareExchange(ref _disposal, tcs, null)` and returns `_disposal.Task` to every caller. Only the caller that won the exchange runs the steps below; every other call gets that same task and starts nothing, including a re-entrant one made before the first call returned (step 2's `Cancel()` runs user token callbacks inline, plus any continuations they resume; a naive `_task ??= Core()` would start a second disposal and dispose middleware twice: `Dispose_ReentrantFromLifetimeCallback_RunsOnce`).

1. Under `_gate`: `_state = Disposed`, which also closes materialization (capture `_materializationStarted`, §6.6); detach every queued and init-buffered `Pending`; capture `_drainExited` if a drain is active. After releasing the lock, complete the detached pendings with `Disposed` (a detached `StoreInitialized` completes the shared init task with them), call `InitCoordinator.Retire()` (§6.7) and keep the task it returns, complete the shared init TCS if it is still pending (a `TrySetResult`: `Ready` was never reached), and complete every idle waiter. Idle waiters complete here, not at the end: a wait registered before disposal (a middleware `InitializeAsync` or a `LongRunning` effect awaiting `Store.WhenIdleAsync()` without a token) can only end when its waiter completes, and step 4 awaits exactly those tasks, so completing it later would hold disposal until the bound and leave middleware undisposed; a `WhenIdleAsync` called after disposal completes at once, so both paths now agree (`Dispose_InitAwaitingWhenIdle_MiddlewareStillDisposed`).
2. Cancel the store lifetime CTS (every effect run and `_initCts`) with `Cancel()` in a `try/catch (AggregateException)` that logs Error (EventId 1013), so a throwing user callback never faults `DisposeAsync` and steps 3–6 still run (`Dispose_ThrowingLifetimeCallback_CompletesAndDisposesMiddleware`). The callbacks run inline here, on the disposing thread, possibly while a drain is still in flight (INV-29 names this exception): effect-token registrations and Ducky.Reactive's subscription disposal (with its pending resubscribe timers, §14). Persistence writer loops use their own CTS (§11.5) and keep running.
3. If a drain was active, **always** await `_drainExited.Task`, bounded by `DisposeTimeout`, including when the caller is the drainer itself (a reducer, middleware hook, subscriber or effect prefix). This cannot deadlock: the TCS runs its continuations asynchronously and nothing blocks on it, so a re-entrant caller simply receives an incomplete task, the drainer finds `Disposed` at its next loop check and exits, and the continuation runs afterwards. There is no "am I the drainer?" test, so a racing effect continuation can't skip the wait (`Dispose_FromRacingEffectContinuation_WaitsForDrain`).
4. Waits, started together, each bounded by `DisposeTimeout`:
   - the **materialization wait**: `_materialized.Task` when materialization had started before step 1 (§6.6). When it had not, nothing was ever constructed, nothing ever will be, and steps 5a and 5b dispose nothing;
   - the **init waits**: the task `Retire()` returned (`_prefixDone`, when a `Start()` was inside its synchronous prefix), then one wait per middleware on **its own** init task in `InitTasks` (§6.7), never on the others', so no middleware is disposed while its own `InitializeAsync` is still running (`Dispose_MiddlewareInitInFlight_DisposeAsyncAfterInitTaskEnds`, `Dispose_DuringStartSyncPrefix_AwaitsStartedInitsAndStartsNoMore`), and a middleware whose init was never started has nothing to wait for;
   - the **run wait**: for every registered effect run in `_runs` (§6.6), `Task.WhenAny(run.Task, run.Token.DisposeCalled.Task)`. A run that has called `DisposeAsync`, this call or any other, before or after this wait started, is therefore never waited for: an effect that awaits `DisposeAsync` doesn't wait for itself, whether it made the first call or a later one (a second effect disposing the store, or an effect's cancellation handler resumed inline by step 2: `Dispose_SecondCallFromEffectRun_DoesNotWaitForItself`). Identity is the `EffectRunToken` reference, never `CancellationToken` equality: every Merge and Exhaust run shares the store-lifetime token, so a Merge run calling `DisposeAsync` still waits for the other Merge runs (`Dispose_AwaitsMergeAndLongRunningRuns`).
5. Two disposal phases, each disposal in its own `try/catch` that logs Error (EventId 1015) and continues, so one throwing `DisposeAsync` never skips the others and `DisposeAsync` never faults (`Dispose_MiddlewareDisposeThrows_OthersStillDisposed`; `Api_AllAsyncMembers_NeverFault` injects a throwing middleware `DisposeAsync`):
   - **5a**, after the drain exited and the materialization wait: middleware in reverse registration order. A middleware whose init wait ended (its init task completed, or was never started) is disposed now, and the store awaits `DisposeAsync().AsTask().WaitAsync(DisposeTimeout, TimeProvider)`: on expiry it logs Warning (EventId 1016, naming the middleware) and goes on to the next, leaving that `DisposeAsync` running. A middleware whose init wait expired gets its `DisposeAsync` chained on its own init task alone (it runs when that init ends, never early, and may land after 5b) and 5a goes on at once. So a user middleware whose `InitializeAsync` ignores its token (the supported case of `Init_HangingMiddleware_TimesOutAndReleasesBuffer`) or whose `DisposeAsync` hangs delays only its own disposal: the persistence middleware, registered earlier by `AddBlazor` and so disposed later, still flushes, because its own init returns on the init-token cancellation that step 2 triggers (`Dispose_HungMiddlewareInit_PersistenceStillFlushed`, `Dispose_HungMiddlewareDispose_OthersDisposedAndDisposeAsyncCompletes`). Persistence bounds its flush by the same `DisposeTimeout` (the protected `Middleware.DisposeTimeout`, §5.6, §11.5). 5a does not wait for effect runs: after step 1 every dispatch from a run completes `Disposed`, so a run can no longer reach a middleware hook or change state, and one run that ignores its token and never ends (a `LongRunning` listener on a non-cancellable read, a third-party call without cancellation) can't block the flush (`Dispose_HungEffectRun_PersistenceStillFlushed`);
   - **5b**, after 5a and the run wait: dispose store-owned effects and reactive effects, then the store scope (browser).
6. Clear subscribers.

If step 3 times out (Warning EventId 1011), `DisposeAsync` **completes at that bound**, and steps 4–6 are chained as a continuation of `_drainExited.Task`: they still run, in order and each with its own bound, when the drain finally exits, but nothing awaits them, and they are never run inline while a drain may still be in flight. Likewise, if a step-4 wait expires, the work that depends on it is chained on exactly the task it waited for (5a and 5b on the materialization task, one middleware's `DisposeAsync` on that middleware's own init task, 5b on the runs), never run early. Otherwise `DisposeAsync` completes once every phase has finished or reached its bound (`Dispose_DrainTimeout_MiddlewareDisposedAfterDrainExit`), so its completion is always bounded: at most one `DisposeTimeout` for the drain, or the drain plus the phase bounds. A hung effect run therefore delays only 5b, and a hung middleware init or `DisposeAsync` only that middleware's disposal. On the server, where `StoreDisposeHook.DisposeAsync` returns when `DisposeAsync` completes and the container then disposes the scoped dependencies, only effect disposal and a hung middleware's own disposal can land after that point, plus, when the drain itself overran `DisposeTimeout` (one in-flight `Process` step outlasting it: a blocking effect prefix, a slow subscriber, DevTools `AfterReduce` with `Trace` on a large state), the persistence flush and middleware disposal: a drain that overruns `DisposeTimeout` moves them past the hook's return. The chained flush then catches `ObjectDisposedException`, and any other provider exception, per key and counts that key `ducky.persistence.lost` (§11.5); a chained middleware disposal that throws is the logged Error 1015 (`Dispose_DrainOverrunsTimeout_ServerScope_DisposeAsyncCompletesAtBound_LateFlushCountsLost`).

`Dispose()` (sync; containers that dispose synchronously call it) is `_ = DisposeAsync();` (the task never faults, so there is nothing to observe) plus Warning EventId 1010: "prefer DisposeAsync; pending persistence writes may be lost". All ordering rules therefore come from `DisposeAsync` (`SyncDispose_DuringDrain_NoAfterReduceOnDisposedMiddleware`, `Dispose_FromReducer_MiddlewareDisposedAfterDrainExit`).

**Server disposal order.** With `Lifetime = Scoped`, the DI scope disposes its services in reverse creation order, and the store is created before the scoped dependencies its effects and middleware pull in at materialization, so the container would dispose those dependencies first and the store's drain, effect wait and persistence flush would run against disposed services. `AddDucky` therefore registers an internal scoped `StoreDisposeHook : IAsyncDisposable, IDisposable`, resolved from the store's scope as the last step of materialization (skipped when disposal has already begun, §6.6), after every effect and middleware dependency exists: created last, it is disposed first, and its `DisposeAsync` awaits `store.DisposeAsync()` (its `Dispose` calls `store.Dispose()`), so the store drains, awaits effects and flushes while its dependencies are alive; the container's later disposal of the store is the idempotent no-op. Dependencies an effect resolves lazily from an injected `IServiceProvider` after materialization are not covered (documented). In the browser the store owns its store scope and disposes it last (step 5), so no hook is needed (`ServerScopeDispose_EffectScopedDependencyDisposedAfterStoreDispose`). After disposal, whether or not the store was ever materialized, and without materializing it (§6.6): `Dispatch` is ignored (Debug log), `DispatchAsync` returns `Disposed`, `Restore` is ignored, `State` returns the last snapshot (the initial one if nothing was ever reduced), `Select` returns an inert `Selection` whose `Value` reads the last snapshot, `WhenIdleAsync` and `InitializeAsync` complete at once, no effect starts (§6.4 step 11), and none of them throws DUCKY353.

---

## 7. Threading contract

This text appears verbatim in the XML docs of `IDispatcher` and in `docs/threading.md`.

1. `Dispatch`, `DispatchAsync`, `Restore`, `Select`, `State`, `InitializeAsync` and `WhenIdleAsync` may be called from any thread at any time, including from inside a reducer, a middleware hook, a subscriber or an effect, with two exceptions for `WhenIdleAsync`: from a non-`LongRunning` effect run of the same store it throws `InvalidOperationException` (the run counts itself as running, so the wait could never end; another store's run is an unrelated caller, §6.5), and awaited from a middleware's `InitializeAsync` it waits until `InitTimeout` (idle requires initialization). None of them ever blocks waiting for another thread, except that concurrent first users wait for the one-time construction of effects and middleware (whose constructors must not block).
2. Actions are reduced one at a time, never concurrently, in the order in which they were enqueued, with one exception: user actions dispatched before initialization are replayed after `StoreInitialized`, behind library actions enqueued later (rule 5). Actions from one thread keep their relative order.
3. If no drain is active, the calling thread becomes the drainer and reduces the whole queue inline on its own thread and synchronization context, including actions enqueued by other threads, before its call returns. Otherwise the call enqueues and returns immediately. **A synchronous `Dispatch` gives read-your-writes only when the caller became the drainer.** Use `await DispatchAsync(a)` for read-after-write. It completes after `a` is reduced, not after its effects. Use `WhenIdleAsync()` (or `TestStore.Settled()`) to also wait for effects.
4. `DispatchAsync` never faults or cancels, and never completes inline on the drainer. At run time no member throws for a reducer, middleware or effect failure. The only exceptions are programmer errors, thrown synchronously, before any task exists: `ArgumentNullException` (a null argument); the store's cached `DuckyConfigurationException` (effects or middleware could not be constructed, DUCKY353); `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` from `Restore` with a non-restore origin; `InvalidOperationException` from a constructor that uses the store during materialization; and misuse, `DuckyConfigurationException` DUCKY351 (a `SliceStore` used before the store attached it), `InvalidOperationException` DUCKY352 (`Select` after a component's first render) and `InvalidOperationException` from `WhenIdleAsync` (or `TestStore.Settled`) called inside a non-`LongRunning` effect run (`WhenIdle_FromNonLongRunningEffectRun_ThrowsNotHangs`). **Never block on it** (`.Result`, `.Wait()`, `GetAwaiter().GetResult()`): if the caller is the drainer, that is a self-deadlock. DUCKY010 flags it.
5. Until the store is initialized, user actions are buffered and replayed in order after `StoreInitialized`. Initialization starts automatically on the first dispatch, select, `State` read or idle wait.
6. Reducers, middleware hooks, subscriber callbacks, `Select` `onChange` callbacks and the synchronous part of effects run on the draining thread. On Blazor Server that may be a thread-pool thread. Ducky.Blazor's components marshal to the renderer themselves. Custom callbacks must marshal UI work through `InvokeAsync`.
7. Reducers, `SliceStore` mutators and selector projectors must be pure. Projectors may run concurrently on two threads.
8. A dispatch or restore made while its parent action is being processed (synchronously) is a child with depth + 1, whatever its origin. Chains deeper than `MaxDispatchDepth` (64) are dropped with `ReducerFailed(DispatchLoopException)`. A dispatch made after an effect has really yielded, or while the store is idle, starts a new chain.
9. `State` reads take no lock and always return a complete, immutable snapshot.
10. After disposal begins, dispatches are ignored and complete with `Disposed`, and effect tokens are cancelled.

Known liveness ceilings, stated in `docs/threading.md`:
- ponytail: the drainer drains other producers' actions with no bound, so on Blazor Server a renderer-thread caller that became the drainer can be held for as long as a hot background producer (a fast poller, a reactive stream) keeps the queue non-empty. "Never blocks" holds; latency does not. Upgrade path: a per-drain action budget that hands the rest to a thread-pool drainer.
- ponytail: the depth guard bounds linear synchronous loops only. A synchronous fan-out (each action dispatches two children) grows the FIFO queue as 2^depth and exhausts memory long before depth 64. Upgrade path: a per-correlation cap on synchronous-chain actions that drops with `DispatchLoopException`.

---

## 8. Error model

### 8.1 Behaviour table

| Situation | Behaviour | Surface |
|---|---|---|
| Configuration error | `DuckyConfigurationException` listing **every** problem, thrown at the first store resolution (`AddDucky` throws only DUCKY300) | exception, once |
| Effect/middleware constructor throws at materialization | the instances already built are disposed (Error 1015 per failing disposal); `DuckyConfigurationException` (DUCKY353) from the first store use; every later call before disposal rethrows the same instance; after disposal, never (§6.6) | exception, cached |
| Misuse | `InvalidOperationException` DUCKY352 (`Select` after a component's first render); `DuckyConfigurationException` DUCKY351 (`SliceStore.State`/`Set`/`SetAsync` before attach); `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (`Restore` with a non-restore origin); `InvalidOperationException` (a constructor uses the store during materialization, or `WhenIdleAsync` from a non-`LongRunning` effect run) | exception, synchronous (developer bug, §7.4) |
| Null action or selector | `ArgumentNullException`, thrown synchronously (by async members too, before a task exists, §7.4) | exception |
| Reducer / `MayDispatch` / `BeforeReduce` throws | no commit, `ReducerFailed`, result `Failed`; after a `BeforeReduce` or reducer throw, `AfterReduce` (no changed key) and the action's effects still run, after a `MayDispatch` throw nothing more (§6.4) | action + Error log |
| `AfterReduce` throws | logged; the other middleware still run | log |
| Subscriber or `onChange` throws | logged; stays subscribed | log |
| Effect throws (not our cancellation) | `EffectFailed` | action + log + `ducky.effect.failures` |
| Failure while handling a failure action (reducer, synchronous descendant, effect run it triggered, depth drop under it) | log only | log |
| Unhandled action | Debug log, or `ReducerFailed(UnhandledActionException)` if strict | log / action |
| Depth exceeded | `Dropped`, `ReducerFailed(DispatchLoopException)` at depth 0 | action + counter |
| Init timeout / buffer overflow | Error log, store becomes `Ready`, buffer replayed, nothing dropped | log |
| Hydration failure / timeout / init abort | `HydrationFailed`, Status `Failed`, persistence resumes | action |
| Persistence save failure (storage-provider error, such as a Redis timeout) | the key stays dirty and is retried with capped backoff until success or dispose; `PersistenceFailed(key, type, msg)` once at the start of each failure streak per key, and a success resets the streak (§11.5); the dispose flush cancels a pending backoff and tries once more, and a key still dirty when it ends is counted `ducky.persistence.lost` | action + counter |
| Persistence serialization failure | `PersistenceFailed` through the same streak; the next change is written (the same state would fail again, so it is not retried on a timer) | action |
| Unserializable value | DevTools and logs degrade to name only. Persist/Prerender without type info is a configuration error | Debug log once per type per store |
| JS disconnected / interop timeout during a write | caught (`JSDisconnectedException`, `TaskCanceledException`); the value stays pending and is retried with capped backoff until success or dispose; counted `ducky.persistence.lost` only if still unwritten at dispose | metric + Debug log |

The never-fault set of INV-10 (`IDispatcher.DispatchAsync`, `EffectContext.DispatchAsync`, `SliceStore.SetAsync`, `DuckyComponent.DispatchAsync`, `IStore.DisposeAsync`, and `InitializeAsync`/`WhenIdleAsync` with a non-cancelled token) never returns a faulted or canceled task; the three carve-outs are named in INV-10 (`TestStore.DisposeAsync` sits in the third, next to `TestStore.Settled` and `EffectTest.Run`, because it must report a hung disposal as a test failure, §15). Test pattern: `Api_{Member}_NeverFaults`, and the property `Api_AllAsyncMembers_NeverFault`, which enumerates exactly that set with random failure injection (throwing reducers, hooks, effects, middleware `DisposeAsync`, and middleware `InitializeAsync` throwing synchronously or asynchronously) over non-null arguments, a constructible configuration and attached SliceStores, since the §7.4 programmer errors (a `SetAsync` before attach included) are thrown synchronously and are not faults. `Api_CallerTokenCancelled_OnlyThatWaitCanceled` pins the first carve-out: only the cancelled caller's wait completes Canceled, and init and other waiters are unaffected.

### 8.2 Configuration and runtime codes

Analyzer IDs are in §16. Runtime codes appear in `DuckyError.Code`, in exception messages and in log EventIds, and each has `docs/diagnostics/{code}.md`.

| Code | When | Package |
|---|---|---|
| DUCKY300 | `AddDucky` called twice | Ducky |
| DUCKY301 | `Lifetime = Transient` | Ducky |
| DUCKY302 | generic slice without an explicit `Key` | Ducky |
| DUCKY303 | invalid or reserved `Key` | Ducky |
| DUCKY304 | duplicate slice key (names both types) | Ducky |
| DUCKY305 | two slices share a `TState` | Ducky |
| DUCKY306 | `RequireJsonTypeInfo` type not resolvable by the `UseJson` resolver (message contains the `[JsonSerializable(typeof(X))]` line), or `UseJson(options)` whose `TypeInfoResolver` is null | Ducky |
| DUCKY307 | `On<T>`/`EffectGroup.On<T>` with a non-concrete `T` (runtime twin of DUCKY005); recorded from the throwing constructor | Ducky |
| DUCKY308 | duplicate `On<T>`/`EffectGroup.On<T>` for one `T` (runtime twin of DUCKY014); recorded from the throwing constructor | Ducky |
| DUCKY309 | an effect, middleware or reactive-effect constructor has a parameter no candidate constructor can resolve (`IServiceProviderIsService`, or `IServiceProviderIsKeyedService` for a `[FromKeyedServices(key)]` parameter; a `[ServiceKey]` parameter never resolves), first resolution (§5.1); names the type, the missing service and its key | Ducky |
| DUCKY310 | `Persist<T>`/`Prerender<T>` for a slice not added | Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY311 | migration chain gap (`Version = 3` without `Migrate(2, …)`) | Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY312 | `SyncAcrossTabs` without `PersistStorage.Local` | Ducky.Blazor |
| ~~DUCKY313~~ | retired: `PersistStorage.Server` in the browser is inert, not an error (§11.6), so shared Interactive Auto registrations start on both sides | – |
| DUCKY314 | `PersistStorage.Server` with no `IDistributedCache` registered (first resolution; not evaluated when `BlazorOptions.IsBrowser`, where Server storage is inert, §11.6) | Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY315 | `PersistStorage.Server` with the default scope but no `AuthenticationStateProvider` registered (first resolution; not evaluated when `BlazorOptions.IsBrowser`) | Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY316 | `BlazorOptions.HydrationTimeout` ≥ `DuckyBuilder.InitTimeout` (the persistence timeout must fire first), or `PrerenderSeedWaitTimeout` ≥ `HydrationTimeout` | Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY317 | `PersistStorage.Server` is used but `BlazorOptions.KeyPrefix` is still the default: "PersistStorage.Server requires BlazorOptions.KeyPrefix to be set to an app-unique value" (a shared `IDistributedCache` would otherwise mix apps and environments) | Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY350 | `State.Get<T>()` for an unregistered state type (names the likely `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}`) | Ducky |
| DUCKY351 | `SliceStore.State`/`Set`/`SetAsync` before the store attached it ("inject it, don't `new` it"); thrown synchronously as a `DuckyConfigurationException` | Ducky |
| DUCKY352 | `Select` after the first render in a Ducky component | Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY353 | an effect, middleware or reactive-effect constructor threw when the store materialized them (first store use, §6.6); wraps the original exception and names DUCKY307/DUCKY308 when it is one of those; the instances built before the throw are disposed first; cached and rethrown by every later call until disposal begins, never after | Ducky |

### 8.3 Message template (INV-31)

Every message has four parts, and the unit test `EveryErrorCode_FollowsMessageTemplate` enforces this for every code:

`DUCKY306: Slice 'cart' (MyApp.Cart.CartState) is persisted, but the resolver passed to UseJson has no JsonTypeInfo for MyApp.Cart.CartState. Add [JsonSerializable(typeof(MyApp.Cart.CartState))] to your JsonSerializerContext. https://github.com/phmatray/Ducky/blob/main/docs/diagnostics/DUCKY306.md`

1. What happened.
2. The concrete types and keys.
3. The exact fix.
4. The link.

---

## 9. Diagnostics (DT-02, DT-04)

- **Action type names**, computed once per type per store: `[ActionType("todos/added")]` when present (read with `GetCustomAttribute<ActionTypeAttribute>(inherit: false)`, §5.8); `SetState<T>` → `{sliceKey}/{name}`; `HydrateSlices` → `@ducky/restore`; otherwise `Namespace.Name`, with generic arguments as `Name<Arg>` and nested types as `Outer.Inner`.
- **Logs**: `[LoggerMessage]`-generated, with EventIds 1000–1999 (core), 2000–2999 (Blazor), 3000–3999 (Reactive). They contain types and keys, never values.
- **Tracing**: `ActivitySource("Ducky")` emits a `ducky.dispatch` activity per action, with tags `ducky.action.type`, `ducky.origin`, `ducky.depth` and `ducky.correlation_id`. Its parent is the producer's `Activity.Current` captured when the action was dispatched, never the drainer's, and `Activity.Current` is restored when processing ends (§6.4 step 2).
- **Metrics** (through `IMeterFactory` when registered; nothing otherwise): `ducky.dispatch.duration` (histogram, ms), `ducky.dispatch.dropped` (tag `ducky.drop.reason`: `depth` or `run`, INV-02), `ducky.effect.failures`, `ducky.effect.dropped` (Exhaust), `ducky.persistence.saves`, `ducky.persistence.save.duration`, `ducky.persistence.lost`. Every telemetry call goes through `SafeTelemetry` (§6.3).

---

## 10. Serialization and AOT (CORE-07, ENG-08, INV-23, INV-24)

- `UseJson` fixes one read-only `JsonSerializerOptions` per store. `UseJson(JsonSerializerOptions options)` only stores the reference inside `configure`: it never calls `MakeReadOnly()` there, so a null resolver can't throw out of `AddDucky` (which throws only DUCKY300). At first resolution a null `TypeInfoResolver` is reported as DUCKY306 with the other errors (INV-31); only after validation passes does the store build `new JsonSerializerOptions(options)` and freeze that copy, so the caller's instance, which the app may share and mutate later (for `HttpClient` JSON, say), is never frozen (a case of `Build_WithFiveMisconfigurations_ReportsAllFive`). `UseJson(resolver)` given a `JsonSerializerContext` copies the context's own options, so its `[JsonSourceGenerationOptions]` (naming policy, converters such as the AOT-required `JsonStringEnumConverter<T>`, number handling, ignore conditions) apply to persisted, seed and DevTools JSON: `new JsonSerializerOptions(ctx.Options) { TypeInfoResolver = resolver }`. Any other resolver gets `new JsonSerializerOptions { TypeInfoResolver = resolver }` (`Persist_ContextWithCamelCaseAndEnumStringConverter_RoundTripsUsingContextOptions`). That includes `JsonTypeInfoResolver.Combine(A.Default, B.Default)`, which carries no options of its own, so the contexts' `[JsonSourceGenerationOptions]` would silently not apply: an app growing from one context to two would switch its persisted JSON from camelCase to PascalCase, and existing envelopes would deserialize "successfully" with every renamed property at its default. DUCKY025 (Warning) reports `UseJson(JsonTypeInfoResolver.Combine(...))` when any combined context's `[JsonSourceGenerationOptions]` sets a non-default option, by a named argument or by its `JsonSerializerDefaults` constructor argument (`JsonSerializerDefaults.Web` sets camelCase naming, case-insensitivity and number handling at once), and its message gives the fix, `UseJson(new JsonSerializerOptions(A.Default.Options) { TypeInfoResolver = JsonTypeInfoResolver.Combine(A.Default, B.Default) })`. `DuckyJson` (public, §5.2, reachable as `IStore.Json`) resolves `JsonTypeInfo` and caches hits and misses per store. It never uses a static cache. It is the single enforcement point of INV-23 for every package: Ducky.Blazor serializes state, seeds and DevTools payloads only through it, because R-PKG-2 forbids `InternalsVisibleTo` between shipped assemblies.
