  - **State** is always serialized and deserialized with the `JsonTypeInfo` of the declared `Slice.StateType`, so `[JsonPolymorphic]`/`[JsonDerivedType]` on an abstract state base apply and `RequireJsonTypeInfo(typeof(TState))` covers every write (`Persist_PolymorphicState_RoundTripsViaDeclaredType`). Only action payloads (DevTools, logs) use the runtime type.
  - `TrySerialize(value, declaredType, out json)` and `ToNode(value, declaredType)` (for sanitizers, through `JsonSerializer.SerializeToNode(value, typeInfo)`) return false/null when type info is missing **or** when serialization throws any non-fatal exception (anything except `OutOfMemoryException`: `NotSupportedException` for an action carrying an `Exception`, a `Type` or a delegate, `JsonException` for a cyclic graph, `ArgumentException` for NaN or ±Infinity without `AllowNamedFloatingPointLiterals`, and whatever a user getter or converter throws). Only a **missing type info** is cached as unserializable for this store (it can't change); an exception fails only that call, because it often depends on the value (one NaN, one cyclic instance), and the next value of the same type is tried again. Each failing type is logged at Debug once per store. Callers fall back per path: DevTools and logs to name-only; a persistence write reports `PersistenceFailed` through the normal failure streak (§11.5), so the next valid value is written (`Persist_TransientNaN_NextValidValueIsWritten`); a prerender seed leaves that slice out with the budget Warning (EventId 2021).
  - `TryDeserialize(JsonElement, Type, out value)` catches every non-fatal exception (anything except `OutOfMemoryException`), including exceptions that user constructors and `init` accessors throw during deserialization and that STJ passes through unwrapped (Ducky's own `EntityState` constructor throws `ArgumentException` on duplicate ids). It returns false and logs a Warning naming the key (its optional `key` parameter, §5.2) and the type; it never throws and never ignores a failure silently. Every library caller passes the slice or storage key (`TryRestore`, `EnvelopeReader`, `CrossTabSync`, `IMPORT_STATE`), and `Hydration_OneSliceCtorThrows_OtherSlicesStillRestored` asserts that the Warning names it. Callers keep the slice's current state: `TryRestore` in a batched restore (§6.4 step 6), `CrossTabSync` (`CrossTab_CtorThrowingPayload_Ignored`), hydration and DevTools `IMPORT_STATE`.
- Ducky core sends nothing over a wire (logs carry types and keys, never values), so it has no JSON context of its own. Ducky.Blazor keeps one internal `BlazorWireContext : JsonSerializerContext` for the persistence and prerender envelopes, the DevTools FSA, config and inbound messages, and the DevTools projections of library actions (§11.8): the user's resolver doesn't know Ducky's and Ducky.Blazor's own actions, and `ReducerFailed`/`EffectFailed` carry an `Exception`, so DevTools projects them to fixed wire records such as `{type, message, sliceKey?}` before it tries the user resolver. Exceptions are never serialized.
- `Persist<T>()` and `Prerender<T>()` call `RequireJsonTypeInfo(typeof(TState), …)`, so a missing type fails at startup (DUCKY306).
- **JS interop takes only primitives.** `JsBridge` is the single wrapper over `IJSObjectReference`. Its methods accept only `string`, `int`, `long`, `bool` and `DotNetObjectReference`. It is the only type allowed to call into JS, and the banned-API list below enforces that elsewhere. BannedApiAnalyzers reports the symbol a call binds to, and `InvokeVoidAsync` and the everyday `params object?[]` overloads of `InvokeAsync<T>` are extension methods, so the list bans the extension types themselves, not just the interface members. RS0030 has no per-type allow-list: each `JsBridge` call site carries `#pragma warning disable RS0030 // justification: JsBridge is the single interop wrapper (§10, INV-23)`, the form of the `IsBrowser` pragma of §5.1.
- **Banned APIs** in `src/` (`BannedSymbols.txt`, Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.BannedApiAnalyzers, errors):
  - `JsonSerializer` overloads without `JsonTypeInfo`;
  - `Type.GetType`, `Assembly.Load*`, `Activator.CreateInstance(Type…)`, `Type.MakeGenericType`;
  - JS interop outside `JsBridge`: `T:Microsoft.JSInterop.JSRuntimeExtensions`, `T:Microsoft.JSInterop.JSObjectReferenceExtensions`, `T:Microsoft.JSInterop.JSInProcessRuntimeExtensions`, `T:Microsoft.JSInterop.JSInProcessObjectReferenceExtensions`, and every overload of `IJSRuntime.InvokeAsync`, `IJSObjectReference.InvokeAsync`, `IJSInProcessRuntime.Invoke` and `IJSInProcessObjectReference.Invoke`, each by documentation ID (the synchronous WASM `Invoke` included);
  - by exact documentation ID, so the `TimeProvider` overloads stay usable: `M:System.Threading.Tasks.Task.Delay(System.Int32)`, `M:System.Threading.Tasks.Task.Delay(System.TimeSpan)`, `M:System.Threading.Tasks.Task.Delay(System.Int32,System.Threading.CancellationToken)`, `M:System.Threading.Tasks.Task.Delay(System.TimeSpan,System.Threading.CancellationToken)`, `Thread.Sleep` (both overloads), and the `DateTime.Now`/`DateTime.UtcNow`/`DateTimeOffset.Now`/`DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` properties (use `TimeProvider`). `Task.Delay(TimeSpan, TimeProvider[, CancellationToken])` is allowed and is how the persistence debounce and retry backoff wait;
  - `StackFrame.GetMethod` (use `DiagnosticMethodInfo.Create(StackFrame)`);
  - `ExcludeFromCodeCoverageAttribute`.
- **Activation**: `ActivatorUtilities.CreateInstance<T>` only, and only with `[DAM]`-annotated generic parameters (`AddEffect`, `Use`, `AddReactiveEffect`). This avoids IL2091.
- **DevTools trace**: frames are captured with `new StackTrace(skipFrames, false)`, and each frame is described with `System.Diagnostics.DiagnosticMethodInfo.Create(frame)` (.NET 9+, trim-safe; it returns null when metadata was trimmed, and the frame then renders as `<unknown>`). No `[RequiresUnreferencedCode]`, no suppression. The AOT smoke test runs with `Trace = true`. The call goes through an internal instance seam, `internal Func<StackFrame, DiagnosticMethodInfo?> DescribeFrame { get; set; } = DiagnosticMethodInfo.Create;` on `DevToolsOptions`, which `DevToolsMiddleware` reads from its options, because metadata is trimmed only in the AOT publish, which coverage doesn't measure. The seam sits on the options object, not on the middleware, because the store creates and owns middleware instances and no test can reach one: `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` sets it inside `UseDevTools(o => o.DescribeFrame = _ => null)` through Ducky.Blazor's own `InternalsVisibleTo` and covers the `<unknown>` branch under JIT (`DevTools_Trace_TrimmedFrame_RendersUnknown`).
- **One audited suppression** is allowed: `PersistentComponentState.PersistAsJson<byte[]>` / `TryTakeFromJson<byte[]>` for the prerender seed (the UTF-8 envelope, §11.4), if .NET 10 offers no `JsonTypeInfo`-based overload. It carries `UnconditionalSuppressMessage` with the justification "byte[] is intrinsic to STJ". Spike S-3 picks the path, and the trimmed smoke test is the arbiter.
- **Smoke gates**:
  - `test/Ducky.AotSmoke`, a `PublishAot=true` console app, lives in its own `Ducky.Aot.slnx`, outside the locked `Ducky.slnx`: with `PublishAot` the lock file would record the host-RID ILCompiler package, so one committed lock file would fail locked restore (NU1004) on Windows or macOS. It is restored unlocked, only by the `AotSmoke` target on Linux. It exercises the store, effects (all four policies), selectors, `SliceStore`, `EntityState`, Draft, Reactive, and, through public API only, a persistence round-trip with `PersistStorage.Server` over `MemoryDistributedCache` (`KeyPrefix = "smoke"`, `Scope = (_, _) => new("smoke")`) and the DevTools FSA (non-empty payload) through `UseDevTools()` with a fake `IJSRuntime` whose import succeeds, so the gate is `Interactive`. It prints one `PASS <name>`/`FAIL <name>` line per named assertion (`AotSmoke`, `DevToolsPayload_NonEmpty`, `ActionType_AttributeName_SurvivesAot` (an `[ActionType("todos/added")]` action's DevTools type and `ActionContext.ActionType` are `todos/added` after ILC), …) and exits non-zero on any failure; the `AotSmoke` target checks the printed names against the manifest (§19), because `Ducky.AotSmoke` is built only in the `aot` job and `SpecTraceGate` never runs it.
  - `samples/Ducky.Samples.Wasm` stays workload-free in its csproj (no `RunAOTCompilation`, which would need the wasm-tools workload for every build, NETSDK1147). Only the `AotSmoke` target publishes it with `-p:RunAOTCompilation=true -p:TrimMode=full -p:RestoreLockedMode=false`, after installing wasm-tools. The output is served from its `wwwroot` by the E2E static-file host (§19) and driven by the Playwright test `WasmTrimmedSmoke` in `Ducky.E2E` with an injected fake `__REDUX_DEVTOOLS_EXTENSION__`. It asserts a non-empty payload for `todos/added`, a persisted slice that survives a reload, and a Trace stack that is present (possibly `<unknown>` frames). The fixture reads the publish directory from `DUCKY_TRIMMED_PUBLISH_DIR` and skips only when `DUCKY_REQUIRE_TRIMMED_PUBLISH` is unset (a plain local `E2E` run); the `AotSmoke` target sets both, so a publish that landed elsewhere (a TFM or RID folder, a `wwwroot` path change) fails the test instead of skipping it, and the target also fails unless the TRX reports exactly one executed and passed test with zero skipped (§19).


---

## 11. Ducky.Blazor

Namespace `Ducky.Blazor`. It is built only on Ducky's public API (R-PKG-2). Prerender handoff, persistence (which hosts cross-tab sync, §11.7) and DevTools are internal sealed subclasses of the public `Middleware`, registered through `Use<T>()` by the extension methods below (ADR-0032). Cross-tab sync is not a middleware of its own: it must issue its restores under persistence's private `_issue` lock (§11.5).

### 11.1 Public API

```csharp
public static class DuckyBlazorBuilderExtensions
{
    public static DuckyBuilder AddBlazor(this DuckyBuilder builder, Action<BlazorOptions>? configure = null);   // the others call it (no configure).
                                                                       // Registration is idempotent: the first call does Use<PrerenderHandoff>()
                                                                       // then Use<PersistenceMiddleware>() (fixed positions; each is inert without
                                                                       // Prerender/Persist slices). Configuration composes: every configure
                                                                       // delegate of every call is applied, in call order, to one BlazorOptions
                                                                       // (last value written per property wins), like Persist<T>
    public static DuckyBuilder Persist<TSlice>(this DuckyBuilder builder, Action<PersistOptions>? configure = null)
        where TSlice : Slice, new();                                   // composes: every configure for a slice is applied, in call order,
                                                                       // to one PersistOptions (last value written per property wins)
    [EditorBrowsable(EditorBrowsableState.Never)]                      // GEN-02 only, emitted for every [Persist]. Registers TSlice as persisted
    public static DuckyBuilder PersistAttributeDefaults<TSlice>(this DuckyBuilder builder, Action<PersistOptions> configure)
        where TSlice : Slice, new();                                   // exactly like Persist<TSlice>() (AddBlazor(), RequireJsonTypeInfo,
                                                                       // the same validations), and records the attribute's explicitly
                                                                       // supplied values as the base layer, applied before every
                                                                       // Persist<TSlice> delegate whatever the call order, so builder calls
                                                                       // always win over the attribute (ADR-0012)
    public static DuckyBuilder Prerender<TSlice>(this DuckyBuilder builder) where TSlice : Slice, new();
    public static DuckyBuilder Prerender<TSlice, TState>(this DuckyBuilder builder, Func<TState, bool> include)
        where TSlice : Slice<TState>, new() where TState : class;       // seeds the slice only while include(state) is true (§11.4)
    public static DuckyBuilder UseDevTools(this DuckyBuilder builder, Action<DevToolsOptions>? configure = null); // enabled when called (ADR-0043);
                                                                       // configure delegates compose in call order, as for AddBlazor
}

public sealed class BlazorOptions
{
    public string KeyPrefix { get; set; } = "ducky";                                  // storage key: {prefix}:{scope?}:{sliceKey}; must be app-unique
                                                                                      // (not the default) when PersistStorage.Server is used (DUCKY317)
    public Func<IServiceProvider, CancellationToken, ValueTask<string?>>? Scope { get; set; } // user/tenant segment (§11.6). Server keys: Scope ??
                                                                                      // DuckyScopes.NameIdentifier. Local/Session keys: scoped only
                                                                                      // when Scope is set explicitly; the default never applies
    public TimeSpan HydrationTimeout { get; set; } = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5);         // < DuckyBuilder.InitTimeout (validated, DUCKY316)
    public TimeSpan PrerenderSeedWaitTimeout { get; set; } = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(250); // browser only: bound on waiting for the seed (§11.4);
                                                                                      // < HydrationTimeout (DUCKY316)
    public TimeSpan PrerenderIdleTimeout { get; set; } = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5);
    public int InlinePayloadBytes { get; set; } = 16 * 1024;                          // JS→.NET above this: IJSStreamReference
    public int MaxPayloadBytes { get; set; } = 5 * 1024 * 1024;
    public int PrerenderSeedMaxWireBytes { get; set; } = 20 * 1024;                   // estimated bytes the seed adds to the start-circuit message
                                                                                      // (§11.4); the page shares the 32 KB limit with every other
                                                                                      // PersistentComponentState user
    public DistributedCacheEntryOptions? ServerEntryOptions { get; set; }             // PersistOptions.MaxAge overrides expiry
}

public static class DuckyScopes
{
    /// The authenticated user's ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier; null (no I/O) when anonymous. Default scope for PersistStorage.Server.
    public static Func<IServiceProvider, CancellationToken, ValueTask<string?>> NameIdentifier { get; }
}

public sealed class PersistOptions
{
    public PersistStorage Storage { get; set; } = PersistStorage.Local;
    public int Version { get; set; } = 1;
    public PersistOptions Migrate(int fromVersion, Func<JsonNode, JsonNode> step);    // one step fromVersion → fromVersion+1
    public TimeSpan? MaxAge { get; set; }
    public TimeSpan? Debounce { get; set; }                                           // null: 0 in the browser and for Server storage; 250 ms for Server + Local/Session
    public bool SyncAcrossTabs { get; set; }                                          // Local only (DUCKY312)
}

public sealed class DevToolsOptions
{
    public string Name { get; set; } = "Ducky";
    public int MaxAge { get; set; } = 50;                                             // history cap and the extension's maxAge
    public Func<ActionContext, bool>? Filter { get; set; }                            // replaces 1.x ShouldLogAction + ExcludedActionTypes
    public Func<string, JsonNode?, JsonNode?>? ActionSanitizer { get; set; }          // (actionType, payload) → payload
    public Func<string, JsonNode?, JsonNode?>? StateSanitizer { get; set; }           // (sliceKey, state) → state
    public bool Trace { get; set; }                                                   // off
    public int TraceLimit { get; set; } = 10;
}

// Library actions and the status slice
public sealed record HydrationCompleted(bool StateRestored) { internal int ScopeEpoch { get; init; } }   // epoch of the attempt (§11.5)
public sealed record HydrationFailed(string ErrorType, string Message) { internal int ScopeEpoch { get; init; } }
public sealed record PersistenceFailed(string SliceKey, string ErrorType, string Message);
public sealed record ClearPersistedState;                                             // removes the exact {prefix}:{scope?}:{sliceKey} key of each persisted slice;
                                                                                      // storage only: this store's in-memory state is unchanged (§11.5)
public sealed record PersistenceState(PersistenceStatus Status)                       // slice key "@ducky/persistence"
{ internal int ScopeEpoch { get; init; } }                                            // allocated from a counter by each scope switch (§11.6);
                                                                                      // the slice ignores a terminal whose epoch differs
public enum PersistenceStatus { NotStarted, Hydrating, Hydrated, Failed }

// Components (C# classes, hand-written BuildRenderTree; no .razor in the library)
public abstract class DuckyComponent : ComponentBase, IHandleAfterRender, IDisposable
{
    [Inject] protected IStore Store { get; private set; }
    protected Selection<T> Select<T>(Func<StateSnapshot, T> selector, IEqualityComparer<T>? comparer = null);
    protected Selection<T> Select<TState, T>(Func<TState, T> selector, IEqualityComparer<T>? comparer = null) where TState : class;
    protected void Dispatch(object action);
    protected Task<DispatchResult> DispatchAsync(object action);
    public void Dispose();                                                            // idempotent
    protected virtual void Dispose(bool disposing);
}
public abstract class DuckyComponent<TState> : DuckyComponent where TState : class
{
    protected TState State { get; }                                                   // one whole-slice selection
}
public abstract class DuckyLayout : LayoutComponentBase, IHandleAfterRender, IDisposable { /* same members as DuckyComponent */ }

public sealed class DuckySelect<TResult> : ComponentBase, IDisposable
{
    [Parameter, EditorRequired] public Func<StateSnapshot, TResult> Selector { get; set; }
    [Parameter] public RenderFragment<TResult>? ChildContent { get; set; }
    [Parameter] public IEqualityComparer<TResult>? Comparer { get; set; }
}

public sealed class DuckyInitializer : ComponentBase                                  // optional, unstyled; records RendererInfo.IsInteractive
{                                                                                     // (and hands over its scoped services) before InitializeAsync
    [Parameter] public RenderFragment? ChildContent { get; set; }                     // rendered once store.InitializeAsync() completed; it is awaited in
                                                                                      // OnInitializedAsync (normative), so the static SSR/prerender quiescence
                                                                                      // wait covers hydration and ChildContent renders hydrated state (§11.6)
    [Parameter] public RenderFragment? Loading { get; set; }                          // rendered until then (optional): only under streaming rendering
                                                                                      // or interactively
}

public static class StoreSelectionExtensions                                          // CMP-05 for components with a foreign base class
{
    public static Selection<T> Select<T>(this IStore store, Func<StateSnapshot, T> selector,
        Func<Func<Task>, Task> invokeAsync, Action stateHasChanged, RendererInfo rendererInfo,   // pass the component's RendererInfo (gate, §11.4)
        IServiceProvider services,                                    // and its [Inject] IServiceProvider (the renderer-scope hand-off, §11.2)
        IEqualityComparer<T>? comparer = null);
}
```

Ducky.Blazor's configuration checks (DUCKY310–312 and DUCKY314–317) are `AddValidation` rules (§5.1), evaluated at first resolution on the final composed `BlazorOptions` and `PersistOptions`, never when a builder call runs: options keep composing until `configure` returns (attribute defaults, later `AddBlazor`/`Persist<T>` delegates), so a check made earlier could report on a value a later call fixes. The core offers no configure-time error API.

### 11.2 Components and the subscription core (CMP-01, CMP-05, INV-19, INV-20)

`internal sealed class SubscriptionCore` is the only implementation behind `DuckyComponent`, `DuckyLayout`, `DuckySelect` and `StoreSelectionExtensions.Select`.

- On the first `Select`, it takes one store subscription: `store.Select(static s => s, _ => OnCommit(), ReferenceEqualityComparer.Instance)`. Its `onChange` runs on the drainer.
- `OnCommit` (drainer): `if (Interlocked.CompareExchange(ref _scheduled, 1, 0) == 0) Schedule();`
- `Schedule`: `using (ExecutionContext.SuppressFlow()) task = invokeAsync(static async s => { await Task.Yield(); s.Evaluate(); }, this);` (shape, not exact signature). `SuppressFlow` alone only stops capture for *posted* work: when the drainer is already on the renderer's sync context (an event handler, an effect continuation that captured the context), `InvokeAsync` runs the delegate inline with the drainer's causal scope (the store's `_causal`, an `AsyncLocal` that flows with the `ExecutionContext`, §6.5) still current. The `await Task.Yield()` inside the suppressed region captures an empty `ExecutionContext`, so `Evaluate`, and any render or child lifecycle dispatch it triggers, always runs on the renderer with no causal scope or correlation (`Schedule_DrainerOnRendererThread_InlineInvoke_DoesNotFlowCausalScope`). `Evaluate` catches and logs its own exceptions after its step 1 (a user selector that throws during the recomputation included), so once `Evaluate` has started the scheduled task never faults. Only a failure before `Evaluate`'s step 1 (a synchronous throw from `invokeAsync`, or a rejected dispatcher task) resets the flag, and it does so with `Interlocked.CompareExchange(ref _scheduled, 0, 1)` and a log: an unconditional reset could clear a newer commit's 1 and let a third `InvokeAsync` be scheduled while one is pending, breaking "at most one pending `InvokeAsync`" (`Evaluate_SelectorThrows_AtMostOnePendingInvoke`). `ObjectDisposedException` (renderer torn down) is swallowed.
- `Evaluate` (renderer thread):
  1. `Interlocked.Exchange(ref _scheduled, 0)` **first**;
  2. `if (_disposed) return;`
  3. recompute every registered selection with the current snapshot and the current component fields, and compare each with the value **the renderer last observed** (updated whenever `Selection.Value` is read during render, and by `Evaluate` itself);
  4. if any differs under its comparer, call `StateHasChanged()` once;
  steps 2–4 run inside one try/catch that logs (a throwing selector or comparer is treated as changed, so the render surfaces the exception through the component's normal error path).
- No lost wake-up: interlocked operations are totally ordered full fences. If the drainer's CAS saw `1`, the pending `Evaluate` has not yet reset the flag, so its later snapshot read observes the drainer's earlier `Volatile.Write`. The CsCheck `SampleParallel` property `SubscriptionCore_RandomPublishInterleavings_LastPublishAlwaysEvaluated` checks this.
- The drainer never reads component fields (D9). A `[Parameter]` change is correct on the next render with no dispatch, because `Selection.Value` evaluates on read.
- **Registration guard (INV-20).** `DuckyComponent` and `DuckyLayout` re-implement `IHandleAfterRender`, mirroring `ComponentBase`: `first = !_rendered; _rendered = true; OnAfterRender(first); return OnAfterRenderAsync(first);`. Registration closes when `_rendered` is set, so no user override can bypass it. A `Select` after that throws `InvalidOperationException` (DUCKY352), and DUCKY009 catches a `Select` in markup at compile time. `DuckyComponent<TState>` creates its whole-slice selection on first access of `State` or, at the latest, in the same `IHandleAfterRender` hook just before registration closes, so conditional markup can read `State` later.
- Each component's first `Select` records `RendererInfo.IsInteractive` into the store's `InteractivityGate` (§11.4; one instance per store, never a scoped DI service), and hands over its scoped `IServiceProvider` (for `PersistentComponentState` and `AuthenticationStateProvider` in the browser), before it touches the store (which may start init). `DuckyInitializer` does the same before calling `InitializeAsync`, and `StoreSelectionExtensions.Select` does it from its `rendererInfo` and `services` arguments, so all three touchers hand over the same pair and a foreign-base component in a WASM app with a `Scope` hydrates its scoped keys without `<DuckyInitializer>` (`StoreSelectionExtensions_Wasm_HandsOverScopedServices`, bUnit with `IsBrowser = true`). **Ducky components work without `AddBlazor()`.** `SubscriptionCore` and `DuckyInitializer` resolve the gate with `GetService` (through the `PersistenceSlice` registration, which only `AddBlazor` adds, §11.4) and skip the recording and hand-off when it is absent: without `AddBlazor` no built-in reads the gate, so there is nothing to inform, and an app that persists nothing (`AddDucky(d => d.AddDuckyGenerated_App())`) never sees a container error naming an internal type (`Component_WithoutAddBlazor_SelectsAndRerenders`). `SubscriptionCore` builds each `Selection<T>` with `Selection<T>.Create(read, onRead: v => lastObserved = v, onDispose: …)`, which is how it tracks the value the renderer last observed.
- `DuckySelect` reads its value synchronously, renders only `ChildContent(value)`, re-subscribes when the `Selector` parameter reference changes (in `SetParametersAsync`), and unsubscribes on dispose.
- `Dispose` is idempotent and disposes the store subscription. Components use no reflection, so there is no `DynamicDependency` hack.

### 11.3 Render-mode matrix

| Host / pass | Store (lifetime) | Interactivity | Browser storage (Local/Session) | Server storage (`IDistributedCache`) | Prerender seed | Cross-tab | DevTools |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WASM standalone | 1 per app (Singleton) | Interactive (`IsBrowser`) | read during init; write-through: issued on the next scheduler turn after the commit (a page unload in between can lose it; unload never disposes the root provider); flush only on explicit host disposal | inert: not persisted, Info log once (EventId 2020) | n/a | yes | when `UseDevTools()` |
| Web App, static SSR page | 1 per request (Scoped) | NonInteractive | skipped, Status `NotStarted` | NonInteractive rule: read during init (in the HTML only when a lifecycle method awaits `InitializeAsync`, such as `<DuckyInitializer>`, §11.6); write-through; flush on request-scope dispose, which runs after the response was sent, so a redirect after a form post (Post/Redirect/Get) can render the value from before the post (§23, `DistributedCache_StaticSsrPostRedirect_StaleReadDocumented`) | n/a | no | inert |
| Web App, prerender pass of an interactive page | 1 per request, throwaway (Scoped) | NonInteractive | skipped | NonInteractive rule (identical to static SSR; the flush can land after the circuit store read or wrote the key, §23) | written in `OnPersisting` from a quiescent store, within `PrerenderSeedMaxWireBytes` (§11.4) | no | inert |
| Interactive Server (circuit) | 1 per circuit (Scoped) | Interactive | read during init (before `StoreInitialized`); debounce 250 ms; writes that fail on a transient disconnect are retried; **lost only if the circuit is disposed before a successful write** | read during init; write-through; flush on dispose (closes the loss window) | taken synchronously at init, before the first render; `OnPersisting` is also registered, so a paused and resumed circuit takes it again (§11.4) | yes | yes |
| Interactive WebAssembly | 1 per app (Singleton) | Interactive | as WASM standalone | inert (restored only from the seed when the slice is also `Prerender<T>`) | taken at init; if not yet restored, at the first component registration, within a short bound (§11.4) | yes | yes |
| Interactive Auto | circuit store and WASM store, each its own store. With enhanced navigation **both can be live in one document** | as above per side | both stores use the same keys; `ducky.js` relays each write and removal of any Local or Session key to the other stores of the document, which apply it through the CrossTab path (§11.7) | circuit side as Interactive Server; WASM side inert (no DUCKY313, so the shared `.Client` registration starts on both sides: `Auto_SharedServerPersistRegistration_WasmStartsAndSkipsServerSlices`) | hands off to whichever side takes over | yes, and between the stores of one document | yes |

**One load-effect rule in every mode:** a load effect on `StoreInitialized` checks `ctx.State.WasRestored<TState>()` and returns early. `StoreInitialized` fires only after every middleware init, including every hydration read the host can perform, so there is no Server-only "react to `HydrationCompleted`" exception (ADR-0015). To avoid loading twice (prerender + interactive), also mark the slice `Prerender<T>()`.

### 11.4 Interactivity, init and prerender handoff (SSR-01/02/03, INV-13–15)

**`InteractivityGate`** (internal, one per store): `Unknown`, `Interactive` or `NonInteractive`, plus the renderer-scope `IServiceProvider` handed over by the first component. It is never registered in DI: it is held by the store-owned `PersistenceSlice` instance (every Blazor store has one, §11.5), and every party reaches it through that slice's DI registration, which follows the store's lifetime and resolves to the store-owned instance from any scope (§6.10). In WASM, a component (renderer scope) and `PrerenderHandoff`, `PersistenceMiddleware` and `DevToolsMiddleware` (store scope) therefore share one gate; a scoped gate would give the store scope a second instance that never sees a registration (`Gate_Wasm_ComponentRegistrationVisibleToMiddleware`).
- `IsBrowser` (§5.1) → `Interactive`.
- Otherwise the first toucher that knows its renderer records `RendererInfo.IsInteractive`: a Ducky component's first `Select`, `DuckyInitializer` before it calls `InitializeAsync`, or `StoreSelectionExtensions.Select` from its `rendererInfo` and `services` arguments (the prerender store is always non-interactive; the circuit store is interactive).
- If it is still `Unknown` when a built-in needs to know (a store touched first by non-component code, such as an injected `SliceStore.Set` in `OnInitialized` of a foreign component), the gate **probes** once, synchronously: it calls the `ducky.js` import through `JsBridge` without awaiting. A synchronous `InvalidOperationException` (the unsupported prerender JS runtime) ⇒ `NonInteractive`. Any other outcome ⇒ `Interactive`, and the returned task is kept as the lazy module handle, under the module rule of §11.5: a faulted or cancelled import task is never cached, so a disconnect during the import doesn't poison every later call. The probe outcome is covered in bUnit (fake runtime) and E2E, and spike S-7 confirms the synchronous-throw contract for .NET 10.
- The gate announces the first component registration through a **synchronous callback**, `gate.OnFirstRegistration(Action)`: the registering component invokes the registered callbacks directly (outside every lock, once) after recording its renderer and services and **before** it touches the store, so work done in the callback happens inside that component's `Select`, before it renders. This does not rely on TPL continuation inlining (an awaited TCS behind `WaitAsync` and `ConfigureAwait(false)` gives no such guarantee). A callback registered after the first registration runs at once on the registering thread.

**`SeedSettled`** (internal, shared by `PrerenderHandoff` and `PersistenceMiddleware`, same store): a TCS completed once the seed restore has been enqueued or skipped, carrying what a **pause** seed hands to persistence (below): the unscoped browser-storage keys it restored that are in its `dirty` list, and its scoped keys with their values, their dirty flags and the recorded scope hash, which were not restored. It is completed at construction when there are no `Prerender<T>` slices, and in the synchronous prefix outside the browser. `PersistenceMiddleware` awaits it before it enqueues its hydration restore (never before its read), so the seed restore always precedes the storage restore in the queue, in every host and whatever the timing (ADR-0011).

**Prerender handoff** (`PrerenderHandoff : Middleware`, registered by `AddBlazor` ahead of persistence; inert without `Prerender<T>` slices). It never depends on an `Unknown` gate:
- **Outside the browser**, `InitializeAsync`, synchronous prefix (before any `await`):
  1. always call `PersistentComponentState.TryTakeFromJson<byte[]>("ducky:seed", out utf8)` (it returns false when nothing was persisted, for example in the prerender pass itself). On success, parse the envelope `{"v":1,"src":"prerender"|"pause","scope":"<sha-256 hex>"?,"dirty":[key, …]?,"ver":{key: version}?,"slices":{key: state}}` and `Store.Restore(values, Origin.Hydration)`, leaving out every persisted key whose `ver` entry differs from its current `PersistOptions.Version` or is missing (Debug log; below); for a pause seed, the values of scoped keys are left out of this restore and handed to persistence through `SeedSettled` (see the pause rules below). Because §6.7 starts every middleware init before awaiting any, the restore is reduced before the component that triggered init renders, whoever touched the store first, a `State` read included (`PrerenderSeed_AppliedBeforeFirstInteractiveRender`, `DuckyInitializerFirstToucher_SeedWrittenOnPrerenderAndAppliedBeforeFirstInteractiveRender`, `SliceStore_StateReadFirst_SeedAndStorageApplied`). Complete `SeedSettled`;
  2. always `RegisterOnPersisting(OnPersisting, RenderMode.InteractiveAuto)` (a no-op when no persist happens). The render mode is explicit, never `null`: when an app configures both the server and WebAssembly render modes (the Interactive Auto template, and `Ducky.Samples.Server`), the framework persists through a composite store and infers a render mode for every registration, and a null-mode registration whose callback target is not an `IComponent` (this middleware) throws `InvalidOperationException`, which fails the page's whole prerender state persistence, other `PersistentComponentState` users included. The prerender stores and the protected prerender store accept `InteractiveAutoRenderMode`, the composite path copies the Auto store into both the server and the WebAssembly payloads, and the circuit-pause collector accepts every mode, so one registration serves single-mode, mixed-mode and Auto apps and circuit pause (`Prerender_OnPersistingRegisteredWithInteractiveAutoMode` is behavioural: its fake store accepts only `InteractiveAutoRenderMode`, so the seed is persisted iff the registration's mode is Interactive Auto, §17.2; E2E `Prerender_ServerAndWasmModesConfigured_SeedPersistedAndApplied` runs against the Auto sample). The WebAssembly copy of the seed is not data-protected: it is in the page HTML, like the rendered markup, so a `Prerender<T>` slice must hold nothing the rendered page couldn't show (documented on `Prerender<T>`). In the prerender pass this writes the seed (`src: "prerender"`). In an interactive circuit it runs when .NET 10 pauses the circuit and writes `src: "pause"`, so a resumed circuit takes the seed through step 1 (`Circuit_Resume_SeedRestoresPrerenderSlices`). Slices that are neither `Prerender<T>` nor persisted reset on resume: a resumed circuit is a fresh store rehydrated from storage and the seed (documented).
- **In the browser**, `InitializeAsync`: WASM populates `PersistentComponentState` only once the host has started (`RunAsync`), so a Program.cs preload may start init before the seed exists.
  1. Synchronous prefix: `TryTakeFromJson`; on success restore as above, complete `SeedSettled`, done. (A browser store never receives a pause seed: pausing is a circuit feature.)
  2. Otherwise register a take-the-seed callback on `gate.OnFirstRegistration`. Exactly two things settle the browser wait, behind an `Interlocked` once-flag: that first-registration callback, and the timeout of step 3. The callback does the work **inside the callback**: `TryTakeFromJson` again, restore on success, complete `SeedSettled`. It always settles, because WASM restores `PersistentComponentState` in `RunAsync`, before the first render, so a failed take at the first registration really means there is no seed. Because the callback runs synchronously inside that component's `Select`, the restore happens before it renders (`Prerender_WasmPreloadBeforeRun_SeedStillApplied`). `PersistentComponentState.RegisterOnRestoring` is **not** used: in .NET 10 it does not signal the initial restore (it invokes the callback synchronously at registration, and the initial restore never calls restoring callbacks), so it would win the once-flag at once with nothing to take and lose the seed. The bUnit tests drive the real framework path (§17.2): a real `ComponentStatePersistenceManager` whose `RestoreStateAsync(fakeStore)` the test calls at the chosen point, which is how WASM's late restore is reproduced. **Both settle paths (this callback and the timeout of step 3) first check the init token: when it is cancelled** (an init-buffer overflow abort made the store `Ready` before either ran, possible from a Program.cs preload that dispatches more than `InitBufferCapacity` user actions before `RunAsync`), **they skip the restore with a Debug log and still complete `SeedSettled`**: a restore now would land after `StoreInitialized` and the replayed user actions, overwrite freshly loaded or user-changed state and flip `WasRestored` (`Prerender_WasmOverflowAbortBeforeFirstRegistration_SeedIgnored`). WASM is single-threaded, so the check can't interleave with `Abort`/`MarkReady`. `InitializeAsync` only awaits `SeedSettled.Task.WaitAsync(PrerenderSeedWaitTimeout, TimeProvider, initToken)` (250 ms, not `HydrationTimeout`), so an abort or a dispose ends the wait at once instead of holding dispose's init wait for the bound; it catches that cancellation and returns normally, so an aborted init is not logged as a faulted one.
  3. On timeout, if it wins the once-flag and the init token is not cancelled (step 2), `TryTakeFromJson` once more anyway. If nothing is there, log Warning once per store (EventId 2022) naming the fix: don't await a preload before `RunAsync`, or render `<DuckyInitializer>` (or a Ducky component) first. Complete `SeedSettled`. An app whose components are plain `ComponentBase` with an injected `SliceStore` therefore never stalls on this wait (`Prerender_PlainComponentsOnlyWasm_NoInitStall`; such components still need a subscription to *show* later changes, §12), and an awaited preload costs at most the bound, logs, and still can't invert precedence (`Prerender_WasmAwaitedPreload_WarnsAndDoesNotInvertPrecedence`). No `OnPersisting` is registered in the browser.
- Seeds carried by later enhanced navigations or new WASM islands are never read: the singleton store takes the seed once, and after `Ready` the live store is authoritative (`Prerender_EnhancedNavLaterSeed_Ignored`).
- `OnPersisting`: `await Store.WhenIdleAsync(cts(PrerenderIdleTimeout, TimeProvider))`.
  - idle → take **one** snapshot, `snap = Store.State`, and derive everything the envelope carries from it (the one-snapshot rule the writer uses, §11.5): values, left-out keys, scope hash and dirty keys, so no value comes from a later snapshot than the key set or scope that governs it (on Server the auth handler runs on a pool thread and may drain a reset inline between two reads). Serialize each `Prerender<T>` slice's state in `snap` through `Store.Json` (declared type), in registration order, into the envelope, and record in `ver` the current `PersistOptions.Version` of every persisted key it carries. A slice registered with `Prerender<TSlice, TState>(include)` is left out (Debug log) when `include(state)` is false. Quiescence only says no effect is running, not that the load succeeded: a prerender-time load commonly fails (a server-side `HttpClient` calling a relative or self URL), the effect dispatches its failure action, the store goes idle, and a seeded error state would be `WasRestored` on the interactive side, so the recommended load rule would never retry it. Slices whose load can fail during prerender therefore register an `include` that excludes error and incomplete states; the docs say so on `Prerender<T>` and in the load-effect recipe (`Prerender_LoadFailedDuringPrerender_InteractiveSideRetries`). `[Prerender]` maps to the predicate-less overload. A slice whose inclusion would push the envelope's **estimated wire size** past `PrerenderSeedMaxWireBytes` is left out and logged at Warning once per slice (EventId 2021); the interactive side loads that slice again, degraded but correct (`PrerenderSeed_AboveBudget_OmitsSliceAndLogs`, E2E `Prerender_LargeSlice_InteractiveServer_CircuitStarts`). The budget exists because on Interactive Server `PersistentComponentState` travels back inside the start-circuit hub message, capped by the 32 KB `MaximumReceiveMessageSize`, and the UTF-8 size of the envelope understates that. The seed is persisted as `PersistAsJson<byte[]>` of the UTF-8 envelope, never as a `string`: a string would be re-serialized with the default `JavaScriptEncoder` (each `"` becomes `\u0022`, each non-ASCII character `\uXXXX`), a content-dependent growth of up to about 3× for quote-dense or CJK JSON, whereas a `byte[]` becomes a base64 JSON string (×4/3 whatever the content). The framework then stores those bytes base64-encoded inside its state dictionary JSON, and on Server the dictionary is data-protected and base64-encoded again. The estimate reproduces that pipeline: `e = 4·⌈n/3⌉ + 2` (the JSON string STJ writes for `n` envelope bytes), then `wire = 4·⌈(4·⌈e/3⌉ + P)/3⌉` with `P = 256` bytes for the dictionary entry and the data-protection header, IV, padding and MAC; about 2.4× `n` in all. The same budget applies in WASM hosts, where it is conservative. The 20 KB default leaves room on the page for other `PersistentComponentState` users (for example the template's authentication-state persistence), which share the 32 KB (documented). `PrerenderSeed_WireEstimate_NeverBelowActualEncoding` (property: random quote-dense and CJK states, the estimate is ≥ the length the framework's encoding produces) and E2E `Prerender_SeedJustUnderBudget_QuoteDenseAndCjk_InteractiveServer_CircuitStarts` pin it;
  - for a **pause** (the circuit store's callback), three more rules, all read from `snap` and, under `_issue`, from the store's `PersistenceMiddleware` (same store, internal): if `snap`'s `@ducky/persistence` Status is `Hydrating` (a scope switch in flight, whose scoped slices hold the reset initial state, not data), every key in the key set recorded for `snap.ScopeEpoch` is left out; scoped keys are carried only when the scope recorded for `snap.ScopeEpoch` is known and non-null, and the envelope then records `scope`, the SHA-256 hex of that scope (never the raw user id: the pause state may be kept by the client), otherwise they are left out with a Debug log; and a carried browser-storage key is listed in `dirty` iff the local serialization of its value in `snap` differs from `LastKnownPayload[storageKey]` (the last successful write, read or receipt; an absent baseline compares as the initial state's serialization, as in §11.7), i.e. iff the circuit holds a change that storage may not have. `Circuit_PauseDuringScopeSwitch_StorageNotOverwrittenWithInitialState` drives a switch between the idle wait and the serialization deterministically, through the IVT hook `BlazorOptions.AfterSeedIdleHook` (an `Action?`, null in production, like `AfterDeferHook`);
  - timeout → persist **nothing**, log Warning once per store (EventId 2014). The interactive side then loads again: degraded, never wrong (D7).
  - `WhenIdleAsync` excludes `LongRunning` effects, so a poller never blocks the seed (`Prerender_LongRunningEffect_DoesNotBlockSeed`). It also excludes all Ducky.Reactive work (§14): a reactive pipeline is not counted as running, so a reactive load in flight is not waited for and the seed would capture its "loading" state. **A `Prerender<T>` slice must be loaded by an `Effect<T>`**, never by a reactive effect; the docs say so on `Prerender<T>` and in §14 (`Prerender_ReactiveLoadInFlight_Documented` pins the behaviour).
- **Precedence (matrix conflict 8, ADR-0011):** a **prerender** seed applies synchronously (no flicker), then storage, browser or Server, if it holds a valid envelope, **wins** and costs one extra render. The rule is uniform; there is no timestamp comparison (client and server clocks skew), so an *older* valid envelope still wins. It doesn't depend on registration order, on host timing, or on whether the cache completes synchronously (`MemoryDistributedCache` does, Redis doesn't): `PersistenceMiddleware` enqueues its restore only after `SeedSettled` (above). Tests: `Precedence_ValidStorageEnvelope_WinsOverSeed`, `Precedence_OlderStorageEnvelope_StillWins`, `Precedence_ServerStorage_IndependentOfRegistrationOrder`, `Precedence_PrerenderSeed_StorageStillWins`.
- A **pause** seed (`src: "pause"`, written by a paused interactive circuit) carries the live store's latest state, which is newer than browser storage whenever writes failed during the disconnect that preceded the pause. But storage is often newer than the seed: while the circuit is paused, another tab or the WASM store of an Auto document keeps writing the same Local key (synced slices raise a `storage` event the dead circuit can't receive; unsynced Local and Session slices share the key anyway). So only keys that were dirty at the pause override storage. Its rules:
  - **dirty browser-storage keys** (Local/Session, listed in `dirty`) win over storage: `PersistenceMiddleware` still reads those keys (setting their `LastKnownPayload` baseline, §11.5), skips their restore, and signals their writers after the terminal, so storage is rewritten with the seed value (`Circuit_ResumeAfterDisconnect_PauseSeedWinsOverStaleLocalStorage`, the dirty-key case);
  - **clean browser-storage keys** follow prerender precedence: storage wins if it holds a valid envelope, otherwise the seed value stays, so another tab's newer write made during the pause is kept (`Circuit_ResumeAfterOtherTabWrote_CleanKey_StorageWins`);
  - **version-skewed keys**: a seed can outlive a deploy (.NET 10 keeps paused-circuit state client-side, or server-side for hours, so a circuit survives a redeploy), and a seed value never goes through `EnvelopeReader`'s version check or migrations. So a persisted key whose `ver` entry differs from the current `PersistOptions.Version`, or is missing, is dropped with a Debug log from the synchronous restore, from the `dirty` set handed through `SeedSettled` and from the scoped hand-off; storage, read and migrated by `EnvelopeReader`, or the initial state then applies, exactly as for a clean key with no seed value. Otherwise an old-shape value that deserializes "successfully" (a renamed field silently defaulted) would, as a dirty key, skip the storage restore that would have migrated it and rewrite storage as a new-version envelope holding the defaulted data (`Circuit_ResumeAfterVersionBump_DirtySeedKeyDropped_StorageMigrated`). The rule applies to prerender seeds too, at no extra cost;
  - **Server-storage keys**: storage wins, exactly as over a prerender seed. A Server write never fails on a disconnect, so the override would bring no benefit and could only roll back a newer write from another circuit of the same user;
  - **scoped keys** (every Server key, and browser keys when `Scope` is set) are never restored in the synchronous prefix: they reach `PersistenceMiddleware` through `SeedSettled`, and §11.5 step 6 applies them (dirty browser keys with the override above; clean browser keys and Server keys as the fallback when storage holds no valid envelope) only when the scope resolved for epoch 0 hashes to the recorded `scope` and attempt 0 is still current. Otherwise they are dropped with a Debug log and storage, or the initial state, is restored normally. So a circuit resumed under another user (signed out or switched in another tab during the disconnect) never renders user A's scoped slices in B's store and never writes them under B's key (`Circuit_ResumeUnderOtherUser_PauseSeedScopedSlicesNotRestoredOrWritten`), and a pause taken while a switch was in flight never carries the reset initial state that would then overwrite the new user's stored data (`Circuit_PauseDuringScopeSwitch_StorageNotOverwrittenWithInitialState`).

### 11.5 Persistence: browser storage (PER-01/02/03, INV-14, INV-16)

`PersistenceMiddleware` (one per store, registered by `AddBlazor` right after `PrerenderHandoff`, inert without `Persist<T>` slices) and the internal `PersistenceSlice : Slice<PersistenceState>` (key `@ducky/persistence`, registered by `AddBlazor`). `PersistenceSlice.Initial` is always `Hydrated`: a slice built with `new()` can't know whether anything is persisted. It also registers a no-op `On<ClearPersistedState>(s => s)`, so the public `ClearPersistedState` is never "unhandled" in strict mode (`ClearPersistedState_Strict_NoFailure`).

**Issuing restores: the `_issue` lock.** `PersistenceMiddleware` owns one `System.Threading.Lock _issue`. It guards the current hydration attempt, `_scopeVersion`, the epoch→scope map (an immutable map swapped under the lock and read lock-free by the writers) and the per-key cross-tab sequence numbers. Every `Store.Restore` and `DispatchSystem` that Ducky.Blazor's persistence and cross-tab code issue (the `NotStarted`/`Hydrating` status restores, attempt results, terminals, scope-switch resets, cross-tab restores) is issued while holding `_issue`, together with the check that decides it. So those actions reach the store queue in the order of their decisions, and neither an init abort nor a scope switch can slip between a decision and its enqueue. An inline drain started under `_issue` is allowed: the lock is reentrant, it is never acquired while `_gate` is held (the dispatcher runs no user code under `_gate`), and code holding it never waits on another thread except to acquire `_issue` itself (user code running in that drain must not block, §7). A thread that waits for `_issue` (the init-abort callback, a timeout callback, the auth-change handler) holds no other lock and may wait out one inline drain. ponytail: that is the §7 drain-latency ceiling again; upgrade path: a non-draining enqueue in the core.

**Hydration (init).**
- If no slice is persisted, nothing happens and Status stays `Hydrated` (`Hydration_NothingPersisted_Inert`). Slices inert in this host count as not persisted: in the browser a store whose persisted slices are all Server storage has nothing to hydrate, so Status stays `Hydrated` and a loading UI waiting for `Hydrated`/`Failed` never hangs (`Hydration_BrowserOnlyInertServerSlices_StatusHydrated`; the E2E `Auto_SharedServerPersistRegistration_WasmStartsAndSkipsServerSlices` asserts it on the WASM side).
- Otherwise, `InitializeAsync`, synchronous prefix (before any `await`):
  1. Determine the readable slices: Server-storage slices outside the browser (inert in the browser, §11.6); Local/Session slices only when the gate is `Interactive` (probing synchronously if `Unknown`, §11.4).
  2. None readable (only possible in a NonInteractive store: prerender or static SSR with browser-only slices) → under `_issue`, `Store.Restore({"@ducky/persistence": new PersistenceState(NotStarted)}, Hydration)`. No attempt, so no terminal action.
  3. Otherwise, under `_issue`: make attempt 0 current (epoch `_scopeVersion`, which is 0, with its **key set**: every readable key), register on the init token (§6.7 requires it here), arm the **init-phase** `HydrationTimeout` on `TimeProvider` (it acts on whichever attempt is current when it fires, step 7), set `_initPhase` (a field read and cleared only under `_issue`, step 7), and `Store.Restore({"@ducky/persistence": new PersistenceState(Hydrating) { ScopeEpoch = 0 }}, Hydration)`. Then issue the `ducky.js` registration (§11.7) without awaiting it; it is ordered before the reads on the same module task.
- Then, asynchronously:
  4. Resolve the scope when any key is scoped (§11.6; the auth-change subscription already exists, so a change racing this resolution becomes a switch that supersedes attempt 0) and, under `_issue`, record it for epoch 0 if attempt 0 is still current, then drain `_deferred` (Writes, "A skipped key stays dirty"). Read every key of the attempt's key set: the initial attempt reads storage directly (nothing was written before it, below); a switch attempt reads each key through that key's writer loop (Writes, "Switch reads"). Browser storage: `storageGet(area, key, InlinePayloadBytes)` returns `null`, the value, or the too-large sentinel (§11.9), which switches to `storageGetStream` through `IJSStreamReference` with `maxAllowedSize: MaxPayloadBytes` (D11), read under `await using` so the reference (and the `Uint8Array` it pins in the circuit's JS object table) is disposed after `OpenReadStreamAsync`, the over-`MaxPayloadBytes` path included; a reference with `Length == 0` (the key was removed between the two calls: the export returns an empty `Uint8Array`, §11.9) reads as not found. `ducky.js` decides the size in UTF-8 bytes, because .NET can't know it before the value has crossed the hub. Server storage: `IDistributedCache.GetAsync`. Each envelope goes through the one `EnvelopeReader`, shared with cross-tab (§11.7):
     - `v > Version` → not found (Warning, D18);
     - `v < Version` → run `Migrate` steps over `JsonNode` up to `Version`;
     - `now - at > MaxAge` → discard;
     - deserialize `s` through `Store.Json` with the declared `StateType` (§10);
     - malformed, or a deserialization that throws (a state constructor or `init` accessor included, such as `EntityState`'s duplicate-id `ArgumentException`) → that slice keeps its initial state (Warning naming key and type, never throws); the other slices still restore (`Hydration_OneSliceCtorThrows_OtherSlicesStillRestored`, whose cases include an `EntityState` with duplicate ids).
     - a stored value above `MaxPayloadBytes` (the stream open with `maxAllowedSize` throws) → that key reads as **not found**, with a Warning (EventId 2023) naming the key and its size; the exception is caught per key and never fails the attempt, so the other keys hydrate normally (`LargeRead_MaxPayloadBytesBoundary` asserts that a value at the limit restores and one byte above reads as not found; `Hydration_OneOversizedKey_OtherSlicesRestored`). Cross-tab receipt applies the same rule (§11.7). A value that large was written by this library only if the state grew past the limit; the next write replaces it.
  5. Await `SeedSettled` (§11.4), so the seed restore is always enqueued first.
  6. Under `_issue`:
     - if the attempt is no longer current (a scope switch superseded it), discard everything with a Debug log and stop: the superseding attempt owns the terminal, and its key set carries this attempt's unscoped keys (§11.6), so they are read again rather than lost;
     - claim the terminal (`_terminal` 0→1); if the claim is lost (the timeout or an init abort won), discard and log (`Hydration_ResultAfterTimeout_IsDiscarded`);
     - drop keys whose cross-tab sequence number moved since their read began (the newer cross-tab value or removal stays, `CrossTab_EventDuringHydration_NewestWins`, `CrossTab_RemovalDuringHydration_RemovalWins`) and the unscoped browser-storage keys a pause seed restored and listed as dirty (§11.4: baseline set, restore skipped, key added to `_deferred` so its writer runs after the terminal); a clean key the seed restored is restored from storage like any other when storage holds a valid envelope;
     - for the scoped keys a pause seed handed over through `SeedSettled` (§11.4): if this is attempt 0 and its recorded scope hashes to the seed's `scope`, include a dirty browser-storage key's seed value in this restore in place of the stored one (baseline from the read, key added to `_deferred`), and a clean browser-storage key's or a Server-storage key's seed value only when storage held no valid envelope for it; otherwise drop them (Debug log);
     - set `LastKnownPayload[storageKey]` of each read key to the local serialization of its deserialized value (absent → none; see Writes);
     - one `Store.Restore(values, Hydration)` for all restored slices;
     - set the pending terminal `HydrationCompleted(restoredAny) { ScopeEpoch = attempt.Epoch }` and call `IssueTerminal()`.
  7. On an exception: under `_issue`, if the attempt is current and the claim succeeds, set the pending terminal `HydrationFailed(errorType, message) { ScopeEpoch }` (dispatched with `isFailure: true`) and call `IssueTerminal()`. Timeouts come in two kinds. The **init-phase deadline** armed in step 3 and the init-token callback registered there both act on **whichever attempt is current when they run**, not on attempt 0: under `_issue` they claim, set `HydrationFailed` with that attempt's epoch and call `IssueTerminal()`, so a switch during init can't leave the initial terminal to land after `StoreInitialized` or orphan it without a timer. Both act only while `_initPhase` is set when they take `_issue`; otherwise they are no-ops. `_initPhase` is cleared under `_issue` in the same critical section that issues the terminal of the attempt current during init (`IssueTerminal` while `_initPhase`), or when the init-token callback runs, and clearing it disposes the init-phase timer. A **per-attempt** `HydrationTimeout` is armed by a switch iff `_initPhase` is clear, decided under `_issue` in §11.6 step 1: never "once `InitializeAsync` has returned", an asynchronous continuation that can't be ordered against the switch. It acts only if its attempt is still current, and a superseded attempt's per-attempt timer is discarded when it reaches `_issue`. So every attempt has exactly one live deadline: a switch at 4.9 s, just after the initial terminal, arms its own timer and is never failed by the init-phase deadline at 5 s (`ScopeSwitch_JustAfterInitialTerminal_OwnTimerArmed_NotFailedByInitDeadline`: a 3 s read completes), and a switch landing between the initial terminal and `InitializeAsync`'s return (raised from a subscriber of the terminal's commit) still gets a timer, so a hung read ends in `HydrationFailed` (`ScopeSwitch_BetweenInitialTerminalAndInitReturn_HungRead_FailsAtOwnTimeout`). The persistence slice reduces `HydrationCompleted` to `Hydrated` and `HydrationFailed` to `Failed`, and ignores a terminal whose `ScopeEpoch` differs from the state's (defence in depth, `Hydration_StaleEpochTerminal_IgnoredBySlice`).
- **Exactly one terminal** per current attempt. `IssueTerminal()` issues the claimed attempt's pending terminal once (claim state 1→2). The winner calls it right after its restore. The init-token callback also calls it (while `_initPhase` is set), always under `_issue`, whether or not it won the claim. So even an abort raised re-entrantly on the winner's own thread (an init-buffer overflow from a subscriber during the restore's inline drain) finds the terminal already queued, or queues it itself, before `Abort` reaches `MarkReady`.
- **Before `StoreInitialized`.** The init-token callback takes `_issue` before it returns into `Abort` (§6.7), and a winner's {claim, restore, terminal} all happen under `_issue`, so `MarkReady` can never run between them: the initial attempt's restore and terminal always precede `StoreInitialized` (`Hydration_InitAborted_OneTerminal`, and the CsCheck `SampleParallel` test `Hydration_ReadCompletesConcurrentlyWithInitAbort_TerminalPrecedesStoreInitialized`, §17.3 row 6). `InitializeAsync` returns once the current attempt has issued its terminal (a switch during init hands this over to the superseding attempt) or the init token is cancelled. While `_initPhase` is set, a switch arms no timer of its own: the init-phase deadline armed in step 3 acts on whichever attempt is current when it fires (step 7), so the initial terminal, whichever attempt issues it, comes within one `HydrationTimeout` of the synchronous prefix, which DUCKY316 keeps below `InitTimeout` (`ScopeSwitch_DuringInit_TerminalPrecedesStoreInitialized_WithinOneHydrationTimeout`: `HydrationTimeout` 8 s, a switch at 4 s, the terminal lands at 8 s, before `StoreInitialized`).
- **Nothing written before the first read.** `AfterReduce` does not signal writers until this synchronous prefix has run: a change reduced earlier (a `DispatchSystem` from a middleware registered before persistence) only marks its key deferred (see Writes), so it is written after the terminal.
- A failure leaves persistence running. Known ceiling (documented): after a read *failure*, the first user change to that slice overwrites the stored value.

**Envelope** (written with `Utf8JsonWriter`): `{"v":2,"at":"2026-09-30T12:00:00Z","s":<state via the declared type's JsonTypeInfo>}`. It has no AssemblyQualifiedName. 1.x data fails to parse as an envelope and is discarded (Debug log, ADR-0033). Every comparison (write dedupe, cross-tab echo guard) is made on the `s` payload, never on the envelope text, whose `at` changes on every write. Property test `Persist_EnvelopeRoundTrip_Property` (CsCheck, ENG-02): a random state and version, written, read and migrated, equals the migrated state, and a newer version reads as not found.

**Writes (`PersistenceWriter`, one per key).**
- `AfterReduce` signals the key when it is in `ChangedKeys` **and** `Origin ∉ {Hydration, CrossTab, DevTools}`. The per-key `Channel<bool>(capacity 1, DropOldest)` carries a **wake-up signal only**, never a value; a pending removal is a separate per-key command (below).
- One async loop per key, on its own `CancellationTokenSource`, which is **not** linked to the store lifetime: wait for a signal → debounce on `TimeProvider` → run a pending removal first, if any → drain the final-snapshot queue, if any ("Final write under the old scope", below) → read **one** snapshot `snap = Store.State` → **hydration skip**, one predicate `ShouldSkip(snap, key)`, true iff (a) `snap`'s persistence Status is `Hydrating` and the key is in the key set recorded (with the scope, under `_issue`) for `snap.ScopeEpoch` (every readable key for the initial attempt; for a switch, its scoped keys plus the unscoped keys it carries from the attempt it superseded, §11.6; other unscoped keys have no reason to wait), or (b) the key is scoped and the scope recorded for `snap.ScopeEpoch` is still unknown (epoch 0 is recorded when the initial scope resolves, a switch epoch when its scope resolves; unscoped keys ignore the epoch). Case (b) outlives the terminal whenever the terminal is a timeout or abort `HydrationFailed` issued before the scope resolved (a browser `Scope` app with no Ducky component and no `<DuckyInitializer>`, where the hand-off never happens; a slow `AuthenticationStateProvider`/MSAL; a post-init switch hitting its own timeout) → for a scoped key, derive the target key from `snap`'s `ScopeEpoch` through the scope recorded for that epoch: a null scope is skipped without deferral (no I/O for an anonymous scope, and the next switch resets scoped slices anyway) → serialize the slot with the declared type → skip if the payload equals `LastKnownPayload[storageKey]` → write → on success set `LastKnownPayload[storageKey]`. Value and key come from the same snapshot, which is never torn (INV-07), so the last write equals the final committed state and a write can't pair one scope's state with another scope's key. One write or removal per key is in flight at a time (INV-16).
- **Final write under the old scope.** When `AfterReduce` sees the scope-switch reset commit (the `Hydration` restore that changes `@ducky/persistence`'s `ScopeEpoch`, §11.6), it enqueues `context.PreviousState`, the last committed snapshot of the old epoch, on each scoped key's writer (a per-key `ConcurrentQueue<StateSnapshot>`) and signals it. The writer loop drains that queue before it reads `Store.State`: for each queued snapshot it applies the hydration skip **to that snapshot** (without deferral: the old scope is over), derives the key from that snapshot's epoch (unknown or null scope → skipped), dedupes against that key's `LastKnownPayload`, and writes. A failed write of a queued snapshot (a transient Redis timeout, or an interop timeout at the moment the user switches) keeps that snapshot at the head of the queue and retries it with the same 1 s→30 s backoff ("Failed writes are retried", below); each retry re-serializes **that** snapshot against its own epoch's key, never `Store.State`, which already belongs to the new epoch (an unknown scope, or the new key). The loop reads `Store.State`, and runs switch-read commands, only once the queue is empty, so a switch read of a key whose old-scope write keeps failing waits behind it and ends at its attempt's timeout rather than read around the unwritten change (`ScopeSwitch_FinalOldScopeWriteFailsTransiently_RetriedUnderOldKey`, a `MemoryDistributedCache` wrapper failing the first `SetAsync`). The dispose flush treats queued snapshots the same way. The wake-up channel still carries no value; the queue is the only place a snapshot is handed over. `PreviousState` is taken on the drainer at the reset, so it is the newest old-epoch state, and per-key serialization writes it after any older in-flight write of that key: a change committed just before the switch is written under the old key, never lost and never overwritten by an older snapshot (`ScopeSwitch_ChangeCommittedJustBeforeSwitch_WrittenUnderOldKey`).
- **Switch reads.** A switch attempt (epoch > 0) never reads storage directly: for each key of its key set it posts a read command to that key's writer loop, like the removal command, and awaits its result. The loop runs the command after it has drained its final-snapshot queue and after any in-flight write of that storage key, so a switch read of K always sees the old scope's final write to K. That matters whenever the old and new storage keys coincide: `AuthenticationStateChanged` for the same user that is not absorbed as a re-assertion (§11.6: a circuit reconnect, token refresh, claims update or re-notification after sign-in whose scope resolves asynchronously, or that arrives while an attempt is still in flight) or a fast A→B→A switch; otherwise Redis could return the value from before the final write, the attempt would restore it and set the baseline to it, and after the terminal the deferred writer would write that stale value back over the user's last change (`ScopeSwitch_SameScopeReasserted_FinalWriteNotRolledBack`: a TCS-gated cache, a TCS-gated scope delegate that completes asynchronously, so the same-user re-notification stays on the switch path, and A→B→A). The initial attempt needs no such ordering (nothing is written before it). Documented: a same-scope notification that is not a re-assertion (§11.6) still resets scoped slices to their initial state for one render before the re-read restores them; the common case, a circuit reconnect whose scope resolves synchronously, is a re-assertion and resets nothing.
- **A skipped key stays dirty.** A hydration skip consumes the signal, so the key joins `_deferred` (a `ConcurrentDictionary`). `_deferred` is drained, taking the keys out one by one with `TryRemove` and signalling each key removed, at two moments: when `AfterReduce` sees `@ducky/persistence` change from `Hydrating` to `Hydrated` or `Failed` (either terminal, the release of case (a)), and, under `_issue`, right after a scope is recorded for an epoch (§11.5 step 4 and the §11.6 switch; the epoch→scope map is published before the drain), the release of case (b). A key still skipped when its signal arrives (a Hydrating key signalled by a scope recording) is simply deferred again, once. The skip is check-then-act against both releases, so the writer uses **add-then-recheck**: after adding its key, it reads `Store.State` and the epoch→scope map again, and if `ShouldSkip` no longer holds for that fresh snapshot it `TryRemove`s its own key and, if that removal succeeded, re-signals itself; while `ShouldSkip` still holds, the key stays in `_deferred` and the loop goes back to waiting. The recheck uses exactly the predicate of the skip: testing only case (a) there would, after a terminal issued before the scope resolved, remove and re-signal a case-(b) key forever, a hot spin that with the default debounce of 0 runs synchronously on WASM's only thread (`Write_TerminalBeforeScopeResolved_NoSpin_WrittenOnceScopeRecorded`: a TCS-gated scope delegate, a timeout terminal first, then the scope; asserts a bounded number of writer iterations and exactly one write after the recording; `Wasm_ScopeWithoutHandOff_ScopedChange_NoWriterSpin`: a browser `Scope` app with no component, whose scoped key stays deferred without spinning). Whichever side removes the key signals it, so it is signalled exactly once and never stranded by a terminal that committed between the writer's snapshot read and its add (on WASM, where nothing is disposed on unload, a stranded key would be a lost change). The CsCheck `SampleParallel` test `Write_SkipRacesTerminal_DeferredKeyStillWritten` (in `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`) races the two; both branches are also reached deterministically through the internal interleaving hook `BlazorOptions.AfterDeferHook` (an `Action<string, DeferPoint>?`, null in production, set in `AddBlazor(o => …)` by `Ducky.Blazor.Tests`), which the writer invokes at two points: `DeferPoint.BeforeAdd`, after the snapshot read and before the add, and `DeferPoint.AfterAdd`, between the add and the recheck. A terminal committed at `BeforeAdd` is the stranded-key case the mechanism exists for (`AfterReduce` finds nothing to take, and the writer's recheck removes its key and re-signals itself: `Write_TerminalBetweenReadAndDefer_WriterRechecksAndResignals_Deterministic` asserts exactly one write); a terminal committed at `AfterAdd` is taken by `AfterReduce`, and the writer's own `TryRemove` fails (`Write_TerminalAfterDeferAdd_AfterReduceSignalsOnce_Deterministic` asserts exactly one write). So a change to an unscoped slice during a scope switch, or to a scoped key the new scope's storage doesn't hold, is written after the terminal instead of waiting for the next change to that key (`Write_UnscopedChangeDuringScopeSwitch_IsWrittenAfterTerminal`).
- **One baseline per storage key, always a local serialization.** `LastKnownPayload` (a `ConcurrentDictionary` keyed by the full `{prefix}:{scope?}:{sliceKey}`) is updated by successful writes, hydration reads and cross-tab receipts. For a read or a receipt, the baseline is the local re-serialization (declared type) of the value just deserialized, **never** the received text. Serialization is not canonical across processes: `ImmutableHashSet<string>`, `ImmutableDictionary<string,…>`, `HashSet` and `Dictionary` enumerate in string-hash order, and string hashing is seeded randomly per process, so two tabs (or the circuit and WASM stores of one document) emit the same state in different orders; a received-text baseline would make them write to each other forever (`CrossTab_HashOrderedCollection_DifferentProcessOrder_Converges`, at most one write per tab). A CrossTab or Hydration restore of a key sets its baseline **and** signals the key: a pending write dedupes away, and a write that was in flight when the restore landed is followed by a fresh read that writes the current state if it differs. Neither origin triggers a write by itself (`NoWrite_ForHydrationCrossTabDevToolsOrigins`), yet the two tabs converge (`CrossTab_RemoteChangeDuringLocalPendingWrite_TabsConverge`). A DevTools restore sets no baseline; a write that was already pending persists the jumped-to state (dev-only, documented).
- **Failed writes are retried.** A write or removal that fails while the store is not disposed (a `JSDisconnectedException` or `TaskCanceledException` interop timeout on a disconnected circuit, or any storage-provider exception, such as a transient Redis timeout or connection error) keeps the key dirty, leaves the baseline untouched, and is retried with capped backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s) on `TimeProvider` until it succeeds or the store is disposed; a retry of an ordinary write reads `Store.State` afresh, so it writes the current state, while a retry of a queued final snapshot rewrites that snapshot under its own key ("Final write under the old scope", above) (`ServerBrowserStorage_TransientDisconnect_WriteRetriedAfterReconnect`, `DistributedCache_TransientCacheFailure_WriteRetriedUntilSuccess`). **A command ends the backoff sleep.** Posting a command (a switch read or a removal) to a writer that is sleeping in retry backoff, or enqueuing a final old-scope snapshot on it, ends the sleep at once: the loop runs its iteration from the top (pending removal, then the snapshot queue, then the dirty key), so it makes one immediate attempt at its head item and then serves the command. If that attempt fails, the backoff resumes at the step it had reached, so a dead Redis still backs off, and ordinary change signals don't end the sleep (they keep the current backoff). Otherwise a backoff built up during a circuit disconnect (up to 30 s) would hold a reconnect-time switch read far past its attempt's 5 s `HydrationTimeout`, although the write would now succeed: the attempt would end in `HydrationFailed`, and the first user change would overwrite the new scope's stored data (`ScopeSwitch_WriterInRetryBackoff_SwitchReadCompletesWithinHydrationTimeout`: `FakeTimeProvider`, writes fail until the backoff reaches 30 s, the provider heals, a switch is raised, and the old-scope final snapshot is written, the new-scope value restored and `HydrationCompleted` issued before `HydrationTimeout`; `Clear_WriterInRetryBackoff_RemovedImmediately`). A disconnect or interop timeout is logged at Debug only; any other provider failure dispatches `DispatchSystem(new PersistenceFailed(key, type, message), isFailure: true)` once, at the start of the failure streak, and a success resets the streak (`PersistenceFailed_OncePerStreak`). Without the retry, a change that hit a transient cache error would stay unwritten until the next change to that slice or the dispose flush, other circuits would read stale data, and a process restart would lose it, which the Server storage guarantee of §11.6 rules out.
- A **serialization** failure (§10: a NaN, a cyclic instance) dispatches `PersistenceFailed` through the same streak and consumes the signal: the same state would fail again, so it is not retried on a timer, and the next change is written (`Persist_TransientNaN_NextValidValueIsWritten`).
- **`ClearPersistedState`** (in `AfterReduce`) posts a removal command to the writer of each persisted slice. Its storage key is derived from the `ScopeEpoch` of `ActionContext.State` (the snapshot the clear was reduced against); a scoped key whose epoch scope is unknown or null is skipped with a Debug log. The command runs in the key's writer loop, in order with its writes: after any in-flight write has finished, the loop discards the dirty signals raised before the clear (a per-key sequence number: `AfterReduce` stamps each signal, and the clear records the stamp it follows), removes the key (`storageRemove(area, key, id)` or `IDistributedCache.RemoveAsync`), and resets the baseline to "absent", so the next change is written even if it equals the last written content (`Write_AfterClear_SameContent_IsWritten`). Neither a write in flight when the clear was reduced (Redis may reorder concurrent requests) nor a change still in its debounce can bring the key back (`Clear_DuringInFlightWrite_KeyStaysRemoved`, `Clear_WithPendingDebouncedChange_KeyStaysRemoved`). No prefix clear exists; `localStorage.clear()` is never called (`Clear_RemovesOnlyPersistedKeysOfThisScope`). `ClearPersistedState` clears **storage only**: the clearing store keeps its in-memory state (an app that also wants a reset handles the action in its slices, for example `On<LoggedOut>`), while the other tabs and the other store of an Interactive Auto document receive the removal and reset those slices to their initial state (§11.7). INV-18's convergence therefore covers the other stores, not the clearing store's own state, after a clear (documented on `ClearPersistedState`).

**Flush on `DisposeAsync`** (phase 5a: after the drain exited and persistence's own init ended, never waiting for effect runs or for another middleware, INV-29): every loop, deferred keys and queued final snapshots included, is told to skip its remaining debounce **and any pending retry backoff** and to make one immediate attempt per dirty key with the latest state (the hydration skip still applies, so a scoped key under a switch that has not finished is not written: the documented ceiling of §11.6); the flush awaits them, bounded by `DisposeTimeout` (the protected `Middleware.DisposeTimeout`, the bound the store itself waits for this `DisposeAsync`, §6.11); only then are the loops' own CTSs cancelled. Every key still dirty when the flush ends is counted as `ducky.persistence.lost`, whatever its storage. When the drain overran `DisposeTimeout`, this flush runs chained after `DisposeAsync` has completed (§6.11), on the server possibly after the container disposed the cache: a provider exception there, `ObjectDisposedException` included, is caught per key and that key is counted lost like any other failed flush attempt (`Dispose_ChangeInsideDebounce_FlushedImmediately_ServerStorage`; `Dispose_KeyInRetryBackoff_FlushWritesImmediately_ServerStorage`: the cache fails once, the key sleeps in a 25 s backoff, the flush writes it at once; under `FakeTimeProvider` the flush never waits for a debounce or a backoff).
- WASM succeeds when the host is disposed explicitly. A page unload never disposes the root provider, so the WASM guarantee is write-through: the write is issued on the next scheduler turn after the commit, and an unload in between can lose it (documented).
- Server storage succeeds (no JS involved) unless the provider still fails at the flush's one attempt, and then the key is counted lost.
- Server with Local/Session hits `JSDisconnectedException` once the circuit is gone. Each key still dirty is counted as `ducky.persistence.lost` (only at dispose, as for every storage). The test `ServerBrowserStorage_ChangeInsideDebounceThenCircuitClose_IsLostAndCounted` asserts that documented behaviour. The documented upgrade is `PersistStorage.Server`.
