**Providers** are internal and selected by `PersistStorage` (D15). `BrowserStorageProvider` (Local/Session) lazily imports `./_content/Ducky.Blazor/ducky.js` once per store, and caches only a module task that succeeded (the gate probe's handle included, §11.4): a faulted or cancelled import (a `JSDisconnectedException` or interop timeout during circuit init or right after the probe) is dropped, so the next call, the next write retry included, starts a fresh import, and the `watchStorage` registration (§11.7) is re-issued once a later import succeeds; a cached failure would make every later `storageGet`, `storageSet` and `watchStorage` fail even after reconnect and count every change as lost (`ServerBrowserStorage_ImportFailsOnDisconnect_ReimportedAfterReconnect`). Writes pass a string. Every call and every disposal catches `JSDisconnectedException` and `TaskCanceledException`.

### 11.6 Persistence: server-side `IDistributedCache` (PER-04 subset, INV-17)

- `PersistStorage.Server` uses the app's registered `IDistributedCache` (Redis, SQL Server, memory). DUCKY314 if none is registered; like DUCKY315 (and any other Server-storage-only validation), it is not evaluated when `BlazorOptions.IsBrowser` is true, because Server storage is inert there and a WASM client normally registers neither `IDistributedCache` nor an `AuthenticationStateProvider` (`Browser_ServerStorageWithoutCacheOrAuth_NoConfigurationError`, and its server twin `Server_ServerStorageWithoutCache_Ducky314`). DUCKY317 is evaluated on both sides, because the prefix is shared configuration. DUCKY317 if `KeyPrefix` is still the default: a cache is often shared by several apps or environments, and two apps with overlapping user ids and the same slice key would otherwise read and overwrite each other's entries (`DistributedCache_DefaultPrefixWithServerStorage_IsConfigurationError`).
- **In the browser**, Server-storage slices are inert: not persisted, one Info log (EventId 2020), restored only from the prerender seed when the slice is also `Prerender<T>`. This lets the shared Interactive Auto registration (including the generated `[Persist(Storage = Server)]` call) start on the WASM side.
- **Scope**: for Server-storage keys, `BlazorOptions.Scope ?? DuckyScopes.NameIdentifier`. For Local/Session keys, only an explicitly set `BlazorOptions.Scope`: the default never applies to browser keys, so an app that never sets `Scope` keeps unscoped browser keys and anonymous users keep browser persistence (`Scope_DefaultNeverAppliesToBrowserKeys`). Setting `Scope` scopes every browser key as well as the Server keys (the TheBlazorState `KeySuffix` migration relies on it), and a null scope then means no reads and no writes for those browser keys too (documented on `Scope`). ponytail: one scope setting for all keys; a per-slice `PersistOptions.Scoped` switch comes when an app needs mixed scoping. `NameIdentifier` reads `ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier` from the `AuthenticationStateProvider` (DUCKY315 if not registered). The scope must be unique across the identity providers the app accepts (documented). An anonymous user resolves `null`. **A null scope means no reads and no writes** for Server-storage slices in this store (Info log once). There is never a fallback to a shared key. In the browser the delegate receives the renderer-scope `IServiceProvider` handed over through the `InteractivityGate` (§6.10), and hydration of scoped keys waits for that hand-off (spike S-7). That wait, the scope resolution and the reads all run in the asynchronous part of the attempt (§11.5 step 4), under the one `HydrationTimeout` armed in the synchronous prefix (step 3), so the worst case is one `HydrationTimeout`, which DUCKY316 keeps below `InitTimeout`. A browser app with a `Scope` and no Ducky components must render `<DuckyInitializer>` so the hand-off happens; otherwise scoped hydration ends in `HydrationFailed` at the timeout (documented on `BlazorOptions.Scope`).
- Key: `{prefix}:{scope}:{sliceKey}`. Value: the UTF-8 envelope. `MaxAge` sets `AbsoluteExpirationRelativeToNow`. Otherwise `ServerEntryOptions` applies.
- **NonInteractive stores** (static SSR and the prerender pass follow one rule): Server-storage slices are read during init, writes are write-through, and pending writes flush on request-scope dispose. Hydration is asynchronous (§11.5 step 4 awaits `IDistributedCache.GetAsync`), and the static SSR and prerender quiescence wait covers only component lifecycle tasks, so **Server-storage values reach the rendered HTML only when a lifecycle method awaits `InitializeAsync`**: `<DuckyInitializer>`, which awaits `Store.InitializeAsync()` in `OnInitializedAsync` (normative, §11.1), or the component's own `OnInitializedAsync`. A component that only calls `Select` in `OnInitialized` starts init but has no pending lifecycle task, so its HTML shows the initial state, and on a static SSR page that HTML is the only output (`StaticSsr_AsyncDistributedCache_DuckyInitializer_HtmlShowsStoredValue`, `StaticSsr_AsyncDistributedCache_WithoutInitializer_HtmlShowsInitialState_Documented`). The prerender seed carries the hydrated values in any case, because `OnPersisting` waits for idle, which requires `Ready` and therefore the hydration terminal. The static SSR recipe and the docs of `PersistStorage.Server` say so. The real `MemoryDistributedCache` completes `GetAsync` synchronously, so with it the whole attempt restores inline inside `Start()`, before the render, and hides the difference: every NonInteractive Server-storage test runs against both `MemoryDistributedCache` and a wrapper whose `GetAsync` first awaits `Task.Yield()`, as Redis effectively does. The prerender store's flush can land after the circuit store already read or wrote the same key (§23, `DistributedCache_PrerenderFlushAfterCircuitWrite_Documented`). Likewise the request-scope flush of a static SSR form post runs after the redirect response was sent, so the browser's immediate GET builds a new store that can read the value from before the post (§23, `DistributedCache_StaticSsrPostRedirect_StaleReadDocumented`).
- Default debounce 0: write-through with channel coalescing, and a failed write is retried until it succeeds (§11.5). It closes the Server loss window, except for a process crash during an in-flight write or while a retry is pending.
- **One store, not one user.** INV-16 holds per store. Several circuits of one user (one per tab) and static-SSR form-post stores all write `{prefix}:{user}:{slice}`, there is no cross-circuit sync (D17), a circuit reads only at init, and `IDistributedCache` has no compare-and-swap: concurrent stores of one scope are last-writer-wins on whole slices (§23, `DistributedCache_TwoCircuitsSameUser_LastWriterWins`).
- **Scope switch** (every host, whenever a scope is in use: readable Server storage, or a `Scope` segment in browser-storage keys). **In the browser**, inert Server-storage keys are never "scoped keys" for reading: they don't put a scope in use unless an `AuthenticationStateProvider` is resolvable (the hand-off callback resolves it with `GetService`, never `GetRequiredService`, because it runs synchronously inside a component's `Select`, and the Interactive Auto WASM side deliberately registers none, which is why DUCKY315 is skipped there), and they never join a switch's key set or a switch read (there is no `IDistributedCache` to read). A WASM store whose only scoped slices are Server storage and which has no provider therefore makes no subscription and never throws (`Wasm_ServerStorageOnly_NoAuthProvider_NoSubscriptionNoThrow`). When a switch does run in the browser (a browser `Scope`, or a provider is present), its reset restore (step 2) still resets inert Server-storage slices to their initial state, so a prerender-seeded value of the previous user is not kept (`Wasm_UserChange_InertServerSlicesResetNotRead`). The middleware subscribes to `AuthenticationStateChanged` on the renderer-scope `AuthenticationStateProvider` in the browser (handed over by the gate; `Wasm_UserChange_ScopedBrowserKeysMoveToNewScope`) and on the store scope's instance on the server, **before** the epoch-0 scope resolution starts: on the server in the synchronous prefix, in the browser inside the renderer hand-off callback, before scoped hydration resumes. A sign-out or user change that fires between the provider returning the old state and the subscription would otherwise be missed, and the store would write scoped keys under the previous user's key for the rest of the circuit or app; subscribed first, such a change becomes a switch that supersedes attempt 0 (`ScopeSwitch_AuthChangeDuringInitialScopeResolution_NeverWritesUnderOldScope`, deterministic with a TCS-gated provider). It unsubscribes in `DisposeAsync` (phase 5a, §6.11). **Re-assertion check.** Every Blazor Server circuit reconnect raises `AuthenticationStateChanged` for the same user (`ComponentHub.ConnectCircuit` calls `CircuitHost.SetCircuitUser(Context.User)`, which calls `SetAuthenticationState(Task.FromResult(…))`, and `ServerAuthenticationStateProvider` notifies; spike S-7 confirms it for .NET 10 reconnect and resume), so the handler first checks, before any await: outside `_issue`, it calls the scope delegate once (store-lifetime token, inside a try/catch). If the returned `ValueTask<string?>` has already completed successfully (the normal case after `SetAuthenticationState(Task.FromResult(…))` with `DuckyScopes.NameIdentifier`), it takes `_issue` and looks at the current epoch (`_scopeVersion`): if that epoch's recorded scope is known and ordinally equal to the result, and that epoch's attempt has already issued its terminal, the event is a **re-assertion**: no epoch bump, no reset, no reads, a Debug log (`ScopeSwitch_CircuitReconnectSameUser_NoResetNoReRead`, E2E `Circuit_ReconnectWithScopedSlices_NoResetFlicker`). In every other case (a different scope, sign-out to null, an unknown scope, an attempt still in flight, a delegate that completes asynchronously, whose incomplete task is observed and discarded, or throws) it runs the switch below unchanged, reusing nothing from the check. Without the check every reconnect of every app with Server storage (NameIdentifier-scoped by default) or a browser `Scope` would reset every scoped slice (a flicker), drop any change made to one during the re-read, and, if the re-read hit `HydrationTimeout`, leave the slices at their initial state for the next change to overwrite the stored data. The switch, **synchronously, before any await, under `_issue`** (§11.5):
  1. allocates `epoch = ++_scopeVersion` from the counter, never from a snapshot, so two switches in quick succession, or a switch while an earlier reset is still queued behind another thread's drain, always get distinct epochs, and one epoch never maps to two scopes (`ScopeSwitch_TwoOverlappingSwitches_DistinctEpochs_LatestWins`). It makes a new attempt for `epoch` current, with its key set: every scoped key, plus the unscoped keys of the attempt it supersedes when that attempt has not restored yet (carried over transitively, so a switch during the initial attempt still hydrates unscoped slices, such as unscoped Local slices next to NameIdentifier-scoped Server slices, and the writers keep skipping them until they are read: `ScopeSwitch_DuringInitialHydration_UnscopedKeysStillRestored`). The cross-tab sequence and pause-seed rules of §11.5 step 6 apply to the carried keys. It arms a per-attempt `HydrationTimeout` for the new attempt iff `_initPhase` is clear, read here under `_issue`; while it is set it arms none, and the init-phase deadline armed in the synchronous prefix, which acts on whichever attempt is current when it fires, bounds it (§11.5 step 7, "Before `StoreInitialized`"). That supersedes any attempt still in flight, the initial one or a previous switch's: from now on its results, its timeout and its terminal are discarded when they reach `_issue`, so a slow read of the previous user can never be restored into the new user's store or written under the new user's key (`DistributedCache_StaleAttemptAfterSecondSwitch_NeverRestoredOrWrittenUnderNewScope`, `DistributedCache_UserChangeDuringInitialHydration_OldScopeValuesNeverRestored`, both CsCheck `SampleParallel` with a TCS-gated cache, in `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`, §17.3 row 6, with `…_Deterministic` twins in `Ducky.Blazor.Tests`);
  2. `Store.Restore(…, Hydration)` in **one** action: the initial state (`Store.InitialState`) of every scoped slice (inert Server-storage slices included in the browser) plus `PersistenceState(Hydrating) { ScopeEpoch = epoch }`. The new epoch has no scope yet.

  The old scope's last state is flushed from the drainer, not from this handler: when the reset commits, `AfterReduce` hands its `PreviousState` to the scoped writers as their final old-scope snapshot (§11.5, "Final write under the old scope"). A snapshot captured here could be older than commits still queued ahead of the reset and would overwrite them. Writers derive both the value and the key from one snapshot (§11.5), and they skip the attempt's key set while Status is `Hydrating`, and scoped keys while the epoch's scope is unknown; other unscoped keys keep being written. So every write before the reset carries old state under the old key, the old key's last write is the last old-epoch state, nothing reduced after it is written under the old key, and nothing is written under the new key before the new key's value has been read (`DistributedCache_UserChangesInsideCircuit_ActionDuringSwitch_NeverWrittenUnderOtherScope`, `DistributedCache_UserChangeWithConcurrentLocalChange_NeverWritesOldStateUnderNewKey`). Then, asynchronously: resolve the new scope; under `_issue`, if the attempt is no longer current, stop (the newer switch owns the terminal); otherwise record the scope, possibly null, for `epoch`, then drain `_deferred` (§11.5, "A skipped key stays dirty"), so a scoped key deferred while the scope was unknown is written even when the attempt's own terminal came first (its timeout). **Every switch ends in exactly one terminal:** a null scope (logout to anonymous) drops only the scoped keys from the attempt's key set. If no carried unscoped key remains, the attempt claims and issues `HydrationCompleted(false) { ScopeEpoch = epoch }` at once, Status returns to `Hydrated`, scoped writes stay skipped by the null-scope rule and unscoped writes were never blocked (`ScopeSwitch_ToAnonymous_StatusReturnsToHydrated_UnscopedWritesResume`). If it still carries unscoped keys of the attempt it superseded (a sign-out during the initial hydration, with NameIdentifier-scoped Server slices next to unscoped Local slices), it reads those keys through their writer loops and ends through steps 4–7 of §11.5, so their writers, which deferred them while `Hydrating`, get a baseline and never write in-memory state over the user's stored data (`ScopeSwitch_ToAnonymousDuringInitialHydration_UnscopedKeysStillRestored` asserts that the unscoped values are restored and that nothing is written before the terminal). Otherwise the attempt reads its key set, each key through its writer loop (§11.5, "Switch reads"), and ends through steps 4–7 of §11.5, so apps can load for the new user. The handler catches every exception (Error log, and `HydrationFailed` for the current attempt through the same claim); it never escapes to the circuit. Documented ceilings: a change made to a scoped slice during the switch is not persisted and is replaced by the new user's stored value; the reset is a `Hydration` restore, so `WasRestored` is true for the scoped slices afterwards, and a load for the new user reacts to the new attempt's `HydrationCompleted`, not to `WasRestored`.
- Tests use the real `MemoryDistributedCache`: `DistributedCache_TwoScopes_NeverSeeEachOther`, `DistributedCache_NullScope_NoIo`, `DistributedCache_AnonymousUser_NoIo`, `DistributedCache_DisposeFlushesPending`, plus the tests named above.

### 11.7 Cross-tab (XT-01, INV-18)

- `CrossTabSync` is an internal component owned by `PersistenceMiddleware`, not a registered middleware: its restores and sequence bumps are issued under persistence's private `_issue` lock, atomically with hydration decisions (§11.5), which a separate middleware could not share.
- Every store generates one id at construction, `Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N")`, and uses it for every JS registration (`watchStorage`, `storageSet`, `storageRemove`, `devtoolsConnect`). The module-level `Map` in `ducky.js` is shared by every store of the document (in Interactive Auto the circuit store and the WASM store load the same module instance), so a per-store counter or a fixed name would collide (`CrossTab_CircuitAndWasmStoresInSamePage_IndependentListeners`).
- **Registration.** Every store that persists any Local or Session slice registers **once**, before its hydration read (§11.5), with `ducky.js watchStorage(id, dotnetRef, prefix, crossTab, pruneAfterMs)` (idempotent per id), and calls `unwatchStorage(id)` on dispose. Every registered id receives same-document relays. `crossTab` is true only when the store has a `Local` slice with `SyncAcrossTabs`; only then does the module also attach the window `storage` listener for that id.
- The `storage` listener (crossTab ids only) filters `e.storageArea === localStorage && (e.key === null || e.key.startsWith(prefix + ":"))` and forwards `OnStorageChanged("tab", "local", key, value, tooLarge)`:
  - `e.newValue === null` (a per-key removal, such as `ClearPersistedState` in another tab) → `(key, null, false)`;
  - a value within `InlinePayloadBytes`, measured as `new TextEncoder().encode(JSON.stringify(v)).length` → `(key, value, false)`;
  - otherwise → `(key, null, true)`, and .NET re-reads that key through the stream path.
- **Same document.** `storage` events never fire in the document that wrote, so `storageSet(area, key, value, id)` and `storageRemove(area, key, id)` notify every other id registered with the same prefix, skipping the writer's id, as `OnStorageChanged("doc", area, key, value, tooLarge)` (a removal passes `null`). This covers Local and Session slices, synced or not: in Interactive Auto the circuit store and the WASM store share one localStorage and one sessionStorage, and without the relay they would overwrite each other's keys (`Auto_ServerAndWasmStoresInOneDocument_Converge`, `Auto_SessionAndUnsyncedLocalSlices_ConvergeInOneDocument`, `Auto_ClearInCircuitStore_ResetsWasmStore`).
- **Delivery failures.** Every notification is an `invokeMethodAsync`, which rejects while a Blazor Server circuit is disconnected. On a rejection, the module adds that notification's `(source, area)` pair to the id's `needsResync` set and retries with backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s); on the first success it delivers one `(source, area, null, null, false)` notification **per pair in the set**, each taking the existing re-read-all path for that source and area, in place of the events lost meanwhile, then clears the set. One flag per id would name a single source and area, and a circuit store registered for same-document relays can lose Local and Session events, from both `"tab"` and `"doc"`, in one disconnect (`CrossTab_EventDuringCircuitDisconnect_ConvergesAfterReconnect` loses one Local and one Session relay event; `DuckyJs_ResyncBackoff_CapsAt30s_ThenReReadsAll` drives the backoff to its cap on Playwright's clock, §17.2). The module prunes an id itself (its listener, relay registration and DevTools connection) on either of two conditions: its deliveries have failed **continuously for longer than `pruneAfterMs`**, the bound that does not depend on error text (a fixed 10 minutes, `CrossTabSync.DeliveryPruneAfter`, beyond the default 3-minute `DisconnectedCircuitRetentionPeriod`, passed by .NET as the last argument of `watchStorage` and `devtoolsConnect`, so the module holds no magic number); or, as a fast path, a rejection says the .NET object is no longer tracked. An evicted circuit, and a resumed circuit whose old `DotNetObject` still points at the old circuit's dispatcher, reject with a connection error rather than the untracked-object error, so without the cap their ids would retry every 30 s forever and accumulate across resume cycles, while the .NET side's `unwatchStorage`/`devtoolsDisconnect` already failed with `JSDisconnectedException` (`CrossTab_CircuitResumed_OldListenerRemoved` and `DuckyJs_ResyncBackoff_CapsAt30s_ThenReReadsAll` assert pruning at the cap on Playwright's clock). The cap is a guess about `DisconnectedCircuitRetentionPeriod`, which apps configure (mobile-oriented apps often set 15 to 30 minutes), so a live circuit can be pruned and then reconnect, with no .NET reconnect signal and `watchStorage` issued once. The prune is therefore detected and repaired rather than avoided: `storageSet` and `storageRemove` return `false` when their `id` is not registered, and on `false` .NET re-issues `watchStorage(id, …)` (idempotent) and then runs the re-read-all path (`key == null`, below) for each registered area, as after a resync (`CrossTab_StorageSetReportsUnknownId_ReWatchesAndReReads` with a fake runtime; harness spec `DuckyJs_PrunedIdReRegisteredOnNextWrite`). ponytail: a store that only receives, never writes, stays unregistered after such a reconnect until its next write or removal; upgrade path: a periodic `isWatched(id)` probe. DevTools, dev-only, goes inert with a Debug log when its connection was pruned (`devtoolsSend` returns `false` for an unknown id).
- .NET `CrossTabSync`:
  - accepts a key only if it exactly equals the current `{prefix}:{scope?}:{sliceKey}` of one of this store's slices: for `source == "tab"`, a synced (Local + `SyncAcrossTabs`) slice; for `source == "doc"`, any browser-persisted slice whose area matches. Every other key is ignored with a Debug log (`CrossTab_OtherScopeKey_Ignored`). Scoped keys are matched against the scope recorded for the **current** epoch (`_scopeVersion`), read under `_issue`: the switch handler bumps the epoch synchronously (§11.6), so from that moment until the new scope is recorded no scoped key is accepted, and with a null scope none ever is. Another tab still signed in as the previous user keeps writing `{prefix}:{old}:{slice}`, and those events are ignored. The check is made twice, on receipt and again under `_issue` right before the restore is issued (a value accepted before the switch and still in its debounce is dropped), so an old-scope value can never be restored after the reset nor win over the new scope's hydration through the sequence-number rule (`CrossTab_OldScopeEventDuringSwitch_Ignored`);
  - debounces per key for 50 ms on `TimeProvider`;
  - `(key, null, false)` → under `_issue`, first bumps the key's sequence number, exactly as the value branch does, so a hydration read of that key that began before the removal is dropped at §11.5 step 6 and can't restore the value read before it (`CrossTab_RemovalDuringHydration_RemovalWins`: tab B clears while tab A's read is in flight, and the 50 ms-debounced removal lands first). Then, **if the key's baseline is already "absent"**, it restores nothing (Debug log): absence is treated like any other payload by the echo guard, so a removal can't discard a local change that is still waiting for its first successful write. Otherwise it restores that slice's initial state (`Store.InitialState`) with `Origin.CrossTab`, sets the key's baseline to the local serialization of that initial state (an absent key reads as the initial state), then signals the writer like any receipt: a pending or in-flight local write of the now-initial state dedupes away instead of recreating the key the other store just removed (`CrossTab_ClearPersistedStateInOtherTab_ResetsSlice`, `CrossTab_RemovalReceipt_NoWriteBack`);
  - otherwise unwraps the value with the same `EnvelopeReader` as hydration (version, migration, `MaxAge`; a newer version is not found, D18: `CrossTab_NewerVersionEnvelope_Ignored`; a stream re-read above `MaxPayloadBytes` is not found with Warning 2023, §11.5, and never throws out of the `[JSInvokable]` handler). A payload that fails to deserialize is ignored with a Warning (§10, `CrossTab_CtorThrowingPayload_Ignored`). A too-large re-read whose `storageGetStream` returns an empty `Uint8Array` (the key was removed between the notification and the pull; .NET sees `IJSStreamReference.Length == 0`) takes the removal branch above (`CrossTab_TooLargeKeyRemovedBeforePull_ResetsSlice` with a fake stream reference, and its real-runtime E2E twin `CrossTab_TooLargeKeyRemovedBeforePull_RealRuntime_ResetsSlice`, so a runtime that rejects the export's return value can't pass unnoticed); the stream reference is read under `await using`. It drops the value if the local re-serialization of its deserialized `s` equals `LastKnownPayload[key]` (echo and convergence guard, §11.5). Otherwise, under `_issue` (§11.5), it bumps the key's sequence number, calls `Restore(…, origin)`, sets the baseline to that local serialization and signals the writer. The origin is `Origin.Hydration` when the key belongs to the key set of an attempt that has no terminal yet (decided under `_issue`), `Origin.CrossTab` otherwise: the value does come from storage, and step 6 then drops the attempt's own read of that key because its sequence number moved, so with `CrossTab` `WasRestored(K)` would stay false although K holds the stored value, and the load effect on `StoreInitialized` would load again and write and relay its result over the other tab's newer value. Both origins have the same write semantics (no write, baseline set, writer signalled). Removal receipts are unchanged: the slice stays unrestored and its load runs, which is right when storage is empty (`CrossTab_EventDuringHydration_NewestWins` asserts `WasRestored<T>()` and that the load effect does not run);
  - `key == null` (another script called `clear()`, or a resync after failed delivery) re-reads every accepted key of that source; a present value takes the value branch, and a missing value takes the removal branch (sequence bump, then no restore when the baseline is already absent), so a resync after a disconnect never resets a key whose first write is still being retried (`CrossTab_ResyncAfterDisconnect_UnwrittenLocalChangeOnOtherKey_Kept`).
- Persistence never triggers a write for the `CrossTab` origin, and the shared `s`-payload baseline stops reactive ping-pong: `CrossTab_SubscriberThatRedispatches_ConvergesWithinNWrites` asserts at most 2 writes per tab.

### 11.8 Redux DevTools (DT-01, DT-02, INV-28)

- `UseDevTools()` registers `DevToolsMiddleware`. `InitializeAsync` reads the gate in its synchronous prefix and never awaits a component registration: in the browser the gate is always `Interactive`; elsewhere, if it is still `Unknown` (a store first touched by non-component code), it runs the same one-time synchronous probe as persistence (§11.4). Awaiting the registration signal instead would hold init until `InitTimeout` on such a store (Error `StoreInitAborted`, a pending hydration turned into `HydrationFailed`, every user action buffered for 10 s). When the gate is `Interactive`, it calls `ducky.js devtoolsConnect(id, dotnetRef, configJson, pruneAfterMs)` (the prune cap of §11.7) with `{name, maxAge, trace:false}`, where `id` is the store id (§11.7) and the config is a pre-serialized string. If the extension is absent, it gets `false` back and goes inert. The connect is bounded by `HydrationTimeout` (below `InitTimeout`, DUCKY316); on timeout DevTools logs a Warning and goes inert, so it can never trip the init timeout (`DevTools_GateUnknown_ProbesAndDoesNotDelayInit`). It then sends `@@INIT` with the sanitized state.
- **AfterReduce**:
  1. apply `Filter`;
  2. build the FSA `{type: ActionType, payload}`. Library actions (`StoreInitialized`, `ReducerFailed`, `EffectFailed`, `HydrationCompleted`, `HydrationFailed`, `PersistenceFailed`, `ClearPersistedState`) are projected through `BlazorWireContext` first: the failures become `{type, message}` plus `sliceKey` or `effectType` (the exception's type name and message; the exception itself is never serialized, §10), so a failure shows its cause in the extension (`DevTools_FailureAction_ShowsTypeAndMessage`). Any other payload is `Store.Json.ToNode(action, action.GetType())`. Both kinds then go through `ActionSanitizer`; when `ToNode` returns null the payload is `null` + `meta.unserializable = true` (Debug log once per type);
  3. build the state per slice with `ToNode(state, slice.StateType)` → `StateSanitizer`, taking each key's declared `StateType` from `Store.Slices` (never `value.GetType()`, which would drop the `$type` discriminator of a polymorphic state and make `IMPORT_STATE` deserialize into the wrong type; `DevTools_PolymorphicState_SerializedAndImportedViaDeclaredType`), for `@@INIT`, every send and `IMPORT_STATE`; library slices (`@ducky/*`, such as `@ducky/persistence`'s `PersistenceState`, which no user context knows) are serialized through `BlazorWireContext` instead (it declares `[JsonSerializable(typeof(PersistenceState))]`) and then go through `StateSanitizer` too, so DevTools shows the hydration status loading UIs depend on (`DevTools_SanitizerAppliedToInitAndEverySend` asserts that `@ducky/persistence` is non-null in `@@INIT` and in every send); they stay excluded from JUMP, RESET and IMPORT;
  4. when `Trace` is on, attach `stack` from frames captured synchronously right there (§10);
  5. send two pre-serialized strings and increment the DevTools-local counter.

  Sanitizers run on `@@INIT` and on every send.
- **History** is an immutable array of `(devToolsIndex, StateSnapshot)` entries capped at `MaxAge` (`ImmutableArray`, oldest dropped when full): typed, with no JSON round-trip. Only the drainer replaces it, in `AfterReduce`, publishing the new array with `Volatile.Write`; `OnMessage`, which runs on the circuit's sync context while the drainer may be a thread-pool thread on Blazor Server (§7 rule 6), reads the reference once with `Volatile.Read` and looks the index up in that one array, so a jump racing a send never reads a slot being overwritten and always restores the entry it names. The time-travel flag that `OnMessage` sets and `MayDispatch` reads on the drainer is a `volatile` field (`DevTools_JumpConcurrentWithSend_RestoresMatchingEntry` in `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`, and its `…_Deterministic` twin in `Ducky.Blazor.Tests`, where the fake JS runtime delivers the jump re-entrantly from inside `devtoolsSend`). `devToolsIndex` is a DevTools-local counter that mirrors the extension's lifted-state index: 0 for `@@INIT`, then +1 per actual `devtoolsSend`. It is never `ActionContext.Id`, which skips filtered, vetoed and dropped actions differently (`DevTools_JumpAfterFilteredActions_RestoresMatchingEntry`, with a fake runtime that emits extension-style indices).
- **Inbound** messages arrive through `[JSInvokable] OnMessage(string? json, long seq, bool tooLarge)` and are parsed by Ducky.Blazor's internal `BlazorWireContext`. Two rules keep them within the Blazor Server hub limit:
  - `ducky.js` forwards `JUMP_TO_STATE`/`JUMP_TO_ACTION` as a minimal `{type, payload: {type, actionId|index}}` and drops the extension's `message.state`: history is typed on the .NET side (INV-28), so a jump never needs it and is always small, whatever the app's state size (`DevTools_JumpMessage_StateStrippedInline`);
  - any other message above `InlinePayloadBytes` UTF-8 bytes (measured in `ducky.js`; `IMPORT_STATE`, which carries a whole lifted state, in practice) uses the storage pull pattern: `ducky.js` keeps the text under `(id, seq)` and calls `OnMessage(null, seq, tooLarge: true)`; .NET then calls `devtoolsTakeMessage(id, seq)` through `InvokeAsync<IJSStreamReference>` and reads it under `await using` with `maxAllowedSize: MaxPayloadBytes`; a stream reference with `Length == 0` (the message was already taken or pruned: the export returns an empty `Uint8Array`, never `null`) is ignored with a Debug log. Above that limit the message is ignored with Warning 2023 (`DevTools_LargeImport_ReadViaStream`; the `ducky.js` harness spec `DuckyJs_DevToolsLargeMessage_PullBranch` covers the module side). A small message arrives inline as `OnMessage(json, seq, false)`.
  - `JUMP_TO_STATE`/`JUMP_TO_ACTION(index)` → `Restore(snapshot slots except @ducky/*, Origin.DevTools)`. When the index is not the latest, time travel engages: `MayDispatch` vetoes `Local` and `Effect` origins (late effect results included; dev-only, documented). Library origins are never offered to `MayDispatch` (§6.4), so nothing else is needed. Jumping back to the latest releases it. An evicted index is ignored with a log.
  - `RESET` → `Restore(Store.InitialState slots except @ducky/*, Origin.DevTools)` (`DevTools_Reset_RestoresInitialStates`).
  - `IMPORT_STATE` → deserialize per slice through `Store.Json` with the declared `StateType` from `Store.Slices`, skipping slices without type info or whose payload fails to deserialize (Warning, §10).
  - `COMMIT`, `ROLLBACK`, `SWEEP`, `TOGGLE_ACTION` → ignored with a Debug log.
- Tests run against a real store with a fake JS runtime that records the exact strings crossing the boundary: `DevTools_JumpToRecordState_RestoresReferenceEqualSlices` (the 1.x record-state regression), `DevTools_SanitizerAppliedToInitAndEverySend`, `DevTools_DuringTimeTravel_UserActionVetoed`, `DevTools_OptionsReachExtension`, `DevTools_FilterExcludesAction`, `DevTools_JumpAfterFilteredActions_RestoresMatchingEntry`, `DevTools_Reset_RestoresInitialStates`.

### 11.9 `ducky.js` (one ES module, lazy, plain JS, no build step)

Exports (every argument and return value is a string, number or boolean, except the `DotNetObjectReference`):

`storageGet(area, key, maxInlineBytes) → string|null` (returns the sentinel `"\u0000ducky:too-large"`, which can't be an envelope, when the value exceeds the budget), `storageGetStream(area, key) → Uint8Array` (for the stream path; an empty `Uint8Array` when nothing is stored under the key any more), `storageSet(area, key, value, id) → bool` and `storageRemove(area, key, id) → bool` (both also notify the document's other registrations, §11.7, and return `false` when `id` is not registered, so .NET repairs a pruned registration), `watchStorage(id, ref, prefix, crossTab, pruneAfterMs)`, `unwatchStorage(id)`, `devtoolsConnect(id, ref, configJson, pruneAfterMs) → bool`, `devtoolsInit(id, stateJson)`, `devtoolsSend(id, actionJson, stateJson) → bool` (`false` for an unknown id), `devtoolsTakeMessage(id, seq) → Uint8Array` (the pull path for an inbound DevTools message above the inline budget, §11.8; the kept text is deleted once taken, and an empty `Uint8Array` is returned when it was already taken or pruned), `devtoolsDisconnect(id)`. There is no prefix-clear export. The pull exports never return `null`: for an `InvokeAsync<IJSStreamReference>` call Blazor wraps the result with `createJSStreamReference`, which throws a `TypeError` on `null` (review round 6 traced it in the shipped `blazor.webassembly.js` 10.0.6; only `JSObjectReference` maps `null`, to id -1), so .NET would get a `JSException` instead of "not found". An envelope and a DevTools message are never empty, so .NET treats an `IJSStreamReference` with `Length == 0` from either export as not found: a storage read or cross-tab re-read restores as for a removal (§11.5, §11.7), and the DevTools message is ignored with a Debug log. Every `IJSStreamReference` is read under `await using`, so it is disposed after `OpenReadStreamAsync` (the over-`MaxPayloadBytes` path included) and never leaves its `Uint8Array` pinned in the circuit's JS object table. The harness spec `DuckyJs_PullExports_ReturnEmptyWhenGone` covers both empty returns, and the real-runtime E2E `CrossTab_TooLargeKeyRemovedBeforePull_RealRuntime_ResetsSlice` checks that the real Blazor runtime turns one into a zero-length reference, which a fake `IJSRuntime` can't. The module calls one .NET method per kind of registration: `[JSInvokable] OnStorageChanged(string source, string area, string? key, string? value, bool tooLarge)` for storage registrations, and `[JSInvokable] OnMessage(string? json, long seq, bool tooLarge)` for DevTools connections.

Every size decision (storageGet, the storage listener, DevTools inbound) measures UTF-8 bytes of the JSON-encoded value, `new TextEncoder().encode(JSON.stringify(v)).length`, which is what crosses the Blazor Server hub; string `.length` counts UTF-16 code units and would let a 16 Ki-character CJK value reach about 48 KB. bUnit and Playwright cover a 12 Ki-character CJK value (`CrossTab_NonAsciiValueUnderCharLimitOverByteLimit_UsesStream`).

Listeners are kept per id in a module-level `Map`. Every module timer (the delivery-failure backoff, §11.7) goes through the page's `setTimeout`, so the harness specs drive it with Playwright's clock (§17.2). Every .NET-side disposal of the module, of a `DotNetObjectReference` or of an `IJSStreamReference` catches `JSDisconnectedException`, and the module prunes an id itself after `pruneAfterMs` of continuous delivery failure, or at once when an invocation reports an untracked object; a pruned id that is still alive re-registers on its next write (§11.7). The whole module is held to **100% block coverage** (§17.6).

---

## 12. SliceStore facade (API-01, INV-30, ADR-0010)

```csharp
public sealed class CartStore : SliceStore<CartState>            // key "cart"
{
    protected override CartState Initial => new([]);
    public CartStore() => On<LoggedOut>(_ => Initial);           // it is still a slice: it can react to actions
    public void Add(Item i)    => Set(s => s with { Items = s.Items.Add(i) });            // DevTools: "cart/Add"
    public void Remove(int id) => Set(s => s.Produce(d => d.Items.RemoveAll(x => x.Id == id)));   // with Ducky.Draft
    public Task<DispatchResult> ClearAsync() => SetAsync(_ => Initial);
    public int Count => State.Items.Count;
}
// d.AddSlice<CartStore>();
// @inherits DuckyComponent   @inject CartStore Cart
// OnInitialized: count = Select((CartState s) => s.Items.Count);   markup: @count   <button @onclick="() => Cart.Add(item)">
```

- `SliceStore<TState>`'s constructor registers `On<SetState<TState>>((s, a) => a.Mutator(s))`. `SetState<TState>(string Name, Func<TState,TState> Mutator)` is internal.
- `Set` dispatches `SetState` through the **full** pipeline: init buffer, depth, middleware, persistence, DevTools (`{key}/{name}`), effects. The mutator runs **at reduce time, on the drainer, against the latest state**, not at call time. This fixes Bustand's lost updates (`SliceStore_ConcurrentSetFromNThreads_AppliesAll`).
- **Origin and run scope.** `Set`/`SetAsync` read the owning store's `_effectRun` (§6.5, §6.6), which only that store's `RunAsync` sets, for its own run, and `Process` clears for every action it processes (§6.4 step 2), so a subscriber, `onChange` or middleware hook that calls `Set` during a drain owned by an effect continuation is `Local`, never tagged with that unrelated run (`SliceStore_SetFromSubscriberDuringDrainOwnedByEffectContinuation_IsLocal`). Outside an effect run the dispatch is `Origin.Local`. Inside one, it is `Origin.Effect` with `Pending.Run` set to that run's token, so the call-time and process-time run checks (§6.4 step 3, D8) apply exactly as for `EffectContext.Dispatch`: a superseded Switch run that ignores its token and calls `cart.Set(...)` after its `await` is dropped, logged at Debug and counted (`ducky.dispatch.dropped`, reason `run`, INV-02) (`Switch_SupersededRunCallingSliceStoreSet_IsDropped`); once the store's disposal has begun, `Set`/`SetAsync` from any run complete `Disposed` instead, uncounted, because the disposal check comes first (§6.6). This is what makes the recommended pattern below (an effect that injects the SliceStore) as safe as `ctx.Dispatch`.
- A mutator that returns the same reference commits nothing and notifies nobody (`SliceStore_SameReference_NoCommit`).
- `SetAsync` returns the `DispatchResult` of that `SetState`.
- `State` is a **point-in-time read** of `store.State.Get<TState>()`. It starts init (§6.7), so the seed and storage are applied to the store whoever touched it first, and it is fresh when the caller became the drainer (`await SetAsync(...)` gives read-your-writes otherwise). It is not a subscription: markup that must follow changes (hydration and cross-tab restores, which commit after the first render, effect-driven `Set` calls, changes made by other components) reads through one: inherit `DuckyComponent` and `Select((CartState s) => s.Items.Count)` in `OnInitialized`, or use `<DuckySelect>`, or `StoreSelectionExtensions.Select` for a foreign base class, and keep calling the SliceStore's methods for mutations (`SliceStore_RenderedThroughSelect_RerendersOnStorageRestore`). A plain component that renders `Cart.Count` re-renders only after its own event handlers, so it would keep showing a stale value; this is Bustand's `UseState` mapping (§22.2).
- The store attaches itself to its slice instances when it is built (internal). `State`/`Set` before that throws DUCKY351.
- DI: `AddSlice<CartStore>()` registers `CartStore` resolving to the store-owned instance (`SliceStore_InjectedInstance_IsStoreOwnedSlice`).
- SliceStores have parameterless constructors like every slice, so they can't inject services. Work that needs a service (I/O, an API client, what Bustand did in `InitializeAsync`) lives in an `Effect`: an `Effect<StoreInitialized>` for the initial load, or an effect on an action, which injects the service **and the SliceStore** (allowed, §6.6) and calls its methods. Effects also give concurrency policies (Switch, Exhaust), cancellation and supersession, which SliceStore methods don't have; a SliceStore method never awaits work. Such an effect is tested with `EffectTest.Run<TEffect, TAction>(action, configure, arrange, services)`, which lets the store build the effect and inject its store-owned SliceStore, and `TestStore.GetSlice<CartStore>()` reads or drives that instance (§15, `EffectTest_EffectInjectingSliceStore_Works`).
- In DevTools, jumping to a `SetState` entry works; replaying one does not (a closure can't be serialized; the payload is `null` with `meta.unserializable`).
- DUCKY001–003 analyse `Set`/`SetAsync` mutator lambdas like `On<T>` lambdas.


---

## 13. Ducky.Draft: Immer-style `Produce` (IMM-02, absorbs Mutty, INV-26, ADR-0019)

### 13.1 Why a rewrite

Mutty's `Build()` always returns `_record with {...}`, a new instance, so every reducer built on it notifies (matrix conflict 4). It also eagerly copies collections, slices display strings for type mapping (the source of its broken shapes), and injects its attribute through post-init (the CS0436 family). Ducky.Draft keeps Mutty's engineering (one `ForAttributeWithMetadataName` pipeline, value-equatable models, `EquatableArray<T>`, `WithTrackingName` with caching tests, Verify snapshots, analyzer release tracking) and rewrites the codegen around lazy copy-on-write with identity preservation.

### 13.2 Public API (namespace `Ducky.Draft`)

```csharp
[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class, Inherited = false)]
public sealed class DraftableAttribute : Attribute;

public interface IRevocable { void Revoke(); }             // implemented explicitly everywhere, so it stays out of IntelliSense

public interface IDraft<out T> : IRevocable where T : class
{
    bool IsDirty { get; }
    T Build();                                               // returns the original instance when !IsDirty
}

// Runtime collection drafts: lazy (read their source until the first mutation), copy-on-write. Each draft remembers its own
// source (the collection it was created from, by the generated getter or by an implicit conversion on assignment) and its
// Build* methods return that source itself when clean and of the requested kind, else a new instance of that kind.
// The Build* name only selects the return type. Setting an equal value is not a change.
// Every collection draft implements IRevocable explicitly; revoking it revokes its cached element drafts.
public sealed class ListDraft<T> : IList<T>, IReadOnlyList<T>, IRevocable          // ImmutableList<T> / ImmutableArray<T> of non-draftable T
{
    public bool IsDirty { get; }
    public void AddRange(IEnumerable<T> items); public int RemoveAll(Predicate<T> match); public void Sort(Comparison<T> comparison);
    public ImmutableList<T> BuildList();
    public ImmutableArray<T> BuildArray();                  // a default (uninitialized) source reads as empty and is returned as-is when clean
    public static implicit operator ListDraft<T>(ImmutableList<T> list);
    public static implicit operator ListDraft<T>(ImmutableArray<T> array);
}
public sealed class ListDraft<T, TDraft> : IReadOnlyList<TDraft>, IRevocable       // elements are [Draftable] records
    where T : class where TDraft : class, IDraft<T>
{
    public ListDraft(ImmutableList<T> source, Func<T, TDraft> createDraft);          // generated code passes static x => new global::N.Todo.Draft(x)
    public ListDraft(ImmutableArray<T> source, Func<T, TDraft> createDraft);         // also how a caller replaces the whole list
    public bool IsDirty { get; }
    public TDraft this[int index] { get; }                             // lazy element draft; never calls createDraft on a null element (possible
                                                                       // only for an oblivious T, GEN-06): returns null for it (annotation unchanged),
                                                                       // and the element stays null at Build
    public void Add(T item); public void Insert(int index, T item); public void Set(int index, T item);
    public void RemoveAt(int index); public int RemoveAll(Predicate<TDraft> match); public void Clear();
    public ImmutableList<T> BuildList();
    public ImmutableArray<T> BuildArray();
}
public sealed class DictionaryDraft<TKey, TValue> : IDictionary<TKey, TValue>, IReadOnlyDictionary<TKey, TValue>, IRevocable where TKey : notnull
{ public bool IsDirty { get; } public ImmutableDictionary<TKey, TValue> Build();
  public static implicit operator DictionaryDraft<TKey, TValue>(ImmutableDictionary<TKey, TValue> source); }
public sealed class DictionaryDraft<TKey, TValue, TDraft> : IReadOnlyDictionary<TKey, TDraft>, IRevocable
    where TKey : notnull where TValue : class where TDraft : class, IDraft<TValue>
{ public DictionaryDraft(ImmutableDictionary<TKey, TValue> source, Func<TValue, TDraft> createDraft);   // null values: as ListDraft<T,TDraft>
  public bool IsDirty { get; } public void Set(TKey key, TValue value); public bool Remove(TKey key); public void Clear();
  public ImmutableDictionary<TKey, TValue> Build(); }
public sealed class SetDraft<T> : ISet<T>, IReadOnlySet<T>, IRevocable
{ public bool IsDirty { get; } public ImmutableHashSet<T> BuildHashSet(); public ImmutableSortedSet<T> BuildSortedSet();
  public static implicit operator SetDraft<T>(ImmutableHashSet<T> source); public static implicit operator SetDraft<T>(ImmutableSortedSet<T> source); }
```

### 13.3 Generated code (nested: no new top-level type, no `{R}Extensions` collisions)

For `[Draftable] public partial record TodoState(ImmutableList<Todo> Items, Filter Filter);` with `[Draftable] partial record Todo(int Id, string Title, bool Done);`:

```csharp
// <auto-generated/>
#nullable enable
#pragma warning disable CS0612, CS0618 // justification: generated code references user [Obsolete] members (GEN-06)
partial record TodoState
{
    /// <summary>Applies <paramref name="recipe"/> to a draft of this instance and returns the result (this instance when nothing changed).</summary>
    public TodoState Produce(global::System.Action<Draft> recipe)
    {
        var d = new Draft(this);
        try { recipe(d); return ((global::Ducky.Draft.IDraft<TodoState>)d).Build(); }
        finally { ((global::Ducky.Draft.IRevocable)d).Revoke(); }
    }
    /// <summary>Creates a draft for multi-step editing; call <see cref="Draft.Build"/>.</summary>
    public Draft CreateDraft() => new(this);                 // manual multi-step editing; call Build()

    // every public generated member carries a <summary>; the ones below are elided here
    /// <summary>A copy-on-write draft of <see cref="TodoState"/>.</summary>
    public sealed class Draft : global::Ducky.Draft.IDraft<TodoState>
    {
        private readonly TodoState __x_original; private bool __x_revoked;                // fixed privates: __x_ prefix (§13.3 naming)
        private global::Ducky.Draft.ListDraft<Todo, Todo.Draft>? __m_Items;      // __m_{Name}: value; __s_{Name}: assigned flag. Tri-state: untouched (null, false),
        private bool __s_Items;                                               // drafted (non-null), assigned (value or null, true)
        private Filter __m_Filter;                                               // scalar: copied; compared with the original at Build
        public Draft(TodoState original) { __x_original = original; __m_Filter = original.Filter; }
        [return: global::System.Diagnostics.CodeAnalysis.NotNullIfNotNull(nameof(value))]
        public static implicit operator Draft?(TodoState? value) => value is null ? null : new(value);   // `d.Child = record` compiles
        public global::Ducky.Draft.ListDraft<Todo, Todo.Draft> Items
        { get { __x_Guard(); if (__m_Items is null && !__s_Items) __m_Items = new(__x_original.Items, static x => new Todo.Draft(x)); return __m_Items!; }
          set { __x_Guard(); if (!ReferenceEquals(__m_Items, value)) ((global::Ducky.Draft.IRevocable?)__m_Items)?.Revoke();   // replaced draft is revoked
                __m_Items = value; __s_Items = true; } }
        public Filter Filter
        { get { __x_Guard(); return __m_Filter; }
          set { __x_Guard(); __m_Filter = value; } }                               // no dirty flag: Build compares with the original

        bool global::Ducky.Draft.IDraft<TodoState>.IsDirty => __s_Items || (__m_Items?.IsDirty ?? false)
            || !global::System.Collections.Generic.EqualityComparer<Filter>.Default.Equals(__m_Filter, __x_original.Filter);  // conservative: !IsDirty ⇒ base
        TodoState global::Ducky.Draft.IDraft<TodoState>.Build()
        {
            __x_Guard();
            var items = __m_Items is null ? (__s_Items ? null! : __x_original.Items) : __m_Items.BuildList();   // a clean draft returns its own source
            if (global::System.Collections.Generic.EqualityComparer<Filter>.Default.Equals(__m_Filter, __x_original.Filter)
                && ReferenceEquals(items, __x_original.Items)) return __x_original;                // INV-26: no-op (reverts included) returns the base
            return __x_original with { Items = items, Filter = __m_Filter };                 // untouched members keep references
        }
        public TodoState Build() => ((global::Ducky.Draft.IDraft<TodoState>)this).Build();   // omitted when the record has a member named Build
        void global::Ducky.Draft.IRevocable.Revoke()
        { __x_revoked = true; ((global::Ducky.Draft.IRevocable?)__m_Items)?.Revoke(); }       // public contract: works across assemblies
        private void __x_Guard() { if (__x_revoked) throw new global::System.ObjectDisposedException(nameof(Draft), "Draft used after Produce returned."); }
    }
}
#pragma warning restore CS0612, CS0618
```

- **Assigning a record to a draftable member.** Every generated `Draft` declares `implicit operator Draft?(R? value)` (null in, null out), so the everyday `s.Produce(d => d.Selected = action.Todo)` compiles, as Mutty's `implicit operator MutableR(R)` allowed (`Produce_AssignRecordToDraftableMember_Builds`). A list of draftable elements is replaced through the public constructor, `d.Items = new(loaded, static x => new Todo.Draft(x))`, because a generic `ListDraft<T,TDraft>` can't call `TDraft`'s conversion (the round-1 choice of a `Func<T,TDraft>` factory over a static-abstract factory interface stands).
- **Revocation of replaced and flushed drafts.** A setter that replaces a nested draft or a collection draft revokes the draft it replaces (unless it is the same instance), and a structural list operation revokes every element draft it flushes (below). A write through such a stale draft (`var t = d.Items[0]; d.Items.RemoveAt(1); t.Title = "x";`, or `var c = d.Child; d.Child = other; c.Name = "x";`) throws `ObjectDisposedException` ("draft revoked: Produce returned, or an assignment or structural operation replaced it") instead of being silently lost (`Produce_ElementDraftAfterStructuralOp_Throws`, `Produce_ReplacedChildDraft_Throws`).
- **No-op is decided at `Build`, by comparing with the original.** A scalar is compared with the original's value (`EqualityComparer<T>.Default`), not with a dirty flag, so a revert (A→B→A) returns the base (`Produce_RevertedScalar_ReturnsSameReference`); a child or collection member is compared by identity (below). A **no-op recipe** is therefore one whose final member values all equal the originals and which made no structural collection operation (a collection draft that was mutated builds a new instance even if its contents end up equal). Collection drafts build through the original's `ToBuilder()`, so a dictionary's `KeyComparer`/`ValueComparer` and a sorted set's `Comparer` are preserved.
- **Members are tri-state** (untouched, drafted, assigned). Getters draft lazily from the original, and return null while a nullable member's original is null and nothing was assigned (never `new(null…)`). Assigning, null included, is always reflected by `Build`: assigning null sets the member to null; assigning a collection (through the implicit conversions) or a nested draft built from another instance yields that instance when it is clean, because every draft returns **its own** source, not the member's original (`Produce_NullableMemberNullOriginal_GetReturnsNull`, whose cases include an oblivious member declared under `#nullable disable`, which counts as nullable, GEN-06). Nested `C.Draft?` members follow the same rule, and an oblivious `C Child` member is emitted as `C.Draft?` with the same guard, so `d.Child is null` holds while its original is null.
- **The no-op identity check is generated per member kind**: `ReferenceEquals` for reference types, and `ImmutableArray<T>.Equals(ImmutableArray<T>)` (same underlying array; boxing both into `ReferenceEquals` would always be false) for `ImmutableArray<T>`, so a no-op recipe on a record with an `ImmutableArray<T>` member returns the base (`Produce_NoOp_ImmutableArrayMember_ReturnsSameReference`). A `default(ImmutableArray<T>)` member, as left by deserializing a record without that member, reads as empty through the draft and stays default when untouched (`Produce_DefaultImmutableArrayMember_Works`), as Mutty's `IsDefault` guard did.
- The `IDraft<T>` members (`IsDirty`, `Build`, `Revoke`) are implemented explicitly, so a record member named `IsDirty` or `Revoke` becomes an ordinary draft property with no clash. The public convenience `Build()` is emitted only when the record has no member named `Build`. **Private names can't collide:** member-derived privates use the verbatim member name, with no case change, behind a kind prefix (`__m_{Name}` for the value, `__s_{Name}` for the assigned flag of a drafted member), and fixed privates use a prefix no member-derived name can take (`__x_original`, `__x_revoked`, `__x_Guard`). A single `__` prefix plus camelCase would emit CS0102 duplicates in generated code for `record Session(string Id, bool Revoked)` (`__revoked` twice), a member named `O` (`__o`), a collection `Tags` next to a member `TagsSet` (`__tagsSet` twice) and a case-only pair `Items`/`items`. DUCKY104 still covers `Draft`, `Produce` and `CreateDraft`. The shape matrix includes records with members named `Build`, `IsDirty` and `Revoke`, and `Generated_CompilesClean_{Shape}` rows for members named `Revoked` and `O`, a member `TagsSet` next to a collection `Tags`, and a case-only pair.
- Revocation goes through the public `IRevocable` contract, so `App.X.Draft` revokes a nested `Lib.Todo.Draft` or a collection draft from another assembly (`Produce_RevokedDraftAccess_Throws` covers cross-assembly nested members).
- When any base type, in source or metadata, declares `Draft` or `CreateDraft()`, the generator emits them with the `new` modifier, so a draftable record deriving from another (including a base in another assembly) compiles without CS0108.
- **Which members the draft mirrors.** `Draft` is nested in the record, so it could reach private and protected members; it mirrors only instance **properties** (never fields, indexers or statics) whose declared accessibility is `public`, `internal` or `protected internal`, excluding the synthesized `EqualityContract` and every other compiler-synthesized member. Every member left out keeps its value through `with`. Each draft member gets the minimum of the member's accessibility and its type's effective accessibility (the GEN-01 rule, §16.1), so an `internal` property of an `internal` type in a `public` record yields an `internal` draft member instead of CS0053/CS0050 in the `public sealed class Draft`. A draft property whose name hides an inherited `object` member (a record property named `GetType` or `MemberwiseClone`; the record itself can't declare `ToString`, `Equals` or `GetHashCode` properties next to its synthesized methods) is emitted with `new` (no CS0108). Shape-matrix rows: a private property, a protected property, an internal property of an internal type in a public record, and a property named `GetType` (INV-25).
- **Nullable elements are not element-drafted.** A collection element or dictionary value whose type is nullable-annotated (`ImmutableList<Todo?>`, `ImmutableArray<Todo?>`, `ImmutableDictionary<string, Todo?>`) maps to the plain `ListDraft<T>` / `DictionaryDraft<K,V>`, whose elements are replaced by get/set: `ListDraft<Todo?, Todo.Draft>` would violate the `where T : class` constraint under nullable (CS8634, plus CS8604 on the element factory), breaking a warnings-as-errors consumer, and at run time the lazy indexer would build a `Todo.Draft` over a null element. Shape-matrix rows `ImmutableList<Todo?>`, `ImmutableArray<Todo?>` and `ImmutableDictionary<string, Todo?>` in `Generated_CompilesClean_{Shape}`, and edit scripts with null elements in `Produce_EqualsManualWithExpressions`. An **oblivious** element type (a list declared under `#nullable disable`) is different: it keeps element drafting, so nullable-disabled Mutty migrants keep `d.Items[0].Done = true`, and a null element is handled by the element drafts themselves (§13.2).
- **Warnings-as-errors consumers.** The GEN-06 generated-code rules (§16.1) apply to Ducky.Draft.Generators exactly as to Ducky.Generators: the `// <auto-generated/>` + `#nullable enable` prologue (the draft's `?` annotations would otherwise raise CS8669), a `/// <summary>` on every public generated type and member (no CS1591 under `GenerateDocumentationFile`), and `#pragma warning disable CS0612, CS0618 // justification: …` around user-type references. A member marked `[Obsolete(error: true)]` is left out of the draft entirely (it is neither read nor written, since reading it is CS0619 too, which can't be suppressed) and `with` keeps its value; a `[Draftable]` record that is itself `[Obsolete(error: true)]` gets DUCKY108 and no output. The Draft shape matrix runs `Generated_CompilesClean_{Shape}` with `DocumentationMode.Diagnose`, nullable enabled and warning level 9999, with rows for `[Obsolete]` and `[Obsolete(error: true)]` members and records (INV-25).

Type mapping is **symbol-based**. The generator compares `OriginalDefinition` with `SymbolEqualityComparer` against `GetTypeByMetadataName` symbols, and it emits `SymbolDisplayFormat.FullyQualifiedFormat` names with `IncludeNullableReferenceTypeModifier` (oblivious positions rewritten to annotated, GEN-06, §16.1). It never slices strings. A member type `C` **in the same compilation** maps to `C.Draft` iff `C` passes the same shape predicate that makes the generator emit `C.Draft` (one shared function: `[Draftable]`, a partial record class, partial containing types, not generic or nested in a generic type, no DUCKY104 clash, not error-obsolete); otherwise the member is a plain get/set. A generator never sees its own output, so "`C.Draft` exists" can't be evaluated for same-compilation types, and mapping on `[Draftable]` alone would make a parent of an invalid `C` (DUCKY101–104) emit `C.Draft` and cascade CS0426 errors on top of the real diagnostic. The parent's equatable model carries that boolean, so a change to `C` re-keys the parent's cached output (shape-matrix row: a parent whose member type is a `[Draftable]` non-partial record reports only DUCKY101, and the parent compiles). A member type `C` from another assembly maps to `C.Draft` only when `C` actually has a nested public type `Draft` implementing `IDraft<C>` **and** a public constructor of `C.Draft` taking exactly one `C` (the parent's generated code calls `new C.Draft(x)` in lazy getters and element factories): `[Draftable]` in metadata is not enough, because the other assembly may reference the Ducky.Draft runtime without its generator having run (analyzers excluded, a `PrivateAssets` mistake, a skewed version), and a hand-written or skewed nested `Draft` can satisfy the interface without that constructor. Otherwise the member is a plain get/set and Info DUCKY107 names the type (the §16.3 fixture compiles a `Lib` without the generator, and a `Lib` variant whose `Draft : IDraft<C>` lacks the constructor).

| Member type | Draft member |
|---|---|
| `[Draftable]` record `C`: in this compilation, one that passes the generator's shape predicate; in another assembly, one whose nested `Draft` implements `IDraft<C>` and has a public `Draft(C)` constructor | `C.Draft` (nullable if `C?`), assignable from `C` through the generated implicit conversion |
| `ImmutableList<T>` / `ImmutableArray<T>` | `ListDraft<T>`, or `ListDraft<T, T.Draft>` if `T` is draftable and not nullable-annotated (`BuildList()`/`BuildArray()` by member type; identity for arrays = same underlying array) |
| `ImmutableDictionary<K,V>` | `DictionaryDraft<K,V>`, or `DictionaryDraft<K,V,V.Draft>` if `V` is draftable and not nullable-annotated |
| `ImmutableList<C?>`, `ImmutableArray<C?>`, `ImmutableDictionary<K,C?>` of a draftable `C` | the plain `ListDraft<C?>` / `DictionaryDraft<K,C?>`: elements replaced by get/set, never element-drafted |
| `ImmutableHashSet<T>` / `ImmutableSortedSet<T>` | `SetDraft<T>` |
| any of the above, nullable | the same, nullable; reads return null while the original is null and nothing was assigned; assigning `null` sets the member to null |
| mutable collections (`List<T>`, `T[]`, `Dictionary<,>`) | plain get/set, replaced wholesale; DUCKY105 Warning |
| other `System.Collections.Immutable` types (`ImmutableQueue<T>`, `ImmutableStack<T>`, `ImmutableSortedDictionary<K,V>`, …) and Ducky's `EntityState<TKey,TEntity>` | plain get/set; their mutators return a new instance, which must be assigned (`d.Pending = d.Pending.Enqueue(job)`): a discarded result is DUCKY109 (Mutty mapped the first three to mutable `Queue`/`Stack`/`SortedDictionary`, so migrated recipes that call them in place would otherwise compile and lose the edit) |
| anything else (scalars, strings, enums, other types) | plain get/set of the same type |
| member without an accessible `init`/`set` | get-only in the draft; kept as-is by `with` |
| member marked `[Obsolete(error: true)]` | left out of the draft; kept as-is by `with` |

Supported: partial `record class`, top-level or nested (containing types must be partial), including derived records. Not supported: generic records and records nested in a generic containing type (DUCKY103: the nested `Draft` would have to reproduce the container's type parameters and constraints), record structs (DUCKY101; a record struct has no reference identity to preserve). The generator never emits code that fails to compile: unsupported shapes degrade to plain get/set or produce a diagnostic and no output.

Collection-draft rule: for element drafts, any structural operation (insert, remove, clear, sort) first flushes the cached element drafts into the list, revokes them, and then clears the cache, which keeps index shifting trivially correct; a later access re-drafts from the flushed list. ponytail: enumerating `ListDraft<T,TDraft>` materializes one draft per element visited (O(visited)). Add a non-drafting `Find` if profiling says so.

### 13.4 Mutty migration

| Mutty | Ducky.Draft |
|---|---|
| `[MutableGeneration] record R` | `[Draftable] partial record R` |
| `MutableR` | `R.Draft` |
| `r.Produce(d => …)` | unchanged for scalar and nested-record edits; collection edits per the rows below |
| `List<T>`/`List<MutableT>` members (Mutty's view of `ImmutableList`/`ImmutableArray`) | `ListDraft<T>` / `ListDraft<T, T.Draft>`: replace `Find`/`FindIndex`/`ForEach` with LINQ `First` or an index (`d.Items.First(x => x.Id == id).Done = true`), use `AddRange`/`RemoveAll`, assign `.ToImmutableList()` (or the implicit conversion) instead of a `List<T>`, and pass the record, not a draft, to `Add`; `InsertRange`/`RemoveRange` become loops |
| `IReadOnlyList<T>`/`IReadOnlyCollection<T>` members (Mutty: `List<T>`) | plain get/set of the same type; switch the member to `ImmutableList<T>` to get a `ListDraft` |
| `ImmutableQueue`/`ImmutableStack`/`ImmutableSortedDictionary` members (Mutty: mutable `Queue`/`Stack`/`SortedDictionary`) | plain get/set: assign the result (`d.Q = d.Q.Enqueue(x)`); DUCKY109 flags a discarded one |
| `[MutableGeneration] record struct` | not supported (DUCKY101): make it a `record class`, or edit it with `with` |
| `r.CreateDraft()` / `d.FinishDraft()` | `r.CreateDraft()` / `d.Build()` |
| `OnBeforeBuild` | removed (validate in the reducer) |
| `implicit operator MutableR(R)` (assigning a record to a nested mutable member) | kept as `implicit operator R.Draft?(R?)`, so `d.Child = value` still compiles; a list of draftable elements is replaced with `new ListDraft<T, T.Draft>(value, static x => new T.Draft(x))` |
| `With{Prop}`, other implicit/explicit operators, `AsMutable`/`ToImmutable` | removed (use setters / `Produce`) |
| `using Mutty;` | `using Ducky.Draft;` |

DUCKY901 warns while both Mutty and Ducky.Draft are referenced. The `docs/migration/samples/mutty` before/after pair includes a `Find`-based recipe and a queue member.

---

## 14. Ducky.Reactive (EFF-04, R3, INV-27, ADR-0006)

```csharp
namespace Ducky.Reactive;

public abstract class ReactiveEffect                         // DI-constructed; inject TimeProvider for time operators
{
    public abstract Observable<object> Handle(Observable<object> actions, Observable<StateSnapshot> state);
    protected internal virtual TimeSpan? RetryDelay(int attempt)          // null stops resubscribing
        => attempt > 8 ? null : TimeSpan.FromSeconds(Math.Min(30, 1 << (attempt - 1)));
}

public static class DuckyReactiveExtensions
{
    public static DuckyBuilder AddReactiveEffect<[DAM] TEffect>(this DuckyBuilder builder) where TEffect : ReactiveEffect;
    public static Observable<TAction> OfActionType<TAction>(this Observable<object> actions) where TAction : notnull;   // exact type
}
```

Internals, built only on Ducky's public API (R-PKG-2):
- `ReactiveBridge` holds `Subject<object> Actions` and `SynchronizedReactiveProperty<StateSnapshot> State`. It is registered **Scoped in every host**, never with "the store's lifetime": that lifetime is resolved only after `configure` returns (`Lifetime`/`IsBrowser` may be set after the generated `AddDuckyGenerated_*()` call, §5.1), and a Singleton bridge behind a Scoped server store would share one subject across circuits. Its only consumers, `ReactiveMiddleware` and `ReactiveHost`, are created from the store scope (§6.10: the circuit or request scope on the server, the store-owned scope in the browser), so Scoped means exactly one bridge per store whatever `Lifetime` ends up being.
- `ReactiveMiddleware : Middleware` pushes from `AfterReduce`: `Actions.OnNext(context.Action)` and, when the snapshot changed, `State.Value = context.State`. **Values** have a single writer, the drainer (INV-01), so `OnNext` and the value writes need no lock. **Subscription changes** don't: a failure-completion resubscribe calls `Handle(actions, state)` from a `Task.Delay` continuation on a timer thread; operators such as `Take(1)`, `Switch` and `SelectMany` subscribe and unsubscribe inner `state` subscriptions on whatever thread emitted (timer-driven emissions, as in the polling recipe, run on pool threads); and the dispose callback disposes the `CompositeDisposable` on the disposing thread (§6.11 step 2), possibly during a drain. R3's `ReactiveProperty<T>` is documented as not thread-safe, so `State` is R3's `SynchronizedReactiveProperty<T>`, and R3's `Subject<T>` takes its internal lock for `Subscribe` and subscription disposal, with only `OnNext` relying on the single drainer. `Reactive_ResubscribeConcurrentWithDrain_FirstValueCurrent_NoLostSubscriber` (`Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`) is the arbiter for the pinned R3 version; if it shows `Subject` subscription changes unsynchronized, `ReactiveBridge` wraps `Actions` subscribe/dispose in its own lock. Its `…_Deterministic` twin in `Ducky.Reactive.Tests` drives the resubscribe with `FakeTimeProvider.Advance` from inside a user middleware's `AfterReduce` registered before `ReactiveMiddleware`, so the resubscribe lands between a commit and the bridge's `State.Value` write, and asserts that the new pipeline's first value is the current snapshot and that no other subscriber was lost.
- `ReactiveHost : Effect<StoreInitialized>` (Merge; a Merge run's token **is** the store-lifetime token, with no per-run CTS, §6.6, so disposing nothing at run end can't cut the subscriptions loose). `Handle` first sets `bridge.State.Value = ctx.State` (on the drainer, so the single-writer rule holds): the bridge is a DI service that can't read `IStore.State` at construction without re-entering materialization, and in a store where nothing changed state before `StoreInitialized` (no persistence, no seed: the common case) `ReactiveMiddleware` would never have written it, so the property would still hold its constructor value and replay `null` to `state.Select(s => s.Get<X>())` (`Reactive_StateObservable_FirstValueIsCurrentSnapshot`). Only then does it subscribe each registered effect's `Handle(actions, state)`, then emits its trigger once, `bridge.Actions.OnNext(ctx.Trigger.Action)` (on the drainer, in the synchronous prefix, so the single-writer rule holds): `ReactiveMiddleware` pushed `StoreInitialized` in step 9, before any pipeline existed, so without this a reactive load written like the core recipe (`actions.OfActionType<StoreInitialized>().SelectMany(load)`) would compile and never fire. A failure resubscribe doesn't replay it, so a pipeline never loads twice (`Reactive_OfActionTypeStoreInitialized_FiresOnceNotOnResubscribe`). It then **returns immediately**, so it never blocks `WhenIdleAsync`. The run's `EffectContext` stays usable after `Handle` returns.
  - **Reactive work is excluded from quiescence**, like `LongRunning` effects: pipelines are not counted in `_runningEffects`, so `WhenIdleAsync`, `TestStore.Settled` and the prerender seed (§11.4) never wait for a reactive pipeline, even one with a request in flight. This is normative: a `Prerender<T>` slice must be loaded by an `Effect<T>`, and tests of reactive effects wait on the observable outcome (for example `FakeTimeProvider.Advance` plus an awaited `DispatchAsync`), not on `Settled`. The docs carry the warning on `Prerender<T>`, `ReactiveEffect` and `Settled` (`Prerender_ReactiveLoadInFlight_Documented`).
  - `onNext` → `ctx.Dispatch(action)` (`Origin.Effect`). A synchronous emission inside `AfterReduce` is a synchronous child (depth + 1), so an `A→A` loop stops at 64 (`Reactive_SyncSelfLoop_StoppedByDepthGuard`). A timer-driven emission starts a new chain (§6.5).
  - **Failures are system failure actions.** `ReactiveHost` forwards every reactive failure to `ReactiveMiddleware` (same assembly), which publishes it with `DispatchSystem(new EffectFailed(effectType, "reactive", ex), isFailure: true)`. It is therefore `Origin.System`: never offered to `MayDispatch` or DevTools time travel, never buffered, never subject to the unhandled check (strict `TestStore` stays clean), and INV-12 applies to anything it triggers (`Reactive_Failure_IsSystemFailureAction_StrictModeClean`).
  - `onErrorResume` (R3's non-terminal error) → `EffectFailed` as above, and the stream continues.
  - `onCompleted(Result.Failure)` → `EffectFailed` as above, then resubscribe after `RetryDelay(attempt)`, waited with `await Task.Delay(delay, timeProvider, lifetimeToken).ConfigureAwait(false); lifetimeToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();` on the store-lifetime token, so it resubscribes only if that token is still not cancelled when the delay ends. The re-check is branch-free on purpose: under `FakeTimeProvider` the delay's completion runs the continuation inline inside `Advance`, so no deterministic test could cancel between the completion and an `if`, which would be a race-only branch (§17.1); both cancellations (the delay's own and the re-check's) land in the one existing cancellation catch that `Reactive_ResubscribePendingAtDispose_NeverResubscribes` covers. `null` stops (Warning). The attempt counter resets after a successful emission.
  - Every subscription, the resubscribed ones included, is added to one R3 `CompositeDisposable` that a `cancellationToken.Register(...)` callback disposes at store dispose (§6.11 step 2). Adding to a disposed composite disposes the new item at once, which closes the race between a retry firing and the dispose callback: a retry never calls `Handle` on a reactive effect that phase 5b has disposed, and never leaves a live subscription behind (`Reactive_ResubscribePendingAtDispose_NeverResubscribes`). Reactive pipelines are not in `_runs`, so dispose doesn't wait for them; these two rules are what stop them.
- `AddReactiveEffect<[DAM] T>()` does not register `T` in DI. It records a factory `sp => ActivatorUtilities.CreateInstance<T>(sp)`; `ReactiveHost` creates the instances from the **store scope** (§6.10) when effects are materialized, and the store owns and disposes them (§5.6). A reactive effect that injects the WASM template's scoped `HttpClient` therefore resolves under Development scope validation (`Reactive_ScopedDependencyInBrowser_Resolves`). Because the core never sees `T`, `AddReactiveEffect<T>` calls `builder.RequireResolvableConstructor(typeof(T), "AddReactiveEffect<T>")` (§5.1), so an unresolvable constructor parameter is reported as DUCKY309 at first resolution with the other configuration errors, naming the reactive effect, instead of surfacing as DUCKY353 naming `ReactiveHost` at first use (`Build_ReactiveEffectCtorDependencyMissing_ReportedWithOtherErrors`). It then calls `Use<ReactiveMiddleware>()` and `AddEffect<ReactiveHost>()`, both idempotent.
- Reactive effects observe `StoreInitialized` (once, on first subscription) and every action that reaches `AfterReduce` after it. The `state` observable replays the current snapshot on subscribe.
- Docs recipes: debounced search, polling while a flag is set, and a keyed switch with `GroupByUntil` (never `GroupBy`, which leaks one group per key), with a test proving that groups are released.
- R3's trim compatibility is covered by the AOT gate. Ducky.Reactive sets `IsAotCompatible=true` like every library; an R3 IL warning is a release blocker, fixed upstream or by pinning.

Tests use `FakeTimeProvider` (R3 consumes `TimeProvider` natively): `Reactive_DisposedWithStore_NoEmissionAfterDispose`, `Reactive_FailureCompletion_ResubscribesWithBackoff`, `Reactive_OnErrorResume_ContinuesStream`, `Reactive_Failure_IsSystemFailureAction_StrictModeClean`, `Reactive_Debounce_WithFakeTime`, `Reactive_SyncSelfLoop_StoppedByDepthGuard`, `Reactive_DoesNotBlockWhenIdle`, `Reactive_GroupByUntilRecipe_ReleasesGroups`, `Reactive_ScopedDependencyInBrowser_Resolves`, `Reactive_ResubscribePendingAtDispose_NeverResubscribes`, `Reactive_StateObservable_FirstValueIsCurrentSnapshot` (a store with no persisted slices: the first emitted snapshot is non-null and equals `store.State`), `Reactive_ResubscribeConcurrentWithDrain_FirstValueCurrent_NoLostSubscriber_Deterministic`, `Build_ReactiveEffectCtorDependencyMissing_ReportedWithOtherErrors`, `Reactive_OfActionTypeStoreInitialized_FiresOnceNotOnResubscribe` (the pipeline's `StoreInitialized` branch fires once, and not again after a failure-completion resubscribe), `ReducerThrowsOnStoreInitialized_LoadEffectAndReactiveHostStillStart` (§6.4; the pipelines still subscribe), and `SafeLogger_ProviderThrows_NeverEscapes` (§6.3).

---

## 15. Ducky.Testing (TST-01, ADR-0039)

It relies only on Ducky's public API. The library's own tests and the docs use it.

```csharp
namespace Ducky.Testing;

public sealed class TestStore : IStore
{
    public static TestStore Create(Action<DuckyBuilder> configure, Action<IServiceCollection>? services = null,
                                   params Type[] failureTypes);
                                                             // registers FakeTimeProvider; sets ThrowOnUnhandledAction = true *before*
                                                             // running configure, so a test can opt out (e.g. Reactive-only actions)
    public FakeTimeProvider Time { get; }
    public TimeSpan WallClockTimeout { get; set; } = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10);   // real-time bound of Settled() and DisposeAsync
    public IReadOnlyList<ActionContext> Processed { get; }  // every action that reached AfterReduce (reduced, or failed in BeforeReduce or a
                                                             // reducer, §6.4) with origin, depth, correlation, via a recording middleware;
                                                             // the current immutable snapshot (see below)
    public IReadOnlyList<object> Failures { get; }           // ReducerFailed, EffectFailed, and any action whose type is in failureTypes
                                                             // (e.g. typeof(PersistenceFailed); Ducky.Testing doesn't reference Ducky.Blazor)
    public TestStore Seed<TState>(TState state) where TState : class;  // Restore(…, Hydration): WasRestored<TState>() becomes true. The key is
                                                             // Slices.Single(s => s.StateType == typeof(TState)).Key (declared type, so a
                                                             // derived state type never matches its base's slice); no match: DUCKY350
    public TSlice GetSlice<TSlice>() where TSlice : Slice;   // the store-owned instance from TestStore's container, so a test can call
                                                             // cart.Add(...) on the SliceStore the store attached (a `new` one throws DUCKY351)
    public Task Settled(TimeSpan? timeout = null);           // WhenIdleAsync with a wall-clock bound (default WallClockTimeout); DuckyAssertionException
                                                             // on timeout. Like WhenIdleAsync, it does not wait for LongRunning effects or Ducky.Reactive work,
                                                             // and throws InvalidOperationException from a non-LongRunning effect run (§7.4)
    public ValueTask DisposeAsync();                         // the real store's DisposeAsync().AsTask().WaitAsync(WallClockTimeout, TimeProvider.System);
```
