                                                             // DuckyAssertionException when the bound expires (below)
    /* + all other IStore members, forwarded to the real store */
}

public static class SliceTest
{
    public static SliceTest<TState> For<TSlice, TState>() where TSlice : Slice<TState>, new() where TState : class;
}
public sealed class SliceTest<TState> where TState : class
{
    public SliceTest<TState> Given(TState state);
    public SliceTest<TState> When(params object[] actions);
    public void Then(TState expected);                       // Equals comparison; the message shows both values
    public void Then(Action<TState> assert);
    public void ThenUnchanged();                             // ReferenceEquals to the given state
}   // runs on a real store (TestStore): Given seeds through Restore, When dispatches each action with Dispatch (reduced inline,
    // §6.7: the store's only middleware is the synchronous recording one), and each Then throws DuckyAssertionException
    // when an action given to When is missing from Processed (SliceTest_ActionMissingFromProcessed_Throws)

public static class EffectTest
{
    public static Task<EffectRun> Run<TEffect, TAction>(TEffect effect, TAction action,
        Action<DuckyBuilder>? configure = null,              // runs before the store is built: add the slices the effect reads (ctx.State.Get<T>())
        Action<TestStore>? arrange = null)                   // runs after: Seed<TState>(...)
        where TEffect : Effect where TAction : notnull;      // Effect, so an EffectGroup is covered too
    public static Task<EffectRun> Run<[DAM] TEffect, TAction>(TAction action,
        Action<DuckyBuilder>? configure = null, Action<TestStore>? arrange = null,
        Action<IServiceCollection>? services = null)         // fakes for the effect's dependencies
        where TEffect : Effect where TAction : notnull;      // registers AddEffect<TEffect>(): the store builds the effect and injects its
                                                             // store-owned slices and SliceStores (the §12 pattern)
}
public sealed class EffectRun { public IReadOnlyList<object> Dispatched { get; } public IReadOnlyList<object> Failures { get; } }   // immutable snapshots

public sealed class DuckyAssertionException : Exception;   // framework-agnostic (xUnit, NUnit, MSTest, TUnit)
```

`EffectTest` sets `ThrowOnUnhandledAction = false` before running `configure` (which may set it back): the actions the effect under test dispatches (`LoadSucceeded`, …) usually have no reducer in the harness, and they are results to assert on in `EffectRun.Dispatched`, not failures (`EffectTest_EffectReadingState_Works`, `EffectTest_UnhandledResultAction_NotAFailure`). The instance overload registers the given instance with `DuckyBuilder.AddEffect(instance)`: the store uses it and never disposes it (§5.6), and it wins over an `AddEffect<TEffect>()` for the same type that `configure` adds, for example through `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}()`, so the handler runs once per action (§5.1, `EffectTest_ConfigureAlsoRegistersEffectType_HandlerRunsOnce`). An effect that injects a SliceStore can't be built before the store exists, so the type overload lets the store build it and a test reads or drives the SliceStore through `GetSlice` (`TestStore_GetSlice_ReturnsStoreOwnedSliceStore`, `EffectTest_EffectInjectingSliceStore_Works`). There is one resolution path for every other effect, `ActivatorUtilities.CreateInstance<T>`, store-owned. bUnit users call `Services.AddDucky(...)` on their test context and need no bUnit-specific API.

**Recorded lists are immutable snapshots.** `Processed`, `Failures`, `EffectRun.Dispatched` and `EffectRun.Failures` are appended by a recording middleware on the drainer, which can be any thread, while the test thread reads them; `LongRunning` and reactive work keep dispatching after `Settled` returns, and §14 tells reactive tests to wait on observable outcomes. Each list is an `ImmutableList<T>` that the recorder replaces with `ImmutableInterlocked.Update`, and each property returns the current snapshot, so a test that enumerates one (a Shouldly `ShouldContain`) while the drainer appends never gets "Collection was modified" or a torn read, and the atomic swap gives the happens-before edge (`TestStore_ProcessedReadWhileLongRunningEffectDispatches_NoException`, `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`).

**Disposal is bounded in real time.** Every dispose bound of the store runs on its `TimeProvider` (§6.11), which `TestStore` always sets to a `FakeTimeProvider`, and `DisposeAsync` completes only once every phase has finished or reached its bound: a fake clock that nobody advances would never reach them. So a store whose effect run ignores its token (a TCS-gated fake the test forgot to complete, a third-party call without cancellation), whose middleware init hangs, or whose fake JS runtime never answers a flush would never finish disposing, and the test's `await using` would hang forever, hiding the pending failure (xUnit has no default timeout, so CI would stall instead of failing). `TestStore.DisposeAsync` therefore waits for the real store's `DisposeAsync` with the same wall-clock bound as `Settled` (`WallClockTimeout`) and, when it expires, throws `DuckyAssertionException` naming the bound, the likely causes (an effect run, middleware init or JS/cache call that ignores cancellation) and the last actions in `Processed`. It is in INV-10's test-failure carve-out, next to `Settled` and `EffectTest.Run` (whose internal store disposal inherits the bound), not in the never-fault set (`TestStore_DisposeWithHungEffectRun_FailsWithinWallClockBound`, with `WallClockTimeout` set to 200 ms). The message can't list the pending run ids and effect types: Ducky.Testing uses only Ducky's public API (ADR-0039), and the store exposes no run registry.

---

## 16. Generators and analyzers

### 16.1 Generators (`Ducky.Generators.dll`, inside the Ducky package)

- **GEN-01 `DispatchExtensionsGenerator`**: `ForAttributeWithMetadataName("Ducky.DuckyActionAttribute")`. For a record `N.Increment(int By = 1)` it emits into `N`:
  ```csharp
  public static class IncrementDispatchExtensions
  {
      public static void Increment(this global::Ducky.IDispatcher __dispatcher, int By = 1) => __dispatcher.Dispatch(new Increment(By));
      public static global::System.Threading.Tasks.Task<global::Ducky.DispatchResult> IncrementAsync(this global::Ducky.IDispatcher __dispatcher, int By = 1)
          => __dispatcher.DispatchAsync(new Increment(By));
  }
  ```
  - One overload per accessible, **explicitly declared** constructor (the primary constructor counts), with default values kept; only when the type declares no constructor at all does its implicit parameterless constructor get one. Implicitly declared constructors are otherwise skipped (`!IsImplicitlyDeclared`), and so is the record copy constructor (a single parameter of the containing type): a `[DuckyAction] record struct Inc(int By = 1)` also has the implicit `Inc()`, and an extra `Inc(this IDispatcher)` overload would win overload resolution and dispatch `By = 0` (`Helper_RecordStructWithDefaults_DispatchesDefaultValues`, plus the shape in `Generated_CompilesClean_{Shape}`).
  - **Error-obsolete shapes.** A constructor marked `[Obsolete(error: true)]`, or one with a parameter whose type is error-obsolete, is skipped (the helper would be CS0619 in the consumer, which can't be suppressed). When no constructor remains, or the record itself is `[Obsolete(error: true)]`, DUCKY020 is reported and nothing is emitted for it. The GEN-01 shape matrix (`Generated_CompilesClean_{Shape}`) has rows for each case.
  - Parameter names are the constructor's own, verbatim, with C# keywords escaped by `@` (the shared `IdentifierSanitizer`): no case change, so `record Move(int X, int x)` can't produce a CS0100 duplicate, `record R(int Class)` can't produce the keyword `class`, and named arguments read like the constructor's (`store.Increment(By: 1)`). The receiver parameter has the reserved name `__dispatcher`, so a record parameter named `dispatcher` can't clash (CS0100). The shape matrix includes case-only parameter pairs and keyword-named parameters.
  - **Accessibility**: the class and each method get the minimum effective accessibility of the record, the constructor and every parameter type (`internal` as soon as one of them is internal), so an internal record or a public record whose constructor takes an internal type never produces CS0051. The `Generated_CompilesClean_{Shape}` matrix includes internal records and internal parameter types.
  - The method name is `MethodName`, else the record name. For a nested record the class name joins every containing type, outermost first, with `_`: `A_B_RecordDispatchExtensions`. If the generated class name is already declared in the namespace (a user type, or another action whose joined name coincides), DUCKY020 is reported and nothing is emitted for that action.
  - Two `[DuckyAction]` types of one compilation, in different namespaces, whose helpers would have the same name and parameter types raise Info DUCKY024 (naming both and pointing to `MethodName`): importing both namespaces, as `_Imports.razor` or global usings commonly do, makes every call CS0121. The generator does not scan referenced assemblies for their generated helpers (a metadata walk on every compilation step would defeat incremental caching); that case is documented on DUCKY024.
  - Because `IStore`, `EffectContext` and `IDispatcher` all implement `IDispatcher`, the helpers work in components, services **and effects** (and inside effects they follow the supersession rules). C# binds instance members before extension methods, so DUCKY021 skips a helper whose base or `Async` name matches an accessible member of `IDispatcher`, `IStore` (with `IDisposable`/`IAsyncDisposable`), `EffectContext`, `TestStore` or `object`: `record Dispose` would otherwise make `store.DisposeAsync()` dispose the store. The shape matrix includes `Dispose`, `Initialize` and `WhenIdle` records.
  - There is no global `ActionDispatcher` and no injected type.
- **GEN-02 `RegistrationGenerator`** emits:
  ```csharp
  namespace Ducky.Generated;
  public static class {Asm}_DuckyRegistrations
  {
      public static global::Ducky.DuckyBuilder AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}(this global::Ducky.DuckyBuilder b)
      {
          b.AddSlice<global::X.CartStore>().AddEffect<global::X.LoadProduct>();
          global::Ducky.Reactive.DuckyReactiveExtensions.AddReactiveEffect<global::X.SearchEffect>(b);          // only if Ducky.Reactive is referenced
          global::Ducky.Blazor.DuckyBlazorBuilderExtensions.PersistAttributeDefaults<global::X.CartStore>(b,
              o => { o.Version = 2; o.SyncAcrossTabs = true; });                                          // every [Persist]; only the arguments written
                                                                                                          // in [Persist(...)], `o => { }` for a bare [Persist]
          global::Ducky.Blazor.DuckyBlazorBuilderExtensions.Prerender<global::X.TodosSlice>(b);                  // only if Ducky.Blazor is referenced
          return b;
      }
  }
  ```
  - Extensions from other packages are emitted as fully qualified static invocations, never extension syntax or `using` directives: `Ducky.Generated` doesn't enclose `Ducky.Blazor`/`Ducky.Reactive` (CS1061), and a user extension of the same name can't hijack the binding.
  - `[Persist]` always becomes one `PersistAttributeDefaults<T>` call, which by itself registers `T` as persisted exactly like `Persist<T>()` (it calls `AddBlazor()`, `RequireJsonTypeInfo(typeof(TState))` for DUCKY306, and runs the same DUCKY310–312 validations; DUCKY012 already analyzes `[Persist]` at compile time; §11.1), with an empty delegate for a bare `[Persist]` (`PersistAttribute_NoArguments_SlicePersisted`). Its delegate carries only the attribute's explicitly supplied named arguments (never a default such as `Storage = Local`), and those values are applied before every `Persist<T>` delegate whatever the call order, so a builder call always wins over the attribute (ADR-0012), and a user's `Persist<CartStore>(o => o.Migrate(1, …))` combines with the attribute's `Version` (`PersistAttributePlusBuilderMigrate_Composes` runs both call orders and an overlapping property, where the builder value wins). `AddBlazor` composes too, so in the usual chain `d.AddDuckyGenerated_App().AddBlazor(o => { o.KeyPrefix = "app"; o.Scope = … })` the user's options apply even though the generated `PersistAttributeDefaults<T>` called `AddBlazor()` first; a dropped `Scope` would otherwise merge users' browser keys (`AddBlazor_AfterGeneratedPersist_ConfigureApplied`).
  - It registers every non-abstract, non-generic, accessible, non-`file` `Slice<>` subclass with a public parameterless constructor (SliceStores included; a slice without one gets DUCKY023), and every non-abstract, non-generic, accessible, non-`file` `Effect<>`/`EffectGroup`, and `ReactiveEffect` when `Ducky.Reactive.ReactiveEffect` resolves, with at least one public constructor, whatever its parameters: effects and reactive effects are DI-constructed through `ActivatorUtilities` and normally inject services (the matrix's `LoadProduct(IProductApi api)`), and their constructor dependencies are checked at run time by DUCKY309 (§5.1). The GEN-02 shape matrix has an effect whose only constructor injects `HttpClient`, and (from stage 16) a reactive effect likewise. `file`-local types are inaccessible from the generated file (`Accessibility` doesn't model `IsFileLocal`, so the generator checks it): a `file` slice or effect gets DUCKY023 and is not registered, and a `[DuckyAction] file record` gets DUCKY020. A type marked `[Obsolete(…, error: true)]` is never referenced by generated code (CS0619 can't be suppressed).
  - Each call into another package is gated on its **exact method symbol**, not on the extension type: `PersistAttributeDefaults<T>` and `Prerender<T>` on `Ducky.Blazor.DuckyBlazorBuilderExtensions`, `AddReactiveEffect<T>` on `Ducky.Reactive.DuckyReactiveExtensions`, each with the expected arity and parameters. When the type or the method doesn't resolve (no reference, or a skewed or older package without that method), nothing is emitted for it and `[Persist]`/`[Prerender]` or the `ReactiveEffect` raise DUCKY022, so a version mismatch never yields CS0117 in generated code.
  - `{Asm}` comes from `Compilation.AssemblyName`: every character that isn't valid in a C# identifier becomes `_`; a leading digit or a C# keyword gets a `_` prefix; `null` becomes `Anonymous`. The rule is table-tested (`AssemblyNameSanitizer_Table`). Both the class name and the method name depend on the assembly (a shared method name across assemblies gives CS0121, which was reproduced). Assembly names that differ only in such characters (`My.App`, `My-App`, `My_App`) map to one name; a consumer referencing two of them gets CS0121. That collision is documented and table-tested rather than disambiguated with a hash, which would break the readable `AddDuckyGenerated_MyCompany_Todos` form.
  - Middleware is never auto-registered: order matters (MW-01).
- **Engineering rules (GEN-06)**:
  - symbol-free equatable models (`EquatableArray<T>`), `WithTrackingName` on each step, and a caching test per step;
  - Verify snapshots, and any diagnostic in the generated compilation fails the test;
  - these generated-code rules apply to **both** generator assemblies, Ducky.Generators and Ducky.Draft.Generators (§13.3): every generated file starts with `// <auto-generated/>` and `#nullable enable` (generated files otherwise get a disabled nullable context, and the `?` annotations would raise CS8669); every public generated type and member carries a `/// <summary>` (as Mutty's output did), so a consumer with `GenerateDocumentationFile` gets no CS1591; references to user types are wrapped in `#pragma warning disable CS0612, CS0618` / `restore`, so an `[Obsolete]` action, slice or effect doesn't break a `TreatWarningsAsErrors` consumer; the emitted directive is exactly `#pragma warning disable CS0612, CS0618 // justification: generated code references user [Obsolete] members (GEN-06)`, so the template line in the generator's source passes `ExclusionGate`'s pragma rule too (§17.1). Test compilations use `DocumentationMode.Diagnose`, `NullableContextOptions.Enable` and warning level 9999, and the shape matrices include an `[Obsolete]` action and slice;
  - **oblivious reference types** (user source compiled with nullable disabled, `NullableAnnotation.None`, typical of Mutty-era and older action records) count as annotated wherever generated code declares them or guards them for null, in both generators: they are emitted with `?` in helper parameters and draft member types (the generator rewrites each oblivious reference-type position to `Annotated` and displays with `FullyQualifiedFormat` plus `SymbolDisplayMiscellaneousOptions.IncludeNullableReferenceTypeModifier`; plain `FullyQualifiedFormat` prints no `?` at all), and a draft getter for an oblivious member gets the null guard a `C?` member gets. So `record Search(string Query = null)` under `#nullable disable` yields `string? Query = null`, never a CS8625 inside the generated `#nullable enable` file. The one exception is a collection element type argument: an oblivious element `T` keeps element drafting and is written non-annotated (`ListDraft<Todo, Todo.Draft>`, as `where T : class` requires), and the element drafts never wrap a null element (§13.2). The shape matrices (`Generated_CompilesClean_{Shape}`) have rows whose user source sits under `#nullable disable`: a GEN-01 record with a `string x = null` default, a draftable record with an oblivious nested draftable member that is null, and an oblivious list with a null element;
  - **hint names**: each generated file's hint name is the type's fully qualified metadata name (namespace, containing types joined by `.`, generic arity) passed through the shared `IdentifierSanitizer`, which turns every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_.]` into `_` (`+` of nested metadata names, `@` of escaped keywords, `<>`, `` ` ``), plus a per-generator suffix (`.DispatchExtensions.g.cs` for GEN-01, `.Draft.g.cs` for the Draft generator); GEN-02 writes one fixed name, `DuckyRegistrations.g.cs`. `AddSource` throws on a duplicate or invalid hint name, and that exception crashes the whole generator (CS8785, no output for any type), so two `[Draftable] partial record Todo` in different namespaces of one compilation, a nested record or a keyword-named record must each get a distinct valid name. `HintNames_UniqueAndValid` (a table test in both generator test projects) and Draft shape-matrix rows for two same-named draftable records in different namespaces, a record nested two levels deep and a record named `@class` pin it;
  - test compilations reference the real `Ducky.dll`, `Ducky.Blazor.dll` and `Ducky.Reactive.dll` (project references of the generator test project), never attribute or method stubs for any symbol GEN-02 gates on, so a signature change to `PersistAttributeDefaults<T>`, `Prerender<T>` or `AddReactiveEffect<T>` fails `Registration_RealBlazor_EmitsPersistAndPrerender_NoDucky022` or `Registration_RealReactive_EmitsAddReactiveEffect_NoDucky022` (every gated call emitted, no DUCKY022, the output compiles) instead of silently turning into DUCKY022 and nothing emitted; the DUCKY022 skew branch is covered by compilations that omit those references or use a hand-written older-shape package fixture, never by replacing the real one. **Staging (§24):** a symbol-resolved emit branch can be covered only once the real assembly holds the method, so each ships with the story that ships its method: step 7 builds GEN-02's slice and effect registration and its "type or method doesn't resolve" DUCKY022 branch (covered by compilations without those references), step 11 the `Prerender<T>` emission, step 12 the `PersistAttributeDefaults<T>` emission and `Registration_RealBlazor_EmitsPersistAndPrerender_NoDucky022` (stage 12), and step 16 the `ReactiveEffect` registration, the `AddReactiveEffect<T>` emission and `Registration_RealReactive_EmitsAddReactiveEffect_NoDucky022` (stage 16). Until then no stub stands in (the rule above), and `CoverageGate` never sees an unreachable emit branch;
  - no public type is emitted other than the two families above;
  - the `CodeWriter` uses C# raw string literals;
  - `AnalyzerReleases.Shipped.md`/`Unshipped.md` release tracking;
  - shared generator sources live in `src/Shared.Generators/`, one helper per file (`EquatableArray.cs`, `CodeWriter.cs`, `IdentifierSanitizer.cs`, `AssemblyNameSanitizer.cs`), and each generator project links (`<Compile Include … Link>`) only the files it uses (the assembly-name sanitizer only into Ducky.Generators). A linked member that one assembly never calls would be uncovered there, so `CoverageGate` itself catches an over-broad link.
- **No code fixes in 2.0** (ADR-0038). In 2.1, code fixes will ship in a separate `Ducky.CodeFixes.dll` (Workspaces reference, RS1038-clean) inside the same `analyzers/dotnet/cs` folder.

### 16.2 Diagnostic catalogue

Every ID has `docs/diagnostics/{ID}.md` (checked by the `Docs` target for every ID in this table), and `HelpLinkUri` points there. Each generator assembly's `AnalyzerReleases.Shipped.md`/`Unshipped.md` lists exactly the descriptors that assembly declares (DUCKY001–025 in Ducky.Generators, DUCKY101–109 in Ducky.Draft.Generators): the release-tracking analyzer reports RS2002 for a listed ID without a `DiagnosticDescriptor`, which is a build error, so runtime (DUCKY300–353), MSBuild (DUCKY900–902) and `[Obsolete]` (DUCKYM001–M008) IDs are never listed there. Misuse is always reported as a diagnostic, never as broken generated code.

| ID | Severity | Rule | Assembly |
|---|---|---|---|
| DUCKY001 | Warning | Non-deterministic call (`DateTime.Now/UtcNow/Today`, `DateTimeOffset.Now/UtcNow`, `Guid.NewGuid`, `Random.*`, `Environment.TickCount*`, `Stopwatch.*`, `TimeProvider.GetUtcNow`) inside an `On<T>` reducer lambda, a `SliceStore.Set`/`SetAsync` mutator, or a `Selector.Define` projector | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY002 | Warning | I/O or blocking in a reducer, mutator or projector (`System.IO.*`, `HttpClient`, `IJSRuntime`, `Console`, `.Result`, `.Wait()`, `GetAwaiter().GetResult()`, `await`) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY003 | Warning | Mutation in a reducer, mutator or projector: assignment or compound assignment to a member of the state or action parameter, to a captured local or field, or a call to a known mutator (`List<T>.Add`, …) on the state graph. Receivers whose type implements `Ducky.Draft.IDraft<T>` or is a `Ducky.Draft` collection draft (matched by metadata name, with no reference to Ducky.Draft) are exempt, and parameters of nested recipe lambdas (`s.Produce(d => …)`) are not state | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY004 | Warning | `Lifetime = ServiceLifetime.Singleton` in a web project (`UsingMicrosoftNETSdkWeb`) that is not Blazor WebAssembly (`UsingMicrosoftNETSdkBlazorWebAssembly != true`), read through the `CompilerVisibleProperty` items that `buildTransitive/Ducky.targets` declares (§3). Verifier tests supply them as `build_property.*` analyzer config options; PackageSmoke asserts it fires in the Server consumer | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY005 | Error | `On<T>`, `EffectGroup.On<T>`, `Effect<T>` or `OfActionType<T>` with `T` abstract, an interface or `object` (exact-type matching can never hit it) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY006 | Warning | A `[DuckyAction]` type or a slice state type declared in this compilation is outside the type set covered by the context passed to `UseJson(X.Default)` or `JsonTypeInfoResolver.Combine(A.Default, …)`. The covered set is defined observably: the set of `T` for which the context type declares a `JsonTypeInfo<T>` property (the union over the contexts of a `Combine`). The STJ source generator emits exactly one such property per type it generates metadata for, the transitively discovered ones included, so the set is exactly what `GetTypeInfo` answers at run time and DUCKY006 agrees with DUCKY306 by construction, without re-implementing STJ's traversal (`[JsonIgnore]`, `[JsonInclude]`, `[JsonConverter]`, dictionary keys, future STJ versions). The rule is a `DiagnosticAnalyzer`, never part of an incremental generator: analyzers see source-generator output for a same-compilation context and metadata for a referenced one, while a generator never sees another generator's output. Verifier cases include a state type reachable only through another root's property and only as a collection element (no diagnostic), and a type reachable only through a `[JsonIgnore]`d property (reported). Silent for any other argument shape; lost context options under `Combine` are DUCKY025. Cross-assembly coverage of persisted/prerendered slices is enforced at runtime (DUCKY306) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY007 | – | reserved for 2.1: action type with no consumer | – |
| DUCKY008 | Warning | An `Effect`/`EffectGroup` constructor injects `IDispatcher` or `IStore` (use `ctx.Dispatch`, so the supersession and disposal rules apply), or a `Middleware` constructor does (use the `Store` property); using the store from a constructor would re-enter materialization (§6.6) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY009 | Warning | `Select(...)` called in `BuildRenderTree` (Razor markup), `OnParametersSet(Async)`, `OnAfterRender(Async)` or an event handler of a `DuckyComponent`/`DuckyLayout` subclass (it must run before the first render). It calls `ConfigureGeneratedCodeAnalysis(Analyze \| ReportDiagnostics)`, because markup exists only in `<auto-generated>` Razor output, and reports at the `#line`-mapped `.razor` location. DUCKY001–003 do not analyze generated code | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY010 | Warning | Blocking on `DispatchAsync`, `SetAsync`, `WhenIdleAsync` or `InitializeAsync` (`.Result`, `.Wait()`, `GetAwaiter().GetResult()`) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY011 | Info | `s with { }` in a reducer registered with `On<T>`: a new reference, so it always notifies | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY012 | Info | A slice with `[Persist]` or `Persist<T>()` relies on the derived `Key` (pin it so renames don't orphan stored data) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY013 | Warning | `ConcurrencyKey` returns a reference type without value equality (not a record, no `Equals` override) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY014 | Error | Duplicate `On<T>` for the same `T` in one slice or effect-group constructor | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY015 | Error | Two slices in one compilation share a `TState` | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY016 | Warning | The assembly defines slices or effects and calls `AddDucky`, but never calls its own `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}` | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY020 | Error | `[DuckyAction]` on a generic type, a type nested in a generic type, an inaccessible or `file`-local type, a type marked `[Obsolete(error: true)]`, or a type without an accessible constructor that is not error-obsolete and takes no error-obsolete parameter type; or its generated extension class name is already declared in the namespace | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY021 | Warning | A generated dispatch helper's base or `Async` name collides with an accessible member of `IDispatcher`, `IStore` (including `IDisposable`/`IAsyncDisposable`), `EffectContext`, `TestStore` or `object`, which C# would bind first; the helper is skipped (set `MethodName`) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY022 | Warning | `[Persist]`/`[Prerender]` on a non-slice type, or without a Ducky.Blazor reference; or a `[Persist]`/`[Prerender]`/`ReactiveEffect` whose registration method (`PersistAttributeDefaults<T>`, `Prerender<T>`, `AddReactiveEffect<T>`) doesn't resolve in the referenced package version (nothing is emitted for it) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY023 | Warning | A slice without a public parameterless constructor, or a `file`-local slice or effect; it is excluded from generated registration | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY024 | Info | Two `[DuckyAction]` types in this compilation, in different namespaces, generate helpers with the same name and parameter types; importing both namespaces makes calls ambiguous (CS0121). Set `MethodName` on one | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY025 | Warning | `UseJson(JsonTypeInfoResolver.Combine(...))` where a combined `JsonSerializerContext`'s `[JsonSourceGenerationOptions]` sets a non-default option (naming policy, options-level converters, number handling, ignore conditions…, through a named argument or through the `JsonSerializerDefaults` constructor argument, any value other than `General`, as in `[JsonSourceGenerationOptions(JsonSerializerDefaults.Web)]`): `Combine` carries no options, so those settings would silently not apply to persisted, seed and DevTools JSON (§10). The message gives the fix, `UseJson(new JsonSerializerOptions(A.Default.Options) { TypeInfoResolver = JsonTypeInfoResolver.Combine(A.Default, B.Default) })`. Verifier cases: two default-option contexts (no diagnostic), one camelCase context (reported), one context annotated `[JsonSourceGenerationOptions(JsonSerializerDefaults.Web)]` (reported), the recommended form (no diagnostic) | Ducky.Generators |
| DUCKY101 | Error | `[Draftable]` on something other than a `partial record class` (including record structs) | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY102 | Error | A containing type of a `[Draftable]` record is not partial | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY103 | Error | `[Draftable]` generic record, or a record nested in a generic type (not supported in 2.0) | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY104 | Error | A `[Draftable]` record already declares a member named `Draft`, `Produce` or `CreateDraft` | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY105 | Warning | A member of a mutable collection type (`List<T>`, `T[]`, `Dictionary<,>`): structural sharing and immutability can't be guaranteed; it is drafted as a plain get/set | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY106 | Warning | A `Produce` recipe lets the draft escape the lambda (assigned to a field or captured variable, or returned) | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY107 | Info | A member's type is `[Draftable]` in metadata but has no compatible generated `Draft`: no nested public `Draft` implementing `IDraft<C>` with a public `Draft(C)` constructor (its assembly was built without the Ducky.Draft generator, with a skewed one, or by hand); the member is drafted as a plain get/set | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY108 | Warning | A `[Draftable]` record is marked `[Obsolete(error: true)]`: generated code can't reference it (CS0619), so no draft is generated | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY109 | Warning | Inside a `Produce` recipe, an expression statement discards the result of a non-void call whose receiver is a plain get/set draft member and whose return type equals the receiver's type (`d.Pending.Enqueue(job);` on an `ImmutableQueue<T>`, `d.Index.Add(k, v);` on an `ImmutableSortedDictionary<K,V>`, `d.Todos.Upsert(todo);` on an `EntityState<,>`): the method returns a new instance, so the edit is lost and `Produce` returns the base. Message: "result discarded: assign it, d.X = d.X.M(...)". Verifier cases for those three, and a false-positive case where the result is assigned | Ducky.Draft.Generators |
| DUCKY300–353 | Error (exception) | Runtime configuration and misuse codes (§8.2) | Ducky / Ducky.Blazor |
| DUCKY900 | Error | MSBuild: `Ducky.Generator` referenced next to Ducky 2.x (also raised by the Ducky.Generator 2.0.0 tombstone package) | `buildTransitive` |
| DUCKY901 | Warning | MSBuild: `Mutty` referenced next to Ducky.Draft | `buildTransitive` |
| DUCKY902 | Error | MSBuild: a resolved Ducky.Blazor package has a major version below 2 next to Ducky 2.x (a 1.x Ducky.Blazor's `Ducky >= 1.x` range admits Ducky 2, and it fails at run time with `TypeLoadException`/`MissingMethodException`); 1.x shipped no Ducky.Reactive or Ducky.Testing package, so there is nothing else to reject | `buildTransitive` (Ducky) |
| DUCKYM001–M008 | Error (`[Obsolete]`) | 1.x tombstones (§22.1) | Ducky / Ducky.Blazor |

Each analyzer rule has `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp.Analyzer.Testing` verifier tests covering positive cases, negative cases and a false-positive corpus: every sample and docs snippet must produce zero DUCKY001–003 hits. Each generator test project also has `Descriptors_MatchCatalogue`, a table test that asserts every descriptor's ID, severity, title, message format and `HelpLinkUri` (`https://github.com/phmatray/Ducky/blob/main/docs/diagnostics/{ID}.md`) against this catalogue, so descriptor string mutants die instead of surviving the verifier tests, which check only IDs, locations and arguments (§17.8). DUCKY009 is tested with the real Razor source generator in the driver (or its output with the `// <auto-generated/>` header kept), never with Razor output fed as plain source.

### 16.3 Multi-assembly fixture (INV-25)

`test/Ducky.Generators.Tests/MultiAssembly` builds three in-memory compilations with `TreatWarningsAsErrors` on:
- `Lib` runs the generator, assembly `MyCompany.Todos`.
- `App` runs the generator and references Lib. Both Lib and App declare `[DuckyAction] record Increment`, in different namespaces.
- `Tests` references both and does not run the generator. It calls:
  - `AddDuckyGenerated_MyCompany_Todos()` and `AddDuckyGenerated_App()` through extension syntax;
  - `Ducky.Generated.App_DuckyRegistrations.AddDuckyGenerated_App(b)` explicitly;
  - both `Increment` extensions with namespace-qualified calls.

The fixture asserts no CS0121, CS0433 or CS0436. App also declares a second `[DuckyAction] record Increment` in another App namespace, and the fixture asserts DUCKY024 on it (the import-both case is reported at build time instead of surfacing as an unexplained CS0121 in a `.razor` file). The same pattern covers Ducky.Draft: a draftable record in Lib used as a member and as a list element in App, a record in App deriving from a draftable Lib record (`new` modifiers), and access to a nested Lib draft after `Produce` returned (it throws: cross-assembly revocation). A second Lib variant references the Ducky.Draft runtime but runs no generator: App's member of that `[Draftable]` type becomes a plain get/set with DUCKY107, and App still compiles. A third Lib variant declares a hand-written nested `Draft : IDraft<C>` without a public `Draft(C)` constructor: the same DUCKY107 plain get/set, and App compiles.

---

## 17. Quality gates and test catalogue (ADR-0037)

### 17.1 Gate summary (all are required checks unless marked nightly)

| Gate | Threshold / rule | Tool | Fallout target | Workflow |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Build | `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, except the NuGetAudit warnings NU1901–NU1904 (`WarningsNotAsErrors` in `Directory.Build.props`), so a newly published advisory never turns every PR's required checks red with no code change: audit is enforced only by `Audit` (below); `AnalysisLevel=latest-recommended` (AnalysisMode Recommended); `Microsoft.VisualStudio.Threading.Analyzers`; PublicApiAnalyzers (RS0016/RS0017 errors); BannedApiAnalyzers; CA2007 in `src/`; `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild`; Nullable; no global `NoWarn` (no CS0618) | MSBuild | `Compile` | `ci` |
| Format | `dotnet format --verify-no-changes` | dotnet | `Format` | `ci` |
| Locked restore | `RestoreLockedMode=true` with `packages.lock.json` | NuGet | `Restore` | `ci` |
| Unit/contract/property tests | all green, with coverage | xUnit v3 on MTP v2 | `Test` | `ci`, `ci-cross-windows`, `ci-cross-macos` |
| Concurrency tests | all green; each has a 10 s hard timeout (`WaitAsync`, §17.3) ×50 repetitions (`DUCKY_REPEAT`); run without coverage | xUnit v3 on MTP v2 | `ConcurrencyTest` | `ci`, `ci-cross-windows`, `ci-cross-macos` |
| Coverage | **100% line and 100% branch** per `src/` assembly, generators included; generated `*.g.cs` excluded by path. Every `src/` project in `Ducky.slnx` must appear in the merged report (a missing one fails); the list comes from the solution, so it grows with the stories of §24. Two projects are exempt, each logged: the NoTargets tombstone, which builds no assembly (known by its `Sdk` attribute), and an assembly with nothing to cover, whose Release PDB has no non-hidden sequence point outside `*.g.cs` (the tool leaves such a module out of the report instead of listing it at 100%, spike S-2; a missing PDB counts as code). The stage-1 `Ducky.Draft` and `Ducky.Testing` skeletons are code-less this way, because their step-1 types (`DraftableAttribute`, `DuckyAssertionException`) are code-less in the final API too. Measured over the deterministic test projects only: `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests` is not in the coverage run at all (it runs in its own `ConcurrencyTest` target, §19), so every branch is covered by a deterministic test and the gate can't flake with interleavings. **Race-only branches are forbidden**: every branch in `src/` must be reachable deterministically, directly, through `FakeTimeProvider`, or through an internal, IVT-only seam: an interleaving hook (an instance `Action?` that is null in production, such as `AfterSubscribeHook`, §6.8) or an instance delegate defaulting to the production call (such as the store's `QueueWorkItem`, §6.7, which lets a test run a queued overflow abort at a chosen point, or `DevToolsOptions.DescribeFrame`, §10, for a branch only a trimmed AOT publish reaches). Every IVT seam must be settable from the owning package's own test project without touching another shipped assembly's internals: it lives on an object that test can reach, such as the store for Ducky (`AfterSubscribeHook`, `BeforeNotifyHook`, `BeforeProcessHook`, `QueueWorkItem`) or an options object reachable from the `configure` lambda for Ducky.Blazor (`DevToolsOptions.DescribeFrame`, `BlazorOptions.AfterDeferHook`, `BlazorOptions.AfterSeedIdleHook`), never on a store-created middleware instance. Code shapes whose branches only a cross-thread race reaches are restructured instead (the Switch/Queue install through `AddOrUpdate` and the CTS ownership rule, §6.6). Tests that are deterministic by nature live in the covered projects, never only in `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests` (§17.3); conversely, every API that starts real concurrency is banned in the covered test projects, by documentation ID (`test/BannedSymbols.covered.txt`, added per project by `Directory.Build.targets` for every test project except `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`): `SampleParallel`, `M:CsCheck.Check.Sample*` outside `test/Shared/Property.cs` (below), `Task.Run`, `Barrier`, `M:System.Threading.Tasks.TaskFactory.StartNew*`, `M:System.Threading.Thread.#ctor*`, `M:System.Threading.ThreadPool.QueueUserWorkItem*`, `M:System.Threading.ThreadPool.UnsafeQueueUserWorkItem*` and `T:System.Threading.Tasks.Parallel`, so a real interleaving always runs under the §17.3 harness. **Property tests are deterministic where a gate measures.** A committed `CsCheck_Seed` alone is not enough: with a seed set, CsCheck's `Sample` runs that seed first and then its other `iter - 1` iterations from randomly seeded per-thread generators, on `CsCheck_Threads` thread-pool workers (default `Environment.ProcessorCount`), so a gated run would still draw random cases and run the predicate concurrently. Therefore: (1) every property test in a covered project goes through one helper, `Property.Check(gen, assert)` in `test/Shared/Property.cs` (linked into each covered test project, §18); when the environment variable `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS` is set, it runs each seed of the committed list `build/property-seeds.txt` with `gen.Sample(assert, seed: s, iter: 1, threads: 1)` (with a seed and `iter: 1`, CsCheck runs exactly that seed), otherwise it calls plain `Sample`; (2) the `Test`, `MutationPr`, `Mutation` and `MutationForSha` targets set `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS=1` and `CsCheck_Threads=1` in the test-host environment, as they set `DUCKY_REPEAT`, so a branch or a mutant that only some random inputs reach can't make `CoverageGate` or a Stryker score flip between the nightly run and the reused release result, and no property predicate runs concurrently in a covered project; (3) `M:CsCheck.Check.Sample*` joins `test/BannedSymbols.covered.txt`, and only `Property.cs` calls it, under `#pragma warning disable RS0030 // justification: the one CsCheck entry point of the covered projects (§17.1)`, so no test can bypass the helper; (4) `PropertyLong` (nightly) leaves `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS` unset and stays random, and every failing seed it prints is appended to `build/property-seeds.txt` by the fixing PR, so it becomes a permanent deterministic case. Every branch a property test reaches must also be reached by an example-based or fixed-seed test. **Post-await cancellation re-checks** use `ThrowIfCancellationRequested` into the existing cancellation path, never a separate `if` (a delay completed by `FakeTimeProvider.Advance` resumes inline, so nothing could cancel between the completion and the `if`: §14). A race test in `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests` whose branches need coverage has a TCS-gated or hook-driven twin in a covered project, named `…_Deterministic`, so each name maps to one project kind. Every §4 invariant except INV-24 has at least one named test in a covered project (`SpecTraceGate` checks it from the manifest, for each invariant whose own stage is active) | Microsoft.Testing.Extensions.CodeCoverage (cobertura) + ReportGenerator | `CoverageGate` | `ci` |
| Spec trace | name consistency, always: every manifest name appears in SPEC.md, and every name in the §4 "Named tests" column is in the manifest. Staged checks, against the manifest's `activeStage` (§24): every entry whose `stage` is at or below it exists in each of its projects, and for every invariant whose own stage (the manifest's `invariants:` map) is at or below it, except INV-24, at least one active entry mapped to it is in a covered project. `--strict` (release gates of `-rc` and GA tags) also fails while any entry is still inactive, and checks all 32 invariants. One matching rule for all three checks: a `{Placeholder}` segment, at any position (as in `Caching_{Step}_Cached`), matches `\w+`, and a listed test matches on its method name, the last `.`-separated segment before any `(` (so a theory listed as `…Name(repeat: 1)` matches `Name`). Manifest names mapped to `Ducky.AotSmoke` are only checked for presence in SPEC.md and §4 here; the `AotSmoke` target checks them against the binary (§19) | `dotnet test --list-tests` (Ducky.E2E, a Microsoft.Playwright xUnit project, included) | `SpecTraceGate` | `ci` |
| Exclusion ban | zero `ExcludeFromCodeCoverage` in `src/` (banned API); `// Stryker disable` only in the form `// Stryker disable once <mutators> : <reason>` (named mutators, no `all`) and no `// Stryker restore`: a non-once or `all` disable ignores every later mutant of the file, which a `--since` run then scores as zero mutants (spike S-6); the next code line after a `disable once` (comments, attributes and directives skipped) must not open a type, namespace or member, whose every mutant it would ignore (§17.8); `#pragma warning disable` in `src/` must carry `// justification:` (a text check; the generators' emitted `#pragma warning disable CS0612, CS0618` lives in raw string literals under `src/`, so it carries the justification too, §16.1); no `#line` directive in `src/` (`#line hidden` hides sequence points and `#line N "x.g.cs"` remaps them onto the path exclusion, so either is a coverage exclusion; a text check). The same effect is available from configuration, so the gate pins it too: exactly one committed coverage settings file, `build/coverage.settings.xml` (the `Test` target passes it with `--coverage-settings`), whose only source exclusion is `**/*.g.cs` (the tool's regex `.*\.g\.cs$`; plus the tool's default `CompilerGenerated`/`GeneratedCode` handling that spike S-2 records), no module, source, function or attribute exclusions beyond that; since those two exclusions must only ever remove generated code, no `*.g.cs` is committed anywhere in the repository outside `artifacts/`, `spikes/` and build output (matched case-insensitively, as the tool matches it; a file elsewhere can be linked into a `src/` project), and no `[GeneratedCode]` type or method of a `src/` Release assembly (containing types included, read from metadata so the generators' raw-string templates don't count) has a sequence point outside `*.g.cs`; no `.cs` file is committed anywhere (with the same exceptions) that is named `*.designer.cs`, `*.generated.cs`, `*.g.i.cs` or `TemporaryGeneratedFile_*`, or holds `<auto-generated` or `<autogenerated` in the blank, comment and directive lines before its first code line (case-insensitive): Stryker mutates no such file, so it counts in no score, and Roslyn analyzers that skip generated code skip it (the generators' raw-string templates don't count, since only leading lines are read); `CoverageGate` passes ReportGenerator no filters other than the explicit assembly list; every `stryker-config.json` has `thresholds {high: 90, low: 85, break: 85}`, `mutate` exactly `["**/*.cs"]` (the whole project under test: Stryker matches the globs relative to that project, so a `src/…` pattern would match no file and report no score) with no `!` pattern, `test-projects` equal to the set computed from the project references (§17.8), a positive `additional-timeout`, `coverage-analysis: "off"` (spike S-6), and no other key: no `mutation-level` override, and no `ignore-mutations` or `ignore-methods` keys (equivalent mutants go through inline `// Stryker disable once … : reason` only) | grep + BannedApi + JSON/XML checks | `ExclusionGate` | `ci` |
| No sleeps | the `Task.Delay` overloads without a `TimeProvider` (by documentation ID, as in §10), `Thread.Sleep`, `SpinWait.SpinUntil`, `DateTime.Now/UtcNow` banned in `test/`; `Task.Delay(TimeSpan, TimeProvider[, CancellationToken])` with a `FakeTimeProvider` is allowed (`PollingEffect_RunsBeyondMaxDepthTicks`, §17.5) | BannedApiAnalyzers (`test/BannedSymbols.txt`) | `Compile` | `ci` |
| Mutation (PR) | Stryker `--since:origin/<base>`, break < 85 (zero mutants in the diff is legitimate; a project whose tests or build inputs changed gets a full run instead, §17.8) | Stryker.NET (`test-runner: mtp`) | `MutationPr` | `mutation` |
| Mutation (full) | score ≥ 90 target (issue opened below), break < 85; fails when an **active** mutated project reports zero mutants or no score (a project is active when its stage in the manifest's `projects:` map is at or below `activeStage`; inactive projects are skipped; with `--strict`, rc and GA tags, all seven) | Stryker.NET | `Mutation` (`MutationForSha` on release) | `nightly-mutation`, `release` |
| JS | Playwright functional specs + **100% block coverage** of `ducky.js` (Chromium CDP precise coverage) | Microsoft.Playwright (.NET) | `E2E` | `e2e` (Chromium); Firefox/WebKit functional in `nightly-e2e` |
| AOT/trim | zero IL2xxx/IL3xxx; NativeAOT console smoke (`Ducky.Aot.slnx`, unlocked, Linux only); trimmed AOT-WASM smoke (`WasmTrimmedSmoke`) | .NET SDK + Playwright | `AotSmoke` | `aot` |
| Package | `EnablePackageValidation` (baseline 2.0.0 committed only after the GA push, §21); nupkg layout and dependency-leak checks; consumer build | dotnet pack | `Pack`, `PackageSmoke` | `ci` |
| Workflows in sync | generated YAML equals the committed files; job names are unique across all workflows | Fallout | `VerifyWorkflows` | `ci` |
| PR title | Conventional Commits (feeds git-cliff) | `amannn/action-semantic-pull-request` | – | `pr-title` |
| Audit | the only place audit is enforced: NU1901–NU1904 as errors, with explicit `<NuGetAuditSuppress>` items only; audit stays off for `Ducky.Migration.slnx`, whose "before" samples reference legacy packages on purpose | NuGet | `Audit` | `nightly-audit` |
| Docs | every snippet is a `#region` of a compiled, tested file: the target fails on any fenced ```` ```csharp ```` block in `docs/**/*.md` except under `docs/spec/**` and `docs/adr/**` (the copied design record, not user docs). "Bad example" snippets (diagnostic pages such as `DUCKY005.md`, `DUCKY014.md`, `DUCKYM00n.md`) come from regions over the raw string literals of the analyzer verifier tests; migration "before" samples come from per-guide projects under `docs/migration/samples/`, in `Ducky.Migration.slnx` outside `Ducky.slnx` (referencing Ducky 1.x, Bustand, Mutty… from nuget.org with explicit versions; `docs/migration/samples/Directory.Build.props` imports the root props and sets `ManagePackageVersionsCentrally=false`, `RestoreLockedMode=false` and `NuGetAudit=false`, as PackageSmoke does, since a versioned `PackageReference` under the root's CPM is NU1008 and a locked restore without a lock file fails; `Docs` restores it with no lock file), each paired with a compiled "after" project; docfx builds | docfx + grep | `Docs` | `ci` |
| Benchmarks | informational, vs a committed baseline | BenchmarkDotNet | `Benchmarks` | `nightly-audit` |

### 17.2 Tooling

| Need | Choice |
|---|---|
| Test framework | xUnit v3 on Microsoft.Testing.Platform v2 (`global.json` `"test": { "runner": "Microsoft.Testing.Platform" }`) |
| Assertions | Shouldly (library tests); `DuckyAssertionException` in Ducky.Testing |
| Mocks | **none**. Hand-written fakes at I/O boundaries only (`FakeJsRuntime` recording every argument, `FakeJsStreamReference`, `FakeComponentStateStore`). `MemoryDistributedCache` is used as-is. The store is always real. `PersistentComponentState` has only an internal constructor and non-virtual members, so it is never faked: tests register a real `ComponentStatePersistenceManager`, forward `PersistentComponentState` to its `State`, and drive it with `FakeComponentStateStore : IPersistentComponentStateStore`. A test reproduces WASM's late restore by calling `manager.RestoreStateAsync(fakeStore)` at the chosen point, and persists with `manager.PersistStateAsync(fakeStore, renderer)` using bUnit's renderer. The fake store's `SupportsRenderMode` accepts only `InteractiveAutoRenderMode` and records what it receives, so `Prerender_OnPersistingRegisteredWithInteractiveAutoMode` is behavioural (the seed is persisted iff the registration's mode is Interactive Auto) |
| Time | `FakeTimeProvider` everywhere; bUnit `WaitForAssertion`. The `ducky.js` harness specs drive every module timer through Playwright's clock API (`page.Clock.InstallAsync()` before the module loads, then `RunForAsync`/`FastForwardAsync`), so the delivery-failure backoff reaches its 30 s cap and the resync with zero real waiting (`DuckyJs_ResyncBackoff_CapsAt30s_ThenReReadsAll`) |
| Logs | `FakeLogCollector` (`Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.Testing`, test projects only). Every EventId of §6–§11 is asserted by some test, so log-statement mutants are killed rather than ignored (§17.1 forbids `ignore-methods`); descriptor strings and `ConfigureAwait` literals follow §17.8 |
| Components | bUnit 2.x (sets `RendererInfo`) |
| Properties / linearizability | CsCheck (`Sample` through `Property.Check` in covered projects, `SampleParallel` with a sequential model in `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`); wherever a gate measures, the committed seed list `build/property-seeds.txt`, one seed per `Sample` call with `iter: 1` and one thread (§17.1); nightly long runs with `CsCheck_Iter=100000` and random seeds |
| Generators | `CSharpGeneratorDriver`, Verify.SourceGenerators, analyzer testing packages |
| Coverage | `Microsoft.Testing.Extensions.CodeCoverage` → cobertura, merged by ReportGenerator, gated by `CoverageGate` (fallback coverlet.MTP per spike S-2) |
| Mutation | `dotnet-stryker` local tool, one `stryker-config.json` per mutated project |
| JS / E2E | Microsoft.Playwright for .NET: one toolchain, CDP `Profiler.startPreciseCoverage({callCount:true, detailed:true})` in every page and browser context of the Chromium run (harness page, two-page cross-tab tests, sample pages). A ~60-line helper collects `Profiler.takePreciseCoverage` from each, keys ranges by (URL normalized to `ducky.js`, `startOffset`, `endOffset`), sums the counts with innermost-range precedence, and fails only on a block whose **merged** count is zero. It runs once, at the end of the `E2E` target |
| Hosts for E2E/AOT | a Playwright fixture runs `dotnet <published dll> --urls http://127.0.0.1:0`, reads the bound port from stdout, polls a health endpoint and kills the process on teardown; the trimmed WASM publish is served from `wwwroot` by a small Kestrel static-file host in `test/Ducky.E2E` |
| Public API | Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.PublicApiAnalyzers |

### 17.3 Named concurrency tests (`test/Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`, TST-03)

Each test is `async`: it runs its choreography with `Task.Run` and then `await task.WaitAsync(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10), TimeProvider.System)`, failing with a thread-dump message on timeout, so a synchronous deadlock (the INV-05 regression) can't hang the run. Each runs `DUCKY_REPEAT` times (default 50; the `MutationPr` and `Mutation` targets set 1 on the Stryker process, §17.8) through `[Theory][MemberData(nameof(Repeat))]`, and uses only Barrier/TCS choreography (no sleeps).

| # | Test | INV |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Dispatch_FromNThreads_AppliesEveryActionExactlyOnce` | 02 |
| 2 | `Reducers_NeverRunConcurrently` (Interlocked "inside" counter ≤ 1) | 01 |
| 3 | `Dispatch_PreservesPerProducerOrder` | 04 |
| 4 | `Dispatch_AfterDrainRelease_NoStrandedAction` | 03 |
| 5 | `Dispatch_OwnerBlocksOnCrossThreadDispatch_DoesNotDeadlock` (fails on 1.x, kept as a regression) | 05 |
| 6 | Ducky.Blazor persistence races (CsCheck `SampleParallel`, through Ducky.Blazor's IVT): `Hydration_ReadCompletesConcurrentlyWithInitAbort_TerminalPrecedesStoreInitialized`, `DistributedCache_StaleAttemptAfterSecondSwitch_NeverRestoredOrWrittenUnderNewScope`, `DistributedCache_UserChangeDuringInitialHydration_OldScopeValuesNeverRestored` (TCS-gated cache), `Write_SkipRacesTerminal_DeferredKeyStillWritten`. Each has a `…_Deterministic` twin in `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` (§17.6); the write race has two, one per `AfterDeferHook` point (`Write_TerminalBetweenReadAndDefer_WriterRechecksAndResignals_Deterministic`, `Write_TerminalAfterDeferAdd_AfterReduceSignalsOnce_Deterministic`) | 14, 16, 17 |
| 7 | `Snapshot_AfterConcurrentReduces_IsNeverStale`, `Snapshot_VersionStrictlyMonotonic`, `Snapshot_NeverTorn` | 07 |
| 8 | `Dispose_DuringConcurrentDispatch_NoExceptionNoLeak_AllTasksComplete` | 02, 10, 29 |
| 9 | `Restore_JsonElementFromDisposedDocument_WhileOtherThreadDrains_Restores`, `ReplayDeterminism_SameActionsSameSnapshot` (replaying the recorded linearization of a concurrent run on a fresh store yields an equal final snapshot); each has a `…_Deterministic` twin in `Ducky.Tests` (§17.5) | 01, 04, 07 |
| 10 | `Subscriber_ReadsStateAndDispatchesDuringNotify_NoDeadlock` (NotRedux regression) | 05, 09 |
| 11 | `DispatchAsync_QueuedBehindOtherThreadsDrain_CompletesAfterReduce` | 07, 10 |
| 12 | `DispatchAsync_ContinuationNeverRunsInlineOnDrainer` | 10 |
| 13 | `Linearizability_DispatchVsModel` (CsCheck `SampleParallel` over the linearizable operations only: awaited `DispatchAsync`, effective at reduce, `ReadState` and `Selection.Value`, against a sequential reducer model). Sync `Dispatch` is not linearizable (§7.3: a non-drainer returns before its action is reduced), so it is checked separately: exactly-once and per-producer order in the final state after `WhenIdleAsync` | 01–04, 07 |
| 14 | `InitBuffer_PreservesUserActionOrderAcrossGate` (property), `InitStart_NeverRunsUnderGate`, `Init_OverflowBetweenFlagAndStart_MiddlewareInitStillRuns`, `InitializeAsync_WithConcurrentDrainer_CompletesAfterStoreInitializedReduced` (another thread drains at `MarkReady`; the awaited `InitializeAsync` observes the hydration restore and `StoreInitialized` committed) | 04, 05, 13 |
| 15 | `SubscriptionCore_RandomPublishInterleavings_LastPublishAlwaysEvaluated` | 19 |
| 16 | `Dispose_WaitsForInFlightDrainBeforeMiddlewareDispose`, `Dispose_FromRacingEffectContinuation_WaitsForDrain`, `SyncDispose_DuringDrain_NoAfterReduceOnDisposedMiddleware` | 29 |
| 17 | `SliceStore_ConcurrentSetFromNThreads_AppliesAll` | 30 |
| 18 | `Queue_InstallRacesCompletionRemove_HandlerRunsOncePerAction`, `Select_ConcurrentWithCommit_NoLostOnChange` (CsCheck `SampleParallel`) | 09, 11 |
| 19 | `Materialization_ConcurrentFirstUse_SecondCallerWaitsThenSucceeds` (two first callers on two threads, a TCS-gated effect constructor; both calls complete, one materialization) | 05 |
| 20 | `Reactive_ResubscribeConcurrentWithDrain_FirstValueCurrent_NoLostSubscriber` (failure-completion resubscribes and timer-driven inner subscriptions racing a producer's drain; each new pipeline's first value is the current snapshot, no subscriber is lost); twin `…_Deterministic` in `Ducky.Reactive.Tests` (§14) | 27 |
| 21 | `DevTools_JumpConcurrentWithSend_RestoresMatchingEntry` (through Ducky.Blazor's IVT: jumps delivered on one thread while the drainer sends on another; every jump restores the entry its index names); twin `…_Deterministic` in `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` (§11.8) | 28 |
| 22 | `TestStore_ProcessedReadWhileLongRunningEffectDispatches_NoException` (the test thread enumerates `Processed` and `Failures` while a `LongRunning` effect keeps dispatching from pool threads; no exception, every snapshot is a prefix of the final list) | – |
| 23 | `Dispose_ConcurrentWithFirstUse_EveryConstructedMiddlewareDisposed` (a first `Dispatch` on one thread, TCS-gated middleware constructors, and `DisposeAsync` on another; every constructed middleware and effect is disposed exactly once and nothing is resolved after its scope is gone); twin `…_Deterministic` in `Ducky.Tests` (§6.6) | 29 |

Only real interleaving tests (Barrier/TCS choreographies across threads, `SampleParallel`) live here. Rows 6, 15 and 21 target Ducky.Blazor internals through the `InternalsVisibleTo` that R-PKG-2 allows to this project; rows 20, 22 and 23 use only public API. Tests that are deterministic by nature (single-threaded, or driven by `FakeTimeProvider` or an interleaving hook) live in the covered projects (`Ducky.Tests`, §17.5; `Ducky.Blazor.Tests`, §17.6), which `CoverageGate` measures: the depth-guard tests of INV-06, the subscriber tests of INV-09 and `Dispose_FromDrainer_DoesNotDeadlock` (a reducer on the test thread calls `DisposeAsync`) are among them.

### 17.4 Effects (INV-11, `test/Ducky.Tests`)

- The full matrix of 4 policies × {global, key A, key B} × {complete, fault, foreign OCE, policy cancel, store dispose}, driven by TCS-gated fakes. Each case asserts the dispatched actions, the `EffectFailed` count and `SlotCount == 0` at idle.
- Named tests: `Switch_OldRunCompletion_DoesNotRemoveNewSlot`, `Switch_SupersededRunIgnoringToken_CannotDispatch`, `Switch_StaleResultQueuedBeforeSupersession_IsDropped`, `Effect_ForeignOce_IsEffectFailed`, `Effect_PolicyCancellation_NeverEffectFailed`, `Effect_FaultDuringDispose_StillObserved` (the 1.x lost-fault bug), `Exhaust_SecondWhileRunning_DroppedAndCounted`, `Queue_ThreeRuns_NeverOverlap_SlotEmptyAtIdle` (the Queue flaw of all three proposals; it also asserts that idle never fires between runs, and its last run completes on a thread-pool thread, so it pins the `finally` order of §6.6), `Effect_KeyedSwitch_ProductB_DoesNotCancelProductA`, `WhenIdle_NeverCompletesWhileEffectCausedActionPending`, `WhenIdle_IgnoresLongRunningEffects`, `EffectContext_Run_HttpTimeout_DispatchesFailed`, `Switch_SupersededRunCallingSliceStoreSet_IsDropped`, `Switch_CancelCallbackThrows_DrainerSurvives`, `Queue_DisposeFromRunWithQueuedSuccessor_CompletesWithoutTimeout`, `Queue_AfterDispose_HandlerNeverInvoked`, `Effect_ConcurrencyKeyThrows_EffectFailedOtherEffectsStart` (a throwing `ConcurrencyKey` and a throwing `EffectGroup` key function next to a healthy effect), `Dispose_FromAfterReduce_NoEffectStartedForThatAction`, `WhenIdle_FromNonLongRunningEffectRun_ThrowsNotHangs` (a Merge effect calling `WhenIdleAsync` and `TestStore.Settled` gets `InvalidOperationException` synchronously, and a `LongRunning` one may wait).
- Property: `EffectPolicies_ModelBased`. Random action streams over 4 policies × 3 keys with random completion order, checked against a reference model: no overlap per key for Queue/Exhaust, only the latest run per key dispatches for Switch, Queue order equals dispatch order, and slots are empty at quiescence.

- `Reducers_DispatchFromReducer_QueuedNotNested` (01), `Dispatch_ReentrantFromSubscriberAndEffectPrefix_EachProcessedOnce` (02), `Snapshot_DispatchAsyncReduced_StateVersionAtLeastCommitVersion` (07): single-threaded tests, so every invariant has a named test in a covered project (§17.1)

- `Drain_ProcessThrowsUnexpectedly_NextActionStillDrained` (through `BeforeProcessHook`, §6.3), `Drain_ProcessThrowsAndLogRethrowsFatal_ActionCompletedAndNextDrained` (`BeforeProcessHook` throws and a logging provider throws `OutOfMemoryException` only for EventId 1012, which `SafeLogger` rethrows: the action's `DispatchAsync` task completes `Failed`, the next queued action is reduced, and `_drainExited` completes, so a following `WhenIdleAsync` completes; swapping `Complete` and the log call, or dropping the inner `try/catch`, fails it), `SafeLogger_ProviderThrows_NeverEscapes`, `Telemetry_ListenerThrows_LaterStepsStillRun` (03), `Tracing_ActionFromOtherThread_ParentIsProducerActivity`, `Tracing_ActivityCurrentRestoredAfterDispatch` (§6.4 step 2), `Lifecycle_ThrowingLoggerProvider_StoreReadyAndDisposeComplete` (13, 29; §6.3), `Select_CommitBetweenAddAndRead_InstalledByDrainer` (through `AfterSubscribeHook`, §6.8)
- `Init_HangingMiddleware_TimesOutAndReleasesBuffer`, `Init_BufferOverflow_AbortsInitDropsNothing`, `Init_OverflowDuringSyncPrefix_AbortsFromRunning`, `Init_AbortCallbackThrows_StoreStillReady`, `Init_MiddlewareInitializeThrowsSynchronously_StoreStillReady`, `Init_OverflowAbort_NeverRunsOnCaller`, `Init_OverflowTwiceBeforeQueuedAbortRuns_QueuedOnce`, `Init_QueuedOverflowAbortRunsAfterComplete_NoOp` (all three through `QueueWorkItem`, §6.7), `Dispose_Twice_IsNoOp`, `SyncDispose_LogsWarningAndDelegates` (Warning 1010, then the `DisposeAsync` path)
- `BrowserLifetime_RootAndScopes_ResolveOneStore`, `ServerLifetime_TwoScopes_TwoStores`, `ServerLifetime_HandlerScope_GetsDistinctStore`, `Lifetime_Default_FollowsIsBrowserSetInConfigure` (table over `IsBrowser` × explicit `Lifetime`, including the non-browser Singleton Warning 1004) (22)
- `Build_EffectCtorDependencyMissing_ReportedWithOtherErrors`, `Build_EffectCtorKeyedDependency_NotReported`, `Build_EffectCtorKeyedDependencyMissingKey_Reported`, `Materialization_CtorThrows_EveryCallRethrowsSameConfigurationException`, `Materialization_CtorThrows_EarlierInstancesDisposed` (31)
- `Init_AutoStartsOnFirstDispatch`, `Init_StoreInitializedExactlyOnce`, `PreInitDispatches_AreBufferedAndReplayedInOrder`, `Restore_BypassesInitBuffer`, `InitBuffer_ActionsAfterReady_FollowBufferedActions`, `Init_MiddlewareSyncPrefixesAllRunBeforeStartReturns`, `Init_Completed_TimerNeverAborts`, `InitializeAsync_DisposedBeforeReady_Completes`, `Init_MiddlewareAwaitsDispatchAsync_TimesOutNotHangs`, `Init_MiddlewareAwaitsWhenIdle_TimesOutNotHangs`, `Init_AllInitsCompleteSynchronously_FirstDispatchReducedInline` (13: a store whose middleware inits all complete synchronously reduces the first `Dispatch` before it returns)
- `SliceKey_FromType_Table`, `SliceStore_InjectedInstance_IsStoreOwnedSlice`, `SliceStore_SameReference_NoCommit`, `SliceStore_StateBeforeAttach_Throws`
- `ActionType_Names_Table`, `Unserializable_Action_DegradesToNameOnce` (a missing type, a `Type`-carrying action and a cyclic payload), `NoStaticMutableFields` (defined below), `NoStaticMutableFields_DetectsPlantedMutableStatic`
- **`NoStaticMutableFields`**, one test with this name in each package's test project (`Ducky.Tests`, `Ducky.Blazor.Tests`, `Ducky.Reactive.Tests`, `Ducky.Draft.Tests`, `Ducky.Testing.Tests`, `Ducky.Generators.Tests`, `Ducky.Draft.Generators.Tests`), reflects over that package's `src/` assembly. It fails on any non-`readonly` static field not declared in a `[CompilerGenerated]` type (the compiler's lambda-cache fields `<>c.<>9__*` are non-readonly `public static` delegate fields of the `<>c` closure class), and on any `static readonly` field outside this allow-list:
  - members and types marked `[GeneratedCode]` or `[CompilerGenerated]` (the `[LoggerMessage]` delegate fields, the STJ source generator's output, the `<>c` singleton `<>9`);
  - Ducky.Blazor's internal wire context `BlazorWireContext` (`JsonSerializerContext`-derived, with its `Default` instance and `JsonSerializerOptions`): the one accepted static cache, because its wire types are fixed and it holds no user or circuit data;
  - fields of delegate types, `object` sentinels (`NullKey`, `Unset`), `EventId`, `ActivitySource`, `string`, frozen/immutable collections (`ImmutableArray<DiagnosticDescriptor>` included), and immutable types owned by the library (`EntityState<,>.Empty`);
  - named immutable framework types: `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.DiagnosticDescriptor`, `SymbolDisplayFormat` and `LocalizableString` (the idiomatic `static readonly` analyzer fields for DUCKY001–025 and DUCKY101–109), `System.Text.Json.JsonEncodedText` (the envelope property names written with `Utf8JsonWriter`, §11.5), `System.Buffers.SearchValues<T>` and `System.Text.CompositeFormat`.
  - under coverage only, the tracker type that MTP CodeCoverage's static instrumentation injects into the audited assembly (on every OS: dynamic instrumentation records nothing on macOS): a type whose namespace is exactly `Microsoft.CodeCoverage.Instrumentation.Static.Tracker` **and** whose name starts with `StaticManagedTrackerTemplate_`. It is the tool's state, not the library's; a type matching only one of the two is still audited.
  - under mutation only, the two helper types Stryker.NET injects into the mutated assembly: a type named exactly `MutantControl` or `MutantContext` **and** whose namespace is `Stryker` followed by one or more ASCII letters or digits (a per-run random suffix, spike S-6). Without it the audit fails under every mutant and kills it, so every mutant counts as detected; a type matching only one of the two is still audited.

  `NoStaticMutableFields_DetectsPlantedMutableStatic` runs the same check on a fixture assembly with one planted mutable static, which must be reported, and one planted `static readonly DiagnosticDescriptor` and one non-capturing lambda (whose compiler cache is a non-readonly static in `<>c`), which must not, so the list is exact in both directions. The same fixture plants a mutable static in a `StaticManagedTrackerTemplate_*` type of the tracker namespace (not reported), in another type of that namespace and in a `StaticManagedTrackerTemplate_*` type of another namespace (both reported). Likewise it plants mutable statics in `MutantControl` and `MutantContext` of a `Stryker<suffix>` namespace (not reported), in another type of that namespace and in a `MutantControl` of another namespace (both reported). The causal and effect-run scopes are per-store instance fields (§6.5), so no `AsyncLocal` is on the list.
- Selectors (properties): `Memoized_EqualsUnmemoized`, `SameInputRefs_SameOutputInstance`, `ArgSelector_ArgChange_Recomputes`, `TwoStores_ShareDefinition_NoSharedCache`, `Select_DedupesOnProjectedValue` (the `WhenSliceChanges` regression), `TypedSelect_FastPath_SkipsProjectorWhenSlotUnchanged`
- `EntityState_ModelBased` (property against `Dictionary` + insertion-ordered `List`), `EntityState_NoOp_ReturnsSameInstance`, `EntityState_JsonRoundTrip`
- `ReplayDeterminism_SameActionsSameSnapshot_Deterministic` (single-threaded twin of the §17.3 row 9 property: a recorded interleaving replayed on a fresh store yields an equal final snapshot)

### 17.6 Blazor (`test/Ducky.Blazor.Tests`, bUnit) and E2E (`test/Ducky.E2E`, Playwright)

### 17.7 Generators, Draft, Reactive, Testing

- Generators: the multi-assembly fixture (§16.3), `Caching_{Step}_Cached` per tracking name, Verify snapshots, a shape matrix (records, positional/non-positional, defaults, record structs with defaults, nested at several depths, `file`-local types, `[Obsolete]` actions and slices, `[Obsolete(error: true)]` action records, constructors and parameter types (DUCKY020 when no constructor remains), global namespace, keyword names and keyword-named or case-only-differing parameters), `Helper_RecordStructWithDefaults_DispatchesDefaultValues` (asserts the dispatched payload), `AssemblyNameSanitizer_Table`, `HintNames_UniqueAndValid` (§16.1, in both generator test projects), `Registration_RealBlazor_EmitsPersistAndPrerender_NoDucky022` (stage 12) and `Registration_RealReactive_EmitsAddReactiveEffect_NoDucky022` (stage 16) (against the real Ducky.Blazor and Ducky.Reactive assemblies, §16.1), GEN-02 rows for an effect whose only constructor injects `HttpClient` and, at stage 16, a reactive effect likewise (registered, no DUCKY023), rows under `#nullable disable` (GEN-06: a `string x = null` default), `Descriptors_MatchCatalogue` (§16.2, in both generator test projects), and verifier tests per analyzer rule. Generated-code compilations run with `DocumentationMode.Diagnose`, nullable enabled and warning level 9999 (§16.1).
- Draft: the INV-26 properties (CsCheck random nested records and random edit scripts; the edit generator of `Produce_EqualsManualWithExpressions` includes assigning null, replacing a collection through an implicit conversion, replacing a nested draft, assigning a record to a draftable member, and no-op edits on `ImmutableArray<T>` members). Its oracle is a **structural** comparer, never record `Equals` (synthesized equality compares `ImmutableList`/`ImmutableDictionary` members by reference, so a correct `Produce` would never equal the `with` result once a collection changed): a recursive element-wise comparison that also asserts the identity of dictionary `KeyComparer`/`ValueComparer` and set `Comparer`. oblivious list with a null element), with a nullable member whose original is null and nullable-annotated elements and values (`ImmutableList<Todo?>`, `ImmutableArray<Todo?>`, `ImmutableDictionary<string, Todo?>`, never element-drafted), × member accessibility (a private property, a protected property, an internal property of an internal type in a public record) and a
