### 17.8 Mutation testing

- One `stryker-config.json` per mutated project (Ducky, Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive, Ducky.Draft, Ducky.Testing, Ducky.Generators, Ducky.Draft.Generators): `"test-runner": "mtp"`, `thresholds: { high: 90, low: 85, break: 85 }`, `mutate: ["**/*.cs"]` (Stryker.NET matches the globs relative to the project under test, whose files are `Dispatcher.cs`, `Execution/…`, never `src/…`; this equals its default, the whole project), and nothing that narrows it (`ExclusionGate` pins these keys, §17.1).
- Each config's `test-projects` lists every covered test project that references the mutated assembly, the same set whose cobertura files feed that assembly's coverage in `CoverageGate` (a line of Ducky covered only by `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` or `Ducky.Testing.Tests` would otherwise come back NoCoverage and count against the thresholds), **plus `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`** for the assemblies it references (Ducky, Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive, Ducky.Testing), run once per mutant through `DUCKY_REPEAT=1` (below). `ExclusionGate` checks that each list equals the set computed from the project references.
- `Mutation` (full) and `MutationForSha` fail when any **active** mutated project reports zero mutants or no score (a pattern that silently matches nothing must not pass as a green run); `MutationPr` with `--since` may legitimately report zero, but only for a project none of whose other inputs changed: with `--since` and `coverage-analysis: "off"` Stryker cannot map a changed test to the mutants it covers, so a diff that weakens or deletes tests, or changes a shared helper, scores zero mutants (spike S-6). `MutationPr` therefore lists the paths changed since the merge base with the base commit (committed and uncommitted) and gives a project a full run, with the zero-mutant check, when one is a non-`.cs` file of `src/<project>/` (its csproj, `stryker-config.json`, lock file), lies in the directory of one of its `test-projects`, in `test/Shared/` or directly in `test/` (the banned-symbol lists), is a `Directory.Build.*` or `Directory.Packages.props` at the root or under `src/` or `test/`, or is `global.json`, `.config/dotnet-tools.json` or `build/property-seeds.txt`. It also gives a full run to a project active now but not in the `docs/spec/tests.yaml` of the merge base (or with no manifest there): the change that raises `activeStage` seldom touches the project's `.cs` files, so a `--since` run would never score the code that landed while the project was inactive (a change to the manifest alone triggers nothing: every story edits it). For the same reason it fails when the manifest's `activeStage` is below the merge base's: `activeStage` only moves up, and a lowered one would silently stop mutating active projects. A `--since` run fails when any mutant of a changed `.cs` file of `src/<project>/` is `Ignored` by the since filter ("Removed by since filter", "Mutant not changed compared to target commit"): Stryker rescores every mutant of a changed file, so that is a `--since` diff of the wrong tree (the linked-worktree bug of spike S-6), which would otherwise pass with nothing scored. Any, not every: in that bad run a mutant under a `// Stryker disable once` comment keeps the comment's reason. Every run, `--since` or full, also fails when Stryker wrote no `mutation-report.json` with a `files` object (Stryker writes one even with zero mutants, so a missing report is a broken run, never "zero mutants"), when a `Killed` mutant has no `killedBy` test ids (the runner stopped reporting them, so the next check would pass vacuously), and, on a full run only, when at least 10 killed mutants spread over at least 3 files were all killed by one test: such a test fails on every mutated build whatever the active mutant (as `NoStaticMutableFields` did on Stryker's injected helper types before the §17.5 exemption, spike S-6) and would make every score 100%. A `--since` run scores only the changed files, where one broad test (an end-to-end or golden-snapshot test) can rightly kill every mutant; a test that fails on every mutated build comes from a test change, which gets a full run, or from Stryker, which the nightly catches. The guard is staged like the manifest: `docs/spec/tests.yaml` has `projects: { Ducky: 2, Ducky.Generators: 7, Ducky.Testing: 9, Ducky.Blazor: 10, Ducky.Reactive: 16, Ducky.Draft: 17, Ducky.Draft.Generators: 17 }` (the §24 step that gives each project real logic), and both targets fail when that map differs from this one in a name or a stage, since a raised stage would silently skip a project; both targets skip the Stryker run of every project whose stage is above `activeStage`, whose one skeleton type (§24 step 1) typically yields no mutant, and apply the zero-mutant check to the others. With `--strict` (set for `-rc.N` and GA tags, as for `SpecTraceGate`) they run and check all seven; at those tags every project is active anyway, because `SpecTraceGate --strict` refuses inactive entries. `Mutation` also takes an optional, repeatable `--project <name>`, so the nightly can be split into per-project jobs if S-9 says so.
- `stryker-config.json` has no environment-variable setting, so the `MutationPr` and `Mutation` targets start the Stryker process with `DUCKY_REPEAT=1` (and `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS=1`, `CsCheck_Threads=1`) in its environment (they run the `dotnet-stryker` local tool as `dotnet stryker` from `src/<project>`, spike S-6), which the test hosts it launches inherit: concurrency tests run once per mutant instead of 50 times. Every config sets an `additional-timeout` that kills a deadlocking mutant quickly, and `"coverage-analysis": "off"`: with several test projects the MTP runner's per-test coverage keeps only the last project's hits and reports the other mutants `NoCoverage` (spike S-6).
- `MutationPr` runs Stryker in the checkout when `.git` is a directory (a CI clone). In a linked git worktree (`.git` is a file) Stryker 4.16's `--since` reads the main checkout's working tree instead (spike S-6), so the target resolves the base ref to a SHA, makes a throwaway plain clone of `HEAD` in `artifacts/mutation/clone` (`git clone --no-local --single-branch`), commits the checkout's uncommitted changes, tracked and untracked, on top, fetches the base commit into it and runs Stryker there. Locally, `--base-ref <commit>` names the base: `--base-ref HEAD` scores a story's uncommitted work.
- PR: `--since:origin/<base>` (required). Nightly (`nightly-mutation`): full run. Release: gates on the successful nightly result for the tagged SHA, and runs the full `Mutation` target itself only when none exists (§20.1). The HTML report is an artifact; below 90 an issue is opened automatically.
- Equivalent mutants are documented inline with `// Stryker disable once <mutator> : <reason>` directly above the one statement that holds them: `once` covers the next syntax node, so above a member or a type it ignores every mutant inside (spike S-6). `ExclusionGate` rejects a disable without a reason, one whose reason starts (in any case) like Stryker's own (`Removed by`, `Mutant`, `Ignored via`), and one whose next code line opens a type, namespace or member. Every `Mutation`/`MutationPr` run logs per project how many mutants were `Ignored` by such comments (a reason that does not start with `Removed by` or `Mutant`, Stryker's own), and fails when a comment ignored a mutant ending more than 5 lines below it (a backstop for placements the text check misses, such as a block statement).
- **Systematic survivors are planned for, not ignored.** (a) CA2007 puts `.ConfigureAwait(false)` on every await in `src/`, and flipping that literal is unobservable under xUnit and bUnit (no blocking `SynchronizationContext`). Spike S-6 records whether Stryker's boolean mutator mutates it; if it does, `src/` awaits a `Task` with `ConfigureAwait(ConfigureAwaitOptions.None)` (no boolean literal; S-6 also confirms CA2007 accepts it), and only the `ValueTask` and `await using` lines, which have no options overload, carry `// Stryker disable once Boolean : ConfigureAwait is unobservable without a SynchronizationContext`. `ignore-methods` stays banned (§17.1). (b) Analyzer verifier tests check IDs, locations and arguments, not descriptor strings, which would leave about four string mutants per descriptor; `Descriptors_MatchCatalogue` (§16.2), in both generator test projects, asserts every descriptor's ID, severity, title, message format and `HelpLinkUri`.

### 17.9 AOT, API, packaging

- `IsAotCompatible` in every runtime library (the net10.0 packages; generator and tombstone projects excluded, §3), enforced by a `Compile` check. IL2xxx/IL3xxx are errors. Smoke tests: §10.
- PublicAPI: `PublicAPI.Shipped.txt`/`Unshipped.txt` per package. RS0016/RS0017 are errors, so API changes need review.
- Package validation: `EnablePackageValidation=true`. `PackageValidationBaselineVersion=2.0.0` is committed only after the 2.0.0 packages are on nuget.org: the maintainer runs `./build.sh SetBaseline` in an ordinary PR once the push succeeded (§21), so the required checks run on it; the tagged GA commit has no baseline.
- `PackageSmoke` (`test/Ducky.PackageSmoke`) lives in its own `Ducky.PackageSmoke.slnx`, outside `Ducky.slnx` (so the locked `Restore` never sees it), with `RestoreLockedMode=false` and `ManagePackageVersionsCentrally=false`, and is built with `-p:DuckyVersion=$(MinVerVersion)`. Its own `test/Ducky.PackageSmoke/nuget.config` clears inherited sources and lists nuget.org plus three local feeds, `artifacts/packages`, `artifacts/tombstone` and the committed test feed `test/Ducky.PackageSmoke/feed/`, with `<packageSourceMapping>` that routes `Ducky`, `Ducky.*`, `Ducky.Generator` and `Mutty` **only** to the local feeds and everything else (`Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, R3, …) to nuget.org, so the real Ducky.Generator 1.0.292 on nuget.org can never shadow the committed fake. It sets `RestorePackagesPath=artifacts/smoke-packages`, so no copy cached in the developer's global packages folder (the real 1.0.292, or an alpha of the same version built from other content) is ever used, and the `PackageSmoke` target deletes `artifacts/smoke-packages` before restoring: MinVer's version doesn't change with uncommitted edits, so a local rerun at the same height would otherwise silently test the packages it extracted last time. Its consumers and assertions are **staged** like the manifest (§24): step 1 creates the smoke solution with only what the skeleton packages can pass (the nuspec, layout and R-PKG checks of steps 2 and 3, and the DUCKY900/DUCKY902 checks of step 4 and of the step-1 variants, since `buildTransitive/Ducky.targets` and the tombstone ship from step 1), and each later story adds the consumer or assertion of the feature it ships: the generator consumers and DUCKY004 at step 7, the Blazor consumers' `[Persist]`/`[Prerender]` slices at step 12, the `ducky.js` publish check of step 5 at step 12, the Reactive consumer at step 16 and the Draft consumer at step 17. It:
  1. builds a WASM consumer and a Server consumer with `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, calling `AddDuckyGenerated_PackageSmoke()` and a generated dispatch helper. A third consumer references **only** Ducky.Blazor (the normal install): it must compile a generated dispatch helper and `AddDuckyGenerated_*`, must raise DUCKY004 in its Singleton variant, and must raise DUCKY900 when Ducky.Generator is added (R-PKG-5). A fourth consumer references **only** Ducky.Draft, with `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, nullable and `GenerateDocumentationFile`: a `[Draftable] partial record` with a nested draftable member, `Produce` and `CreateDraft().Build()` (ADR-0002: usable without Ducky); its variant that also references the committed fake `Mutty` nupkg must raise DUCKY901. A fifth consumer references **only** Ducky.Reactive, declares a `ReactiveEffect` and calls `AddDuckyGenerated_*`, so the generator-emitted `AddReactiveEffect<T>` call is compiled from packages. The Server consumer also declares a `[Persist(Version = 2, SyncAcrossTabs = true)]` slice and a `[Prerender]` slice, so the generator-emitted `PersistAttributeDefaults<T>` and `Prerender<T>` calls compile from packages under `TreatWarningsAsErrors` (no DUCKY022). A variant of the Server consumer that also references the committed fake `Ducky.Blazor.1.0.292.nupkg` (a stub lib assembly) must fail with DUCKY902. The Server consumer (implicit usings plus `global using Ducky;`) also implements `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Http.IMiddleware`, proving no tombstone makes it ambiguous, and a variant that sets `Lifetime = Singleton` must fail under `-warnaserror` with DUCKY004;
  2. inspects every `.nuspec`: Ducky's dependencies are exactly the two Extensions abstractions; the Ducky dependency of Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive and Ducky.Testing carries no `exclude` attribute (R-PKG-5); `analyzers/dotnet/cs/Ducky.Generators.dll` is present; Ducky.Draft's nuspec has zero dependencies and its package holds `analyzers/dotnet/cs/Ducky.Draft.Generators.dll` and `buildTransitive/Ducky.Draft.targets`; Ducky.Blazor's package holds `staticwebassets/ducky.js` and the Razor SDK's `build/Ducky.Blazor.props` and `buildTransitive/Ducky.Blazor.props`; the tombstone's nuspec has no dependency groups (`SuppressDependenciesWhenPacking`, §3); `artifacts/packages` contains no `Ducky.Generator.*.nupkg` (the tombstone is only in `artifacts/tombstone`, so the `Publish` push of `artifacts/packages/*.nupkg` can never carry it); no dependency of any package uses a bracketed exact range such as `[1.3.0]` (R3 is a minimum, §3); nothing matches R-PKG-1;
  3. runs `dotnet list package --include-transitive` and fails on forbidden IDs;
  4. asserts that DUCKY900 fires when Ducky.Generator 1.0.292 is referenced (a committed minimal fake `Ducky.Generator.1.0.292.nupkg` with no assemblies in the local test feed; the condition only needs the PackageReference to exist), and that the Ducky.Generator 2.0.0 tombstone package (from `artifacts/tombstone`) fails a consumer build with DUCKY900;
  5. publishes the WASM consumer and the Server consumer from the packed nupkgs and asserts that the published static-web-assets endpoints manifest (or `wwwroot/_content/Ducky.Blazor/`) contains `ducky.js` (a fingerprinted name is allowed): E2E drives the samples through `ProjectReference`, so this is the only check that a consuming app can actually serve the module.


---

## 18. Repository layout (ADR-0001)

Work happens in `phmatray/Ducky` on the orphan branch **`v2`** (`git switch --orphan v2`). 1.x history and tags stay on `main`. `v2` is made the repository's **default branch** at the start of development (§24 step 1), because GitHub runs `schedule` workflows only from the default branch and the nightly workflows live only on `v2`. At GA `main` is renamed `release/1.x` (no further releases), `v2` becomes `main`, and `main` is the default branch again.

```
Ducky/
├─ .config/dotnet-tools.json        Fallout.GlobalTool 10.4.0, dotnet-stryker, dotnet-reportgenerator-globaltool, docfx
├─ .github/
│  ├─ workflows/ ci.yml ci-cross-windows.yml ci-cross-macos.yml e2e.yml aot.yml mutation.yml
│  │             nightly-mutation.yml nightly-e2e.yml nightly-props.yml nightly-audit.yml               (GENERATED by Fallout)
│  ├─ workflows/ release.yml pr-title.yml docs.yml                                           (hand-written, header says so)
│  ├─ renovate.json  pull_request_template.md  CODEOWNERS
├─ build/  _build.csproj  Build.cs  Build.Gates.cs  Build.Test.cs  Build.Release.cs  Build.CI.cs  DuckyGitHubActionsAttribute.cs  protect-branch.sh
│          property-seeds.txt (the committed CsCheck seed list of the gated runs, §17.1)
│          coverage.settings.xml (the one coverage settings file: only the `*.g.cs` source exclusion, §17.1)
├─ build.sh  build.ps1  build.cmd
├─ global.json                       sdk 10.0.100, rollForward latestFeature, allowPrerelease false; "test": { "runner": "Microsoft.Testing.Platform" }
├─ Directory.Build.props             LangVersion latest, Nullable, ImplicitUsings, TreatWarningsAsErrors, AnalysisLevel latest-recommended,
│                                    EnforceCodeStyleInBuild, GenerateDocumentationFile (src), Deterministic, ContinuousIntegrationBuild (CI),
│                                    MinVerTagPrefix v, MinVerMinimumMajorMinor 2.0, MinVerDefaultPreReleaseIdentifiers alpha.0,
│                                    EnablePackageValidation (packable), RestoreLockedMode (CI), IsPackable=false (R-PKG-6),
│                                    RepoRoot=$(MSBuildThisFileDirectory), PackageOutputPath=$(RepoRoot)artifacts/packages/ (anchored:
│                                    a relative path resolves per project, into src/<Project>/artifacts/),
│                                    WarningsNotAsErrors NU1901;NU1902;NU1903;NU1904,
│                                    SignAssembly=true + AssemblyOriginatorKeyFile=$(RepoRoot)ducky.snk for src AND test projects,
│                                    DuckyPublicKey (the full public key of ducky.snk, defined once, for InternalsVisibleTo)
├─ Directory.Build.targets           PublicApiAnalyzers + BannedApiAnalyzers + VSTHRD + CA2007 for src; test defaults for test;
│                                    DuckyUseGenerators / DuckyUseDraftGenerator switches for in-repo consumers (below)
├─ Directory.Packages.props          CPM + CentralPackageTransitivePinningEnabled; Roslyn pinned (S-1); R3 pinned
├─ BannedSymbols.txt  test/BannedSymbols.txt  test/BannedSymbols.covered.txt  cliff.toml  Ducky.slnx  Ducky.Tests.slnf  Ducky.PackageSmoke.slnx  Ducky.Aot.slnx  Ducky.Migration.slnx
│  ducky.snk  LICENSE (Apache-2.0)  NOTICE  CHANGELOG.md
├─ src/
│  ├─ Ducky/                          PublicAPI.*.txt, buildTransitive/Ducky.targets, Migration/Tombstones.cs
│  ├─ Ducky.Generators/               netstandard2.0 → packed into Ducky; AnalyzerReleases.*.md
│  ├─ Ducky.Blazor/                   Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Razor; wwwroot/ducky.js; Migration/Tombstones.cs
│  ├─ Ducky.Reactive/
│  ├─ Ducky.Draft/                    buildTransitive/Ducky.Draft.targets
│  ├─ Ducky.Draft.Generators/         netstandard2.0 → packed into Ducky.Draft
│  ├─ Ducky.Testing/
│  ├─ Ducky.Generator.Tombstone/      Microsoft.Build.NoTargets; packs Ducky.Generator 2.0.0 into artifacts/tombstone/ (buildTransitive only;
│  │                                  IncludeSymbols=false, SuppressDependenciesWhenPacking=true, §3)
│  └─ Shared.Generators/              EquatableArray.cs, CodeWriter.cs, IdentifierSanitizer.cs, AssemblyNameSanitizer.cs (linked per use, not a project)
├─ test/
│  ├─ Ducky.Tests/  Ducky.Concurrency.Tests/  Ducky.Generators.Tests/ (MultiAssembly/)
│  ├─ Ducky.Blazor.Tests/  Ducky.Blazor.Generated.Tests/ (step 12)  Ducky.Reactive.Tests/  Ducky.Draft.Tests/  Ducky.Draft.Generators.Tests/  Ducky.Testing.Tests/
│  ├─ Shared/Property.cs              the one CsCheck entry point of the covered projects (§17.1), linked into each
│  ├─ Ducky.Docs.Tests/  Ducky.PackageSmoke/ (+ feed/, nuget.config)  Ducky.AotSmoke/ (in Ducky.Aot.slnx only)
│  └─ Ducky.E2E/                      Microsoft.Playwright tests; harness/ (static page for ducky.js specs); host fixture + static-file host
├─ samples/  Ducky.Samples.Wasm/  Ducky.Samples.Server/ (+ .Client, Interactive Auto)   (referenced by tests only through E2E URLs: ENG-04)
├─ benchmarks/ Ducky.Benchmarks/ (+ baseline/*.json)
├─ spikes/     week-1 throwaway prototypes (§23), outside every solution and gate
└─ docs/  docfx.json  guides/  threading.md  diagnostics/{ID}.md  migration/{ducky-1x,bustand,theblazorstate,blazormvu,mutty,duckynext,blazor-state}.md
          migration/ducky-1x-api.txt (the Ducky/Ducky.Blazor 1.0.292 public type list, checked by the Docs target, §22.1)
          migration/samples/{guide}/{Before,After}/ (Ducky.Migration.slnx; its Directory.Build.props imports the root one and sets
          ManagePackageVersionsCentrally=false, RestoreLockedMode=false and NuGetAudit=false, as PackageSmoke does, §17.1)
          spec/SPEC.md, spec/tests.yaml and adr/ (SPEC.md and adr/ copied from this spec at repository creation; tests.yaml written
          then from §4, §14 and §17, each entry with its `stage` (§24) and one top-level `activeStage`; SpecTraceGate reads tests.yaml
          and checks it against SPEC.md)
```

**Strong naming and friend assemblies.** Every shipped assembly is strong-named with `ducky.snk` (§3), and a strong-named assembly whose `InternalsVisibleTo` names a friend without a public key fails with CS1726, so the test projects that receive IVT (R-PKG-2) are signed with the same key, and every IVT item carries it: `<InternalsVisibleTo Include="Ducky.Tests" Key="$(DuckyPublicKey)" />`. `Directory.Build.props` signs `src/` and `test/` projects alike, so step 1's skeleton builds. A signed assembly that references one without a strong name gets CS8002; step 1 records any such reference (a test-only package, R3 for Ducky.Reactive), and each is suppressed only in the referencing project's csproj, with a justification comment, never globally. The key is checked in, so the `@ducky/` reservation of §6.1 guards against accidents, not against a determined user.

Hermeticity (ENG-04): library test projects reference only `src/` projects. `dotnet test` and `dotnet build Ducky.slnx` on a clean machine need no workload: no project in `Ducky.slnx` sets `RunAOTCompilation` or `PublishAot` (§10). Only the `aot` job installs `wasm-tools`.

**In-repo generator consumers.** Generator assets never flow through a `ProjectReference` (R-PKG-5), so `Directory.Build.targets` wires them for projects that reference `src/`: a project that sets `DuckyUseGenerators=true` gets `<ProjectReference Include="$(RepoRoot)src/Ducky.Generators/Ducky.Generators.csproj" OutputItemType="Analyzer" ReferenceOutputAssembly="false" />` and an `<Import Project="$(RepoRoot)src/Ducky/buildTransitive/Ducky.targets" />` (the `CompilerVisibleProperty` items DUCKY004 reads, and DUCKY900/902); `DuckyUseDraftGenerator=true` does the same for `src/Ducky.Draft.Generators` and `src/Ducky.Draft/buildTransitive/Ducky.Draft.targets`. They are set by `samples/Ducky.Samples.Wasm`, `Ducky.Samples.Server` and its `.Client` (Ducky), `test/Ducky.AotSmoke` (both: it exercises Draft), `test/Ducky.Docs.Tests` (both), `test/Ducky.Draft.Tests` (Draft), `test/Ducky.Blazor.Generated.Tests` (Ducky: it holds only `PersistAttribute_NoArguments_SlicePersisted`, `PersistAttributePlusBuilderMigrate_Composes` and `AddBlazor_AfterGeneratedPersist_ConfigureApplied`, which run on the calls the generator emits from `[Persist]`, never on hand-written ones, and the `[Persist]`/`[Prerender]` fixtures they share, written to compose. It is separate from `Ducky.Blazor.Tests`, which does not set the switch, because `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}()` registers every accessible slice and effect of its compilation: in a project with hundreds of fixtures it would register other tests' slices (same-named top-level `CounterSlice`s in different namespaces derive one short key, DUCKY304, ADR-0031), effects that inject fakes those three tests don't register (DUCKY309) and every other `[Persist]` slice. It arrives at §24 step 12 with its tests, joins `Ducky.Tests.slnf`, the coverage run and the computed Stryker `test-projects` of Ducky.Blazor and Ducky, and gets Ducky.Blazor's `InternalsVisibleTo` like `Ducky.Blazor.Tests` (R-PKG-2); test code that deliberately breaks an analyzer rule suppresses it with a justified pragma), `benchmarks/Ducky.Benchmarks` (Ducky) and every `docs/migration/samples/*/After` project (as its guide needs). The generator test projects use `CSharpGeneratorDriver` instead. PackageSmoke remains the check of the packaged path (§17.9).

---

## 19. Fallout build (ADR-0035)

`build/_build.csproj` references `Fallout.Common` 10.4.0 (exact pin). The Fallout global tool 10.4.0 is pinned in the tool manifest. The build uses only `FalloutBuild`, `IHasSolution`, `IConfigureGitHubActions` and the `DotNetTasks` wrapper, which also runs the tool manifest's local tools (`dotnet reportgenerator`, `dotnet stryker`; `ReportGeneratorTasks` and `StrykerTasks` would resolve those tools as NuGet packages of the build project, a second pin, spike S-6). **It does not compose `ITest` or `IReportCoverage`.** Their sealed `TestSettingsBase` adds `--logger trx` and coverlet properties whenever `IsServerBuild`, and Fallout has no Microsoft.Testing.Platform support. `IPack`/`IPublish`/`ICreateGitHubRelease` are not composed either (the Octokit path is removed per ENG-05). Every target is a thin, auditable wrapper, so replacing Fallout with plain workflow steps is mechanical if it stalls.

```csharp
partial class Build : FalloutBuild, IHasSolution, IConfigureGitHubActions
{
    public static int Main() => Execute<Build>(x => x.Ci);
    AbsolutePath Artifacts => RootDirectory / "artifacts";
    [Parameter] readonly string ReleaseBranch = "v2";              // the branch releases come from; flipped to "main" by the GA rename PR (§21)
    [Parameter] readonly string BaseRef;                            // PR base for Stryker --since; default origin/$GITHUB_BASE_REF,
                                                                    // else origin/{ReleaseBranch} (never a hard-coded branch name)
    [Parameter] readonly string ReleaseVersion;                     // version the release notes and GitHub release are labelled with (no `v`):
                                                                    // default GITHUB_REF_NAME minus its `v` in release.yml, else MinVerVersion
                                                                    // read with `dotnet msbuild src/Ducky/Ducky.csproj -getProperty:MinVerVersion`
}
```

Every `--strict` in this spec (`SpecTraceGate`, `ReleaseGates`, `Mutation`, `MutationForSha`) is spelled `--strict-stages` on the Fallout command line, one `StrictStages` parameter shared by those targets: Fallout 10.4 reserves `--strict` for its execution planner, which then demands one total target order and fails every run (spike S-6).

| Target | Depends on | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `Clean` | – | deletes `artifacts/`, `bin/`, `obj/` |
| `Restore` | – | `dotnet restore Ducky.slnx` (locked mode in CI; PackageSmoke is not in this solution) |
| `Format` | Restore | `dotnet format Ducky.slnx --verify-no-changes --no-restore` |
| `Compile` | Restore | `dotnet build Ducky.slnx -c Release --no-restore` (warnings are errors via props) |
| `ExclusionGate` | Compile | fails on reasonless `Stryker disable` or one above a type or member declaration, unjustified `#pragma warning disable` and any `#line` directive in `src/` (coverage exclusions are already banned APIs); on a committed `*.g.cs` anywhere in the repository (case-insensitive); on a `[GeneratedCode]` type or method of a `src/` Release assembly with a sequence point outside `*.g.cs`; and on any coverage-settings or `stryker-config.json` key that narrows what is measured or mutated (§17.1) |
| `Test` | Compile | fails first unless the test projects of `Ducky.Tests.slnf` equal the test projects of `Ducky.slnx` minus exactly the **coverage exclusion list** {`Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`, `Ducky.E2E`} (the one place that list is stated; AotSmoke and PackageSmoke live in their own solutions), so a test project added to `Ducky.slnx` and forgotten in the filter fails the build instead of running only under Stryker; then, with `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS=1` and `CsCheck_Threads=1` (§17.1), `dotnet test --solution Ducky.Tests.slnf -c Release --no-build --coverage --coverage-output-format cobertura --coverage-settings build/coverage.settings.xml --report-trx --results-directory artifacts/test` (MTP syntax, no `--` separator; no `--coverage-output`, so MTP writes a uniquely named file per test project). The solution filter lists the unit, contract and property test projects, and leaves out `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests`, E2E, AotSmoke and PackageSmoke, so every cobertura file in `artifacts/test` comes from a deterministic project |
| `ConcurrencyTest` | Compile | `dotnet test --project test/Ducky.Concurrency.Tests -c Release --no-build --report-trx --results-directory artifacts/concurrency` (no `--coverage`): the real-interleaving tests, ×`DUCKY_REPEAT` |
| `CoverageGate` | Test | ReportGenerator merges every `artifacts/test/**/*.cobertura.xml` (the concurrency project never writes there) → `artifacts/coverage/Cobertura.xml` + HTML. Fails if the set of assemblies in the report differs from the `src/` projects listed in `Ducky.slnx` (a missing assembly fails; the tombstone project, a NoTargets `Sdk`, has no assembly and is excluded; an assembly whose Release PDB has no non-hidden sequence point outside `*.g.cs` has nothing to cover and is allowed to be absent, §17.1; both exemptions are logged), or if a `line-rate`/`branch-rate` is below 1.0 for any of them, printing the uncovered `file:line` list |
| `SpecTraceGate` | Compile | reads `docs/spec/tests.yaml` (the only input: name → project(s) → invariant; a name may list several projects), runs each .NET test project of `Ducky.slnx` with `--list-tests` (Ducky.E2E is a Microsoft.Playwright xUnit project, so its specs are listed the same way). It always fails when a manifest name does not appear in `docs/spec/SPEC.md` or when a backticked name in the §4 "Named tests" column is missing from the manifest. For **active** entries only (`stage <= activeStage`, §24) it also fails when the name is missing from any of its projects (names mapped to `Ducky.AotSmoke` excepted: the `AotSmoke` target checks those) and when an invariant other than INV-24 whose own stage (the manifest's `invariants:` map) is at or below `activeStage` has no active manifest entry in a covered project. With `--strict` (set by `ReleaseGates` for `-rc.N` and GA tags; alpha milestones release the active stage) it also fails while any entry is inactive, and checks all 32 invariants. Matching follows the one rule of §17.1 (a `{Placeholder}` matches `\w+` at any position; a listed test matches on its method name). No other prose is parsed |
| `Pack` | Compile | deletes `artifacts/packages` and `artifacts/tombstone`, then `dotnet pack Ducky.slnx -c Release --no-build` (no `-o`: `PackageOutputPath` from `Directory.Build.props` sends the libraries to `artifacts/packages`, and the tombstone's own `PackageOutputPath` sends it to `artifacts/tombstone`; MinVer versions; package validation runs here, not for the tombstone). Fails unless `artifacts/packages` holds exactly the five library IDs plus five `.snupkg` at `$(MinVerVersion)` and `artifacts/tombstone` holds exactly `Ducky.Generator.2.0.0.nupkg` (R-PKG-6) |
| `PackageSmoke` | Pack | deletes `artifacts/smoke-packages`, then §17.9 |
| `AotSmoke` | Compile | `dotnet workload install wasm-tools`; `dotnet restore Ducky.Aot.slnx` (unlocked); publish `Ducky.AotSmoke` (`-r linux-x64` into a cleaned `artifacts/aot`; `PublishAot=true` is set in its csproj, §10, never as a global `-p:`, which would also reach the netstandard2.0 generator `src/Ducky` builds, NETSDK1207), fail if the output holds any `*.dll` (a managed assembly means `PublishAot` is not in effect and a JIT app would still pass), and run it with a 5-minute limit; it must print only `PASS` lines, each name at most once (P8: an area story extends an assertion, it does not print a name twice), and its set of printed `PASS <name>` names must equal the set of manifest names mapped to `Ducky.AotSmoke` (the manifest check for those names lives here, not in `SpecTraceGate`); the expected set is the **active** manifest entries mapped to `Ducky.AotSmoke`; from stage 18 of §24 on: publish `Ducky.Samples.Wasm` with `-p:RunAOTCompilation=true -p:TrimMode=full -p:RestoreLockedMode=false -o artifacts/wasm-trimmed`; `pwsh test/Ducky.E2E/bin/Release/net10.0/playwright.ps1 install --with-deps chromium`; `dotnet test --project test/Ducky.E2E -c Release --no-build --filter-method "*.WasmTrimmedSmoke" --report-trx --results-directory artifacts/aot` (MTP syntax; Ducky.E2E is built by `Compile`) with `DUCKY_REQUIRE_TRIMMED_PUBLISH=1` and `DUCKY_TRIMMED_PUBLISH_DIR=artifacts/wasm-trimmed/wwwroot`, so a missing publish fails instead of skipping; then it parses the TRX and fails unless exactly one test ran and passed with zero skipped (INV-24) |
| `E2E` | Compile | publishes each sample that exists in `Ducky.slnx` (samples arrive at §24 step 18, so earlier stages publish none and run only the harness specs; sample-driven specs carry stage 18 in the manifest); `pwsh test/Ducky.E2E/bin/Release/net10.0/playwright.ps1 install --with-deps chromium`; `dotnet test --project test/Ducky.E2E -c Release --no-build` (MTP syntax; Chromium, selected by the `DUCKY_BROWSER` environment variable the fixture reads), whose fixture starts and stops the sample hosts (§17.2); then the merged JS block-coverage gate (from step 1 on, over the one-export stub `ducky.js` that §24 step 1 ships, then over the real module from step 12) |
| `E2EAllBrowsers` | Compile | same with `DUCKY_BROWSER=firefox` and `webkit` (functional only) |
| `MutationPr` | Compile | `dotnet stryker --test-runner mtp --since:<BaseRef as a SHA>` (the `dotnet-stryker` local tool, run from `src/<project>` with `DUCKY_REPEAT=1`, `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS=1` and `CsCheck_Threads=1` in the process environment, spike S-6) per active mutated project; a full run for a project whose tests or build inputs changed, or that is inactive at the merge base (§17.8); fails when Stryker's diff misses a changed file (§17.8); in a linked git worktree it runs in a throwaway plain clone (§17.8); break 85 |
| `Mutation` | Compile | full Stryker per **active** mutated project (the manifest's `projects:` stages, §17.8; all seven with `--strict`), or only those named by the optional repeatable `--project <name>`, with `DUCKY_REPEAT=1`, `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS=1` and `CsCheck_Threads=1` in the Stryker process environment (§17.1); HTML reports to `artifacts/mutation`; fails when an active project reports zero mutants or no score (§17.8); opens an issue below 90 (needs `issues: write`, §20.1) |
| `PropertyLong` | Compile | CsCheck long runs (`CsCheck_Iter=100000`) of the property tests, with `DUCKY_REPEAT=1` forced (the 50× repetition would multiply the iterations) and `DUCKY_PROPERTY_SEEDS` unset (random seeds); a failing seed it prints goes into `build/property-seeds.txt` with the fix (§17.1) |
| `Audit` | – | `dotnet restore Ducky.slnx -p:NuGetAudit=true -p:NuGetAuditMode=all -p:WarningsAsErrors=NU1901%3BNU1902%3BNU1903%3BNU1904` (suppressions only as `<NuGetAuditSuppress>` items); the only place audit is enforced, and never over `Ducky.Migration.slnx` |
| `Benchmarks` | Compile | BenchmarkDotNet vs `benchmarks/baseline` (report only) |
| `Docs` | Compile | builds `Ducky.Migration.slnx` (the migration before/after samples, unlocked restore), runs the fence check (§17.1), fails when a diagnostic ID of §8.2 or §16.2 has no `docs/diagnostics/{ID}.md`, fails when a type listed in `docs/migration/ducky-1x-api.txt` has neither a tombstone nor a mapping row in `docs/migration/ducky-1x.md` (§22.1), then docfx to `artifacts/docs` (snippets from regions in `Ducky.Docs.Tests`, the analyzer verifier tests and the migration samples) |
| `VerifyWorkflows` | – | regenerates the workflow YAML into a temp dir and diffs against `.github/workflows` |
| `Changelog` | – | `git-cliff --tag v{ReleaseVersion} -o CHANGELOG.md`; `git-cliff --latest -o artifacts/notes.md`. The GA release PR runs `./build.sh Changelog --release-version 2.0.0`: in a PR, MinVer yields a prerelease height version (`2.0.0-rc.2.7`) and the `v2.0.0` tag doesn't exist yet, so the committed section would otherwise carry the wrong label. git-cliff is not a dotnet tool: `release.yml` installs a pinned binary (§20.1); locally the target fails with an install hint when `git-cliff` is not on `PATH`. It needs full history (`fetch-depth: 0`) |
| `Publish` | –; ordered `After(Ci, E2E, AotSmoke, MutationForSha, PackageSmoke)` | no `DependsOn`: in CI it runs in the `publish` job on the packages that `release-gates` built and uploaded, and a dependency would rebuild the tree (in Fallout 10.4.0, `--skip` leaves a skipped target's dependencies, `Restore` and `Compile`, scheduled) while the short-lived key is live; locally, the `Release` chain has already run `Pack` and `PackageSmoke` through `ReleaseGates`. It first applies `Pack`'s exact-set check to `artifacts/packages` and `artifacts/tombstone`. It requires a `v*` tag and fails unless `git merge-base --is-ancestor $GITHUB_SHA origin/$ReleaseBranch` (`v2` until the GA rename PR flips the default to `main`; the `v2.0.0` tag is pushed before the rename, §21, so it is checked against `origin/v2`); `dotnet nuget push artifacts/packages/*.nupkg --source https://api.nuget.org/v3/index.json --api-key $NUGET_API_KEY --skip-duplicate` (+ `.snupkg`; no committed `nuget.config` defines a `defaultPushSource`, so `--source` is mandatory); then, **only when the tag is exactly `v2.0.0`**, `dotnet nuget push artifacts/tombstone/*.nupkg --source https://api.nuget.org/v3/index.json --api-key $NUGET_API_KEY --skip-duplicate` (without `--api-key` this push would fail after the libraries were pushed, and the tombstone ADR-0045 depends on would never be published). Prerelease and patch tags never push the tombstone, so no non-deprecated Ducky.Generator version appears after the owner's deprecation (ADR-0045) |
| `GitHubRelease` | Publish, Changelog | `gh release create v{ReleaseVersion} artifacts/packages/* --notes-file artifacts/notes.md` (prerelease flag for `-alpha/-rc`), with `GH_TOKEN` from the job environment (§20.1). It opens no PR: a PR created with `GITHUB_TOKEN` triggers no workflows, so its required checks would never report |
| `SetBaseline` | – | maintainer-run after a GA push: writes `PackageValidationBaselineVersion={latest GA on nuget.org}` into `Directory.Build.props`; committed through an ordinary PR (§21) |
| `ShipPublicApi` | – | moves `PublicAPI.Unshipped` → `Shipped` and `AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped` → `Shipped` (it doesn't touch the package validation baseline) |
| `Ci` | Format, ExclusionGate, CoverageGate, ConcurrencyTest, SpecTraceGate, PackageSmoke, VerifyWorkflows, Docs | aggregate for the main PR job |
| `ReleaseGates` | Ci, E2E, AotSmoke | first job of `release.yml`; runs `SpecTraceGate --strict` for `-rc.N` and GA tags (§17.1); uploads `artifacts/packages` and `artifacts/tombstone` |
| `MutationForSha` | – | the `release-mutation` job of `release.yml`: passes if a successful `nightly-mutation` run exists for `$GITHUB_SHA` (`gh run list --workflow nightly-mutation.yml --commit`, needs `actions: read`), otherwise runs `Mutation` (including its zero-mutant check, `--strict` for `-rc.N` and GA tags). It **fails**, and never falls back, when the query itself errors (a 403, a network error): treating an error as "no run found" would silently start a 330-minute run |
| `Release` | ReleaseGates, MutationForSha, Publish, GitHubRelease | local convenience only; CI splits it into three jobs (§20.1) |

Versioning: the `MinVer` MSBuild package through `GlobalPackageReference`, with `MinVerTagPrefix=v`. `MinVerMinimumMajorMinor=2.0` is required: the orphan `v2` branch has no reachable `v*` tag, and MinVer would otherwise produce `0.0.0-alpha`. An initial `v2.0.0-alpha.0` tag is pushed as well. There is one versioning system. The build reads the version only through `ReleaseVersion` (above), falling back to `dotnet msbuild -getProperty:MinVerVersion`; it doesn't use Fallout's `[MinVer]` attribute, which would need the `minver-cli` tool in the manifest.

---

## 20. CI and branch protection

### 20.1 Workflows (generated from `Build.CI.cs`)

Fallout 10.4.0 names every job after its image (`GitHubActionsAttribute.GetJobs` sets `Name = image.GetValue()`), so the check runs GitHub receives would all be called `ubuntu-latest`, `windows-latest` or `macos-latest`: a required context like `ci / ubuntu-latest` would never report, and a bare `ubuntu-latest` would be satisfied by any one of several workflows. `GetJobs` is `protected virtual`, so `build/DuckyGitHubActionsAttribute.cs` derives from `GitHubActionsAttribute` and sets `job.Name` to the workflow name. Each workflow therefore has one image and one uniquely named job, and `VerifyWorkflows` asserts that job names are unique across all workflows.

```csharp
[DuckyGitHubActions("ci", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, FetchDepth = 0,
    OnPullRequestBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" }, OnPushBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" },
    ConcurrencyGroup = "${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}", ConcurrencyCancelInProgress = true,
    InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(Ci) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("ci-cross-windows", GitHubActionsImage.WindowsLatest,
    OnPullRequestBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" }, OnPushBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" },
    ConcurrencyGroup = "${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}", ConcurrencyCancelInProgress = true,
    InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(Test), nameof(ConcurrencyTest) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("ci-cross-macos", GitHubActionsImage.MacOsLatest,
    OnPullRequestBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" }, OnPushBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" },
    ConcurrencyGroup = "${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}", ConcurrencyCancelInProgress = true,
    InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(Test), nameof(ConcurrencyTest) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("e2e", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, OnPullRequestBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" },
    OnPushBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" }, InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(E2E) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("aot", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, OnPullRequestBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" },
    OnPushBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" }, InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(AotSmoke) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("mutation", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, FetchDepth = 0,
    OnPullRequestBranches = new[] { "v2", "main" }, InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(MutationPr) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("nightly-mutation", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, OnCronSchedule = "0 2 * * *", TimeoutMinutes = 330,
    EnableGitHubToken = true, WritePermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Issues },
    ReadPermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Contents, GitHubActionsPermissions.Actions },
    InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(Mutation) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("nightly-e2e", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, OnCronSchedule = "0 3 * * *", TimeoutMinutes = 90,
    EnableGitHubToken = true, WritePermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Issues },
    ReadPermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Contents, GitHubActionsPermissions.Actions },
    InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(E2EAllBrowsers) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("nightly-props", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, OnCronSchedule = "0 3 * * *", TimeoutMinutes = 180,
    EnableGitHubToken = true, WritePermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Issues },
    ReadPermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Contents, GitHubActionsPermissions.Actions },
    InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(PropertyLong) })]
[DuckyGitHubActions("nightly-audit", GitHubActionsImage.UbuntuLatest, OnCronSchedule = "0 4 * * *", TimeoutMinutes = 90,
    EnableGitHubToken = true, WritePermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Issues },
    ReadPermissions = new[] { GitHubActionsPermissions.Contents, GitHubActionsPermissions.Actions },
    InvokedTargets = new[] { nameof(Audit), nameof(Benchmarks) })]
partial class Build { }
```

The four nightly workflows get an explicit token: their `JobEnd` step opens or updates an issue on failure and `Mutation` opens one below 90 (`issues: write`, with `contents`/`actions: read` for the checkout and for looking up existing runs and issues); on a repository whose default `GITHUB_TOKEN` is restricted, those calls would otherwise return 403. `release.yml` grants its own jobs the same (below). The PR workflows keep the default read-only token (`MutationPr` opens no issue).

`IConfigureGitHubActions.ConfigureSteps(pipeline)`: for `e2e` and `aot`, a `PreRun` step that installs the `wasm-tools` workload (`aot`) or the Playwright dependencies.

`schedule` workflows run only from the default branch, so `v2` is the default branch during development (§18); otherwise no nightly would run before GA and `MutationForSha` would always fall back to a full run. The nightly work is split into four workflows, each with one uniquely named job and a `timeout-minutes` under GitHub's 6-hour job limit; spike S-9 measures the full mutation run and sets the `nightly-mutation` budget.

**`release.yml` is hand-written** (header says so; `VerifyWorkflows` excludes it), the way Fallout's own `publish-packages-release.yml` is. It runs on `v*` tags, with three jobs (`release-gates`, `release-mutation`, `publish`):
- `release-gates` (240-minute limit): checks out with `fetch-depth: 0` (MinVer versions the packages from tags), runs `./build.sh ReleaseGates` (Ci, E2E, AotSmoke), then uploads `artifacts/packages` and `artifacts/tombstone`.
- `release-mutation` (in parallel, 330-minute limit, per S-9; `permissions: { contents: read, actions: read, issues: write }` and `GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}`, for the run query and for the issue `Mutation` opens below 90 when it falls back): checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, runs `./build.sh MutationForSha`, which passes at once when a successful `nightly-mutation` run exists for the tagged SHA and otherwise runs the full `Mutation` target.
- `publish` (`needs: [release-gates, release-mutation]`, environment `nuget` with manual approval, permissions `id-token: write`, `contents: write`): checks out with `fetch-depth: 0` (git-cliff and the `git merge-base --is-ancestor $GITHUB_SHA origin/$ReleaseBranch` reachability check need full history and the remote branch), installs a pinned git-cliff binary (`taiki-e/install-action` with an exact `git-cliff@` version), downloads the packages, runs `NuGet/login@v1` (`user: phmatray`) as its first credentialed step, bridges `NUGET_API_KEY` from the step output into `$GITHUB_ENV`, sets `GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}` for `gh release create`, and immediately runs `./build.sh Publish GitHubRelease` (`ReleaseVersion` defaults to the tag name without its `v`). `Publish` has no `DependsOn` (§19), so no `Restore` or `Compile` runs while the key is live and the pushed packages are exactly the uploaded ones, checked by `Publish`'s exact-set check. The short-lived trusted-publishing key (about one hour) is therefore used minutes after it is issued, never after a multi-hour gate run, and `needs` guarantees that nothing is pushed before every gate passed.

Hand-written as well: `pr-title.yml` (`amannn/action-semantic-pull-request`) and `docs.yml` (on release: `Docs`, deploy to GitHub Pages together with the published WASM sample). A failure in any `nightly-*` workflow opens or updates a GitHub issue through a `JobEnd` custom step.

### 20.2 Required checks and branch protection (ADR-0041)

`build/protect-branch.sh` is a checked-in, idempotent `gh api` script, run for `v2` now and for `main` at GA:
- Pull request required, **0 approvals** (solo maintainer; CODEOWNERS is informational).
- Required status checks (strict, i.e. the branch must be up to date), exactly the job names that report: `ci`, `ci-cross-windows`, `ci-cross-macos`, `e2e`, `aot`, `mutation`, `pr-title`.
- Linear history, squash merges only, no force-push or deletion, conversation resolution required, **admins included**.
- Tag ruleset on `v*`: only the maintainer may create them. Rulesets can't restrict tags to commits reachable from a branch, so `Publish` enforces reachability itself (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`, §19).
- Renovate: automerge only minor and patch updates of dev dependencies, only through platform automerge behind the required checks. Roslyn and R3 majors are on an ignore list.

---

## 21. Release process (ADR-0036)

1. **Development**: `v2` is the default branch (§18). Every PR is one story, links its issue, and is gated by §20.2. A push to `v2` produces `2.0.0-alpha.0.N` from MinVer height (CI artifacts only).
2. **Pre-releases**: tag `v2.0.0-alpha.N` (milestones), later `v2.0.0-rc.N`. `release.yml` runs `release-gates` and `release-mutation`, then the `publish` job (environment `nuget` with manual approval) runs `Publish GitHubRelease` and pushes prerelease packages to nuget.org through trusted publishing (§20.1; `Release` is a local convenience target only). A prerelease tag never publishes the Ducky.Generator tombstone: a stable Ducky.Generator 2.0.0 before Ducky 2.0 exists would break every 1.x user with a floating reference on DUCKY900 while no 2.0 is available.
3. **Release candidate acceptance**: NeoSocial (Ducky 1.0.291 / Ducky.Blazor 1.0.292) and BlazorProtectedStorage (1.0.224) are migrated with the migration guide and pass their tests against the rc packages. The time spent is recorded in the guide.
4. **GA**:
   - A release PR runs `ShipPublicApi` and `./build.sh Changelog --release-version 2.0.0` (the explicit version, because MinVer yields a prerelease height version in a PR, §19). It does **not** set the package validation baseline: the tagged commit must validate without a 2.0.0 baseline that isn't on nuget.org yet.
   - Tag `v2.0.0` → `release.yml` (§20.1) publishes Ducky, Ducky.Blazor, Ducky.Reactive, Ducky.Draft, Ducky.Testing and the Ducky.Generator 2.0.0 tombstone. All six share one version, and the tombstone is published once, with this tag only (its version is fixed at 2.0.0, §3).
   - GitHub Release with git-cliff notes. Once the push succeeded, the maintainer runs `./build.sh SetBaseline` and opens an ordinary PR committing `PackageValidationBaselineVersion=2.0.0`, so the required checks run on it (a PR opened by the workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN` would trigger none). Docs and the WASM demo go to GitHub Pages.
   - Rename branches: `main` → `release/1.x`, `v2` → `main`, and make `main` the default branch again. Re-run `protect-branch.sh` for `main`. The first PR on the renamed `main` flips the `ReleaseBranch` default to `main` (§19), so `v2.0.x` patch tags are checked against `origin/main`; `BaseRef` follows `GITHUB_BASE_REF` and needs no change.
5. **Deprecations** (same day, on nuget.org by the owner): Ducky 1.x and Ducky.Blazor 1.x ("Legacy", alternate 2.0.0); Ducky.Generator, all versions ("Legacy", alternate package `Ducky`); Mutty, all versions (alternate package `Ducky.Draft`; the message says Ducky.Draft needs net10.0 and the last Mutty keeps working, unmaintained, on older TFMs; final README pointing to the guide). Archive Mutty after one Ducky.Draft minor.
6. **Retirements with migration guides**: Bustand, BlazorMVU, TheBlazorState, DuckyNext, blazor-state-experiments and the blazor-state monorepo are archived after their READMEs link to the matching guide (`docs/migration/blazor-state.md` for the monorepo and the experiments, `duckynext.md` for DuckyNext). The local-only trials are deleted. Stale Renovate dashboards and 1.x PRs #6/#7 are closed.
7. **Patches**: `v2.0.x` tags from `main` through the same pipeline; they never push the tombstone (§19 `Publish`).

---

## 22. Migration notes

Full guides live in `docs/migration/`. Each guide has a mapping table, a checklist and a compiled before/after sample.

### 22.1 Ducky 1.x → 2.0

Tombstones (ADR-0038) are member-less **interfaces**, never classes, marked `[Obsolete(message, error: true, DiagnosticId = "DUCKYM00n", UrlFormat = "https://github.com/phmatray/Ducky/blob/main/docs/diagnostics/{0}.md")]` and `[EditorBrowsable(Never)]`. They exist only for 1.x types that user code derives from, implements or calls statically (`MemoizedSelector`), taken from the **published 1.0.292 public API** (the version the rc acceptance consumers, NeoSocial on 1.0.291/1.0.292 and BlazorProtectedStorage, and every nuget.org user build against), in their published namespaces, and only where 2.0 has no type of the same name and arity in a namespace users import alongside. Types that exist only on the unreleased `dev` branch (`IAction`, `AsyncEffectGroup`, `MiddlewareBase`, `IStateProvider`, `DuckySelectorComponent<TViewModel>`, the `Ducky.Middlewares.AsyncEffect` namespace) get none: no released consumer uses them. A tombstone for a 1.x class is still an interface: a user type that derives from it gets the same DUCKYM error on its base-list entry (plus the CS0115 its overrides would get from any member-less tombstone), and an interface has no constructor, so there is nothing to cover and test code, which can't reference an error-level `[Obsolete]` type, never has to. Spike S-2 verifies that the coverage tool reports nothing uncovered for them. They are listed in PublicAPI and removed in 3.0.

| ID | 1.x type (namespace, as published in 1.0.292) | 2.0 replacement |
|---|---|---|
| DUCKYM001 | `IState`, `IKeyedAction` (`Ducky`) | delete the base: any object is an action or a state |
| DUCKYM002 | `SliceReducers<TState>`, `ISlice<TState>` (`Ducky`) | `sealed class X : Slice<TState>` with `protected override TState Initial =>` |
| DUCKYM003 | `AsyncEffect<TAction>`, `IAsyncEffect` (`Ducky`) | `Effect<TAction>`: constructor injection, `Handle(action, EffectContext, CancellationToken)`, `ctx.Dispatch`, `ctx.State` |
| DUCKYM004 | `IReactiveEffect` (`Ducky`) | Ducky.Reactive's `ReactiveEffect` (R3 `Observable<object>`) |
| DUCKYM005 | `MemoizedSelector` (`Ducky`) | `Selector.Define(...)` + `def.Create()` |
| DUCKYM006 | `Fsa`, `Fsa<TPayload>`, `Fsa<TPayload, TMeta>`, `FsaError`, `FsaError<TMeta>`, `FsaMeta<TMeta>` (`Ducky`) | a plain `record` (no base type) |
| DUCKYM007 | `NormalizedState<TKey, TEntity, TState>` (`Ducky`) | an `EntityState<TKey, TEntity>` member of the state record (§5.10) |
| DUCKYM008 | `DuckyLayout<TState>` (`Ducky.Blazor`) | the non-generic `DuckyLayout` + `Select` in `OnInitialized`; the arity-1 tombstone coexists with it |

`docs/migration/ducky-1x-api.txt` commits the 1.0.292 public type list of Ducky and Ducky.Blazor, and the `Docs` target fails when a type in it has neither a tombstone above nor a mapping row in `docs/migration/ducky-1x.md` (§19), so the guide can't silently skip a published type.

Mapping-table-only changes:
- `IMiddleware` (`Ducky`) → `Middleware` (no tombstone: it would clash with `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Http.IMiddleware`, which Web SDK implicit usings import)
- `AddDuckyBlazor`/`BlazorDuckyBuilder` → `AddDucky(d => d.AddBlazor())`
- `ScanAssemblies`/implicit scan → `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}()`
- `UseDefaultMiddlewares` → nothing
- `EnablePersistence(...)` → `Persist<T>(...)` per slice
- `EnableDevTools` → `UseDevTools()` guarded by `IsDevelopment()`
- `EnableJsLogging` → removed (use DevTools and ILogger)
- `CrossTabSync` → `o.SyncAcrossTabs = true`
- `WhenSliceChanges` → `Select(selector, onChange, comparer)`
- `IExceptionHandler`/`DuckyErrorBoundary` → an effect on `ReducerFailed`/`EffectFailed`
- `IStoreEventPublisher`/`*EventArgs` → `ILogger`, `ActivitySource`, `Meter`
- `IsHydrated` → `State.Get<PersistenceState>().Status`
- `StoreInitializer` → optional `<DuckyInitializer>`
- `global using Ducky.Generators;` → `using Ducky;`
- global `ActionDispatcher` → `{Record}DispatchExtensions` in the record's namespace
- `[DuckyEffect]`/`[DuckyReducer]`/`[InitialState]`/`[DuckyComponent]` → removed
- `ReactiveEffect` in `Ducky` → install Ducky.Reactive (R3 `Observable<object>`); no tombstone, because it would be ambiguous with `Ducky.Reactive.ReactiveEffect` for users importing both namespaces
- `ReactiveEffectTestHelper` → Ducky.Testing + R3 `FakeTimeProvider`
- `DuckyComponent<TState>.Dispatcher.X(...)` (55 call sites in the 1.x demos alone) → `Store.X(...)`: the generated helpers work on `IStore`, and 2.0 components have no `Dispatcher` property (`DuckyComponent<TState>` keeps its name and arity, so no tombstone)
- `IRootState.GetSliceState<T>()` / `GetSliceState<T>(key)`, `store.GetSlice<T>()`, `GetSliceByKey`, `TryGetSlice`, `GetSliceKeys` → `State.Get<T>()`, `State.Get(key)`, `State.TryGet<T>()`, `State.Keys`
- `protected override void OnAfterSubscribed()` → `OnInitialized()` after the `Select` calls (2.0 doesn't seal it)
- `Store.StateChanged +=` → `Store.Select(s => s, onChange)` (dispose the `Selection` to unsubscribe)
- `services.AddDucky(IConfiguration?, Action<DuckyOptions>?)` / `AddDuckyCore(DuckyOptions)` / `DuckyOptions` → `AddDucky(d => …)` (same name, incompatible signature, so the compiler reports CS1503; configuration binding is removed, matrix breaking change 24)
- `IDispatcher.ActionStream` → a Ducky.Reactive `ReactiveEffect` (its `actions` observable); `IDispatcher.LastAction` → `TestStore.Processed` in tests, or a `Middleware` in the app
- `Ducky.Blazor.Router` (`RouterStoreState`, `RouterReducers`, `Router*Action`) → no 2.0 equivalent: removed, keep routing out of the store (§1.2)

Behaviour changes, each called out with a test in the guide:
- Change detection is `ReferenceEquals`, not `Equals` (DUCKY011 flags `s with { }`).
- Handler matching is exact-type, including effects: `AsyncEffect<TBase>` no longer sees subtypes (DUCKY005).
- A sync `Dispatch` from another thread no longer blocks until reduced; use `DispatchAsync`.
- Reducer exceptions are never rethrown; they become `ReducerFailed`, and the action commits nothing.
- `MaxReentrantDepth` (queue length) becomes `MaxDispatchDepth` (synchronous causal depth).
- Subscribers and effects may run on a thread-pool thread.
- Actions dispatched before init are buffered.
- Persisted 1.x data is **discarded** (new envelope, no AssemblyQualifiedName).
- DevTools options are trimmed (Realtime, Latency, CollapseActions, StackTrace* removed; Trace is off by default).
- `UseJson` is required for DevTools payloads, persistence and prerender.

Checklist:
1. Update the packages and remove Ducky.Generator (DUCKY900 says so).
2. Fix each DUCKYM error.
3. Add a `JsonSerializerContext` (it may start empty) and pass it with `UseJson(AppJson.Default)`. DUCKY006 then lists every `[DuckyAction]` and slice state type missing from it, and DUCKY306 at startup names any persisted or prerendered state type still missing.
4. Replace the registration chain.
5. Run the app's tests.

### 22.2 Bustand → SliceStore

A mechanical rename for stores without injected services; stores whose methods await injected services need one extra effect. Bustand's `SetAsync` takes a **synchronous** mutator (`SetAsync(Func<TState,TState>)` or `SetAsync(TState)`), so it maps directly; only a store method that awaits I/O and then sets state becomes an effect. The guide has a compiled before/after sample of Bustand's `UserStore(IUserService)`, whose test uses `EffectTest.Run<LoadUser, StoreInitialized>(…, services: s => s.AddSingleton<IUserService>(fake))` and `TestStore.GetSlice<UserStore>()` (§15).

| Bustand | Ducky 2.0 |
|---|---|
| `ZustandStore<TState>`, `InitialState` | `SliceStore<TState>`, `Initial` |
| `IStore<TState>` | inject the SliceStore itself |
| `Set(Func)` / `Set(TState)` | `Set(Func)` unchanged / `Set(_ => value)` |
| `SetAsync(Func)` / `SetAsync(TState)` | `SetAsync(Func)` (returns `Task<DispatchResult>` and completes after the reduce) / `SetAsync(_ => value)`; both are `protected`, as in Bustand |
| constructor-injected services, an overridden `InitializeAsync`, or a store method that awaits a service and then sets state | an `Effect` (an `Effect<StoreInitialized>` for the initial load) that injects the service and the SliceStore, awaits the work with its run token and calls a public SliceStore method wrapping `Set`, or returning `SetAsync`'s task for read-your-writes: the sample's `UserStore` declares `public Task<DispatchResult> SetUserAsync(User u) => SetAsync(_ => new(u));`, which its `LoadUser` effect awaits |
| `Subscribe(Action)`, `Subscribe<TSlice>(selector, callback)`, the `StateChanged` event | `store.Select<TState, T>(selector, onChange)` (dispose the `Selection` to unsubscribe; `onChange` runs on the drainer, §7 rule 6) |
| `IsInitialized` | `State.Get<PersistenceState>().Status`, or an effect on `StoreInitialized` |
| an `OnStateChanged()` override | an `onChange` callback, or a `Middleware` with `AfterReduce` |
| `IMiddleware<TState>` / `IAsyncMiddleware<TState>` | `Middleware` / an `Effect` (middleware hooks are synchronous, ADR-0004) |
| `ZustandComponent<TStore,TState>.UseState(sel)`, `UseStateResult<T>` | `DuckyComponent.Select(sel)` in `OnInitialized` (or `<DuckySelect>`), `Selection<T>` |
| `ZustandComponentScoped<TStore,TState>`, `ZustandScope` | `DuckyComponent` over the app store; no component-scoped store in 2.0 (CMP-03 is post-2.0) |
| `[BustandStore]` + Scrutor | the generated `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}()` |
| `[Persist(StorageType)]` | `[Persist(Storage = PersistStorage.Session)]` (or `Local`) |
| Bustand.DevTools | Redux DevTools (`UseDevTools()`) |
| `BustandCircuitHandler` | nothing (interactivity is automatic, ADR-0029) |

Bustand's lost updates are fixed because mutators run at reduce time.

### 22.3 TheBlazorState → slices

A component `[Persist] partial` property becomes a field of a slice state plus `Persist<TSlice>(o => o.MaxAge = ttl)`. A `[Shared]` POCO becomes a `SliceStore<TState>` whose setters are `Set` methods. `LoadFrom` becomes an `Effect<StoreInitialized>` with `WasRestored<TState>()`. `KeySuffix` becomes `BlazorOptions.Scope` (re-resolved on user change, not frozen). `StateMeta.WasRestored` becomes `State.WasRestored<T>()`. The PersistentComponentState bridge becomes `Prerender<TSlice>()`. `CrossTabHub` has no equivalent (rejected: cross-user leak). `IndexedDbStrategy` comes in 2.1.

### 22.4 BlazorMVU → slices and effects

`Init()` → `Initial`. An `Update(model, msg)` switch → one `On<Msg>` per case. `Cmd.OfTask` → an `Effect<Msg>` (with `ctx.Run` for the triad). `Sub.Timer` → a `ReactiveEffect` with `Observable.Interval(period, timeProvider)`, or a `LongRunning` polling effect. `SimpleMvuComponent` local state → a slice (global), or plain component fields where it is truly local. `Middleware.Debounce/Throttle` → an `Effect` with `Switch` + `ctx.Time`, or an Rx `Debounce`. `TimeTravelDebugger` → Redux DevTools.

### 22.5 Mutty → Ducky.Draft

See §13.4.

### 22.6 DuckyNext and the blazor-state monorepo

These are concept sources only. `docs/migration/duckynext.md` covers DuckyNext; `docs/migration/blazor-state.md` covers the blazor-state monorepo and blazor-state-experiments (its libraries map to the Bustand, TheBlazorState, BlazorMVU and Mutty guides). The guide maps DuckyNext's partial-method reducers to `On<T>`, `[ReduxAsyncEffect]` triads to `ctx.Run`, generated typed roots to `State.Get<T>()`, and its RxMiddleware to Ducky.Reactive.

---

## 23. Spikes and risks (prototypes in week 1; regression tests at their §24 stage)

Each spike has two parts. In week 1, before feature work, a **throwaway prototype** under `spikes/` (outside every solution and gate, §18) answers the platform question with a minimal Blazor app or dispatcher stub; its exit criterion is a prototype check, and a fallback it triggers is designed into the §24 steps before they start. The named test in the last column is the **regression test**: it is already in the manifest with the stage of the step that builds its feature, and it re-checks the answer on the real code then. Several of these tests need persistence (step 12), samples (step 18) or seven populated mutated projects, so none of them can be a week-1 exit criterion.

| Spike | Question | Week-1 prototype exit criterion | Regression (stage) |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-1 | Exact `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` version in SDK 10.0.100 (the generator floor) | pinned in CPM with a Renovate ignore rule; a consumer job builds with exactly 10.0.100 | the pin itself (step 1) |
| S-2 | Can the coverage tool reach 100% line and branch **without exclusions**, with the pinned xunit.v3/MTP versions (the matrix baseline records the MTP coverage extension crashing with xunit.v3 3.2.2)? A probe assembly holds async methods (state-machine `IsCompleted` branches), records (synthesized `Equals`/`PrintMembers`), a lowered `lock` and `await using`, pattern-matching switch expressions (default arms), a member-less error-level `[Obsolete]` interface (the tombstone shape, §22.1), and a generator loaded through `CSharpGeneratorDriver` | the probe's tests reach 100% line and branch with no exclusion and no crash. Otherwise pick coverlet.MTP or the tool settings that skip compiler-generated code (for example auto-properties and synthesized members), re-run the probe, and record the choice | `CoverageGate` (every step) |
| S-3 | AOT-clean `PersistentComponentState` seed path | a prototype WASM app, published trimmed with AOT, persists and takes a `byte[]` seed with zero IL warnings (preferred: a `JsonTypeInfo`-based API; fallback: the audited `byte[]` suppression), before step 11 designs the seed | `WasmTrimmedSmoke` (18) |
| S-4 | CsCheck `SampleParallel` at 50 repetitions within the CI budget (< 3 min) | a prototype dispatcher stub (a lock-protected queue with one drainer) runs a `SampleParallel` property at 50 repetitions under 3 min; operation counts tuned | re-run against the real dispatcher at step 2 (`Linearizability_DispatchVsModel`), resetting the counts |
| S-5 | `IJSStreamReference` read path on Server at 100 KB and about 2 MB | a prototype Server app round-trips 2 MB from `localStorage` through `IJSStreamReference` without closing the circuit, and a pull export returning an empty `Uint8Array` yields a zero-length reference (§11.9) | `Persist_LargeSlice_OnServer_RoundTrips` (18) |
| S-6 | Stryker.NET with `test-runner: mtp` (experimental) on the pinned xunit.v3/MTP v2 stack, which all seven mutated projects use: net10.0 libraries (plain SDK and Razor SDK), netstandard2.0 generators, `--since` and full runs; whether `DUCKY_REPEAT` set on the Stryker process reaches the test hosts; whether the boolean mutator mutates `ConfigureAwait(false)` literals (§17.8) | a mutation score reported with `--since` and with a full run for three prototype projects with real logic: a plain library, a generator, and a **Razor SDK library shaped like Ducky.Blazor** (STJ and `[LoggerMessage]` source generators, IVT to two test projects), with mutants > 0 per project (the `mutate` glob matches files, §17.8), a concurrency test observed running once per mutant, and the `ConfigureAwait` answer recorded | the staged zero-mutant check of `Mutation` (each project's stage, §17.8) |
| S-7 | .NET 10 WASM: are `PersistentComponentState` and `IJSRuntime` resolvable with identical instances from the root/store scope? Is `AuthenticationStateProvider` scoped in the templates and in RemoteAuthenticationService/MSAL (Ducky.Blazor takes it from the renderer scope in any case)? Does the prerender JS runtime still throw `InvalidOperationException` synchronously from the import call? Does a paused and resumed circuit deliver `ducky:seed` to a scoped service's `TryTakeFromJson`? Does a middleware's `RegisterOnPersisting(…, RenderMode.InteractiveAuto)` persist the seed in a single-mode app and in an app with both render modes configured (§11.4)? Does a circuit reconnect (`ComponentHub.ConnectCircuit`) or resume (`ResumeCircuit`) raise `AuthenticationStateChanged` on a live store, and does `DuckyScopes.NameIdentifier` then complete synchronously, so the re-assertion check of §11.6 absorbs it? | prototype WASM, Server and Auto apps answer each question with a page that prints the result; any "no" enables the `DuckyComponent` hand-off fallback for the other services too (§6.10) before step 10 designs the gate | E2E `Wasm_DelegatingHandlerDispatch_ReachesUiStore`, `Wasm_UserChange_ScopedBrowserKeysMoveToNewScope`, `Gate_Unknown_ProbeDetectsPrerender`, `Circuit_Resume_SeedRestoresPrerenderSlices`, `Prerender_ServerAndWasmModesConfigured_SeedPersistedAndApplied`, `Circuit_ReconnectWithScopedSlices_NoResetFlicker` (18) |
| S-8 | `DuckyGitHubActionsAttribute`: does overriding `GetJobs` give each workflow's job the workflow name without other side effects? | generated YAML shows the job names of §20.2; `VerifyWorkflows` passes | `VerifyWorkflows` (every step) |
| S-9 | Full Stryker run time over the seven mutated projects on `ubuntu-latest` | the per-project split is designed up front (`Mutation --project <name>`, §19), and the prototype's per-project time gives the first `timeout-minutes` | re-measured at steps 13 and 17, when the projects hold their real logic: `nightly-mutation` and `release-mutation` get a `timeout-minutes` with 30% headroom under 360, or the nightly is split into per-project jobs |

Residual risks, documented rather than hidden:
- Server + browser storage loses a change that is still unwritten when the circuit is disposed (pending in the debounce, or retrying after a disconnect). Mitigation: `PersistStorage.Server`.
- After a failed hydration, the first user change overwrites stored data.
- DevTools time travel vetoes late effect results.
- Ducky.Reactive work is excluded from quiescence: `WhenIdleAsync`, `Settled` and the prerender seed never wait for a reactive pipeline, so `Prerender<T>` slices must be loaded by an `Effect<T>` (§14).
- Synthetic `SetState` actions are not replayable in DevTools.
- The sync-`Dispatch` contract is weaker than 1.x (§7.3).
- Asynchronous recurrence loops are not depth-guarded (by design, §6.5).
- In the browser, scoped dependencies of effects are distinct from the renderer scope's instances (§6.10).
- Interactive Auto: Server storage is inert on the WASM side (restored only from the prerender seed).
- Server storage: concurrent circuits of one scope (one per tab, plus static-SSR form-post stores) are last-writer-wins on whole slices; `IDistributedCache` has no compare-and-swap (`DistributedCache_TwoCircuitsSameUser_LastWriterWins`). Possible 2.1 mitigation: re-read the key when a circuit regains focus.
- Server storage: a prerender store's writes and its flush on request-scope dispose can land after the circuit store already read or wrote the same key, overwriting newer state (`DistributedCache_PrerenderFlushAfterCircuitWrite_Documented`). With per-page interactivity, a new circuit's hydration can likewise read before the previous circuit's in-flight write lands.
- Static SSR: a redirect after a form post (Post/Redirect/Get) can render the value from before the post, because the request-scope flush runs after the redirect response was sent (`DistributedCache_StaticSsrPostRedirect_StaleReadDocumented`). ponytail: no public flush API in 2.0; upgrade path (2.1): a `FlushPersistenceAsync` over the phase-5a flush, awaited before `NavigateTo` in the static SSR form recipe.
- WASM: a page unload never disposes the root provider; a change committed just before unload can be lost between the commit and the writer's next turn.
- A change made to a scoped slice while the user switches is not persisted and is replaced by the new user's stored value (§11.6).
- On Server, `IStore` is circuit-scoped: code outside the circuit scope (a `DelegatingHandler`, a hosted service, a SignalR hub) gets a different, empty store and must use the app's circuit-accessor pattern (`ServerLifetime_HandlerScope_GetsDistinctStore`).
- A resumed .NET 10 circuit is a fresh store: persisted and `Prerender<T>` slices come back, other slices reset.
- Liveness ceilings of the drainer (unbounded drain latency under a hot producer, synchronous fan-out not bounded by depth) are documented, not guarded (§7).
- Assembly names that differ only in non-identifier characters produce the same generated registration name (§16.1).
- Fallout is not owned by the owner. Mitigation: thin targets and an exit path (§19).

---

## 24. Implementation order (one issue and one PR per story, each behind the full gate for its stage)

**The test manifest is staged**, so every PR can pass the required `ci` and `aot` checks before the last story lands. Every `docs/spec/tests.yaml` entry carries `stage: <step below>` (the step that implements the test), and the manifest has one top-level `activeStage`, which each story PR bumps to its own step. `SpecTraceGate` always runs the name-consistency checks (manifest name ↔ SPEC.md, §4 name ↔ manifest), checks test existence only for entries with `stage <= activeStage`, and checks the covered-test rule per invariant: each invariant has its own stage in the manifest (`invariants: { INV-01: 2, …, INV-32: 8 }`, the step that introduces it), and for every invariant whose stage is at or below `activeStage`, except INV-24, at least one active entry mapped to it must be in a covered project (§19). An invariant whose only active test is in `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests` therefore fails as soon as its stage is active. The `AotSmoke` target compares the printed `PASS` names with the active `Ducky.AotSmoke` entries, and the trimmed WASM smoke joins at step 18. `ReleaseGates` runs `SpecTraceGate --strict` for `-rc.N` and GA tags, which fails while any entry is inactive, so no release candidate ships with part of the catalogue unimplemented. `CoverageGate` takes its assembly list from the `src/` projects in `Ducky.slnx`, and step 1 gives each library project one public type (of its final API) with a test. The other required checks are staged the same way: `Mutation`/`MutationForSha` apply the zero-mutant check only to projects whose stage (`projects:` in the manifest, §17.8) is active; each PackageSmoke consumer or assertion is added by the story that ships its feature (§17.9); `E2E` publishes only samples that exist and sample-driven specs carry stage 18 (§19). **A generator or analyzer branch that targets another package's API, or a Ducky API from a later step, ships in the story that ships that API**, with its verifier or generator tests at that stage (steps 7–17 below name them), so no emit or analyzer branch is unreachable, and no stub stands in for the real symbol (GEN-06), while `CoverageGate` measures 100% of Ducky.Generators from step 7 on. Microsoft.Testing.Platform exits with code 8 when a test module runs zero tests, so step 1 gives **every** test project in `Ducky.slnx` one real test, never `--ignore-exit-code 8` (which would hide a filter that matches nothing). So every gate is green from the first PR (ADR-0037).

1. Repository skeleton, `v2` made the default branch, Fallout build, CI, branch protection, gates (each library project has one public type of its final §5–§15 API and a test, so 100% coverage holds; a project whose step-1 type is code-less in the final API, `Ducky.Draft` and `Ducky.Testing`, is absent from the coverage report and passes as having nothing to cover, §17.1; every test project has one real test, named `Skeleton_{Project}_Smoke` in the manifest (stage 1, no invariant) and deleted with its entry by the story whose tests replace it: a `Ducky.Concurrency.Tests` Barrier test over the step-1 type, a `Ducky.Docs.Tests` region test, and a `Ducky.E2E` harness spec over a one-export stub `ducky.js`, which keeps the 100%-block gate meaningful from day one; the PackageSmoke solution with only its step-1 checks; `activeStage: 1`), `docs/spec/tests.yaml` with every entry, invariant and mutated project staged, and the week-1 spike prototypes (§23).
2. Slice, snapshot, single-drainer dispatcher, causal scope, TST-03 items 1–13; S-4 re-run against the real dispatcher.
3. Init lifecycle and init buffer, middleware, failure router, dispose.
4. Effects and policies (the policy matrix and the model-based property).
5. Selectors, `Selection<T>`, `Select` and subscribers.
6. JSON gateway, action names, diagnostics, unhandled check.
7. Generators and analyzers, the multi-assembly fixture, tombstones: GEN-01, GEN-02's slice and effect registration and its DUCKY022 "type or method doesn't resolve" branch (tested with compilations that omit the Ducky.Blazor and Ducky.Reactive references), and the analyzer rules and arms whose target APIs exist by step 6 (among them DUCKY006, DUCKY008, DUCKY013, DUCKY016, DUCKY025, the `[Persist]` arm of DUCKY012, the `On<T>`/`Effect<T>`/`EffectGroup.On<T>` arms of DUCKY005 and the reducer and projector arms of DUCKY001–003).
8. `SliceStore`, `EntityState`; the `Set`/`SetAsync` mutator arms of DUCKY001–003 and the `SetAsync` arm of DUCKY010, with verifier tests against the real assembly.
9. Ducky.Testing; the `TestStore` member arm of DUCKY021.
10. Blazor subscription core and components; DUCKY009.
11. Interactivity gate and prerender handoff; GEN-02's `Prerender<T>` emission.
12. Browser persistence and the persistence slice; GEN-02's `PersistAttributeDefaults<T>` emission, the `Persist<T>()` arm of DUCKY012, `Registration_RealBlazor_EmitsPersistAndPrerender_NoDucky022` and the three generator-emitted attribute tests (`PersistAttribute_NoArguments_SlicePersisted`, `PersistAttributePlusBuilderMigrate_Composes`, `AddBlazor_AfterGeneratedPersist_ConfigureApplied`, in Ducky.Blazor.Generated.Tests, §18), all at stage 12.
13. Server `IDistributedCache` provider; S-9 re-measured.
14. Cross-tab.
15. DevTools.
16. Ducky.Reactive; GEN-02's `ReactiveEffect` registration and `AddReactiveEffect<T>` emission, `Registration_RealReactive_EmitsAddReactiveEffect_NoDucky022`, and the `OfActionType<T>` arm of DUCKY005.
17. Ducky.Draft runtime, then the Draft generator; the Draft-receiver exemption of DUCKY003 (verified against the real `Ducky.Draft.IDraft<T>`); S-9 re-measured.
18. AOT/trim smoke tests, E2E, docs, samples, migration guides.
19. RC and consumer migrations; GA.

## Appendix B. Matrix conflicts and open questions

### B.1 The 18 conflicts

| # | Conflict | Resolution | ADR |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Hooks vs onion middleware | synchronous hooks; retry inside effects; errors via failure actions | 0004 |
| 2 | Effect shape | one core `Effect<T>`; Rx optional; streams and Cmd later | 0005 |
| 3 | Rx library | R3 | 0006 |
| 4 | Change detection vs Mutty | `ReferenceEquals`; Ducky.Draft preserves identity | 0007 |
| 5 | Handler matching | exact type everywhere; duplicates throw; DUCKY005 | 0008 |
| 6 | Root state shape | registry with typed accessors | 0009 |
| 7 | Two ways to change state | SliceStore ships in 2.0 as a facade over named actions | 0010 |
| 8 | Persistence vs seed precedence | storage wins (one extra render) | 0011 |
| 9 | `[Persist]` vs builder | builder is the source of truth; attribute is generator sugar | 0012 |
| 10 | DevTools extension vs panel | extension now, panel later | 0013 |
| 11 | Dispatch contract | single drainer; `DispatchAsync` after reduce; `WhenIdleAsync`/`Settled` after effects | 0014 |
| 12 | Gate vs load-before-store | the init buffer is the gate, inert when nothing is persisted | 0015 |
| 13 | Server loss window vs `IDistributedCache` | both: documented window for browser storage, `PersistStorage.Server` in 2.0 | 0016 |
| 14 | Registration naming | per-assembly `AddDuckyGenerated_{Asm}` | 0017 |
| 15 | Unhandled strictness | Debug log; strict in `TestStore` | 0018 |
| 16 | Produce: Draft vs Mutty | fold into Ducky.Draft; deprecate Mutty | 0019 |
| 17 | JS coverage bar | 100% block coverage via Playwright CDP | 0020 |
| 18 | TFM and Roslyn floor | net10.0 only; Roslyn of SDK 10.0.100 | 0003 |

### B.2 The 20 open questions (all answered)

1. Package identity → Ducky/Ducky.Blazor 2.0.0; Ducky.Generator deprecated, with a 2.0.0 tombstone package (ADR-0002, 0045).
2. Starting point → clean rewrite on orphan `v2`; nothing from the `wip/local-only-*` branches is carried over (ADR-0001).
3. Rx → R3 (ADR-0006).
4. `IAction` removal → accepted (ADR-0021).
5. Middleware → synchronous hooks (ADR-0004).
6. Weaker sync `Dispatch` → accepted; `DispatchAsync` after reduce; `WhenIdleAsync`/`Settled` for effects (ADR-0014).
7. Exact-type matching → everywhere (ADR-0008).
8. User-owned JSON context → required for payloads; name-only fallback (ADR-0022).
9. Server persistence → `IDistributedCache` provider in 2.0 (ADR-0016).
10. Registration names → per-assembly (ADR-0017).
11. SliceStore → 2.0 (ADR-0010).
12. Mutty → folded into Ducky.Draft and deprecated (ADR-0019).
13. Hosting models → WASM, Interactive Server, Interactive Auto, static SSR with prerender; net10.0 only (§11.3, ADR-0003).
14. Zero-bug gate → 100% line+branch on all library C#, JS 100% block, Stryker break 85 / target 90 (ADR-0037).
15. 1.x persisted data → discarded (ADR-0033).
16. Decommissioning → §21 steps 5–6.
17. Consumer migration → NeoSocial and BlazorProtectedStorage are the rc acceptance test (§21).
18. NUKE vs plain Actions → Fallout (owner decision), thin targets (ADR-0035).
19. License → Apache-2.0; no Fluxor or EF Core code carried over (ADR-0042).
20. EFF-08 / DT-03 → post-GA.
